# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1997

## Introduction by Devāmṛta Swami

Let the planetary systems arrange themselves so that all the constellations become auspicious. May the stars that herald blessedness begin to adorn the sky, as portents of good fortune pervade the earth, especially in the hearts and minds of the people. Everywhere, in all the ten directions, an atmosphere of Vedic peace and prosperity shall prevail.

Nature will reveal the peak of her splendor: pure rivers flowing musically, transparent lakes supporting lotus flowers of various colors, and exotic birds singing with the sweetest voices. Throughout the land, feel the pleasant breezes gently blowing, laden with fragrances of spring. At the seashore mild waves resound, and in the clear blue sky perfect white clouds occasionally thunder harmoniously. Meanwhile, on the heavenly planets, the residents—inaugurating their annual celebration—sing, dance, and shower flowers down upon the earth.

When everything in the universe becomes adjusted like this, then we may fittingly begin our attempt to glorify the advent of Śrīla Prabhupāda, the confidential ambassador of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

\* \* \*

Invoking all reverence and humility, we welcome you to the 1997 version of mission impossible. Full delineation of the glories of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is beyond our capacity. We do indeed have the word “impossible” in our dictionary. Yet once a year our sacred duty and privilege is to attempt the impossible: to offer suitable praise to Lord Kṛṣṇa’s choice for preceptor of the present age.

Certainly we are aware that our sincere efforts to honor the qualities and achievements of Śrīla Prabhupāda verge upon the inconceivable. Amazingly, by his grace we are actually convinced that the inconceivable, *acintya,* is not impersonal. The scriptures reveal that the most unfathomable secret of existence is how the Supreme Personality of Godhead personally reciprocates with his dearest devotees.

The Supreme Lord, who is greater than the greatest, becomes submissive to even a very insignificant devotee because of his devotional service. It is the beautiful and exalted nature of devotional service that the infinite Lord becomes submissive to the infinitesimal living entity because of it. In reciprocal devotional activities with the Lord, the devotee actually enjoys the transcendental mellow of devotional service. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 7.145\]

Additionally, we know Śrī Uddhava’s analysis of how the Lord personally intervenes in the prison house of *māyā* to reform his rebellious parts and parcels:

O my Lord! Transcendental poets and experts in spiritual science could not fully express their indebtedness to You, even if they were endowed with the prolonged lifetime of Brahmā, for You appear in two features—externally as the *ācārya* and internally as the Supersoul—to deliver the embodied living being by directing him how to come to You. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 1.48\]

We have done our homework, studied the *śāstra,* and mined the scriptural gems. Still, glorification of Śrīla Prabhupāda always leaves a lingering question mark, both blissfully inspiring and soberly profound. When we reflect upon Śrīla Prabhupāda, what exactly is the immensity we are dealing with?

First, we seem to have a peculiar case of a solitary missionary, very elderly and frail, who survives two heart attacks at sea just to land in New York almost penniless. With no fixed residence or support, he talks about trying to carry out the orders of his spiritual master, though obviously he is at the end of his life.

Second, we watch, as for the first time in conventional history, *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* spreads beyond Indian shores to every continent of the world.

Third, we witness this most extraordinary person speak of a coming global transformation directly resulting from the movement he founded.

To write matchless Vedic scriptures and set into motion their worldwide distribution is unparalleled, no doubt.

To establish temples with authorized worship of Śrī Vigraha on every continent is unthinkable, certainly.

Yet, for him to dare propose that the future holds more…! Our minds are still numb at the thought.

Vaiṣṇava history is full of extraordinary predictions. Sometimes these divine forecasts far exceed even the comprehension of devotees. Śukadeva Gosvāmī, in the Twelfth Canto, foretold all the present symptoms of the Kali-yuga. Lord Caitanya, as recorded in the *Caitanya-bhāgavata,* predicted, *pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma / sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma:* “In every town and village of the world, My name will be heard.” \[*CB Antya-khaṇḍa* 4.126\] Elderly well-wishers of Śrīla Prabhupāda admitted that before the advent of his ISKCON they thought Mahāprabhu’s words were meant as a poetic devotional outpouring, never to manifest literally. At best, they reasoned, the Lord was simply referring to every town on the Indian subcontinent.

We have all often heard of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s two famous assurances. He wrote that one day foreign devotees would gather in Māyāpur chanting “Jaya Śacīnandana!” We know that Śrīla Prabhupāda actualized that miracle, and then founded the work to realize the second of the Ṭhākura’s astonishing visions. Quoting Lord Nityānanda in his *Navadvīpa-dhāma-mahātmyā,* Śrīla Bhaktivinoda foresaw: *adbhuta maṇḍira ei haibe prakāśa / gaurāṅga nitya-sevā haibe vikāśa:*

“An astounding temple will appear in Māyāpur. From it the whole world will learn Śrī Gaurāṅga’s eternal service.”

In last year’s introduction to the Vyāsa-pūjā book, we read of śāstric references that did more than foretell unprecedented global preaching. These texts obviously directly referred to our Śrīla Prabhupāda’s divine appearance. The *Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa* announced the coming of the *mantra-upāsaka,* the global worshiper of the holy name. The *Caitanya-maṅgala* proclaimed the future appearance of the *senapati-bhakta,* Lord Gaurāṅga’s special military field commander, who would pursue fugitives from *kṛṣṇa-upadeśa* to the ends of the earth.

True to the Vaiṣṇava line, Śrīla Prabhupāda also made his own startling forecasts. Early in his last year of physical presence, he calmly asserted that in the future historians would look back upon his ISKCON years as a major turning point in the course of world history. A “historical renaissance,” he called it. Historians would be able to trace, His Divine Grace stated, how from his twelve years of world preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness changed life on earth.

Obviously these are not light words. Though a transcendental poet and scholar, certainly Śrīla Prabhupāda knew the habit of mundane intellectuals to highlight certain periods of human history as pivotal. Generally, they tell us there have been four or five major transitions in Western so-called civilization. The fall of the Roman Empire and the end of the Middle Ages are the two most recent watersheds, they say.

After studying the classic periods of change in the Western world, the reputable historian Lewis Mumford noted, “Every transformation of man has rested on a new metaphysical and ideological base,…a new picture of the cosmos and the nature of man.” We know that Western mind-sets have come and gone their way every few hundred years. But from Prabhupāda’s preaching, every corner of the globe—east, west, north, and south—finally got it right. The Vyāsa-pūjā *tithi* of Śrīla Prabhupāda is ripe for reflecting upon this gift.

He gave us the true, full, accurate picture—not just another pacifying myth for the masses, or a metaphorical tool for speculators. He won our intelligence by his completely documenting our total dependence on the Supreme—in every aspect of every creature’s existence. A calm, unbiased deliberation easily reveals that Prabhupāda’s authorized, all-encompassing Vedic presentation has overwhelmed all other visions of the cosmos and the purpose of human life. Whether religious or materialistic, when other conceptions encounter Prabhupāda’s Vedic razor, they either tumble over at the root or correctly assume an importance diminutive enough to pass under the blade.

Please briefly consider just one omen of the impending Prabhupāda historical juggernaut. Śrīla Prabhupāda adroitly chose to give his worldwide Hare Kṛṣṇa movement the official name International Society for Krishna Consciousness. The changing times are bearing out the uncanny brilliance of Prabhupāda’s choice.

After at least a century in exile, the study of consciousness has emerged out of academic oblivion and is gradually approaching the corridors of the university campus. “What is consciousness, and how can it arise in a physical body and universe?” On the *abhakta* conundrum list, the puzzle of the universe’s origin perhaps has begun to slide into second place, now that the nature of consciousness is increasingly deemed the greatest frontier facing science.

Obviously, how can we account for the cosmos without understanding the accountant—the conscious agent that evaluates all scientific or religious theory and data? Fifteen centuries ago, the Catholic founding-father Augustine wondered aloud, “What we are looking for is what we are looking with!” Modern scientific circles have fared no better. After nearly three centuries of either deftly sidestepping the issue or squarely confronting it, no actual consensus exists.

As always, we are eternally indebted to Prabhupāda for his famous Hare Kṛṣṇa forthrightness, which leaves no ambiguity:

*Asmin dehe.* In this body there is the spiritual spark, who is the proprietor of the body.…What the scientists cannot imagine, or what they have no idea of, from there we begin our education in spiritual life—beyond the jurisdiction of the scientists. How can they understand this movement? It is beyond their jurisdiction. When they will receive one hundred or one thousand Nobel Prizes, at that time they may be able to understand where the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement *begins.* \[Lecture in Los Angeles, 14 May 1973, slightly edited\]

What is even more significant for Prabhupāda-Renaissance watchers is that the emerging boom in speculation on consciousness doesn’t end with professors. The fascination is spreading among ordinary persons as well. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s preaching stance held that since the problem of consciousness is obviously a problem of self-knowledge, it therefore affects everyone—not just scientists and philosophers. Now the more alert masses are becoming aware of the enormous hole in their knowledge. With a little prodding from Prabhupāda’s Vedic precepts, combined with the holy name and *prasādam,* many can realize that concern about consciousness means concern about *themselves—*in the strongest sense of the word.

Recently, the main international newsweekly proclaimed on its cover, “Scientists peer into the brain, looking for that evanescent thing called consciousness.” The last words of its bewildered feature article hesitantly suggested, “It may be that scientists will eventually have to acknowledge the existence of something beyond their ken—something that might be described as the soul.” Nevertheless, even if they can accept that consciousness is the symptom of something or other transcendental to matter, so what! Part of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s futuristic legacy is the guarantee he gave us that no one can ever adequately understand consciousness without understanding Kṛṣṇa. In other words, he taught genuine holistic knowledge: that is, we cannot understand the *jīva* independent of its subordination to the Complete Whole. The futile attempt leads straight to Māyāvāda.

We all have consciousness, but what is this consciousness? It is Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We have forgotten Kṛṣṇa; therefore we simply say “consciousness.” Actually consciousness really means Kṛṣṇa consciousness, because without Kṛṣṇa, you cannot have consciousness. Therefore the right name is Kṛṣṇa consciousness, not “consciousness.” Just like without the sun, how can there be sunshine? Therefore we say “sunshine,” not just “shine.” Sunshine. Similarly, as soon as there is consciousness, it must be Kṛṣṇa consciousness. If one talks of consciousness minus Kṛṣṇa, he’s a rascal. Consciousness means Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This requires a little brain substance to understand. \[Lecture in Los Angeles, 30 April 1973, slightly edited\]

Much to our dismay, in major parts of the world, instead of the public turning en masse to Lord Kṛṣṇa for understanding the soul and its consciousness, they search every rotting haystack in the kingdom of *māyā*. The pockets of a so-called Āyurvedic, pop impersonalist writer they line to the tune of $15 million. Eagerly the poor fools snap up over five million copies worldwide of a trendy pseudo-spiritual prophecy, and even one million copies of a Westernized version of *The Tibetan Book of the Dead*.

It’s time to bring our big guns of knowledge to bear. As an offering to Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us pledge to work harder to seize back the day. Though by now saying so may be a Vyāsa-pūjā cliche, the prime means of glorifying Śrīla Prabhupāda remains the same: “Yes, you should become the topmost temple for distributing my books.…That is the only way to please me. Distribute my books profusely. That is real preaching” (Letter, October 1976).

We pray that with intelligence purified because the first order of business is executed, then the brain may be able to reverberate with creative strategies to teach the books and draw mass attention to them. Consequently, we pray we will be able to present the format for civilization that Kṛṣṇa Himself devised, and at least to some degree attempt to live by it.

Vyāsa-pūjā day is certainly an appropriate occasion for rejoicing that anyone who actively reads Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books—especially in favorable association—easily solves the riddle of individual consciousness, the supreme consciousness, and how to live in this world in the most suitable way for actual progress. Whether these essential items of knowledge are on the academic agenda or not, each reader—alone and especially in groups—becomes a spark in the future mass explosion of the Prabhupāda Renaissance world transformation.

Our ultimate aim is to take part in politics, because Krishna took part in politics, we have to follow—but if people do not become Krishna conscious it won’t be possible. Actually if we can take up the government, our movement will be very easily spread and beneficial to the people. We can make happy, peaceful men, God conscious—this is our aim. The main thing is to distribute books more and more. \[Letter, October 1976\]

Sometimes it seems that all our efforts are required just to keep a temple afloat, or to fill one *saṅkīrtana* van. Influencing a government, what to speak of transforming the world, seems at most a very distant, hazy dream. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā is the best day for remembering the person Kṛṣṇa chose to bring about the Mission Impossible. It is also the best day for seeing within the pages of this book the potential Śrīla Prabhupāda created—for fulfilling his impossible yet possible plans.

Devāmṛta Swami

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Akhandadhi dāsa

Time—our greatest enemy. It’s not its fault. Time merely carries out its God-given function of moving the world to simulate the pastimes of the spiritual realm.

The fault is ours. Having decided to inhabit and enjoy the mundane sphere, we have put ourselves in its hands. Now we are tormented by the way time separates us from rosy remembrances, present attachments, and future ambitions. Time shrouds our origins in mystery. It diminishes our mental enjoyment of past experiences. It is the fog that makes the future unknowable. And all the while it is manipulating the chemistry of our bodies, aging us bit by bit toward incapacity and senility.

So we are lost in this world, with next to no understanding of what is going on around us and no ability to connect with our past or our future And the attempts and boasts of similarly conditioned souls to lead us to salvation are simply the blind leading the blind into the ditch. No wonder we conclude that a little sense gratification is the all-in-all.

But the Lord is not neglectful of His parts and parcels. He makes tactful arrangements—sometimes so astoundingly wonderful that they cannot be ignored, but more often so subtle that they’re perceptible only to those with a thoughtful mind and open heart. He enjoys providing the living entities full access to all transcendental knowledge and encouraging them to take up the practice of devotional service. And He enjoys it all the more by allowing His servants the opportunity to be the instruments.

Thus, by Kṛṣṇa’s kind arrangement we can understand our origin, current status, and destiny from the testimony of a liberated soul—one who is free from the control of time. To initiate the process, Lord Kṛṣṇa personally enlightened Brahmājī, His first created being within this universe. It is not Brahmā’s fabulous longevity that makes him transcendental to the conditioning of time; it is his submissive hearing of transcendental sound vibration—vibration that fully contains the revelation of the Lord’s name, form, qualities, pastimes, and abode.

Brahmā chose to pass on and expand this vibration to his own son Nārada Muni, who in turn revealed it to the empowered sage Śrīla Vyāsadeva. Vyāsadeva kindly considered ways in which this potent vibration could be preserved and, indeed, made more accessible for future generations about to be struck by the degenerative effects of the Age of Kali. He therefore compiled a vast array of literature presenting all fields of knowledge in such a way that an inquisitive and faithful soul may progress in spiritual life. On Nārada Muni’s insistence, he then produced *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* which, being the most potent explanation of transcendence, is adequate in itself to quickly transport the living entities through the hell of Kali-yuga to the paradise of Goloka.

But conditioned souls should not be confused. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is not merely words on paper. It is the expansion of the original sound vibration from Lord Kṛṣṇa—now sweeter and more easy to assimilate than ever before. It is the wonder of Vyāsadeva’s contribution to humanity that this vibration is so perfectly available within this scripture. And also in the persons *Bhāgavatam*—those liberated souls who have thoroughly digested it, whose lives exemplify it, and whose words express it for others’ consumption.

Time is thus conquered. Through the millennia there is no diminution of the vibration of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. It is perfectly preserved by the *paramparā* process. We hear the metaphor of the children passing the mangoes carefully from one branch down to the next so that the unblemished fruit reaches the eager consumer on the ground. But like any metaphor based on a material situation, it does not adequately do justice to the *paramparā*. Imagine the same circumstance, but every time the fruit changes hands during its descent, it is growing and becoming sweeter, juicier, and more delectable. That is the effect of the realizations and preaching of the representatives of Vyāsadeva within the *paramparā*.

In our calculation of the duration of time, over five thousand years have passed since the compilation of the *Bhāgavatam,* but we are not at any disadvantage. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* has become all the more easy to relish, especially following the appearance of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. It is hard to conceive that anyone could be as merciful and expert in revealing Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And how pleased Śrīla Vyāsadeva must be to see his *Bhāgavatam* being so widely disseminated in this iron age! How he must applaud Śrīla Prabhupāda’s unique ability to present the message for the benefit of a fallen population!

We therefore celebrate the appearance of the spiritual master as Vyāsa-pūjā—literally “the worship of Vyāsadeva.” Our aim is to constantly glorify the spiritual master in his true context—as the empowered representative of Vyāsadeva.

Disciples have a profound awareness of the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā. The day fills us with intense soul-searching as we desperately plumb the depths of our hearts to express our *śraddhāñjali*. What can be said that adequately glorifies such a personality as Śrīla Prabhupāda? How do we verbalize our debt, gratitude, or commitment?

We promise to carry the realizations and mood of Vyāsa-pūjā into every other day of the year. We vow to strive for improved application of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We resolve to serve his mission and to make ISKCON a happy and dynamic society that is a credit to him. And we beg Śrīla Prabhupāda and the predecessor *ācāryas* to shower their mercy on us, our fellow devotees, and all others yet to be blessed.

But, since 1977, we can no longer relish Vyāsa-pūjā without acknowledging the emotions of separation from our beloved *gurudeva*. Hearing the message of the *guru,* studying his literature, discussing his pastimes, and, particularly, meditating on and actively pursuing his mission are all part of *vāṇī-sevā*. In such activities we experience our eternal personal relationship with Śrīla Prabhupāda. Time is defeated.

Nor has 1977 countered the *paramparā’s* rout of time and ignorance. The transcendental vibration continues to expand, but the beacon of Śrīla Prabhupāda is so effulgent that he remains the key reference for the souls of the modern age. We know nothing of Kṛṣṇa, Vyāsadeva, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the *paramparā,* Lord Caitanya other than what Śrīla Prabhupāda revealed and still reveals to us. And anything that is contrary to what Śrīla Prabhupāda teaches, we do not consider as truth.

There is no doubt that each follower of Śrīla Prabhupāda carries wonderful realizations bestowed upon him or her. But none of us are independent reference points. Whatever an individual may express can be judged with reference to Śrīla Prabhupāda. The task then becomes how to make that judgment in the physical absence of Prabhupāda. That is no single person’s ability or prerogative, but it is embodied in the nature of ISKCON itself—not simply as a management instrument, but in the vision that the total fellowship of ISKCON incorporates the full realizations and mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Rather than view the continuation of the *paramparā* in terms of individual branches and strands, we are being called by Prabhupāda to see it in the holistic context of his movement.

Hence, the future is secure only in the unity of ISKCON. As individuals, our realizations may be easily subsumed by the material energy. Together, we can manifest Śrīla Prabhupāda in an ever more potent and merciful form for the benefit of a grateful human race and to the eternal credit of us all. I humbly offer this as my paltry comprehension of the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā.

May Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy flow in uninterrupted torrents through his glorious movement and bless all of you on this most blessed of days.

*Your servant,*  
Akhandadhi dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Today, on your Vyāsa-pūjā, the devotees worldwide will present offerings filled with deep realizations and heartfelt surrender. What can I offer compared to them? I credit myself that at least I am attached to you. But actually that is your qualification, not mine. Who, having experienced even a few moments of your association, does not remember you and appreciate, at the bare minimum, that “Yes, I have met a saintly person”?

When asked about your own spiritual master, you replied, “What can I say? He was a Vaikuṇṭha man.” You are also a Vaikuṇṭha man, and perhaps the best use of this time and paper is to simply offer some small samples of your sublime nature.

You have said that the first qualification of a spiritual master is that he is a good disciple of his own Guru Mahārāja. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura ordered you to preach to the English-speaking world. How you gave your heart and soul to that order!

I remember your schedule when you came to Chicago in 1974. From the minute you stepped off the plane (having just arrived from a whirlwind tour of the West Coast) to the moment you stepped back on to your departing flight, you never slackened the pace.

I remember being the evening guard outside your room. Long after your youthful disciples (most barely a third your age) had collapsed into bed—exhausted after just trying to keep up with you—late into the evening you were still personally showing the last of your new *brāhmaṇa* initiates how to chant their Gāyatrī *mantras,* and I knew that while we were all still sleeping you alone would be awake, dictating your Bhaktivedanta purports.

You prayed, “I want the benediction to fight like Arjuna, up to my last breath.” We see how Kṛṣṇa fulfilled your prayer: to your last breath you were translating the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* absorbed in the order of your spiritual master to “preach to the English-speaking world.”

Actually, your field was the whole world. You said that one must first execute the order of the spiritual master and then one can expand on that order (you cited the example of Hanumān, who first completed his mission to deliver Lord Rāma’s message to Sītā and then proceeded to engulf all of Laṅkā in flames).

Even though you are the true *jagad-guru,* you always remained meek and humble. Once, an airport departure lounge became so crowded with your followers (many offering full *daṇḍavats,* thus blocking passengers exiting their planes) that it threatened to become an ugly scene. The local GBC stood up and announced in a booming voice, “All ISKCON devotees, all ISKCON devotees, please clear the terminal!” You immediately stood up, ready to leave, thinking of yourself as also an “ISKCON devotee.”

While in Paris during the communist /student riots, you were speaking in a public hall. You were explaining about the Vedic social order—first *brāhmaṇas,* second *kṣatriyas,* third *vaiśyas,* and fourth *śūdras*. A man in the audience, fired with Marxist rhetoric of a classless society, interrupted by shouting, “And I suppose you think you are a first-class man?”

In a soft yet emotional voice you replied, “No, I am a fifth-class man. I am a servant of all the other classes.”

Girirāja Mahārāja related how once he had come back to the Bombay temple very late at night from a preaching engagement. Seeing a light still on in your room and being trained by you to be frugal, he reached in and shut it off. You called out, “Oh, what is this?” as you were still up late working at your desk. Girirāja Mahārāja switched the light back on and apologized, explaining that he did not think anyone was in the room.

You replied that you had been praying to Kṛṣṇa to help you reduce your sleeping and that this had come to pass. You then reflected, “I don’t eat much, there is no question of sex, and now sleeping is reduced—maybe I am liberated?” We know that, what to speak of “liberated,” you are the *sena-pati bhakta—*the great devotee general—sent directly by Lord Caitanya to lead the *saṅkīrtana* army in spreading His glories all over the world, as predicted by Locana dāsa Ṭhākura in his *Caitanya-maṅgala*.

You said the measure of our love for you would be shown in how we cooperated with one another after your physical departure. One aspect of that cooperation is learning to tolerate one another’s shortcomings. As in every other field, you have taught us by your example.

One senior devotee, when writing a letter to you, was complaining about the trouble he was having working with one particularly difficult temple president. You wrote back, “I ask that you tolerate him, just as I tolerate you.”

If you can tolerate reaching all the way down from the Vaikuṇṭha platform to pick us up, who are we to complain about the small stretch required for us to work with one another?

Due to a change of plans, your arrival date in Detroit was moved up, leaving us only a few days to spruce up the temple and prepare your quarters. We launched into a marathon effort, the result of which was that by the time you arrived no one had really slept for days. For the first morning’s class you read from the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. The verse described how usually a poor man does not become rich unless something extraordinary happens (some inheritance, some unexpected good fortune, etc.) In your lecture you asked, “So how is it that in this Age of Kali this movement is spreading all over the world?” You then answered in a strong voice, “It is spreading by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa alone.”

Looking over your glasses, you saw that your audience was, almost to a person, sound asleep (it being the first time in days they had finally had a chance to sit down). With a resigned yet affectionate chuckle you said to yourself softly, “That is a fact.”

For one of your arrivals in Hawaii, the devotees constructed a large and elaborate ceremonial umbrella. With difficulty one *brahmacārī* managed to hold it over you while you spoke at the airport. He struggled to keep it over you as you walked to your car. When you arrived at the temple and began settling in on your *vyāsāsana* to begin your lecture, the *brahmacārī* climbed up behind you to again hold the umbrella over your seat. Eyeing the umbrella, the young man, and his precarious perch, you remarked, “I don’t think it is going to rain in here.”

You are the pole star by which we set and measure our course. You are the foundation upon which all we do rests. You are our father, guide, shelter, and ever well-wisher. Via your Bhaktivedanta purports you have made yourself available to the whole world.

In the early morning hours, while you were doing your translating work, there was the chance of being called into your room for some small service. Sometimes you would then share charming details of your childhood, your latest philosophical insight, or perhaps a cutting critique of material life.

Knowing this, and eager for such an opportunity, one of your servants began straightening up the foyer in front of your room, deliberately making noise, with the hope that you might hear that he was up and invite him in.

You did. But in a very serious voice you instructed, “Please, don’t make noise. Don’t you know that I have to select every word so carefully? These pages will stand as the law books for the next ten thousand years.”

As a new devotee in New Dvārakā in 1970, I remember you trying to teach us the Sanskrit for the *Śrī Īśopaniṣad* verses. After some days, and seeing our still weak attempts, you said soberly, “Don’t you know these books are not just for selling—they are for you.”

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is known as *mahā-vadānyāvatāra,* the most merciful of all the Lord’s incarnations. You, as the greatest devotee of Lord Caitanya, are the quintessence of that mercy. You have poured out your kindness on me in the form of your *vāṇī* and *vapu,* your instructions and your pastimes.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* it is described how when the *gopīs* serve Kṛṣṇa He becomes pleased and reveals even more of His beauty. That fresh revelation inspires more surrender and service from the *gopīs,* which in turn induces the Lord to reveal still more of His sublime nature. This sweet reciprocation continues eternally.

In a similar fashion, the more I become absorbed in serving you, by serving your mission and your followers, the more I find myself happy and enlivened. You have given me everything; the more I follow, the more I realize how deep and expansive your guidance is.

I don’t find myself afraid of old age and even death; my only real fear is that I may somehow or other forget all that you have done for me (and the countless other conditioned souls struggling in this material world).

You once said, “There is no over-population problem in the spiritual world—Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet are big enough for everyone.” I pray that you will always keep me under the shade of your lotus feet.

*Your servant,*  
Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

## Bhakti Chāru Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to your divine appearance on this planet. Once again we are preparing to celebrate your Vyāsa-pūjā and I am compelled to assess whether my attachment to your lotus feet is still intact, whether I am still serving you with sincere dedication and you are still the lord and master of my heart.

You appeared due to your causeless mercy to dispel the darkness of this material world. Therefore, it is natural that if the cloud of ignorance covers my vision then everything will become dark again. You imparted transcendental knowledge unto us and made us realize that we are not these material bodies but pure spirit souls; therefore we will continue to exist even after death. However, so often we forget that teaching and think that since your physical form has disappeared from our vision you are not here anymore. That is the worst enemy of our consciousness. This mentality confines our consciousness to our sense perceptions and ties us down to material nature.

You came to free us from the bondage of matter, and therefore after teaching us the essential facts about our spiritual identity you are putting us through this test. If we pass the test we will progress on our spiritual journey; otherwise we will get carried away by the illusory energy of the Lord.

You told us that when an *ācārya* disappears there is bound to be a crisis. The most prominent *ācārya* of this time, Your Divine Grace, disappeared, so how can we be so deluded as to think that there will not be any crisis? Nevertheless, like a bunch of immature children we thought that despite your absence we would be able to manage ISKCON most efficiently and that due to our expert preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness would continue to spread most effectively. However, it did not take us very long to realize how foolish those ideas were. Kṛṣṇa made it clear to us that all the achievements of ISKCON were due to your spiritual potency and that without you we are absolutely helpless. Today, when your ISKCON is devastated due to innumerable crises, we are forced to realize that we cannot survive without you.

Therefore, it is better for us to face all kinds of calamities and come to the point of total helplessness so that we can depend upon you wholeheartedly and surrender unto you with all sincerity. Let us truly understand your teachings and act accordingly. Let us recognize your greatness and appreciate your spiritual position by singing your glory all over the world. Just as Kṛṣṇa is present in His holy name, you are also present in your glorious achievements. Therefore, when we sing the glory of your extraordinary activities, we begin to understand that you are still with us. Then all the difficulties disappear and everything becomes easy, and ISKCON begins to thrive with successful achievements. Therefore let your glory spread throughout the universe, and let us become the instruments for that sublime activity. Then this earth will become nondifferent from the spiritual sky and our hearts will become filled with transcendental bliss.

Although I am totally incompetent and unqualified, at one time I wishfully desired to spread your glory through television, the media that we did not properly tap as yet. And I feel very happy to report to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that this endeavor has been rewarded with inconceivable success. Not only did the Indian National Television sign a contract to telecast a 104-episode series on your life, but after telecasting twenty-six episodes we got the offer to re-telecast it on one of the prime time slots of the Indian National Television channels.

Two years ago, in 1995, I came across a book called *The Greatest Personalities of this Century*. It elaborately described the lives and achievements of a few hundred people. I expected that your name would obviously be there. However, unfortunately it was not. I felt extremely sad. Although you are the greatest personality of not only this century but of all times, there was no mention of you. With my heart heaving with anger, I resolved that I must do something to make the world recognize and appreciate your greatness. That was the time when I decided to use this most powerful medium to spread your glory throughout the world, to make everyone recognize your greatness. At that time it was just a wishful thought. However, Kṛṣṇa very mercifully fulfilled that desire and made it a reality.

So far, the production of thirty-four episodes has been completed. Besides being telecast on the Indian National TV channel, they are being dubbed into English, Bengali, Tamil, and Telegu and subtitled in five major European languages, and I am sure gradually they will be distributed throughout the world. His Holiness Mukunda Mahārāja recently informed us that he is signing a contract with an American television channel to show this series for two years, and he expects that many other TV channels in America will also do the same.

Your dedicated devotees have appreciated this endeavor very much and showered their blessings upon me. It fills my heart with joy to see that somehow I have been able to please the Vaiṣṇavas and have been able to render some service to Your Divine Grace. Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing me this opportunity to absorb myself totally in your most wonderful pastimes.

When you disappeared from my vision, I was submerged in an ocean of sorrow. Now, through this project you are reappearing to me, inundating my heart with indescribable joy. Let your glory spread throughout the universe, and please allow me to offer my body, mind, and soul to be engaged in that most wonderful spiritual activity.

*Your most insignificant servant,*  
Bhakti Chāru Swami.

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who have so mercifully brought the message of pure devotional service to these Western countries, where one finds the most fallen and degraded conditioned souls of Kali-yuga. Without your having come here we would still be drowning in the ocean of nescience, with no hope of deliverance whatsoever. Thank you for having come, and thank you for having given us the method by which we are never separated from you, the process of pure devotional service.

This year marks the twentieth anniversary of your disappearance from our gross material vision on November 14th, 1977, in Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma. Yet although twenty years have passed without your physical presence, we still see the immense potency of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee at work as you continue to touch and transform the hearts and lives of countless living entities. One new devotee from the French province of Quebec recently revealed how simply by seeing you once on video he felt your presence so intensely that he could not check the tears from his eyes. Simply by hearing your transcendental voice on your lecture tapes, he immediately captured and realized your transcendental presence and potency. In this way you continue to enter the hearts of all sincere seekers of the Absolute Truth.

You once explained that in the same way that Arjuna directly associated with Lord Kṛṣṇa on the battlefield of Kurukṣetra, even today if one attentively and submissively hears the same message of the *Bhagavad-gītā* through the process of disciplic succession, then one experiences that same direct association with the Lord. Similarly, if one simply hears from the lips of the Lord’s pure devotee like yourself, if one simply associates with you through your instructions and through those who purely represent you, then one will experience that same direct association with you also. In this way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are actually never separated from you.

Although it is now thirty-one years since you came to these Western shores of North America, still so many people have yet to hear of your glories. With the progress of an ever-increasingly degraded Western materialistic culture, more and more people are becoming extremely frustrated and searching out a proper shelter. The youth in different countries especially is becoming more and more desperate for such shelter. Recently in Malaysia, the front-page headline of a major newspaper quoted the Prime Minister on the country’s deplorable and critical condition concerning the youth drug-addiction crisis. The headline read “WANTED: LEADERS!” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are that proper shelter, and it is our obligation to make you known to all such disturbed and forlorn living entities.

The beginning of one’s advancement in devotional service is the association of devotees. As Lord Caitanya says (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 22.83),

*kṛṣṇa-bhakti-janma-mūla haya ‘sādhu-saṅga’*  
*kṛṣṇa-prema janme, teṅho punaḥ mukhya aṅga*

This association with the pure devotee is the secret to our success. We must seek out such association and learn how to nurture it properly. Good association in Kali-yuga is not only rare but extremely difficult to maintain. Lord Caitanya has come to teach us the process of association through the *harināma-saṅkīrtana* movement. *Ādau śraddhā tataḥ sādhu-saṅgaḥ…* These are the beginning phases of devotional service, as described in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. If we do not learn to associate properly with one another, then our advancement in spiritual life will be hampered. And if our personal advancement in spiritual life is hampered, so is the advancement of your mission and that of Lord Caitanya. We will remain on the platform of *prākṛta bhaktas,* materialistic devotees, and thus will not be able to properly execute your plans and aspirations.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the emblem of a soul fully surrendered to the instructions of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda. Because of your purity, because of your full faith in the words of your Guru Mahārāja, you have conquered the world with these simple words: “Hare Kṛṣṇa.” You have taught us that “Purity is the force.” Please help us become pure in our words, deeds, and thoughts, as you are. This total surrender to the words of Guru and Kṛṣṇa is the guarantee of one’s success in preaching. One will thus become empowered to become victorious in all situations and under all circumstances.

Here in Canada the year 1997 will also commemorate the twentieth anniversary of the installation of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Manohara at ISKCON Montreal, and the twenty-fifth anniversary of the installation of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā–Kṣīracora-Gopīnātha at ISKCON Toronto. The devotees in Canada need your continued guidance and mercy in order to overcome the various forces of Māyā that are always lurking close by, attempting to create havoc and confusion. You personally came to these two centers, and we owe it to Your Divine Grace to serve you nicely, properly, as one united family.

Although there are various obstacles, and although I am limited in so many ways, still I remain hopeful and confident that your mission and that of Lord Caitanya will come out successful. The twenty-first century is just around the corner. There is so much to be done. You have given us everything in your books, and we must remain vigilant and faithful to your instructions. Please allow me to continue serving you, although so imperfectly, for you are my only hope and inspiration. I have no hope and no shelter other than your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The devotional wave of the Centennial *mahotsava* of the appearance of Your Divine Grace has made me meditate deeply regarding the *seva* of the disciple toward the spiritual master. Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is only by your mercy that an insignificant servant like this useless servant can think of doing something to glorify Your Divine Grace. You have written very clearly about the prime duty of a disciple in your purports to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*

After being initiated and receiving the orders of the spiritual master, the disciple should unhesitatingly think about the instructions or orders of the spiritual master and should not allow himself to be disturbed by anything else. This is also the verdict of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, who, while explaining a verse of *Bhagavad-gītā* (*vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana*, Bg. 2.41), points out that the order of the spiritual master is the life substance of the disciple. The disciple should not consider whether he is going back home, back to Godhead; his first business should be to execute the order of his spiritual master (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.24.15 purport).

\[Translation:\] One should obey the command of his father or spiritual master with due deference, saying “Yes, sir.”… \[Purport\] The son or disciple should accept the words of his spiritual master and father without hesitation. Whatever the father and the spiritual master order should be taken without argument: “Yes.” There should be no instance in which the disciple or the son says, “This is not correct. I cannot carry it out.”… Sometimes the father and the spiritual master may be the same man, and sometimes they are different men. In any case, the order of the father or the order of the spiritual master must be carried out without hesitation, with an immediate yes. There should be no argument. That is real service to the father and to the spiritual master. Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura has stated that the order of the spiritual master is the life and soul of the disciples. As a man cannot separate his life from his body, a disciple cannot separate the order of the spiritual master from his life. If a disciple follows the instruction of the spiritual master in that way, he is sure to become perfect. This is confirmed in the *Upaniṣads:* the import of Vedic instruction is revealed automatically only to one who has implicit faith in the Supreme Personality of Godhead and in his spiritual master. One may be materially considered an illiterate man, but if he has faith in the spiritual master as well as in the Supreme Personality of Godhead, then the meaning of scriptural revelation is immediately manifested before him.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.24.13, translation and purport).

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I can see that many of your stalwart disciples are broadcasting Your Divine Glories in many wonderful ways. Because of their dedicated devotional qualities, they have all received your divine mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda, when can I receive a drop of your divine mercy? I have not spoken to Your Divine Grace for the last twenty years, since you left me in 1977. During your physical presence I could receive the answers to all my silly queries immediately. After your divine disappearance, questions of a different nature have appeared in my mind. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please reveal to this most insignificant son the answers to my silent questions.

When can I come to the platform of understanding the disciple’s real relationship with and duty toward the spiritual master? You gave me many instructions during a span of eight years. Only by your mercy can I at least dream of executing these instructions. Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most important day, the anniversary of your divine appearance, please bless me so that I can always remain absorbed in planning how to execute your divine instructions.

*Your most insignificant servant,*  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you and this wonderful process that you have given to all of us.

As the years go by, it is becoming more and more clear that you are one of the most merciful entities that has come into this world during this present age. Please allow me to thank you so much for your great compassion, and especially for your wonderful sons, daughters, grandsons, and granddaughters; and allow me to submit formally this abbreviated offering.

*Please forgive me for not being able to hear you directly, so as to know exactly how to assist your mission in all areas. And please forgive me for any disturbances that I have caused to your serious devotees.*

*Your son,*  
Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear and most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated and humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This year in Vṛndāvana it happened that during one *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class we discussed a verse related to Hiraṇyakaśipu’s chastising his son Prahlāda Mahārāja. Hiraṇyakaśipu tried to rebuke Prahlāda by saying he was *durvinīta*—ungentle, uncivilized, or impudent. But in the purport by Your Divine Grace you point out how Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, by the mercy of the goddess of learning, Sarasvatī, has derived a different meaning for the word *durvinīta.* He says that *duḥ* refers to this material world (as in the word *duḥkhālayam* in the *Bhagavad-gītā*) and that the word *vinīta* can be broken into the words *vi* and *nīta. Vi* means *viśeṣa,* “specifically,” and *nīta* means “brought in.” Thus the true meaning of *durvinīta* is that, by the mercy of the Supreme Lord, Prahlāda Mahārāja was especially brought to this material world to teach people how to get out of the material condition.

I immediately thought how much this wonderful explanation so much befits Your Divine Grace as well. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were without a doubt brought into this material world by the mercy of the Lord to free the poor conditioned souls from the clutches of Māyā, who chastises them, as you further explain in that same purport.

Just as Prahlāda Mahārāja is protected and glorified in this way by mother Sarasvatī, the goddess of learning, Your Divine Grace will eternally be protected and glorified. Those who develop their love and deep appreciation for your unfathomable accomplishment of having spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world and at the same time having planted the seeds for unlimited projects to benefit humanity—such fortunate souls begin to see your divine presence everywhere.

In 1968 you wrote in a letter, “Our Krishna Consciousness movement must be all-pervading, as Krishna is all-pervading.” This is what we learned from Your Divine Grace because you really perceived Kṛṣṇa’s presence everywhere. You taught us in a most profound and practical way how to realize the see Lord Kṛṣṇa everywhere. In a lecture you mentioned that to see Kṛṣṇa everywhere means to think, “This particular living entity has forgotten Kṛṣṇa.” You carefully trained us to receive Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy by engaging us in the preaching mission and thus giving us the opportunity to become qualified to perceive Kṛṣṇa’s presence everywhere in such a most practical way. Others will attempt to jump over that stage of maturity, which means to become expert in helping you continue to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. They will artificially try to create the vision of an elevated devotee without first attaining the necessary qualifications.

Except for Your Divine Grace:

• Nobody else taught us to educate our devotees and children in Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over world.  
• Nobody else taught us to go out every day on street *saṅkīrtana* in every city all over the world.  
• Nobody else taught us to distribute Kṛṣṇa conscious literature all over the world.  
• Nobody else taught us to regularly hold the Sunday feast in our temples for the visitors all over the world.  
• Nobody else taught us to hold Ratha-yātrā festivals all over the world.  
• Nobody else taught us to establish temples and farm projects all over the world.  
• Nobody else taught us to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in every imaginable way to every country all over the world.  
• Nobody else taught us to distribute *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* to needy people all over the world.  
• Nobody else taught us to preach to scientists and educated people all over the world.  
• Nobody else taught us to manage and organize this movement all over the world.

AND

• Nobody else taught us how to love Kṛṣṇa and how to attain the highest possible perfection in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You gave us everything.

Nobody else except Your Divine Grace continues to push on this movement, despite all obstacles and adversities. In a lecture on your Guru Mahārāja’s appearance day you explained,

This movement should go on, you see, this movement started by Caitanya Mahāprabhu, then accelerated by Gosvāmīs, and then again Viśvanātha Cakravartī. And when—Just like whenever there is—*yadā yadā hi dharmasya glānir bhavati*. When there is some slowness, either Kṛṣṇa Himself or some representative comes to give it a push. So you should know it, that this movement should be pushed on. This should not be neglected.

Our duty is to carefully preserve, protect, and expand what you have given us. On this auspicious day of your divine appearance in this world, I pray to your lotus feet that you kindly help this poor soul become a useful tool in accomplishing this difficult task.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I am forever dependent on you in so many ways. I’ve listed several below:

I depend on your compassion;  
I depend on your tolerance;  
I depend on your kindness;  
I depend on your forgiveness;  
I depend on your blessings;  
I depend on your accepting my attempts at service;  
I depend on your being pleased with me;  
I depend on your instructions;  
I depend on your Society of devotees;  
I depend on the Deities you’ve installed;  
I depend on the holy *mantras* that you’ve given me;  
I depend on the holy *dhāmas* that you’ve blessed me to enter;  
and I depend on your books.

On this holy day on which we celebrate your appearance, I wish to glorify one of the gifts on which I depend—the *Bhagavad-gītā,* the practical book of life that teaches how to live in Kṛṣṇa consciousness while in the material world. You kindly made that book my book in a letter to me early in 1969. I had written to you and informed you, amongst other things, that I was graduating from the university and that I wished to preach. Your reply expressed your pleasure, and you instructed me to study *Bhagavad-gītā* and preach throughout the world. I thus became a student of the *Bhagavad-gītā*.

When I first read the texts and your purports, I thought it an easy book to master. But I, at that time, was young. Now, having turned 50, I view the *Bhagavad-gītā* differently, as one views a great and distant goal. I’ve discovered it essential for study and preaching, but even more essential as a light-shedding beacon of life.

As an offering to you on this day, I would like to briefly summarize the effulgent message of the *Bhagavad-gītā* as I have learned it from you and by which I aspire to live.

I should surrender to the service of my spiritual master, which is Kṛṣṇa’s service, with my mind focused upon Kṛṣṇa with great love and appreciation.

I should live submissively, purely for His pleasure, and my life should be prayerful, fearless, bold, devoted, and thankful.

I should depend upon Kṛṣṇa and accept all that occurs to me—good, bad, neutral—with detachment and as His mercy.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*.

*Forever your servant,*  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Bīr Krishna dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The relationship between spiritual master and sincere disciple becomes closer with time, even though there may be physical separation.

In mundane relationships, the longer the period of time of physical separation, the more distant the relationship grows. In fact, one can make the case that there is no possibility of closeness in a mundane relationship because one does not come in contact with the other person himself but only with the modes of material nature that are affecting the other person.

You, being an eternally liberated associate of the Lord, are always expressing your devotional ecstasies—with every word and every action. That is, everything you do and everything you say is a manifestation of your love for Kṛṣṇa. It comes directly from your unalloyed heart. So, to have a close relationship with you is easy: one simply has to observe your activities and hear submissively the words that emanate from your lotus mouth.

Time and physical separation are not impediments to being your intimate disciple. In fact, time can help one come closer to you if utilized properly. Time should be used to eradicate the one impediment that exists to having a deep relationship with you. That impediment is our material contamination.

When we are strict in following your orders, the contamination in our hearts, the one thing that is separating us from you, is eradicated very quickly. And when we do not follow your instructions, the contamination in our hearts increases and we become further separated from you.

You have given us orders that pertain to our personal *sādhana* (rising early, going to *maṅgala ārati,* chanting sixteen rounds, etc.) and orders that pertain to our service to the movement (dedicating ourselves to spreading your movement.) Both types of orders are essential to purifying our hearts.

I have found that by my engaging in the service of your Society and being very strict in my personal *sādhana,* all the *anarthas* or impediments quickly disappear and my relationship with you gets deeper and deeper.

I am hankering to become a real disciple. You tell us what a real disciple is:

*Śiṣya* means: “Whatever you’ll say, I’ll accept.” That is *śiṣya*. The *śiṣya* word comes from *śas-dhātu*. *Śas-dhātu*. *Śāstra*. *Śāstra*. *Śāsana*. *Śiṣya*. These are from the same root. *Śas-dhātu. Śas-dhātu* means rule, ruling. So we can rule in various ways. We can be ruled, becoming a *śiṣya* of a proper *guru*. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* lecture, London, 7 August 1973\]

I want to be ruled by you at every moment. I want to be close to you at every moment. This is my aspiration.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bīr Krishna dāsa Goswami

## Giridhārī Swami

**The Secret of Success**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On auspicious days such as this, I often think of asking you, “What is the secret of pleasing you, Śrīla Prabhupāda?”

Devotees throughout the world are working hard to fulfill your desires. Some of these great souls are leading book distribution, others are educating children, some are developing *varṇāśrama,* and some have worshiped Their Lordships and maintained temple programs for nearly three decades. Their advancement is shown by their detachment from sense gratification, their *divya-jñāna,* and their strong attachment to the service of *guru* and Kṛṣṇa. Your satisfaction with their service is obvious. But the question remains, “What is the secret of pleasing you, Śrīla Prabhupāda?”

Could it be that these devotees are particularly gifted with intelligence, talent, or scholarship? Are they the chosen few who will obtain your mercy? Or is your mercy causeless? Is it like rain, falling everywhere indiscriminately?

This year, another thought came to me: Maybe the secret lies in Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s commentary on *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.41:

The best kind of intelligence I can have is intelligence used in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Intelligence is defined as “fixed” when it is intent upon my spiritual master’s instructions, such as chanting the name of Kṛṣṇa, remembering His activities, and performing service to His lotus feet. My spiritual master’s instructions are my *sādhana* and my life, both in the beginning stages of *bhakti* as well in *bhakti’s* perfectional stage. I desire only to follow his instructions, I accept nothing else as my life’s work, even in dreams. Whether I am happy or distressed, whether the material world remains or is destroyed, I don’t care. There is no loss for me. I simply must carry out the orders of my spiritual master. Fixedness upon his orders is determined intelligence and devotional service, and only by being fixed on his orders can such determined intelligence prosper.

Now I can see why you quote *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.41 so often and recall this commentary. In fact, after hearing these words from Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura—these words that inspired you so much—I can better understand these same feelings expressed in your own writings:

When one becomes serious to follow the mission of the spiritual master, his resolution is tantamount to seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. As explained before, this means meeting the Supreme Personality of Godhead in the instruction of the spiritual master. This is technically called *vāṇī-sevā.* Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura states in his *Bhagavad-gītā* commentary on the verse *vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana* (Bg. 2.41) that one should serve the words of the spiritual master. The disciple must stick to whatever the spiritual master orders. Simply by following on that line, one sees the Supreme Personality of Godhead. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.51, purport\]

This is the secret of success. After being initiated and receiving the orders of the spiritual master, the disciple should unhesitatingly think about the instructions or orders of the spiritual master and should not allow himself to be disturbed by anything else. This is also the verdict of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, who, while explaining a verse of *Bhagavad-gītā* (*vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana,* Bg. 2.41), points out that the order of the spiritual master is the life substance of the disciple. The disciple should not consider whether he is going back home, back to Godhead; his first business should be to execute the order of his spiritual master. Thus a disciple should always meditate on the order of the spiritual master, and that is perfectional meditation. Not only should he meditate upon that order, but he should find out the means by which he can perfectly worship and execute it. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.24.16, purport\]

This is called *vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ*. I may be quite incomplete. It doesn’t matter. But if I try to follow the words of my Guru Mahārāja, then I become complete. This is the secret. *Yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*. If one has strong faith in the Supreme Personality of Godhead and as much faith in the *guru, yathā deve tathā gurau,* then the revealed scriptures become manifest. It is not the education. It is not the scholarship. It is faith in Kṛṣṇa and *guru*. Therefore *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* says *guru-kṛṣṇa-kṛpāya pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*. Not by education, not by scholarship, never says. Caitanya Mahāprabhu says, *guru-kṛṣṇa-kṛpāya,* by the mercy of *guru,* by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. It is a question of mercy. It is not a question of scholarship or opulence or richness. No. The whole *bhakti-mārga* depends on the mercy of the Lord. So we have to seek the mercy. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.15.30, lecture\]

That is the secret. Although there was no possibility, I never thought, but I took it little seriously by studying a commentary by Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura on the *Bhagavad-gītā*. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* the verse *vyavasāyātmikā-buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana,* in connection with that verse, Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura gives his commentary that we should take up the words from the spiritual master as our life and soul. We should try to carry out the instruction, the specific instruction of the spiritual master, very rigidly, without caring for our personal benefit or loss. So I tried a little bit in that spirit. So he has given me all facilities to serve him. Things have come to this stage, that in this old age I have come to your country, and you are also taking this movement seriously, trying to understand it. We have got some books now. So there is little foothold of this movement. So on this occasion of my spiritual master’s departure, as I am trying to execute his will, similarly, I shall also request you to execute the same order through my will. I am an old man, I can also pass away at any moment. That is nature’s law. Nobody can check it. So that is not very astonishing, but my appeal to you on this auspicious day of the departure of my Guru Mahārāja, that at least to some extent you have understood the essence of Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. You should try to push it on. People are suffering for want of this consciousness. \[Lecture on Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Goswami Prabhupāda’s Disappearance Day, 1969\]

By your mercy, the secret of success is revealed. Those devotees who become fixed on following your instructions will please you. They will gain your mercy and make rapid advancement in spiritual life. It’s as simple as that. Your disciples and granddisciples the world over are living proof. Thus, both your *śikṣā* and *dīkṣā* disciples have equal access to the constant stream of mercy flowing from Your Divine Grace. And nothing can check this flow of mercy except our own unwillingness to follow your instructions.

But I’m convinced that following your instructions is not a private matter. Either we serve your order together or not at all. ISKCON, you said, was created to facilitate the six loving exchanges between devotees. And by these six exchanges we can surmount all obstacles and serve your order purely. We advance by assisting one another in serving Your Divine Grace. How could it be any other way? The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* is brimming with the nectar of Mahāprabhu’s exchanges with His devotees based on these six principles. This standard was your vision for ISKCON. Only in such an atmosphere can all conflicts be resolved and pure *bhakti* thrive.

On this auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, I vow to faithfully serve your servants and by their mercy live the secret of success. Always.

Your lowly servant in the Far East,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāja Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

One Bengali boy from a *brāhmaṇa* family met you in Calcutta.

**Boy:** I have become a Christian.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Why? You are coming from a good family. In India, no high-class persons become Christians.

**Boy:** There is no difference between Christianity and Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

**Prabhupāda:** There is. Christians eat meat and we don’t.

**Boy:** What is your philosophy?

**Prabhupāda:** Our philosophy is very simple: ‘God is great, and we are very small. Therefore our duty is to serve God.’ The rest is all detail.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, over the years we have come to realize the simple truth: “You are great, and we are very small. Therefore our duty is to serve you.”

Although I may not know or understand many of the details, I do hope I have realized this eternal truth: “You are great, and I am very small. Therefore my duty is to serve you.”

For me the best way to serve you is to serve your servants and servants’ servants.

*keha māne, keha nā māne, saba tomāra dāsa*  
*ye nā māne, tāra haya sei pāpe nāśa*

**“Some accept you whereas others do not, yet everyone is your servant. One who does not accept you, however, will be ruined by his sinful activities” (based on** ***Caitanya-caritāmṛta*****,** ***Ādi*** **6.85).**

You are the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead. Just as everyone is meant to serve Kṛṣṇa, now on earth all are meant to serve you. And our duty is to bring them to your service. Only by engaging in your service can they be happy.

*naiṣāṁ matis tāvad urukramāṅghriṁ*  
*spṛśaty anarthāpagamo yad-arthaḥ*  
*mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo-’bhiṣekaṁ*  
*niṣkiñcanānāṁ na vṛṇīta yāvat*

**“Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava completely freed from material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord, who is glorified for His uncommon activities. Only by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious and taking shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord in this way can one be freed from material contamination” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **7.5.32).**

Nobody wants miseries. But because we accept this material body, we are bound to suffer. To become free from the material body and its concomitant miseries, one must take shelter of you and your servants. As your purport concludes,

Only one who has surrendered to a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa and taken the dust of his lotus feet can understand Kṛṣṇa. First one must understand how to get out of the clutches of *māyā*. The only means is to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. And to become Kṛṣṇa conscious very easily, one must take shelter of a realized soul—a *mahat,* or *mahātmā*—whose only interest is to engage in the service of the Supreme Lord.…That is the way to return home, back to Godhead.”

I pray to remain eternally engaged in the service of the dust of your lotus feet—in the service of your servants and servants’ servants.

Your eternal servant, servants’ servant, and servants’ servants’ servant,  
Girirāja Swami

## Gopāl Krishna Goswami

Our dear lord and master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. On this most auspicious occasion commemorating you appearance, all your disciples and granddisciples have gathered to offer prayers at your lotus feet.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have offered the entire world a way in which to live peacefully under the banner of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You have done what no one else could have done—turned thousands of *mlecchas* into devotees of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Your appearance in this world is, along with the appearance of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the most significant event in Kali-yuga. If not for your unlimited kindness, I and thousands of others would still be rotting in *māyā*. Your every thought, word, and action is the perfect example of pure devotional service. The brilliance of your transcendental activities illuminates the dense darkness of ignorance and will continue to expand with the passage of time. Your books are the only hope for humanity. You have opened the treasure of love of God stored in the ancient Vedic texts. You established temples all over the world so that humanity could practically learn the science of God consciousness. These temples are like embassies of the spiritual world. By practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness according to your instructions, one can obtain residence in the spiritual world rather than the material world, which is full of misery.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the perfect *ācārya* and master of the whole world. You are the greatest Vaiṣṇava preacher and staunch soldier of Lord Caitanya’s army. Your determination to preach and transcend all material impediments can be compared to the mood of Bhīṣmadeva, who remained in perfect meditation on Lord Kṛṣṇa even while pierced by so many arrows. Even in advanced age and failing health, you went on translating and preaching—thus teaching us to serve and preach until one’s final breath.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we need to constantly be aware of the fact that it is simply by following your instructions that we can advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Knowing how unqualified I am, with all humility I pray at your lotus feet that I may become fully surrendered to your desire of preaching the message of Lord Caitanya to every conditioned soul.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me the strength to remember your transcendental pastimes every day and at every moment. This will be the perfection of my life.

I beg to remain your eternal servant,  
Gopāl Krishna Goswami

## Guru Prasād Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most fallen obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Every event in my life has become Kṛṣṇa-ized by your mercy. As I was preparing to write an offering to you, my mother left her body. As I sat among the lamenting family members, I witnessed how this death caused pain and suffering. By contrast, both your life and your leaving are glorious and inspiring. Unlike the other members of my family, I felt enlivened, closer to you, feeling that I had not lost anything and realizing that you and service to you are the only thing I have, and that they are completely satisfying.

I am grateful that you have awakened me to the awareness that so many living entities are living and dying without any purpose to their existence. I remembered this more intensely this week as I saw old acquaintances saddened; I understood more than ever that you are demonstrating how to feel real compassion for all living beings in so many miserable situations. I beg that you grant me the benediction to be a small instrument in your *saṅkīrtana* strategy, which is the best way to serve and develop the quality of transcendental mercy toward all.

Since my service involves helping others to develop their Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I pray that you guide me so that I can offer them the maximum exposure to Kṛṣṇa and devotional service. I am sorry to be always asking you. I should simply be offering and glorifying, but being so incomplete, I need your constant intervention to be able to accomplish anything. You are my only hope and qualification.

Your eternal fallen servant,  
Guru Prasād Swami

## Harikeśa Swami

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances thrown in the transcendental dust of your lotus feet. All glory to you, who are the most perfect and empowered representative of Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda. All glory to you, who at the advanced age of sixty-nine went from your comfortable home in the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple in Vṛndāvana to New York City to capture us, who were wasting time in frivolous pursuits of so-called knowledge and the illusory happiness of sense enjoyment. Thank you a million upon millions of times again and again for your kindness and patience with me, who am the lowest of the low-class and useless fellows.

Today, on this occasion of your 101st appearance anniversary, I am meditating on your kindness. If I ask myself honestly what I really want in life, I can only conclude that I want one thing: to be fully and absolutely engaged in your service. I am not fit for highly advanced philosophical speculation or even ordinary mental speculation. I possess a body which is unfit for sense gratification and which has been trained to accept only that which is favorable for your service. How has this occurred? It wasn’t like this many years ago, before I met your representatives, the blissful devotees of the New York ISKCON temple. Rather, I was a fool and rascal number one, and there is simply nothing more to be said about my condition at that time. But neither is there a need to hide the fact that I was simply absorbed in material existence, without any hope of release from the bondage of *māyā*. Therefore it is a most interesting and intriguing question how I have changed. What is the cause and who is responsible for all of this?

The answer to this is obvious, and everyone knows because your glory is all-pervading in this world. It was you who came in the form of the preaching party at my former university, and it was you who called me to come to that program so I could reunite with my eternal brothers and sisters in glorification of your sublime characteristics and qualities. From the very start, your kindness was showered upon me to such a degree that I could no longer hold onto my stones of material attachment that I was carrying in my shoulder bag of life, birth after birth. Your kindness was not only shown to me, who am the most insignificant of all your servants, but it was shown to the whole world. Even those who never saw you or heard you were benefited simply by being residents of the same planet as you while you preached.

But because I tend often to think in terms of myself, I am most amazed at what you have done for me. I was a wild creature of the night, who wandered around the streets of the most degraded city on earth searching for a few crumbs of sense enjoyment, yet you, without anger or aversion, accepted me as your servant and engaged me in your service. Now that service is my life and soul. Indeed, I cannot live without being engaged in your service. I have only one prayer, one goal, and one desire: Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please keep me engaged in your service life after life, as you desire. Please keep me engaged in the most meaningful manner, following your instructions and satisfying you. Your satisfaction is everything to me. I have no means of satisfaction other than to taste the remnants of your satisfaction. When you are satisfied with me, then I am satisfied and I feel that there is no greater gain to be achieved than this. Therefore I pray to you, please always engage me in works which will satisfy you.

Some time ago, I used to think that I had to be engaged according to activities which would give me satisfaction. I would manipulate my life to create opportunities which would materially satisfy my desires for certain kinds of work. You saw that and mercifully tried to change my habits, but I resisted because I was ignorant of what you were trying to do. Had I known then what I know now, I would have immediately surrendered to your will and followed you without hesitation. But then I was just new to spiritual practices and unable to comprehend the vastness and wonder of devotional service. While you were on the planet you gently pushed me in the direction you wanted, and by the time you passed away you had situated me as you so desired. I was not fully aware of the significance of your actions, but I was somehow happy simply to be engaged in satisfying you.
