I came to learn that bringing you newly produced books was the ultimate way to satisfy you, and your reaction when you received the books solidified my understanding. By carefully reinforcing the activities which you wanted of me by expressions of pleasure and rejecting activities which you found useless by your expressions of displeasure, you gradually trained this dog, your servant, in the art of doing the right tricks on command.

To take so much care of such a worthless street dog as me demonstrates to the whole world your immense kindness and compassion. I did not come from a high-class or wealthy family; in fact, I never even gave you much in return for what you did for me. But you did demand some *dakṣiṇā*—the absolute surrender to your mission, which is nothing else than the desire of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Now when you are no longer with us, I can feel your push behind me. You are demanding more surrender as time goes by, for you can see my capacity to surrender increase incrementally as inevitable advancement occurs as we follow the process of *sādhana-bhakti*. This is your incredible kindness. You are giving me a lifelong mission, enough engagement to keep me off the streets and in your loving arms for as long as I desire. And you have made your desire my desire. I am your dog, and I am so very happy to be *sanātha-jīvitam,* to have a wonderful and perfect master who will protect me from the material nature and will keep me always engaged in his transcendental devotional service. I will sit by your door and not allow any enemy to enter your home. I will place my head at the tips of your lotus shoes and will accept any scrap of *prasādam* you kindly throw me for my sustenance. Please do not forget me. Always look favorably upon me even if I am not able to complete your mission in this lifetime. I know your granddisciples have already and will in the future accept your mission with the same enthusiasm and complete your world plan to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every town and village, for your potency is so great that it spans countless generations of devotees.

May your glory be known throughout all three worlds. May you always be merciful to us and grant us your favor. And above all, may we always be engaged in your service without deviation for all eternity.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything. Thank you, thank you, and thank you again. Please bless me with your merciful glance of kindness.

Your humble and devoted slave,  
Harikeśa Swami

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

As the years advance, the desire grows to know you as you really are. You once wrote to a disciple that just as when a person sees the clouds moving across the moon he thinks he sees the moon flying in the sky, so when a neophyte sees the external appearance of the spiritual master he thinks he sees the spiritual master as he is, but he does not see the actual form of a great devotee.

Our prayer on this occasion is to be blessed with increasing Kṛṣṇa consciousness so that we may behold your true glory. Even this prayer we must offer in a spirit of wanting to serve you with increasing love and appreciation, and not with a desire to enjoy your glories.

You are surely present here with us, but you are certainly not limited to our association. Undoubtedly you are engaged in various ways in the Lord’s loving service, and we can only imagine how many worlds you are saving, just as you are saving this one. Our fervent wish is to go back to home, back to Śrīla Prabhupāda, the pure loving servant of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Let us eternally assist you in your mission, wherever it may take you.

Falling in the dust of your lotus feet,  
Your servant,  
Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

My Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful prostrated obeisances and *puṣpāñjali* offered again and again and again at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I, with my disciples and devotees in Bangalore, have taken the greatest pleasure on April 30th, 1997, in dedicating and offering for the pleasure of Your Divine Grace, in the presence of the assembled Vaiṣṇavas, the ISKCON Bangalore Temple Complex as a special Centennial offering. It is only by Your Divine Grace’s causeless mercy that this was at all possible. We beg for your blessing that this incomparable facility can be fully utilized to spread the message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, which Your Divine Grace has given us.

Meditating on Your Divine Grace’s greatness and compassion for uplifting the fallen souls, I am feeling totally inadequate and unqualified. Although I have been serving in your Kṛṣṇa consciousness Society for twenty-seven years, I feel somehow I have failed to really become as serious and qualified a disciple of Your Divine Grace as I should be. In order to fulfill your divine mission and see Kali retreat, it is imperative that your disciples and followers take up your dedication, mood, and vibration wholeheartedly. I see so many areas in which I must improve. It is my humble prayer, as your spiritual son, to reaffirm my total commitment to serving your lotus feet and assisting in fulfilling your mission. I pray for your causeless mercy that soon I can become a worthy and fully empowered servitor of Your Divine Grace.

Earlier this year, in one of Your Divine Grace’s letters, I read that you requested the individual GBC members to act as *śikṣā-gurus* for your own *dīkṣā* disciples (what then to speak of your granddisciples). This made me appreciate how you wanted each and every devotee to be cared for, guided, and appreciated by the senior devotees, and especially the GBC members. I began to realize more deeply how special and dear all the souls are who have surrendered to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu through Your Divine Grace and taken shelter of your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. These realizations have inspired me to increase my service to your dear devotees. Your Divine Grace has so kindly given me wonderful opportunities to do so as a custodian and director of Śrī Māyāpur Chandrodaya Mandir. May my desire to purely serve Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His pure devotees ever increase day by day through your mercy and example!

Your Divine Grace always taught how Lord Kṛṣṇa was more desirous for us to reach Him than we were to return. That for every one step we made toward Lord Kṛṣṇa He would make ten steps toward us. We can understand that Your Divine Grace also had so much desire for us to quickly advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and reach the highest levels of pure loving devotional service. But now I am becoming aware of how our slowness to advance caused you so much concern. I would like to see my disciples and all the devotees in ISKCON advance very quickly and steadily in their Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but we see that so many have still not developed a profound taste for devotional service and chanting the holy names. It is a gradual process, and we have made so many mistakes. We pray for your merciful glance in order to speed up and enhance the process so that all devotees quickly become fully satisfied in devotional service and fully empowered to defeat Kali and his evil influence. So that the world will soon become flooded with the bliss of pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness! When will we see that day! Please engage us as effective instruments in your divine hands for achieving this end.

You taught how the whole world is burning due to the lack of the mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas. Now, by your causeless mercy, through the Nama Haṭṭa and congregational preaching programs we have the opportunity to engage everyone who cannot take shelter in your temples in devotional service. We look forward to a world filled with devotees practicing pure devotional service under the shelter of your transcendental movement. You ordered me to “increase the propagation \[of Kṛṣṇa consciousness\] unlimitedly,” and I beg for your mercy to achieve this soon.

Now, in the aftermath of Your Divine Grace’s Centennial celebrations, we are reminded of the need to complete the Temple of the Vedic Planetarium, for which you laid the foundation stone on Gaura Pūrṇimā in 1977. This most important task is possible only with Your Divine Grace’s full mercy and the total enthusiastic cooperation of all ISKCON devotees. We pray for this mercy as well.

Having made these offerings and meditations, I beg to remain steadfastly under the shelter of your divine lotus feet eternally.

Your eternal servant,  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Kavicandra Swami

To His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda:

Father to everyone in ISKCON. You are never out of reach for those who follow.  
Original: No one else will ever do what you did. You have already spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, founding an international Society.  
Under the shelter of the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa, always fully surrendered.  
Never forgetting the order of your Guru Mahārāja.  
Determined to follow the orders of your Guru Mahārāja.  
Expert at everything.  
Ready to do anything for the pleasure of your Guru Mahārāja and the service of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission.

Abhay—Fearless.  
Caran—At the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa.  
Always remembering Kṛṣṇa.  
Rādhārāṇī’s servant.  
Youthful due to knowing yourself as an eternal associate of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.  
At home in Śrī Vṛndāvana Dhāma, wherever you are.

The International Society for Krishna Consciousness

Actions speak louder than words. I pray that I will be able to remember your lotus feet and act in such a way that I can be of some service to ISKCON, which is your body. You have given us everything we need and shown us how to do everything. Your books are being distributed in greater quantities, and in more languages, every year. Therefore more and more souls all over the world are becoming fortunate in that they are getting your divine association.

We are praying to be able to follow your instructions and thereby be with you, and to be able to make a society wherein everyone can live safely, free from the influence of Kali-yuga. In the purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.8.5 you have given us some very important guidelines, and in the coming years we will work very hard to see that these things are firmly established in ISKCON.

TRANSLATION

The clever Duryodhana and his party cunningly usurped the kingdom of Yudhiṣṭhira, who had no enemy. By the grace of the Lord, the recovery was executed, and the unscrupulous kings who joined with Duryodhana were killed by Him. Others also died, their duration of life having decreased for their rough handling of the hair of Queen Draupadī.

PURPORT

In the glorious days, or before the advent of the Age of Kali, the *brāhmaṇas,* the cows, the women, the children and the old men were properly given protection.

1\. The protection of the *brāhmaṇas* maintains the institution of *varṇa* and *āśrama,* the most scientific culture for attainment of spiritual life.  
2\. The protection of cows maintains the most miraculous form of food, i.e., milk for maintaining the finer tissues of the brain for understanding higher aims of life.  
3\. The protection of women maintains the chastity of society, by which we can get a good generation for peace, tranquillity, and progress of life.  
4\. The protection of children gives the human form of life its best chance to prepare the way of liberty from material bondage. Such protection of children begins from the very day of begetting a child by the purificatory process of *garbhādhāna-saṁskāra,* the beginning of pure life.  
5\. The protection of the old men gives them a chance to prepare themselves for better life after death.

This complete outlook is based on factors leading to successful humanity as against the civilization of polished cats and dogs. The killing of the above-mentioned innocent creatures is totally forbidden because even by insulting them one loses one’s duration of life. In the Age of Kali they are not properly protected, and therefore the duration of life of the present generation has shortened considerably. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* it is stated that when the women become unchaste for want of proper protection, there are unwanted children called *varṇa-saṅkara*. To insult a chaste woman means to bring about disaster in the duration of life. Duḥśāsana, a brother of Duryodhana, insulted Draupadī, an ideal chaste lady, and therefore the miscreants died untimely. These are some of the stringent laws of the Lord mentioned above \[in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.8.4\].

Hoping to become your servant,  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā!

Śrī Kṛṣṇa never left us.

Being on the Battlefield of Kurukṣetra and hearing Lord Kṛṣṇa speak is identical with reading your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* today.

Lord Caitanya never left us.

The company of Lord Caitanya and His associates is nondifferent from becoming part of the *saṅkīrtana* movement at any point in time, be it past, present, or future.

You never left us either.

There is no difference between having had association with you when you manifested your *vapu* form on this earth and joining your ISKCON movement now.

Some people could appreciate Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s, Śrī Caitanya’s, and Śrīla Prabhupāda’s transcendental position and activities when they were present, others could not.

Some people could perceive Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s, Śrī Caitanya’s, and Śrīla Prabhupāda’s transcendental presence after their so-called disappearance, others could not.

We have your monumental books, pictures, videos, and relics as our inestimable and inalienable treasure. In addition to that, the delicate presence of your unmistakable personality can be felt in the association of devotees.

So many times during various conversations with Godbrothers I detected your intervention coming through them in the form of a wise comment, a word of encouragement, a sobering look, or a correction.

Sometimes you speak to me even through my disciples or young devotees.

One very particular incident that caught me by surprise happened three years ago.

At that time my son was almost five years old, and possibly, up till then, I hadn’t fulfilled my function as a father to the best of my ability for reasons that go from being too busy, misconceiving what a Kṛṣṇa conscious father should do, and indifference.

I was sitting on a bed when my son came in. He had been generally behaving as a thoughtless child and at times a silly Billy; this time, though, he was unusually grave. Standing in front of me, he caught hold of my hand, and looking as if to a distant place, he asked me, “So, are you happy that this child is born of you?”

I was shocked. I had the clear impression that you were asking me this question to point out my deficiencies as a father. Also, the way the question was phrased was totally different from my son’s usual way of speaking. I answered, “Yes, I am happy.”

Then Nitāi Prasād (my son) inquired further, “Why are you happy?” Feeling like a fool, I answered “Because this son can become a devotee and glorify his family.”

Then he looked at me approvingly, a serene deep look that made me perceive your presence beyond doubt.

Many times after this incident I considered whether this could all be due to my being an attached father prone to autosuggestion, but I found no evidence of this.

On the contrary, since then I have tried to be a better father, thinking that this boy could one day become a receptacle of your mercy, and that since all the members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement—present and future—are to one degree or another your living *samādhis,* I should strive to detect your presence in them, thereby becoming surrendered beyond limitations.

I have no desire to look for inspiration anywhere else, and I feel sorry for those who cannot grasp the ontological nature of ISKCON, the matchless gift: your all-pervasive and vitalizing sublime personality.

On this auspicious day I would like to thank you for allowing me to appreciate and feel dedicated to your movement, for granting me the benediction of realizing, in my humble way, that you never really left me but are caring for and protecting me in all times, places, and circumstances.

Never for a moment have I doubted that you are my eternal master who have come to rescue me from a miserable life of artificial identity, intelligence, and desires.

Please neglect my numerous faults and allow this conviction to remain incontrovertibly established in my heart up to the moment of death.

This I wish for all the devotees of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Your humble servant.  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Mukunda Goswami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

While maintaining unrequited enthusiasm for the amazing progress ISKCON is making, I am saddened to see that sometimes these days the “house in which everyone can live” slogan is being used as the basis for followers to defect from your movement, seeking “shelter” elsewhere. So I think it appropriate to recount in more detail a passage from your biography by His Holiness Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami, *Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta*. It is found in Volume Three, Chapter Five, called “Swamiji’s Departure.”

It was the summer of 1967 at Stinson Beach, California. Your slow rate of recuperation from deteriorating health had alarmed several of us. You were going back to India, and, we thought, we might never see you again.

One of the devotees in the house asked me to inquire of you whether someone should take over in your absence. He wanted to know if there would be a process to continue our education about Kṛṣṇa consciousness if you were to leave your body. It would be a request about a procedure for continuing education. According to the person asking me, this would entail a particular teacher or *guru* who would carry on the specific work you had started so that it would proceed under the wise direction of someone you would recommend or appoint. The logic of a person’s being involved was that, unless you recommended an individual, the *personal attention* aspect of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which we depended so much on, would be absent. It didn’t really occur to me that the spiritual master’s potency could carry on after his physical departure. Nor did I question why I was being put forward to ask something on another’s behalf. I think I realized it was a bit delicate, asking someone what to do after they die. But I thought it should be asked anyway, not realizing the implications of what I would be asking and not anticipating the kind of answer I would get.

Suddenly I found myself alone in the room with you. When you die, I queried, who would take over the movement, your work, our training and education? I think that the notion of becoming a spiritual orphan had crossed my mind. Your answer did not come forth quickly. After what seemed several minutes of silence, you almost distractedly uttered the name of one of your Godbrothers. Because you didn’t look decisive, I expected you to say more. I would have gladly accepted any recommendation, but you didn’t seem satisfied with your answer. So I waited. Time seemed to stretch almost to eternity. You looked off in the distance. Finally you said, “Actually, it is an insult to the spiritual master.”

If I could have manifested *aṇimā-siddhi* at that point I would have instantly shrunk into the carpet. Another long silence. And you were looking wistful, this time, apparently miles away from the house we were in, from Stinson Beach, from America, from our planet. I knew I had committed some kind of offense, but I didn’t know what it was. I had absolutely no intention of insulting you, but I had done it anyway, and I knew that ignorance would be no excuse. I had done something wrong, and I was going pay for it. What *was* a spiritual master, anyway? I was going to have to find out the hard way. I was preparing for the worst, a rebuke, a stern lesson, an explanation of why my seemingly innocent question had taken on the contours of an offense. Then you closed your eyes. I waited and watched and waited.

Your next response was completely unexpected and astonishing. Though you didn’t open your eyes, change your expression, or say a word, a shallow stream of tears appeared below each of your lotus eyes. You sat perfectly still. Slowly you wiped away the tears, which had gathered into little puddles covering both cheeks. Then you gasped, “My spiritual master—” More was coming. But again you were quiet, as if to regain composure. This time you seemed to be crying, “He was no ordinary spiritual master.” After another long pause, you said in a choked voice, “He saved me.”

I was too emotionally overwhelmed, almost crying myself, to even apologize for what I knew then was a grave error. Maybe I muttered something about being sorry. I should have. I can’t remember. But I really wished I could have just vanished into the woodwork or turned back the clock and started over.

Now the answer was crystal clear. It couldn’t have been more obvious. You weren’t just telling me: you were showing me, imparting a lesson I would and could never forget. It was a lesson told by the spiritual emotions of a pure devotee. *Vāṇī* was greater than *vapu*—words I then did not know. How could it have been said more poignantly? What I had never known was brought home in a way words alone could never express. You were no ordinary spiritual master. Not only were you saving me, but you were saving countless others. You were teaching by practice, not precept. How brilliant! How wonderful! In this one incident I learned volumes about a spiritual master. I learned that the spiritual master does not die with the passing of the physical body; that you must love the spiritual master unconditionally; that love for the spiritual master means fidelity, loyalty, and obedience first; that there could be no other shelter than you; that you were no ordinary spiritual master—you started a movement that would save not only me but the whole the world as well.

Mukunda Goswami

## Naveen Kṛṣṇa dāsa

**Perfect Leader, Perfect Mission**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Last year when I was participating in an Australian leadership conference, we had the opportunity to discuss how your example of leadership perfectly corresponds with all models of ideal leadership. For example:

• Leaders possess character and competency, enabling them to lead others (*ācārya*).  
• Leaders are driven by a mission.  
• Leaders develop an organization to carry out that mission.  
• Leaders align all resources within the organization to serve that mission.  
• Leaders always monitor to see that the organization is working to fulfill that mission.  
• Leaders know how to make adjustments and corrections to keep everything on course.  
• Leaders base their decisions on consistent, fundamental principles.  
• Leaders care for the well-being of followers in every way.

For several days, the Australian devotees and I delved into your life, śāstric principles, and our own lives to see how we could become better ISKCON leaders. After the presentation, the devotees gave me a wrapped, framed present. I thought it was a picture of the Deities, but when I opened it I was surprised to find a nicely framed copy of your original seven purposes of ISKCON. After opening this wonderful gift, I sat back and began to contemplate your words in a new light.

The purposes now appeared to be in a special, carefully defined order.

1\. To systematically propagate spiritual knowledge to society at large and to educate all peoples in the techniques of spiritual life in order to check the imbalance of values in life and to achieve real unity and peace.

This emphasized to me that our purpose is primarily educational. We are to educate all people in practical matters for the purpose of bringing balance and peace to their lives. This confirmed what I’d been thinking. By developing courses, retreats, newsletters, consulting services, and audio- and videotapes that give people practical help in their lives, we’d fulfill the mandate of this statement. Further, we would be appreciated as teachers and educators. Finally, I realized that here was an excellent way to create a sustained, satisfying career for many ISKCON preachers. An effective teacher can generate income simply by offering such courses in practical matters supported by underlying spiritual principles.

2\. To propagate a consciousness of Krishna as revealed in the Bhagavad Gita and Srimad Bhagavatam.

This indicated to me that the deep study of the *śāstras* would be a second step once people have developed a relationship with us from the education we’ve offered, as described above.

3\. To bring the members of the Society together with each other and nearer to Krishna, the prime entity, and thus to develop the idea, within the members and humanity at large, that each soul is part and parcel of the quality of Godhead (Krishna).

This demonstrates that the third step would be that people who benefited by our teaching would naturally be drawn to congregate and understand the underlying scriptural principles.

*4. To teach and encourage the sankirtan movement, congregational chanting of the holy name of God as revealed in the teachings of Lord Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu.*

This points to the congregational chanting’s developing as a fourth step in the process of education. This includes the idea of *nāma-haṭṭa,* further engaging people who have become convinced of the previous steps.

*5. To erect for the members and for society at large a holy place of transcendental pastimes dedicated to the Personality of Krishna.*

Here’s where temple construction comes in. You showed a very systematic development from the first four purposes to this one, where people are now prepared to build a temple for hearing and chanting about Kṛṣṇa.

*6. To bring the members closer together for the purpose of teaching a simpler and more natural way of life.*

To me this indicates the development of village *varṇāśrama* communities so that people, wherever they may be, can come together to live more naturally in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We see that this is happening in our Society, and the GBC is discussing more actively how to facilitate this development, as you desired.

*7. With a view towards achieving the aforementioned purposes, to publish and distribute periodicals, magazines, books, and other writings.*

Coming full circle, this statement confirms that the foundation for everything we’re supposed to do is publishing and distributing knowledge in all the different ways that knowledge is distributed in any particular time.

Reading these purposes again reminded me that ISKCON is meant to be an educational institution, and that you have given us a very systematic way to deliver this education. I feel that we’ve made a little progress in the very first purpose, and when we properly establish the first purpose we’ll have the foundation for completing all the other purposes. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the perfect leader, and you have given us this starting point: *To systematically propagate spiritual knowledge to society at large and to educate all peoples in the techniques of spiritual life in order to check the imbalance of values in life and to achieve real unity and peace.*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please engage me eternally in your service, although I am most unfit and fallen.

Your servant,  
Naveen Kṛṣṇa dāsa

## Nirañjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the course of my trying to serve your mission, along with your order to tell everyone I meet about the teachings of Lord Kṛṣṇa as given in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā,* I have been accepting and training disciples on your behalf. Sometimes, out of uninformed enthusiasm, they’ve written statements about me such as “You have descended to deliver the fallen conditioned souls,” “You were personally sent by Kṛṣṇa,” “You have kindly appeared to distribute Lord Caitanya’s mercy,” and so on. In truth my birth was simply the appearance of another material body in my seemingly endless chain of births and deaths throughout 8,400,000 species of life. In my heart, such statements as these must be offered directly to you. I cannot accept them.

In the Fourth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Maitreya Muni describes that at the time of Pṛthu Mahārāja’s coronation, many *brāhmaṇas,* professional reciters, and denizens of the upper planets appeared on the scene to offer him select words of glorification. When Pṛthu Mahārāja heard their praise, he responded,

“O gentle *sūta, māgadha,* and other devotees offering prayers, the qualities of which you have spoken are not distinct in me. Why then should you praise me for all these qualities when I do not shelter these features? I do not wish for these words meant for me to go in vain, but it is better that they be offered to someone else. O gentle reciters, offer such prayers in due course of time, when the qualities of which you have spoken actually manifest themselves in me. The gentle who offer prayers to the Supreme Personality of Godhead do not attribute such qualities to a human being, who does not actually have them” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.15.22–23)

You clearly explain in the purports to these two verses, “Because the qualities were not yet manifest, however, King Pṛthu very humbly asked why the devotees should praise him with such exalted words. He did not want anyone to offer him prayers or glorify him unless he possessed the real qualities of which they spoke.” And in the next purport you elaborate further, “He \[Pṛthu Mahārāja\] wanted to stress that one who does not actually possess these qualities should not try to engage his followers and devotees in offering him glory for them, even though these qualities might be manifest in the future. If a man who does not factually possess the attributes of a great personality engages his followers in praising him with the expectation that such attributes will develop in the future, that sort of praise is actually an insult.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although I know it’s my duty to at times accept statements of praise, I can accept only that which clearly gives all credit to you. Not only is it untrue to say that I willfully descended from the spiritual world to preach, but it also gives no credit to you. But when something perceivable is praised—some quality others may see in me that is connected to the effect of your preaching—then it is clear, at least in my mind, that although the praiser is seeing something in me which has given him or her faith to serve Kṛṣṇa, every drop of the credit goes to you. A disciple must see how his spiritual master is following the order of his spiritual master. To speak about that is perceivable, and that is what gives us faith.

On this most auspicious day, I would like to glorify you from this same platform, because who could possibly serve as a better example of strictly following the order of *guru* than you? What inspires me most about you, the qualities of which I most often speak when lecturing about you, is your firm faith in the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, your incisive preaching, and your profound compassion for the conditioned souls. While remembering you, hearing you lecture, hearing about your dealings with your disciples, or hearing about your preaching pastimes, I am inspired to surrender my life to you. To see you and appreciate you most, in my humble estimation, is to see you as an undaunted warrior against the degrading influence of Kali, preaching boldly, though with immeasurable compassion, on the strength of the order given to you by your Guru Mahārāja. These inspirational activities of yours are perceivable to anyone who is willing to open his eyes, and if coupled with a submissive attitude, one can look and see them for himself. One need not project into your eternal relationship with Kṛṣṇa to recognize that you are both special and someone worthy of all glorification.

Seeing your activities in relationship to the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta does not diminish your glory. On the contrary, it enhances it, decorating you with all the ornaments of one who is dear to Kṛṣṇa on the strength of your preaching and resolute faith in your spiritual master. You have written in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.51, “If a disciple is very serious to execute the mission of the spiritual master, he immediately associates with the Supreme Personality of Godhead by *vāṇī* or *vapuḥ*. This is the only secret of success in seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, one point you so boldly preached about again and again and again was how those of even the lowest birth could be elevated to the platform of Vaiṣṇava by the association of a Vaiṣṇava. We find this point everywhere in your teachings. You did not discriminate, thinking that only those of “high birth” are qualified to take up the mission of Vaiṣṇavas. That’s not what Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta preached, and that is not what you were ordered to do by your Guru Mahārāja. If the low-born could not become Vaiṣṇavas, then why did you come to the land of *mlecchas* and Yavanas? And why did Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta order you to come? You were convinced of this, one hundred percent convinced, because you were convinced about your own spiritual master. Therefore despite a total lack of support from your Godbrothers, you did what none of them were willing to do, although the order was there for any of them to follow. With unflinching faith you freely gave your association and preached the pure and transparent message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness without the slightest discrimination against those who had taken their birth outside the borders of Bhārata-bhūmi, and, as you personally said, you achieved success beyond your imagination.

It’s clear to me that my birth was not an act of the Lord’s internal potency. I was not born in a devout family, in my childhood I did not show any visible inclinations towards devotional life, and in my youth I was addicted to all kinds of sinful activities. Moreover, considering what I was doing throughout those years, and then considering my activities today, there is no doubt in my mind that any recognizable difference between then and now is a tribute to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and not to myself. I was heading on the course of *adānta-gobhir viśatāṁ tamisraṁ punaḥ punaś carvita-carvaṇānām*.

It is not that I particularly find my present-day example to be so elevated that I am making these statements. I can barely be called a devotee. Still, despite my own lack of qualification, and despite such a low birth and low life prior to meeting you, out of your causeless mercy you have given someone as unqualified as I am the opportunity to carry on with Lord Caitanya’s mission. In my humble estimation, that glorifies you, because *that* is what you preached so boldly about. You preached that anyone, no matter how low his birth, no matter how sinful his character, even if he was born in a family of dog-eaters, could take up the confidential service of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Countless times I’ve both seen and heard you quote the following verse:

*kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye ’nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*

**“Kirātas, Hūnas, Āndhras, Pulindas, Pulkaśas, Ābhīras, Śumbhas, Yavanas, and members of the Khaśa races, and even others who are addicted to sinful acts, can be purified by taking shelter of the devotees of the Lord, due to His being the supreme power. I beg to offer my respectful obeisances unto Him” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **2.4.18).**

Therefore on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā I humbly prostrate myself at your lotus feet and beg you to please keep me eternally engaged however you see fit. I am your dog, forever and ever, and even if you want me to take more births like this one again and again, then I beg you to please grant me the benediction that I’ll come back repeating your words. They are perfect and complete, and I can say with firm faith that it was your words, and nothing else, that have made this lifetime of mine auspicious.

Your eternal servant,  
Nirañjana Swami

## Paraṁgati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear master, please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. On this sacred occasion of your divine appearance on this planet, my thoughts are absorbed in your extraordinary characteristics. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 22.78–80), Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu describes to Sanātana Gosvāmī some of the wonderful qualities of a Vaiṣṇava:

*kṛpālu, akṛta-droha, satya-sāra sama*  
*nidoṣa, vadānya, mṛdu, śuci, akiñcana*  
*sarvopakāraka, śānta, kṛṣṇaika-śaraṇa*  
*akāma, anīha, sthira, vijita-ṣaḍ-guṇa*  
*mita-bhuk, apramatta, mānada, amānī*  
*gambhīra, karuṇa, maitra, kavi, dakṣa, maunī*

**“Devotees are always merciful, humble, truthful, equal to all, faultless, magnanimous, mild, and clean. They are without material possessions, and they perform welfare work for everyone. They are peaceful, surrendered to Kṛṣṇa, and desireless. They are indifferent to material acquisitions and are fixed in devotional service. They completely control the six bad qualities—lust, anger, greed, and so forth. They eat only as much as required, and they are not inebriated. They are respectful, grave, compassionate, and without false prestige. They are friendly, poetic, expert, and silent.”**

Out of all these transcendental qualities that you possess, *kṛpālu*—being merciful—is very prominent. The Supreme Personality of Godhead is *karuṇā-sindhu,* an ocean of mercy. The blind conditioned soul doesn’t understand that the root cause of his terrible situation in this world is his misconception of false ego—“I’m this body”—and he’s obliged to accept this terrible material body life after life. And those wonderful transcendental qualities are covered by ignorance. Your instructions are not different from those given by the Supreme Personality of Godhead and the *ācāryas* in His line. And your main instruction is that by practicing regularly the principles of devotional service, which start with hearing and chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* one becomes free from ignorance and then his relationship with Kṛṣṇa becomes revealed.

Therefore you personify *kṛpālu*—merciful. By your mercy everyone now has access to this most confidential knowledge, and by your mercy people in Kali-yuga have the rare chance to become free from demoniac tendencies and become divine.

Of course, we can see all twenty-six transcendental qualities fully manifested in your character, but *kṛpālu*—merciful—is exceptionally prominent. In a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class you gave in Los Angeles on May 30, 1972, you mentioned that the Vaiṣṇava is kind to everyone. Not only to the human beings but also to the animals, birds, plants, etc. This is the specific characteristic of a devotee. O master, you’ve shown us how to develop such kindness, and by your own personal example you’ve revealed that when one preaches and gives this Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* and this knowledge to the people, by the grace of the spiritual master and Supreme Lord one becomes decorated with all these transcendental qualities. Therefore again and again I bow down at your divine lotus feet, which are my only shelter, and pray that by your grace such qualities will one day manifest in my life.

Your humble servant,  
Paraṁgati Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

To become a disciple of a bona fide spiritual master is a really deep commitment. It requires enduring steadfastness and determination.

Material life is like a roller coaster. There are so many sudden tosses and turns that at any moment one may be thrown off balance and lose his grip. We have seen so many devotees become influenced by the modes of material nature and suddenly or gradually lose their Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Māyā has so many tricks up her sleeve—so many ways to distract one from spiritual life.

Therefore, the aspiring devotee has to be ever vigilant to maintain his Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the midst of the ever-changing phantasmagoria of this world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are an example of real determination and steadfastness. You had to face so many obstacles in your life, yet you surmounted each and every one of them by dint of your strong faith in *guru* and Kṛṣṇa—not only by your faith in them, but by your unwavering practical application of their teachings. You had such real compassion for the suffering living entities in this world that you fully dedicated your life to curing them. By both example and precept you taught how any person in any part of the world, coming from any background, can become Kṛṣṇa conscious. You were always encouraging everyone to advance in spiritual life. “Just do as I am doing,” you would say, thus showing us how Kṛṣṇa consciousness is readily accessible to one and all.

It is regrettable that many who accepted you as their spiritual master have for one reason or another not maintained their commitment to you and, in some cases, even acted in ways detrimental to your ISKCON. On the other hand, it is very inspiring to see those great souls who have maintained their commitment to you and who go on following your instructions. By their activities and example they bring credit and glory to you and carry on your work. Thus you continue to live in their hearts.

On this most auspicious day I pray that I may continue to serve you in your ISKCON and thus be of some use to your mission, even if not very significant. I pray that I may not forget you and not be misled by the many allurements of Māyā, gross or subtle. I pray to go on following the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness constantly, in all circumstances.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me shelter in the shade of your lotus feet.

Your unworthy servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Rādhānātha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances.

On this auspicious day I pray that I may wholeheartedly dedicate my life to becoming a truly humble servant of your servants.

Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has so dearly entrusted you with the very essence of His causeless mercy. You have freely given the entire world the most blessed opportunity to receive the priceless treasure of *kṛṣṇa-prema* through the congregational chanting of the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Your oceanic compassion has made the path of spiritual perfection a most joyful way of living. Within our movement there have been many mistakes and shortcomings. It is your divine presence that sustains and nourishes the spiritual lives of all devotees within the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. In your service we may accept various positions, but in truth we are tiny particles of dust at your lotus feet. I pray that we may never pursue any aspiration other than this, to be an insignificant particle of dust in the service of your lotus feet. As long as we remain united in the spirit of your humble servants, we will be protected and empowered to assist you in your glorious service to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga. I am helplessly dependent upon your causeless mercy. I pray that I may eternally be a surrendered puppet in the merciful hands of Your Divine Grace.

Your servant,  
Rādhānātha Swami

## Rāmāi Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*vijita-hṛṣīka-vāyubhir adānta-manas tura-gaṁ*  
*ya iha yatanti yantum ati-lolam upāya-khidaḥ*  
*vyasana-śatānvitāḥ samavahāya guroś caraṇaṁ*  
*vaṇija ivāja santy akṛta-karṇa-dharā jaladhau*

**“The mind is like an impetuous horse that even persons who have regulated their senses and breath cannot control. Those in this world who try to tame the uncontrolled mind, but who abandon the feet of their spiritual master, encounter hundreds of obstacles in their cultivation of various distressful practices. O unborn Lord, they are like merchants on a boat in the ocean who have failed to employ a helmsman” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **10.87.33).**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is said that of all things in this world, the mind is the most difficult to overcome. Somehow you knew the mental conditioning of the Westerners. Furthermore, you were already familiar with the Eastern mentality. In fact, all different types of mentalities were known to you. No one else was so empowered by the Supreme Personality of Godhead to preach all over the world, to cut through the dense covering of the modes of material nature, especially the modes of passion and ignorance.

Kali-yuga people are so engrossed in their worldly pleasures that they know of nothing else. For them spiritual life is like a small dot on the vast horizon, practically imperceptible. So thick is their illusion that for all intents and purposes it is impenetrably deep and frightening as well. Only something or someone very powerful could ever hope to penetrate such an all-encompassing darkness. Many nondevotees, thinking they know how, profess to having the key that opens the firmly shut door, the light that illuminates the pervasive darkness. What foolish creatures they are! What does a blind man know of the moon and the sun? What does a desert animal know of the vast ocean? All of us, your disciples and granddisciples, were in the same position as these unfortunate and wretched beings—wallowing, that is—until we came in contact with your infinite and divine mercy.

Prabhupāda, before meeting you we were ignorant. There were so many things we didn’t know or care about—transcendental knowledge, kindness and compassion, etc. You were also firm and strict, however: you wouldn’t allow us to deviate even an inch from the path of devotional service given by Kṛṣṇa and the predecessor *ācāryas*. Our conditioning was so strong that even a little bit of leeway on your part would have seen us speeding off at a hundred miles an hour in the opposite direction. Recently, while reading one of your conversations with your disciples, I saw how you would very mercifully reprimand us and keep us in check.

**Prabhupāda:** So J. should be strictly advised not to become very learned to correct authorities. No.

**Devotee:** I think that instruction you gave will help him very much about even if he thinks there is some mistake, just forget about it.

**Prabhupāda:** He is mistake. He should not think his authority mistake.

**Devotee:** He didn’t know what he should do. He didn’t know…

**Prabhupāda:** So why he should be given this business? He’s such irresponsible man. He should not be given any responsible work. Our first business should see how he is advanced in devotion. We don’t want so-called scholars.

**Devotee:** They just leave us.

**Prabhupāda:** Leave, I don’t mind. Just like \[N.\] became a big scholar.… He’s a rascal.

It was an undeniable fact that no one could escape your transcendental scrutiny, but you were motivated by pure love for us and you wanted to see us make further advancement in devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. You were pushing us more and more:

“A true devotee never thinks there is a limit to his devotional service, that he is satisfied.” You would not tolerate any nonsense from the materialists, nor from the devotees.

*Pāgale ki nā bole, chāgale ki nā khāya:* “A madman, what does he not say, and a goat, what does he not eat?”

I remember sitting on the floor in front of your *vyāsāsana* when you gave lectures. We were like inexperienced, youthful grandchildren, hanging on every word and sentence you spoke. Everything was a wonderful revelation. We felt proud of such a glorious spiritual master; we felt protected because your presence emanated Kṛṣṇa and the inconceivable spiritual abode. When you wore your glasses and read from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* or *Bhagavad-gītā,* you looked very scholarly, and we felt that we were before a great venerable sage, the true representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

Prabhupāda, the emissary from the spiritual world,  
Message from Kṛṣṇa you forcefully unfurled.  
Those who would not hear and ran away,  
Captured and brought back to sit in the sun, the light of day.

Like a physician, cutting masterfully the disease—  
Great precision, but with transcendental ease.  
Scream and yell, they might at first,  
But in the end thankfully cured, ecstatic feeling, illusion burst.

With great enthusiasm you sought more and more,  
Traveling the world more than half a score.  
People were hiding in remote parts of the globe,  
But you found them out and led them to their transcendental home.

Forever indebted to you, many have now realized.  
How to repay—that must be emphasized:  
Follow in your footsteps and do as you did.  
We pray to satisfy and please you, some day we will personally be near you.

Every year, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I get ever newer realizations of what an incredible and amazing personality you were and are, just by looking at your picture, by reading your books, hearing a tape, meeting some fortunate soul who met you and never forgot. In so many ways you are so kind on this lowly so-called disciple; it is your and Kṛṣṇa’s mercy on me. I don’t know if I’ll ever be a proper disciple, but I must keep trying, for all in this world is temporary and illusion, and you are my reality and my link to what is actual reality.

Your aspiring servant,  
Rāmāi Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*vāñchā-kalpatarubhyaś ca kṛpā-sindhubhya eva ca*  
*patitānāṁ pāvanebhyo vaiṣṇavebhyo namo namaḥ*

My dear followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We are all agreed that Śrīla Prabhupāda was a miracle: someone supernatural who entered into our mundane affairs and altered the normal course of nature. The laws of nature—our karmic destiny and, by extension, the destiny of the world—have been broken.

It is essential to the nature of that miracle that it keep on going, perpetuate after Śrīla Prabhupāda’s physical departure. After all, the miracle of Prabhupāda lies precisely in his supernatural ability to create similarly miraculous persons. He was a devotee who could make others into devotees who could in turn make others into devotees, and so on and on.

The archetypically Vaiṣṇava miracle is thus beyond your ordinary wonder-working: Its miracle is to miraculously create more miracle-creators. The Vaiṣṇava miracle is one that perpetually grows.

Consequently, those of use who aspire to be the heirs of Śrīla Prabhupāda need to make this our study: How does this miracle come to be made? It is our obligation to be able to answer, scientifically, these questions: How did Śrīla Prabhupāda come to be a miracle-creator? And how do we do the same?

Miracles are mysterious because, according to Prabhupāda, miracle means simply that “you don’t know how it is done.” So miracles are typically surrounded by secrets. Thaumaturges guard their secrets in order to bedazzle the many. But Prabhupāda abhorred mystification. He kept nothing back. He gave away his secret.

In 1968, while speaking in Los Angeles on the anniversary of the disappearance of his Guru Mahārāja, Prabhupāda looked at the rows of American faces lifted up to hear him, and he was led to contemplate a miracle: how, by the will of Kṛṣṇa—not the ordinary course of *karma*—the spiritual master and the disciple come together:

I was born in a different family; my Guru Mahārāja was born in a different family. Who knew that I will come to his protection? Who knew that I would come in America? Who knew that you American boys will come to me? These are all Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement. We cannot understand how things are taking place.

But then Śrīla Prabhupāda goes on to explain how, by the grace of Kṛṣṇa, the miracle of his presence in Los Angles came about. This is Prabhupāda’s “course in miracles.”

In 1936—today is ninth December, 1968—that means thirty-two years ago, in Bombay, I was then doing some business: All of a sudden—perhaps on this date, sometimes between ninth or tenth of December (at that time, Guru Mahārāja was indisposed little, and he was staying at Jagannātha Purī, on the seashore)—so, I wrote him a letter: “My dear master, your other disciples—*brahmacārī, sannyāsī*—they are rendering you direct service. And I am a householder: I cannot live with you, I cannot serve you nicely. So I do not know. How can I serve you?” Simply an idea: I was thinking of serving him—“How can I serve him seriously?”

So the seed of the miracle is the desire to serve. Discontented with his occupation in business, feeling himself incapacitated by the obligations of his *āśrama,* on an impulse (“all of a sudden”) Prabhupāda wrote his Guru Mahārāja with a plea, a cry from the heart. He felt himself locked in a position that made proper service impossible, yet still the desire to do it was there. So he confessed his desire and his frustration to his spiritual master.

Prabhupāda continues:

So the reply was dated 13 December, 1936. In that letter he wrote, “My dear such and such, I am very glad to receive your letter. I think you should try to push our movement in English.” That was his writing. “And that will do good to you and to the people who will help you.” That was his instruction. And then in 1936, on the thirty-first of December—that means just after writing this letter a fortnight before his departure—he passed away. But I took that order of my spiritual master very seriously, but I did not think that I’ll have to do such and such thing. I was at that time a householder. But this is the arrangement of Kṛṣṇa. If we strictly try to serve the spiritual master, his order, then Kṛṣṇa will give us all facilities. That is the secret. Although there was no possibility—I never thought—

It was a startling, an unexpected, an incongruous, an entirely *improbable* order. Prabhupāda took it “very seriously” nonetheless, even though he could not envision any concrete circumstances in which it could be realized. He was then doing business in Bombay, entangled in household and commercial affairs, so the order seemed remote from reality. At the same time, it was the *last direct communication* he received from his spiritual master. That gave it even more weight. So he understood he must take it seriously, even though he was initially baffled. How in the world would it happen? As it turns out, it happened by a miraculous, divine intervention: “the arrangement of Kṛṣṇa,” Prabhupāda says.

Yet Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement is not all that is required. For what prompts Kṛṣṇa to make such an arrangement? The seriousness of the disciple. “If we strictly try to serve the spiritual master, his order, then Kṛṣṇa will give us all facilities. That is the secret.”

Now Prabhupāda goes on to tell us how—again by Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement—he learned this secret:

Although there was no possibility—I never thought—But I took it little seriously by studying a commentary by Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura on the *Bhagavad-gītā*. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* there is the verse *vyavasāyātmikā-buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana*. In connection with that verse, Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura gives his commentary that we should take up the words from the spiritual master as our life and soul. We should try to carry out the instruction, the specific instruction of the spiritual master, very rigidly, without caring for our personal benefit or loss.

Here is the immediate source of Prabhupāda’s inspiration. The realization he acquired from reading Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s commentary on *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.41 became the cornerstone of his life and achievement, the only secret of his success. Again and again Prabhupāda directly and indirectly refers to this defining moment of his life, when he was granted the realization to make the firm commitment that, come what may, he would make the order of his spiritual master his life and soul. Because of that commitment alone, Kṛṣṇa has brought him to America and given him success:

So I tried a little bit in that spirit. So he has given me all facilities to serve him. Things have come to this stage, that in this old age I have come to your country, and you are also taking this movement seriously, trying to understand it. We have got some books now. So there is little foothold of this movement.

Now Prabhupāda requests his followers to enact the same commitment to his order that he has evinced to that of his Guru Mahārāja:

So on this occasion of my spiritual master’s departure, as I am trying to execute his will, similarly, I shall also request you to execute the same order through my will. I am an old man, I can also pass away at any moment. That is nature’s law. Nobody can check it. So that is not very astonishing, but my appeal to you on this auspicious day of the departure of my Guru Mahārāja, that at least to some extent you have understood the essence of Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. You should try to push it on.

“Execute his will” is a play on words. The expression means, of course, to carry out the order of some one, but it is also the formal legal term to refer to the process by which a person’s assets legally become those of his heirs. By his commitment to execute Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī’s will, Śrīla Prabhupāda inherited from him his specific potency to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. On this occasion Śrīla Prabhupāda is now making his will: “I shall also request you to execute the same order through my will. I am an old man.” By his will, Prabhupāda has made us his heirs. He gives us the instructions which, if we accept them as our life and soul, will transfer to us the same potency to make people devotees of Kṛṣṇa.

What specifically are those instructions? Prabhupāda is very clear:

…at least to some extent you have understood the essence of Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. You should try to push it on. People are suffering for want of this consciousness. As we daily pray about devotees:

*vāñchā-kalpatarubhyaś ca*  
*kṛpā-sindhubhya eva ca*  
*patitānāṁ pāvanebhyo*  
*vaiṣṇavebhyo namo namaḥ*

A Vaiṣṇava, or devotee of Lord, his life is dedicated for the benefit of the people. You know—most of you belong to Christian community—how Lord Jesus Christ, he said that for your sinful activities he has sacrificed himself. That is the determination of devotee of the Lord. They don’t care for personal comforts. Because they love Kṛṣṇa or God, therefore they love all living entities because all living entities are in relationship with Kṛṣṇa. So similarly you should learn. This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement means to become Vaiṣṇava and feel for the suffering humanity.

And what does “feel for suffering humanity” concretely mean?

So to feel for the suffering humanity—there are different angles of vision. Somebody is thinking of the suffering of the humanity from the bodily conception of life. Somebody is trying to open a hospital to give relief to the diseased condition. Somebody is trying to distribute foodstuff in poverty-stricken countries or places. These things are certainly very nice, but actual suffering of the humanity is due to lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. These bodily sufferings: they are temporary; neither they can be checked by the laws of nature. Suppose if you give some distribution of foodstuff in some poverty-stricken country, that does not mean that this help makes solution of the whole problem. The real beneficial work is to invoke every person to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

In short, Prabhupāda’s order to all of us is: Become a Vaiṣṇava yourself, and make other people Vaiṣṇavas. Not all of us have received some specific, tailor-made order from our spiritual master, and we may feel that we have failed to receive the spiritual master’s gift, that we may have been left out of his will. That is not so. Here Prabhupāda very clearly delivers his order to every one of us. It has two parts. One, we should become Kṛṣṇa conscious, and two, we should give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others. Our full commitment, our single-minded intelligence, dedicated to this order will itself make the order achieve fructification by the will of Kṛṣṇa.

In a lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.2.11 given in Vṛndāvana on October 22, 1972, Prabhupāda again refers to Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s commentary on *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.41. Prabhupāda begins by telling us we should act so that we will attract Kṛṣṇa’s attention. How to do that? The answer: Serve Kṛṣṇa by following His representative, the spiritual master. Anyone who does this thereby becomes himself a representative of Kṛṣṇa. And how is that? One simply has to transmit the message of Kṛṣṇa without any adulteration. This leads Prabhupāda to cite *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya* 7.128:

Just like Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, *āmāra ājñāya guru hañā,* “You become a spiritual master under My order.” So if you carry out the order of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Kṛṣṇa, then you become guru. *Āmāra ājñāya guru hañā*. Unfortunately, we do not wish to carry out order of the *ācāryas*. We manufacture our own ways. We have got practical experience how a great institution was lost by whimsical ways. Without carrying out the order of the spiritual master, they manufactured something and the whole thing was lost.

Let us reflect on this passage. Here, Prabhupāda cites *āmāra ājñāya guru hañā* as conveying the essential qualification for becoming a spiritual master: to obey the order of the *ācāryas*. When Prabhupāda remarks, “Unfortunately, we do not wish to carry out order of the *ācāryas*. We manufacture our own ways,” he is alluding to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.18.5: “A foolish person who manufactures his own ways and means through mental speculation and does not recognize the authority of the sages who lay down unimpeachable directions is simply unsuccessful again and again in his attempts.” To illustrate the failure that attends disobedience to the order of the *ācārya,* Prabhupāda then refers to the break-up of the Gauḍīya Maṭha (“a great institution was lost”) after Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s departure.

As we have seen, in his lecture on the anniversary of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s departure, Prabhupāda had invoked Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s direction as the key to his success. Now he will go on to cite the neglect of that same direction in explaining the failure of other disciples of his Guru Mahārāja:

Therefore Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura stresses very much on the words of the spiritual master. *Vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana*. If you stick to the order of spiritual master, without caring for your own convenience or inconvenience, then you become perfect.

*yasya deve parā bhaktir*  
*yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ*  
*prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

This is the confirmation of all authorities. We have to carry out very faithfully the order of the bona fide representative of Kṛṣṇa. Then our life is successful. Then we can understand Kṛṣṇa in truth.

In the above passage, Prabhupāda alluded to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.18.5 itself. In his purport to that text, Śrīla Prabhupāda brings Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s direction and Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s together in a way that further elucidates the potency of strictly following the spiritual master’s order:

At the present moment it has become fashionable to disobey the unimpeachable directions given by the *ācāryas* and liberated souls of the past. Presently people are so fallen that they cannot distinguish between a liberated soul and a conditioned soul. A conditioned soul is hampered by four defects: he is sure to commit mistakes, he is sure to become illusioned, he has a tendency to cheat others, and his senses are imperfect. Consequently we have to take direction from liberated persons.

Prabhupāda is going to tell us how to discriminate between a liberated and a conditioned soul, so that we can know from whom we should take directions. In fact, he tells us that ISKCON is guided by Kṛṣṇa through such liberated souls:

This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement directly receives instructions from the Supreme Personality of Godhead via persons who are strictly following His instructions.

This sentence contains two surprises. First: Prabhupāda says that our movement is “directly” receiving instruction from Kṛṣṇa “via” other persons. This is paradoxical on the face of it. Since Prabhupāda would not state an absurdity, we have to conclude that although Kṛṣṇa’s instructions may come via the medium of another agent, the instructions are nevertheless direct and unmediated. In other words, the proper medium is entirely transparent to Kṛṣṇa (and to the previous *ācārya* as well) so that the intermediary does not really come between. The question about how a disciple of Prabhupāda’s disciple can have a direct relationship with Śrīla Prabhupāda is answered herein.

Second: Since the previous sentence enjoins that one has to take direction from liberated souls, we are primed to expect this sentence to end “via persons who are liberated.” Prabhupāda upsets that expectation by saying “via persons who are strictly following his instructions.” The next sentence addresses this subversion of our expectations:

Although a follower may not be a liberated person, if he follows the supreme, liberated Personality of Godhead, his actions are naturally liberated from the contamination of the material nature. Lord Caitanya therefore says: “By My order you may become a spiritual master.” One can immediately become a spiritual master by having full faith in the transcendental words of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and by following His instructions.

It is a clumsy error to interpret this passage as implying that a bona fide spiritual master may be a conditioned soul. Rather, this passages tells us what “liberated” effectively means: it means to strictly follow the orders of the spiritual master.

Prabhupāda makes this even clearer in his purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.20.13, a verse that quotes the orders given to Pṛthu Mahārāja directly by Lord Viṣṇu. Prabhupāda remarks:

One has to execute the order of Lord Viṣṇu, whether receiving it directly from Him or from His bona fide representative, the spiritual master. Arjuna fought the Battle of Kurukṣetra under the direct order of the Supreme personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. Similarly, here Pṛthu Mahārāja is also being given orders by Lord Viṣṇu regarding the execution of his duty. We have to stick to the principles stated in the *Bhagavad-gītā*. *Vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ:* every man’s duty is to receive orders from Lord Kṛṣṇa or from His bona fide representative and take these orders as his life and soul, without personal considerations. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura states that one should not care very much whether he is going to be liberated or not, but he should simply execute the direct order received from the spiritual master. If one sticks to the principle of abiding by the order of the spiritual master, he will always remain in a liberated position.

Our concern should therefore be single: stick to the order of the spiritual master. That principle is sufficient in itself to answer the questions of securing liberation and direct guidance from the Lord. Prabhupāda makes this point with equal directness in his purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.24.15:

This is the secret of success. After being initiated and receiving the orders of the spiritual master, the disciple should unhesitatingly think about the instructions or orders of the spiritual master and should not allow himself to be disturbed by anything else. This is also the verdict of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, who, while explaining a verse of *Bhagavad-gītā* (*vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana*, Bg. 2.41), points out that the order of the spiritual master is the life substance of the disciple. The disciple should not consider whether he is going back home, back to Godhead; his first business should be to execute the order of his spiritual master. Thus a disciple should always meditate on the order of the spiritual master, and that is perfectional meditation. Not only should he meditate upon that order, but he should find out the means by which he can perfectly worship and execute it.

If we look back at Prabhupāda’s various presentations of Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s understanding of *vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ,* we find that the instruction that we should simply fix our entire attention on the spiritual master’s order and forget all personal considerations becomes explicated in greater and greater depth: “We should try to carry out the instruction, the specific instruction of the spiritual master, very rigidly, without caring for our personal benefit or loss.” And: “If you stick to the order of spiritual master, without caring for your own convenience or inconvenience, then you become perfect.” And then: “Although a follower may not be a liberated person, if he follows the supreme, liberated Personality of Godhead, his actions are naturally liberated from the contamination of the material nature.” And again: “Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura states that one should not care very much whether he is going to be liberated or not, but he should simply execute the direct order received from the spiritual master. If one sticks to the principle of abiding by the order of the spiritual master, he will always remain in a liberated position.” And in the last purport: “The disciple should not consider whether he is going back home, back to Godhead; his first business should be to execute the order of his spiritual master.”

Following the order of the spiritual master is the same as attaining not only liberation but also the direct association with the Supreme Personality of Godhead. We should therefore fix our attention exclusively on that order: all success is thereby obtained. Prabhupāda makes this brilliantly clear when he explicitly discusses how the disciples should keep faith with the spiritual master after the master’s physical departure. Prabhupāda says (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.47, purport):

The disciple and spiritual master are never separated because the spiritual master always keeps company with the disciple as long as the disciple follows strictly the instructions of the spiritual master. This is called the association of *vāṇī* (words). Physical presence is called *vapuḥ*. As long as the spiritual master is physically present, the disciple should serve the physical body of the spiritual master, and when the spiritual master is no longer physically existing, the disciple should serve the instructions of the spiritual master.

This *vāṇī-sevā* is further explain in the purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.51:

When one becomes serious to follow the mission of the spiritual master, his resolution is tantamount to seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. As explained before, this means meeting the Supreme Personality of Godhead in the instruction of the spiritual master. This is technically called *vāṇī-sevā*. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura states in his *Bhagavad-gītā* commentary on the verse *vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana* (Bg. 2.41) that one should serve the words of the spiritual master. The disciple must stick to whatever the spiritual master orders. Simply by following on that line, one sees the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Prabhupāda goes on to say that there may be different ways of associating with the Supreme Personality of Godhead. “Nonetheless,” he continues,

if one sticks to the principles enunciated by the spiritual master, somehow or other he is in association with the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Since the Lord is in the heart, He can advise a sincere disciple from within. This is also confirmed in *Bhagavad-gītā* (10.10):

*teṣāṁ satata-yuktānāṁ bhajatāṁ prīti-pūrvakam*  
*dadāmi buddhi-yogaṁ taṁ yena mām upayānti te*

**“To those who are constantly devoted and worship Me with love, I give the understanding by which they can come to Me.”**

In conclusion, if a disciple is very serious to execute the mission of the spiritual master, he immediately associates with the Supreme Personality of Godhead by *vāṇī* or *vapuḥ*. This is the only secret of success in seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Instead of being eager to see the Lord in some bush of Vṛndāvana while at the same time engaging in sense gratification, if one instead sticks to the principle of following the words of the spiritual master, he will see the Supreme Lord without difficulty.

Śrīla Bilvamaṅgala Ṭhākura has therefore said:

*bhaktis tvayi sthiratarā bhagavan yadi syād*  
*daivena naḥ phalati divya-kiśora-mūrtiḥ*  
*muktiḥ svayaṁ mukulitāñjali sevate ‘smān*  
*dharmārtha-kāma-gatayaḥ samaya-pratīkṣāḥ*

**“If I am engaged in devotional service unto You, my dear Lord, then very easily can I perceive Your presence everywhere. And as far as liberation is concerned, I think that liberation stands at my door with folded hands, waiting to serve me—and all material conveniences of** ***dharma*** **\[religiosity\],** ***artha*** **\[economic development\] and** ***kāma*** **\[sense gratification\] stand with her.” (*****Kṛṣṇa-karṇāmṛta*** **107)**

If one is very highly advanced in devotional service, he will have no difficulty in seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. If one engages in the service of the spiritual master, he not only sees the Supreme Personality of Godhead but attains liberation. As far as material conveniences are concerned, they automatically come, just as the maidservants of a queen follow the queen wherever she goes. Liberation is no problem for the pure devotee, and all material conveniences are simply awaiting him at all stages of life.

In conclusion, it is our duty—we are so ordered on highest authority—to perpetuate the miracle of Śrīla Prabhupāda, just as Prabhupāda perpetuated the miracle of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Prabhupāda has given us everything we need to do that, patiently and clearly spelled out to us “the only secret of success.” Now let us resolve to take that priceless gift to heart, cherish it and nurture it. Let us make his order our life and soul, and let us live our lives solely for the sake of others. We owe this to Prabhupāda for what he has done for us.

Begging at your feet for your blessings so I may someday come to serve in your association the order of Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Rohiṇī-suta dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your golden lotus feet.

Today is your most glorious appearance day, and I want to submit the following prayer at your lotus feet:

With my body, mind, and words I would like to surrender more to Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet. I would like that one day these golden lotus feet will become manifested in the innermost core of my heart. Only then can I do something wonderful for Your Divine Grace’s pleasure; only then I can be really satisfied. When will this day come, that I can remember only you, your instructions, your pastimes, and your divine characteristics? Now my mind is so far away from you. I try to serve you, I try to think about you, I try to speak about you, but due to my misfortune I mostly fail. My mind always wants to do something different, and therefore I feel so lonely, so far away from you, and so lost. This is my misfortune. And this makes me desperate. Therefore I cry out to you: Prabhupāda! Please help me, I am so fallen, helpless, my heart is so dark, so empty, full of offenses and a fault-finding mentality, as hard as a stone and devoid of devotional feeling. How can one go on like that? Material life is so miserable, but still I think it is so nice.

Prabhupāda! I try to be your servant and the servant of your servant; there is no other way. Please accept my little endeavor. Let me use this body more and more in your wonderful mission. Let me always fall down at your lotus feet and offer my *daṇḍavats* and chant your *praṇāma* eternally. Please use my lifeless body in your mission. The body gets older day by day and gradually becomes useless for your mission. I would like to be able to engage every cell and pore of my body and burn all my blood in the *saṅkīrtana* mission.

Prabhupāda! Although my mind is full of horrible things, I still have the hope and desire that it will become purified in your mission, and that I will purely fix my mind on your lotus feet and become like a honeybee always drinking the nectar from them. Then only will my mind find satisfaction.

Prabhupāda! Please drop some nectar on my desertlike tongue. This nectar comes from your lotus mouth—*guru-mukha-padma-vākya*. Please let me eternally drink this nectar through my ears: “A devotee of the Lord cannot give up the service of the Lord, for he is ordered by his spiritual master. Pure devotees like Nārada and Nityānanda Prabhu take up the order of the spiritual master as the sustenance of life. They do not mind what becomes of the future of their lives. They take the matter very seriously as the order comes from the higher authority, from the representative of the Lord, or from the Lord Himself” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.8.6)

To speak about you is the perfection of life. Unfortunately, I don’t speak much about you, because I always think about myself, and that is a great stumbling block in my life. But I always desire and hope that I will get the opportunity to associate with and hear from those faithful servants of yours who always glorify you and speak about your nectar pastimes and characteristics and faithfully try to execute your divine instructions. Fortunately there are such faithful servants of yours on this planet, and I always beg for their mercy and association birth after birth. To see them, to hear them, and to speak with them always reminds me of you. I also would like to read your books more. Once one begins to read them, one cannot stop, and the day seems to be too short.

There is such a power in your books, and one really can experience that you are fully present. Your books are nondifferent from you. One immediately becomes inspired to live according to your books and to speak about them. The empty heart becomes filled up with bliss, and the stone-and-steel frame melts away. Then life gets meaning. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing me to be in this movement, which is destined to spread all over the world, to every town and village. You once said, “It is not a religion: it is the greatest welfare movement on the planet.” You appeared in this world as the messenger from the spiritual world. Your mission is to make everyone happy in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your insignificant servant,  
Rohiṇī-suta dāsa

## Romapāda Swami
