*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*tad-vijñānārthaṁ sa gurum evābhigacchet*  
*samit-pāṇiḥ śrotriyaṁ brahma-niṣṭham*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful and heartfelt obeisances. I come before you as but one insignificant disciple amongst so many, who are so qualified to serve you and your mission. Profound gratitude and strong feelings of protection and spiritual upliftment are filling my heart as I come before you today to offer my respects.

**A New Beginning**

The first hundred years since your appearance in this world has given way to another chapter of time, the beginning of a new century of history, marked by your divine presence in this world. The Centennial celebration was replete with explosive exaltation proclaiming your gifts to the entire world and your unparalleled achievements in assisting the mission of Rūpa Gosvāmī to establish the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in this world. So much glorious and pure devotional inspiration your life has supplied to so many, reaching a crescendo in your Centennial celebration.

As time continues to relentlessly transform all things, our Centennial bells and whistles, along with many once-in-a-lifetime ornaments and special decorations, are carefully being put into safe storage. Some storage places are our memories of how you are to be appreciated and served, always. Another “storage place” is the continuation of commemorative events such as World Holy Name Day and World Enlightenment Day throughout ISKCON. Many other milestone achievements from your Centennial celebration will stay with us, too.

But a new chapter of time is turning, moving us all into a new century, like one huge thin page, turning of its own accord, in a massive book stored on the shelves of eternal time; we are all being relentlessly moved along into a new beginning, pondering deeply, “What will carry us further, toward the fulfillment of all the grand visions Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted to see manifested in this world?”

The awesomeness of the challenge is staggering. You set the stage for a spiritual revolution—a revolution in the impious lives of Kali-yuga’s victims. Your “recovery plan” was both simple and nothing short of a spiritual miracle—to bring traumatized Kali-yuga victims to the elevated level of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* that is entirely unmotivated, unmixed, and unchecked by anything.

**Back to Basics**

The seed of all answers and all requisite inspiration for fulfilling such momentous tasks, individually or institutionally, is found in your instructions.

When describing the birth ceremony of Mahārāja Parīkṣit, you reveal in wonderful instructions how to stay always in a position of pure devotion to Kṛṣṇa. “To follow the instructions of the Lord is to associate with the Lord. *The Lord is not a material object whose presence one has to feel for such association.*… It is quite possible to have His association simply by following His instructions.”

Your teachings, Śrīla Prabhupāda, equip us fully to face any challenge of life, especially the challenge of overcoming our own Kali-yuga-ized qualities and rising up to the standard of pure *bhakti*. Once again, you explained this so succinctly by enunciating the proper mindset of a disciple. In Mauritius, you taught us:

Then the next question is that “What is our duty?” Our duty is to feel obliged to God and try to serve Him. That is our duty. Just like a child, when he is unable to move or to work, the parents, the father, supplies everything. But when the child is grown up, if he thinks as a young man that “Father has given us so much service. Now we shall give service,” oh, that is perfection. That is wanted.

We want to serve you in that way also, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You have given us everything; now we want to serve you, effectively preserving and furthering your mission.

Everything is completely arranged by the Supreme Lord. *Tena tyaktena bhuñjīthā*. So we should be cognizant how we are benefited by the Supreme Personality of Godhead and what is our duty. Our duty is to serve God. That is our duty.…We are taking so much service.…Now, when we are grown up as human beings, then we should serve God.

We are now your grown-up disciples, in a grown-up ISKCON society, in a grown-up world. We must become your good sons and daughters, who show our love for you by acting with absolute respect and attentiveness to your instructions.

This is our mandate—at a minimum! *Abhigacchet,* as the leading verse of this offering to you indicates. It is imperative! We must be obedient, in full respect and with great feelings of love and duty to you.

You also explained this once in Bombay. “Obedience is the first law of discipline. If there is no obedience, there cannot be any discipline.” How can one be considered your *śiṣya,* or disciple, without obeying your instructions?

It is not possible.

Your mission, similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is resting on your instructions. We therefore dedicate ourselves, on this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, and in this new beginning of the second century of your appearance, to know and to obey and to establish your teachings.

**Back to the Basis**

I am convinced beyond any doubt, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you provided us with everything necessary to make ISKCON become that vehicle which would effect unprecedented worldwide spiritual rejuvenation in Kali-yuga. By systematically establishing the knowledge provided in your books—through distributing your books, by systematically educating and training ourselves and others in that same knowledge, and by implementing those instructions as a practical example for contemporary man—this is what we must painstakingly and tirelessly do (as you showed us by your own divine example!), to make the second century of your appearance a period wherein your vision for mankind becomes even more complete.

Our combined efforts have produced results that leave so much room for improvement! And the modern world is increasing in its turbulence daily. The task is not an easy one. But your instructions are perfect. And you are looking to us to make them properly manifest.

To this end, with all the strength and devotion that I can muster, I dedicate myself on this day to you. Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!!

Your most insignificant servant,  
Romapāda Swami

## Śeṣa dāsa Adhikārī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

I stand now at the crossroads of my life in your service. The youth that embraced me when I joined you has begun to loosen its grip, but I remain enlivened with the strength of body and mind to work vigorously in your service. Looking back on the path of your service, I see only your mercy. Looking forward on the path, I beg for your continued mercy.

Youth is a wonderful time in any man’s life. As stated by a former President of the United States, “Fortunately for us and our world, youth is not easily discouraged. Youth with its clear vista and boundless faith and optimism is uninhibited by the thousands of considerations that always bedevil man in his progress. The hopes of the world rest on the flexibility, vigor, capacity for new thought, and the fresh outlook of the young.” Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for you gave us our youth.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you showed us the clear vista of the Absolute Truth. If not for your appearance, how would we have known of the real purpose of life, or the path to achieve that goal? You harnessed our boundless faith by lovingly and scientifically teaching us to serve Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Substance at last! Hope replaced despair.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your service was and is full of the kind of unparalleled adventure young men dream of. Traveling all over the world, fearlessly distributing books and preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness to all manner of men. How wonderfully challenging and fulfilling was such a life! Whether we met with success or failure in our endeavors, you were there to encourage us. You said, “This is our mission. How many things we have to do for preaching. Live hundreds of years!” The esprit de corps generated by these and countless other words of encouragement made deep, inspirational impressions in the flexible mind of our youth.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your every lecture tested our capacity for new thought. “*Sādhu* means to cut,” you told us. Your words cut away the false shackles of material designation and replaced them with an understanding of our actual identity. By your mercy, our youthful minds somehow withstood the assaults on our false ego. We did not become discouraged; rather, we actually became better persons.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the fresh outlook on life—the idealism—you taught and lived, and that you taught us to live, is the real hope of this world. You convinced us that Kṛṣṇa consciousness holds the key to all the world’s problems and gave us the confidence to use all the youthful energy we possessed to change the world.

Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for allowing us to pass our youth in such a special way. Actually, you have given us a lifetime’s work. Please bless us that each successive stage of our lives may similarly be spent in your service.

Your servant,  
Śeṣa dāsa Adhikārī

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet, over and over again, on the auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration.

It is 1:30 a.m. I sit by candlelight in New Vraja Dhāma, poring over a computer. No contradiction in your world of *yukta-vairāgya*. Aside from the wind outside, there is no sound. Beside me are a few books. Like a soul in transmigration, your glorification takes different forms every year, yet always remains the same. In the silence I gradually find that place where I am with you, once again. In the continuous effort to try to know you better that is known as my life, what has been in the forefront of my meditation I record below. As Jagadānanda Paṇḍita says, “I do not know what is good for others. I write what inspires me the most.”

1\) Six miles north of Jagannātha Purī is the river known as the Bhārgīnadī. Since it is the place where Lord Nityānanda broke Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *daṇḍa,* it is also known as the Daṇḍa-bhāṅgā-nadī. It is a very solitary and peaceful place. The river divides around an island decorated with groves and gardens. On one side the water is said to be that of the Gaṅgā, on the other the Yamunā. When Lord Nityānanda threw the *daṇḍa* into the water, in the presence of bystanders it floated upstream to the far end of the island, then down the other side to sink mysteriously into the water. Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja writes,

*daṇḍa-bhaṅga-līlā ei—parama gambhīra*  
*sei bujhe, duṅhāra pade yāṅra bhakti dhīra*

**“The pastime of the breaking of the staff is very deep. Only one whose devotion is fixed upon the lotus feet of the two Lords can understand it” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **5.158).**

In the purport to the verse you give the explanation of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Lord Nityānanda did not want to see Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the supremely transcendental autocrat, identified with the status of a *varṇāśrama sannyāsī*. Therefore he broke the *daṇḍa,* the representation of regulative ideals. Elsewhere he describes that the *daṇḍa* is the condensed form of Lord Caitanya’s mercy on the fallen *jīvas*. Lord Nityānanda wanted to establish this *tīrtha* as a place where all could receive Gaurāṅga’s grace. In either case, this *līlā* is very mysterious. Mysterious like you. Yes, this reminds me so much of you, my supremely mysterious spiritual master.

Why do I say that you are mysterious? A mysterious person is unfathomable and inexplicable. Like a deep ocean, ever deeper, always expanding. In the same way, just when I think I have understood your instructions and pastimes, I realize once again that they are beyond my grasp, like Kṛṣṇa being chased by Kālayavana, who was never able to catch Him. When I think I understand how great you are, some event transpires wherein you dwarf my past conception, leaving me in awe. Your mercy, kindness, and compassion for saving conditioned souls seem to have no limits. Therefore I have said that you are mysterious.

In your achievements in serving Caitanya Mahāprabhu and distributing His message world-wide, without argument you are unparalleled. No *ācārya* or incarnation of Godhead has, to my knowledge, exhibited such prowess. Who are you to achieve such transcendental feats? By the law of logic known as similarity, from a study of the greatness you manifest in preaching, a hint at your greatness as a servant of the Lord is implied. But when asked to disclose your identity, you replied, “If I told, you would faint.” Very mysterious, that statement. I cannot speculate further, had I even the desire. Perhaps someday you will make me faint. For now it is a mystery.

2\) In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (10.7), Lord Kṛṣṇa encourages Arjuna to understand his prowess and personal potencies. By knowledge of His attributes, one’s attention becomes focused on the Lord, and through such meditation one becomes attracted and then devoted.

*etāṁ vibhūtiṁ yogaṁ ca mama yo vetti tattvataḥ*  
*so ’vikalpena yogena yujyate nātra saṁśayaḥ*

In giving a definition of Bhagavān, Jīva Gosvāmī says, *bhajanīya guṇa-viśiṣṭa*. This means that Kṛṣṇa’s nature is such that whoever comes in contact with Him cannot resist serving Him. No one can resist feeling moved to worship Kṛṣṇa, adore his charming personality, and be drawn into the vortex of His loving dealings.

Is it not the same with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? You are great beyond measure. Your preaching is great, your tolerance of your followers’ immaturity is great, your ability to attract and engage conditioned souls is great. There is no end to the great list of how you are great. And if I continue to meditate on your greatness, will I not develop true attachment for your lotus feet, which is the greatest commodity and the only means to love of Kṛṣṇa?

An example of your greatness is your arrangement for facilitating your followers’ liberation. You quote Bhaktisiddhānta Ṭhākura as saying that those who do not chant sixty-four rounds are “fallen.” I have heard Lord Caitanya said, “I will not accept the offerings of one who does not chant one lakh of *hari-nāma* a day.” And yet you made an adjustment according to our lack of ability and instructed us to chant “a minimum of sixteen rounds.” To do such *japa* in tandem with following “the regulations” would ensure a practitioner’s going back to Godhead “in this lifetime.” Isn’t that wonderful?

You write that according to the arrangement made by the spiritual master in consideration of time and circumstance, Kṛṣṇa will accept the service of a devotee. Śrīla Prabhupāda, what was the content of that discussion you had with Kṛṣṇa? That discussion wherein this arrangement you incessantly repeat to us was made? While some *sādhakas* ponder the validity of your claim, for your followers it is a declaration of your greatness. You made the adjustment and have tendered a guarantee. When questioned about such an extraordinary result of such a seemingly minimal effort, you replied, “Do you think I would lie to you?” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are truly extraordinary.

You are so great that you personally intercede with Kṛṣṇa on our behalf. Then by your humble attitude you entice your followers to continue to serve you, for as you wrote to Upendra, “Your strong desire to serve me is very beautiful; your serving me means serving Krishna.”

3\) When Lord Caitanya met Rūpa and Sanātana at Rāmakeli, the two brothers took bunches of straw between their teeth and, binding a cloth around their necks, fell like rods before the Lord. Although very influential and aristocratic, they abandoned all material considerations and prayed to Lord Caitanya with straw between their teeth. Their attitude was so submissive that Lord Caitanya could not tolerate their waves of humility. He said “Please abandon your humility, for my heart is breaking to see you so humble.” In their prayers they quoted Śrī Yāmunācārya as follows:

*bhavantam evānucaran nirantaraḥ praśānta-niḥśeṣa-manorathāntaraḥ*  
*kadāham aikāntika-nitya-kiṅkaraḥ praharṣayiṣyāmi sanātha-jīvitam*

**“By serving You constantly, one is freed from all material desires and is completely pacified. When shall I engage as Your permanent eternal servant and always feel joyful to have such a fitting master?” (*****Stotra-ratna*** **43).**

What a uniquely exclusive attitude—to “always feel joyful to have such a fitting master”! A fitting master, an extraordinarily wonderful master, you certainly are, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And I am so fortunate to call you mine. “Fitting” means correspondence. For an extraordinarily fallen *jīva* like me, an extraordinarily qualified *guru* is required. And you are so qualified and I am so happy that by some uncharacteristically good fortune I have had the opportunity to receive your grace.

Chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, reading your books, preaching, rendering service to ISKCON constitute your service. The perfection unattainable by Brahmā and Nārada is achieved by serving an eternal associate of the Lord like you. As you deliver the whole world by engaging them in your service, which is Kṛṣṇa’s service, the mist of misconception evaporates and my meditation becomes fixed on your lotus feet. Thus I want to always be your *nitya-kiṅkara,* for that is the place where one is free of desire, completely pacified, and assured of all success.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me unswerving determination to continue serving you in all ways. Determination, of which you are the ultimate example. How you are so mysterious, how you are so great, how very fortunate I am, I will only understand by your mercy. You must reveal these things to me. Like a *cātaka* bird that drinks water only from clouds, I can depend only on your grace. There is no other way forward. If you do not bestow such mercy—mercy by which I become attached to you, mercy which fixes me in your service in all ways, in full knowledge and attachment—of what use are my prayers, my *japa,* my studies, my meditations? If you do not hear my prayers, then who will?

It is almost time for *maṅgala ārati*. I should go see Rādhā-Śyāmasundara. O great, mysterious, and compassionate Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that this meager offering, which with much weeping during this year to attain your loving service and a heart burning with the pain of separation I take from my chest and place at your lotus feet, may give you a little pleasure.

Your eternal servant,  
Śivarāma Swami

## Śrīdhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
On your glorious appearance,  
Please accept these few words  
Of an insignificant incoherent.

At age sixty-five,  
Both settled and secure,  
A scholar cum laude  
In Vṛndāvan you were.

No need to leave,  
But pushed from within,  
Your master’s calling  
To distribute *kṛṣṇa-prem.*

Rādhā-Dāmodar, Rūpa and Jiva—  
They all guided you  
To travel abroad to Mleccha-deśa,  
Bhaktisiddhānta’s fervent request.

You established your mission,  
So noble and pure,  
A desire to save us  
From Māyā’s hellish allure.

Hundreds of temples,  
You took such great pain,  
Now we, your poor students,  
Must struggle to maintain.

Millions of books  
You pushed to distribute.  
How can we please you?  
We *all* must contribute.

Thousands of *bhaktas,*  
You inspired each one  
To give up their ignorance  
For *harināma-saṅkīrtana.*

You accomplished your goals,  
But left some for us  
To prove our surrender.  
We meet and discuss.

You are a preacher, a teacher  
Of the highest knowledge.  
We know that you wanted  
To make Varṇāśram College.

To know myself better  
I must study you.  
O Kṛṣṇa’s pure servant,  
Please make me anew!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Your message is clear,  
But what have I learned?  
Can I live without fear?

In twenty-eight years of study,  
What do I know?  
Who am I, who are you?  
And where do I go?

Individual I am,  
Not homogeneous impersonal,  
Materially not equal but  
Qualitatively one is your version.

Kṛṣṇa dās I am,  
Life after life.  
Kṛṣṇa-bhakta you are,  
Eternally liberated.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
It is my humble conclusion  
The best thing you gave me:  
Sublime *bhakti* contribution.

How to live in this world  
While remembering Kṛṣṇa’s feet?  
All suffering can’t touch me,  
From your shelter no retreat.

Reactions I must suffer  
From all my past sin,  
But you have given the courage:  
Tolerate, chant, and win.

To all ninety-nine,  
Your divine mercy you gave,  
But I am the one,  
Please make me your slave.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am learning day by day how sinful and fallen I am.

You spoke to the world, and you are saving millions from a hellish life, but right now, in my suffering condition, devoid of any Kṛṣṇa consciousness, after years of feeble attempts, I am once again shamelessly (albeit sincerely) begging you: Please save me! Please help me to know you.

Your servant,  
Śrīdhara Swami

## Suhotra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, the rarest of rare pure devotees, who astonish this modern world full of proud, sinful demons by revealing the full potency of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission of renunciation, knowledge, and pure devotion to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madana-mohana, Govinda, and Gopīnātha.

The gravity of your presence silences the gibbering, gesticulating madness of material attachment. The radiance of your wisdom penetrates and dispels the most soul-darkening ignorance. The sweetness of your love for Kṛṣṇa makes plain the bitterness of lusty affairs and awakens in our hearts the taste for devotional service. Your oceanlike compassion is so broad and deep that it accommodates the entire earth and all its living entities. You are the only hope for the most fallen, and your plan to steal the world away from Kali’s grip cannot be thwarted by the envious.

It is said that whatever goes into the holy river Gaṅgā is considered as pure as the Gaṅgā, at least as long as it remains in the Gaṅgā. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, my prayer on this auspicious day of your appearance is that the consciousness of all conditioned souls may plunge and swim forever within the holy waves of transcendental sound vibration that Your Divine Grace conducts throughout the world, O Bhagīratha of preachers! I pray that not one rascal, even the most hurtful and impious, remain immune to the current of that almighty flood of nectar in the form of *paramparā kṛṣṇa-kīrtana*, *kṛṣṇa-tattva,* and *kṛṣṇa-kathā*. For if you grant this prayer of mine, then surely I too, at last, shall be carried to the ocean of pure devotional service.

Remaining eternally indebted to you, with no chance of paying you back, though I hope against hope for the strength to try,  
Your insignificant servant,  
Suhotra Swami

## Tamāl Krishna Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Vapu Seva**

“There are two ways of association—by *vāṇī* and by *vapu*. *Vāṇī* means words, and *vapu* means physical presence. Physical presence is sometimes appreciable and sometimes not, but *vāṇī* continues to exist eternally. Therefore we must take advantage of the *vāṇī,* not the physical presence” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* Concluding words). Though we know this to be true, in Prabhupada’s presence disciples did not neglect his physical service on the plea of serving his *vāṇī*. What servant, for example, would have dreamed of allowing Prabhupāda to go hungry while he completed Prabhupāda’s order to chant sixteen rounds? The specialty of *vapu seva* is precisely its transience. Knowing this, in 1976 I proposed to Prabhupāda that I give up all my other responsibilities (which at the time were many) to become his secretary. My mentality was that I would have a whole lifetime to fulfill Prabhupāda’s instructions, but as long as he was present, if assisting him physically would make a single stroke of his pen easier, that would be worth more than whatever else I might do for him on my own account. He accepted my proposal.

Now that he has departed I have the balance of my life to serve his instructions, but I still find myself hankering to serve his physical presence. Truthfully, I cannot do well without it; nor, I suspect, can any of us. And Prabhupāda knew this despite words to the contrary. That is why, while present, he gave his association so liberally. More important still, it explains his well-known statement, “ISKCON is my body.” This affirmation qualifies the above concluding words of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* ensuring that his followers would not be deprived of his physical presence even after his departure.

“ISKCON is my body” is a difficult truth to evade, though many may try. I can hear the nay-sayers arguing that this was a time-bound declaration that was laid to rest along with Prabhupada’s body in his *samādhi*. It is no more, now, than an empty slogan and, like ISKCON, a body without a soul. Such an appraisal ignores the obvious: ISKCON shows far too many signs of life to be declared dead. I will concede that in some ways it suffers poor health, but nothing that cannot be remedied. If Prabhupada’s “body” is ill, let us nurse it back to good health, not bury it under a mountain of salt. Subtracting ISKCON from the formula of Kṛṣṇa consciousness means we must be able to survive without Prabhupada’s *vapu seva*. If any one of us is strong enough to do so, fine. But I am not. I am one of those overly intelligent disciples who he chastised in Calcutta for saying so often, “I know, I know,” whenever he tried to teach me anything. I can still hear his sarcastic reprimand, “Yes, you know everything.” That was 1970. By 1977 I should have learned at least something. But in Rishikesh, after a foolish mistake, he shook his head in simultaneous disbelief and resignation and, pointing to the area of my brain, said, “Nothing is inside. Nothing. You have learned zero.”

Unfortunately, after twenty more years, I am afraid it is now less than zero. Without doubt I need his *vapu seva* now more than ever before. I cannot accept that when I sealed the chamber of his *samādhi* the chance for serving his *vapu* was forever lost. I desperately cling to his words, “ISKCON is my body.” Whenever the BBT publishes a new book, I hear his early-morning bell and see him hand his secretary the night’s translation. In *padayātrā* I see him take his walk, and through *prasādam* distribution, his breakfast. He still meets with reporters, academics, and patrons but does it through his followers. The cooperative hands of his GBC offer him daily massage—their discernment of ISKCON’s future, the quick rubbing of his head; the worship of our Deities, the massage of his heart; serving the devotees, the cooling touch of his lotus feet. ISKCON’s unfaithful and its enemies are his urine and stool, faithful disciples the semen which rises upward, giving strength. The temples’ cleansing and decoration are his bath and silken robes; *daivi-varṇāśrama-dharma*, his well-conditioned form; *saṅkīrtana,* his voracious appetite; and the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, his sweet dreams at night.

When the entire planet is Kṛṣṇa conscious, ISKCON’s work is done. But that may take lifetimes. Only then will I be prepared to lay Prabhupada’s “ISKCON body” into its *samādhi*. Until that time comes, I will accompany him on walks, be present at his meetings, offer massage, help him to dress, serve him his meal, and fan him while he rests. This will be my happiness, this my liberation, that future time my *samādhi*.

Tamāl Krishna Goswami

## Trivikrama Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

1\. What was my good fortune?  
That I first met with you  
And you so kindly gave the seed  
That now grows in my heart.

2\. What was my good fortune?  
Although I was unclean,  
You picked me up from Māyā’s lap  
And gave my life a sheen.

3\. What was my good fortune?  
Although I am a wretch,  
I got to massage your body,  
The perfection of the touch.

4\. What was my good fortune?  
That I somehow agreed  
To follow your instruction,  
To chant and dance with glee.

5\. What was my good fortune?  
I couldn’t even dream  
How life could be so wonderful;  
By your mercy now it’s seen.

6\. What was my good fortune?  
That you have sent to me  
So many pure and simple souls  
To engage through eternity.

7\. What was my good fortune?  
I think it’s all too clear:  
You gave me that good fortune;  
This all the world can see.

8\. What was my good fortune?  
That, I can hardly say,  
I stumbled on to your lotus feet.  
How can I ever repay?

*Your lowly servant,*  
Trivikrama Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda:

Please accept my humble reverences at thy divine lotus feet and forgive my offenses.

For several days now I have been wishing to write something beautiful to thee on the glorious date of thine advent, but despite my efforts I could not find the right words.

As I chanted my *japa* beneath the sun I thought to myself, “How pretty is the sun, how it nourishes me…” But immediately that thought reminded that the true sun is the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Then, since I have always received the light of the *Bhāgavatam* from thee, there appeared in my mind thy paternal and loving image, which sparked in me a sudden mental eclipse that produced in me a reaction to write this offering of Vyāsa-pūjā, the words of which are practically dictated to me by the intense *śakti* of thy divine presence.

I write, as always, with the sentiment of gratefulness but with an emotional impact stronger than ever before. With every passing year I identify myself more strongly with thy work, and through it I discover the inimitable qualities of thy soul. By meditating on them, I find nourishment and am filled with energy to preach. Thy greatness fills me with trembling, thy mercy overwhelms me. In analyzing thy life, I comprehend the meaning of the word *perfection:* the highest degree of goodness, spiritual virtues, and excellence. Thy chivalry and perfect example in practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness continuously strengthen my faith in all that I do for Kṛṣṇa. By thy grace I comprehend that ISKCON is an expansive wave of Kṛṣṇa consciousness created by thee, which is ever-expanding in all directions as a result of the inconceivable power of thy spiritual purity.

For those who seek spiritual perfection, thou art the perfection sought. Thou hast shown us with thy example that perfection is achieved by faithfully following the instructions of the spiritual master. Thou hast practically demonstrated by thy life that the key to success in the spiritual development of consciousness consists of having complete faith in the words of the spiritual master. Personally, I become increasingly aware that the assiduous reading of thy books is the best way to strengthen and infinitely expand consciousness. All of thy followers must admit that thus far we have been saved from leading a life of error thanks to the help received through thy incomparable books.

In certain periods of my life, the reactionary voice of my mind has attempted to convince me that my relationship with thee is fictitious, that thou exist only in the imaginary remembrance projected by my unsatisfied mind. However, of late I find myself surprised, fascinated, and even astonished at having discovered that thou dwellest in me like my own thoughts, or like my way of being or feeling. Thy presence in me is particularly intense when I set aside useless concerns and remain alone with what I am, with that part of me that I have neither to pretend nor to promise, with the awareness of thee that I unknowingly carry tightly pressed in my soul, with that spirit of an indefatigable fighter that wishes to work only for thee, convinced that no treasure of this world is of any real worth. I know that I owe all to thee, that all of my achievements are the fruit of thy mercy. In my otherworldly solitude I am able to see thee as thou art, and I feel thy true devotional dimension, grandiose and sublime. I feel thee as the supreme reality of Kṛṣṇa that envelopes and sweetens all in the eternity of the moment. Because in thee are contained all of the Truths that thou preacheth, I understand the scriptures and all that I needed to understand about the Absolute Supreme Truth. Only by remembering thee while I perform my service am I able to detect my errors and my successes, because thou art not only the ideal example but also the incarnation of all of the ways and means of pleasing Kṛṣṇa. Thou art the perfect *ācārya* that teaches people like me who are devoid of understanding to perceive the true meaning of things.

I know that, by my being a part of thee, nothing shall vanquish me. Therefore, when problems do their best to make me capitulate, they no longer impress me. Material existence involves many inconveniences and anxieties. Its complex and unexpected adversities render it fearful. But he who by thy grace comprehends that he is a spiritual being and follows the path of devotional service recommended by thee will always be safe.

I think that the fact that thou art always by my side as friend and observer is more real than my attempt to convince myself of that fact. Recently, while delivering a lecture on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* I had the strong sensation that our placement in the transcendency has already been consummated but that, because we are somewhat asleep, we have not yet realized it. Influenced by those feelings, I explained to the devotees that our situation—now that we belong to thy movement—is a very privileged one, even though we may not yet be sufficiently aware of it. I compared our case to that of a group of sightless people transported from a dangerous and abominable place to one that is safe and marvelous. These people, even though they are in a paradise, may not be sufficiently aware of it, simply because they cannot, by themselves, appreciate it, but that does not change the fact that they are safe in a place most excellent. Similarly, we have already been transported to the spiritual world by being accepted into thy movement. Now we must become conscious of it and live eternally in peace by explaining our achievements to others.

In reality, one can live in the greatest of darkness even when in the presence of the sun, simply by closing one’s eyes. Similarly, we can remain submerged in ignorance if we close our ears to thy divine messages. It is for that reason that we must not be negligent about listening to and singing thy glories. We must meditate continuously on thee, on thy instructions, and on thy way of doing things. To meditate on the transcendental qualities of the spiritual master and to avoid seeing him as an ordinary person is as important as seeing Kṛṣṇa in the Deity. Not to do so is harmful to consciousness. As I perceive things, when emotional problems dominate us, the perceptual capacity of the super-senses becomes limited. Thus, it is advisable to take stock of oneself and change that which needs to be changed in order not to continue blindly along the path.

The spiritual knowledge that thou givest us is the light that illuminates our way, but if we fail to assimilate it, it is as though it did not exist. Spiritual knowledge implies all types of knowledge: knowledge of Kṛṣṇa, of ourselves, of the reasons for our conditioning, of how to change bad habits, etc. One must be alert in order to discover it. In reality, only by meditating on thy way of being and acting can we discover our imperfections and correct ourselves immediately.

We must pay considerable attention to the spirit that moved thee to establish the rules and principles that thou gavest us and to the sentiment that impelled thee to carry out thy mission, else we run the risk of becoming thy imitators instead of thy followers. Sometimes, to avoid deviating from thy instructions, we opt for the safety of formal faithfulness instead of daring to investigate the content of those instructions. But the spiritual life is creative. To free ourselves from the formal conditionings that transform us into imitators and thus to become thy true followers, we must penetrate the depths of thy teachings and learn to interpret them by taking into account both circumstances and context. The determining factor is the comprehension of thy teachings, not the accumulation of information or remembrances. To comprehend means to see things as they are at every instant.

The effectiveness of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness method depends on the level of spiritual understanding that one has, not on the artificial adaptation of that method. Times change, and our needs change with them. What in another time or another place was desirable may not be desirable now. Our dedication to thy mission of expanding Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world without limit will succeed to the extent that we are able to adapt thy teachings to the needs of the moment. If in preaching we do not take into account the people to whom we preach, we cannot expect good results. We must become experts at comprehending and correctly applying the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness for the good of the movement and for the good of humanity.

We have recently come to realize that the constant practice of devotional service and singing with purity the holy name of the Lord is not as easy as we had at first imagined. What is difficult is to surrender totally to Kṛṣṇa. We are seeing that each time we somewhat neglect our duties, Māyā captures us. We must not overestimate ourselves, but at the same time we must not let ourselves become discouraged by any difficulty. We must simply remember that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is based on austerity and genuine spiritual understanding and act accordingly. Spiritual knowledge is learned through observing and achieved through practice. We must metabolize what thou teachest us in order to have lives of our own and act on our own without the risk of error.

Perfect knowledge processed by an imperfect mind may be diminished, above all if one limits oneself to intellectualizing what one learns without actually practicing it. And even when we do things well, it may be that we will have accidents, such as those that we have been experiencing of late. Nevertheless, even though we have not yet mastered certain aspects of the philosophy or commit errors in our management of the Society, we must not feel frustrated. The structural dimensions of this movement are increasingly large and complex, and it is natural that our lack of experience will show through. But no matter what happens, we must not become confused. We must accept ISKCON just as it is, conscious that it still requires a long time to achieve the state of development that we all long for.

I think that the idea of conducting new experiments that deviate from thy guidelines must be abandoned and that, if we are patient, the quality of the movement will gradually improve and mature in terms of its excellence. We must remember that, despite its imperfections, this movement continues to function just as it was conceived by thee and by thy Guru Mahārāja, which is a reality that is unsurpassed since the time of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Never has there been a united and cohesive worldwide organization such as the one that exists today, with an unwavering devotion to the mass distribution of Vedic literature in all of the countries of the word and to faithfully complying with every precept and instruction of its founder-*ācārya*. All of this occurs by thy mercy. We are proud of thee. Please intercede with Kṛṣṇa for us so that He will give us sufficient intelligence to constantly renew our consciousness and not become bogged down and lapse into artificiality. For my part, I shall try to work as hard as I can this year to correct my impurities, expand and better organize the preaching, and better utilize energy in thy divine service, which is the only reason for my existence and the goal of my life.

Thy unconditional servant,  
Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

## Bhakti-bhṛṅga Govinda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

I would like to thank you for engaging me in devotional service and for kindly giving me your guidance and instructions. Without your instructions my life is truly useless. Each of your instructions is like a jewel that lights my heart and dissipates the darkness of Kali’s night.

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have shown us the essence of perfection in devotional service. That is to faithfully serve the lotus feet of the spiritual master. This is what you declared was the cause of all of your successes. Simply that you followed faithfully. You did not change the instructions or method of your Guru Mahārāja. You faithfully presented the message as you heard it. And the result is that your success and fame have been spread throughout the three worlds.

We have heard in these recent years of different formulas for success in devotional service. Often, due to our impatience, we jump to these formulas to try to find some shortcut back to Godhead. But when we look to you, O Śrīla Prabhupāda, we see only a pure and sincere soul who with implicit faith followed the order and will of his spiritual master. Despite all difficulties and trials. You followed. No shortcuts. His will became your will, and his glorious love for Kṛṣṇa became your glorious love as well. You simply followed.

Please bless me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I may follow you sincerely. Free me from pretension by allowing me to follow your example. Please let me preach the glories of the divine names of the Lord in all the corners of this earth. Please give me the power to chant the holy names, as you have done, without offense. Please let me rise above the selfish parameters of my normal consciousness and allow the deep compassion and love that you feel for all souls to awaken within my heart. Let me serve them all by giving them the nectar treasure house of the holy names.

You are the greatest Vaiṣṇava that this world has ever seen, not only in the past but at present, and most likely in the future. You have rendered the greatest service to your spiritual master and to the names of the Lord by spreading the holy names in every town and village of this world. Bless me that I may be a true follower of your footsteps. Let me rededicate my life to your service of benedicting this world with the holy names of the Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow me to always keep the association of pure Vaiṣṇavas and always bless me to serve them with humility, faith, and love.

But mostly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, allow me to faithfully serve your lotus feet throughout eternity.

I pray to receive your merciful glance and blessings.

Your eternal servant,  
Bhakti-bhṛṅga Govinda Swami

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to your all-auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day!

Sometimes it is hard rendering service to you. There are so many obstacles within this material world, not the least of which are our own mind and our own desires. The path sometimes seems winding and crooked. It is sometimes slippery, and filled with mud.

But if we stick at your lotus feet we can pass through all the trials and tribulations. Not through some ability of our own, but through the potency of your mercy. As Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī says:

*durgame pathi me ’ndhasya skhalat-pāda-gater muhuḥ*  
*sva-kṛpā-yaṣṭi-dānena santaḥ santv avalambanam*

**“My path is very difficult. I am blind, and my feet are slipping again and again. Therefore, may the saints help me by granting me the stick of their mercy as my support” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya*** **1.2).**

The more we stick at your lotus feet, the more you bring us through the challenges we face in our devotional service, and the more we appreciate how exalted you are. Because you are so dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, He is always ready to fulfill your desires, and anyone who has made your desires his desires will find that all assistance comes, by the Lord’s grace.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (2.6.34) Lord Brahmā says:

*na bhāratī me ’ṅga mṛṣopalakṣyate*  
*na vai kvacin me manaso mṛṣā gatiḥ*  
*na me hṛṣīkāṇi patanty asat-pathe*  
*yan me hṛdautkaṇṭhyavatā dhṛto hariḥ*

**“O Nārada, because I have caught hold of the lotus feet of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Hari, with great zeal, whatever I say has never proved to have been false. Nor is the progress of my mind ever deterred. Nor are my senses ever degraded by temporary attachment to matter.”**

In your purport to this verse you explain:

Śrī Govinda, who pleasingly accepts the loving transcendental service of His devotees, gives all protection to the words and actions of His devotees. The Lord declares in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.31), *kaunteya pratijānīhi:* “O son of Kuntī, please declare it.” The Lord asks Arjuna to declare, and why? Because sometimes the declaration of Govinda Himself may seem contradictory to mundane creatures, but the mundaner will never find any contradiction in the words of the Lord’s devotees. The devotees are especially protected by the Lord so that they may remain infallible. Therefore the process of devotional service always begins in the service of the devotee who appears in disciplic succession.

I certainly have no qualification, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but I am convinced that if I stick to the service of your lotus feet and carefully follow your instructions I will be eternally protected. By dint of your intimate connection with Lord Kṛṣṇa, I will also become intimately connected with Him and cross over this ocean of nescience.

Thank you very much for having given me this opportunity, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I pray I will have the strength to always take it wholeheartedly.

Your humble servant,  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your divine feet, the shelter for all!

**A Picture of You**

I would stare at you  
From a page in a book.  
In ’72  
I had my first look.

Then I didn’t know  
The value of that space.  
On paper was the smile  
Of that amazing face.

Your image made me curious.  
Your dignity and age  
Revealed the marks  
Of a very wise sage.

I studied it so long,  
All the fine detail:  
Lines and contours  
And skin not so pale.

I thought of Jesus,  
I thought of Gandhi,  
I even thought  
Of sitar master Ravi.

I questioned you  
As “real” or as a “cheat.”  
Wanting to believe,  
I hoped my doubt would  
Find defeat.

Then I read your words,  
Matched them to your face.  
Both seemed to meet,  
And things fell into place.

You were old,  
There was age in your speech.  
A young *śiṣya* said,  
“It’s an age I want to reach.”

I looked at the face—  
The face in the book.  
I looked at the youth  
With his eager look.

With utter confusion  
And mind in a spin,  
I probed, “You want death  
And to return here again?”

Calmly he explained to me  
The nature of Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Who is deathless, pure,  
And sent by God.

It is he who guides.  
From him we learn.  
It is his maturity  
For which we yearn.

With that I was reverent  
And more at ease.  
Through you I came to know  
It is Kṛṣṇa that we please.

I stare at you  
From a page in a book.  
Knowing that I’m caught.  
Impaled by your look.

*Your servant,*  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhakti Puruṣottama Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

Your glories are spreading everywhere. Throughout the course of history, no one has spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world as you have. What to speak of history, even in the *Purāṇas* we do not find any accounts of activities such as you have performed. You have spread the glories of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa all over the world, thus saving millions of conditioned living entities by giving them the holy name of the Lord. You have made Kṛṣṇa known to everyone, but not many people know about you.

To please Kṛṣṇa, it is our duty to make you famous all over the world. You are already famous for your great service, but still we have to make people know about your glories more and more. Day by day, as ISKCON grows, your glories will also grow constantly. You are already glorified—it is not that you need me to increase your glories But I need to spread your glories to make myself fortunate. By my spreading your glories, Lord Kṛṣṇa will be pleased. It is not so easy to please Śrī Kṛṣṇa. But when He sees that I am spreading your glories, He will be more pleased than if I glorify Him. Spreading the Lord’s glories is your business, and spreading your glories is the Lord’s business. It gives Him the most pleasure when He sees His devotee glorified. Thus I want to be in Lord’s group, helping Him spread your glories.

But how can I glorify you without your mercy? Please bless me so that I can spend my whole life spreading your glories all over the world and wherever I go after my death. I pray that Lord Kṛṣṇa empowers me to glorify you as much as you were able to glorify Him. My spiritual master becomes very pleased when he sees we are engaged in spreading your glories. I want to please him always by spreading your glories. By this I can please my *guru* and Kṛṣṇa both. Please bestow your causeless mercy upon me so that I can help my Guru Mahārāja complete all the uncompleted services that you and all the previous *ācāryas* desire.

Thank you so much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me shelter in your Society, the worldwide home known as ISKCON.

Your granddisciple and servant of your servant,  
Bhakti Puruṣottama Swami

## Bhaktividyā Pūrṇa Swami

**Praying to Be Yours**

You,  
the perfect,  
preach powerfully,  
spreading the glories of the supreme Lord,  
illuminating the path  
set by the previous *ācāryas*.

We,  
the fallen,  
run behind you,  
attempting your example,  
hoping to please,  
hoping to be of use.

They,  
the Lord and His associates,  
are waiting,  
waiting for our return,  
waiting for us to understand,  
relishing the thought of that moment of reunion,  
that moment of glory.

You,  
their perfect servant,  
understanding their cherished desire,  
wishing to ease their longing,  
have come to us,  
hoping for our victory.

We,  
the undeserving,  
not wishing to disappoint any longer,  
catch hold of your lotus feet,  
praying to be yours,  
praying to understand.

Bhaktividyā Pūrṇa Swami

## Bhakti-vighna-vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who are mercifully preaching the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in a world filled with impersonalism and voidism.

There are millions of living entities everywhere, and they are engaged in suffering and enjoying the results of their fruitive activities life after life. Out of many of these living entities, if one is actually fortunate, *bhāgyavān*, he comes in contact with a bona fide spiritual master by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. Following in the footsteps of Śrī Nārada Muni, the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is rendering service to humanity by giving everyone a chance to come in contact with Kṛṣṇa. If one is fortunate he becomes intimately related with this movement. Then by the grace of Kṛṣṇa his life becomes successful. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 19.151, purport\]

Prior to my contacting this movement, my life was dark and empty, but you so kindly brought me to the *saṅkīrtana* party and allowed me to drink the nectar I had been hankering for, life after life. Now, on this auspicious day, I pray to remain engaged in distributing your transcendental literatures and preaching the glories of the Lord. Through your own example you have taught us to tolerate the difficulties that come with old age and disease. It is twenty years since you finished your manifest pastimes, and the majority of your disciples are now confronted with these problems, which are inevitable for everyone who takes birth in this world. Our own impending old age and disease allow us to develop a deeper appreciation for the sacrifice you made in making our lives fortunate.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, one day I stood by the door of your room in Māyāpura as one of our life members from Calcutta introduced his family to you. He mentioned my name to you and you did not know who I was. This was hardly surprising, because you already had more than five thousand disciples and could hardly be expected to remember all their names. Many of your disciples are still actively preaching throughout the world with astounding success. You have confirmed the Chinese maxim that a famous teacher will produce the best students. Unfortunately, I am not a very good student, but I am thankful to be engaged in some service under the guidance of some of your senior men. Certainly you have given me an infinitely better life.

As Uddhava in his old age never slackened in his service to the Lord, as Haridāsa Ṭhākura, despite his failing health, never reduced his chanting, and as Raghunātha Dāsa Gosvāmī, despite his frailty, never gave up his *vairāgya,* in the same way you tolerated all difficulties to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement, even in your later years. May we be allowed to continue to follow in your footsteps.

Your unworthy servant,  
Bhakti-vighna-vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

## Bhakti-vikāśa Swami

**Prabhupāda Gems**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the ideal *guru* you were always giving knowledge to human society. Tirelessly writing and speaking, you gave all kinds of valuable instructions, phenomenal and numinous: economic, political, psychological, medical, scientific, and of course spiritual. All *siddhāntas,* all *tattvas,* everything is there complete for redirecting human society back to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. Apart from your formally written books, we also have books of your conversations, letters, and lectures. Plus, many devotees are writing their remembrances of you. All of this is like a huge mine of nectar, where any number of devotional scholars can go and happily extract valuable gems.

Personally, I take great pleasure in again and again reading your books and again and again speaking their message to others. I have also set myself at least one lifetime’s work of writing books based on your books, endeavoring to give guidance on particular topics in a systematic way. As I go on mining the nectar from your books, I marvel at your dedication, ever-correctness, cutting analysis, and “as it is” kick-in-the-face frankness.

By enlightening me with knowledge, you gave me a reason to live. Even now, you continue to save me by speaking to me from your books. Your words scythe my mind and keep my Kṛṣṇa consciousness alive. As I read on and on, my gratitude to you is ever increasing. Another marvel is your allowing me to take part in the great global mission of giving these gems to others. Like a greedy man, I go on extracting gems to add to my already voluminous collection. But I am not like a miser, for there is no pleasure in that. I am more like a research student eager to share his valuable discoveries with others. One way I assist in spreading your message is by writing books based on your directions and decorated with gems from your teachings.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books are complete and perfect, and are available to all. Nevertheless, you instructed your disciples, especially the *sannyāsīs,* to write. “My disciples will write purports to my books.” Undoubtedly, our writings must be based on yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda, or they will have no potency, meaning, or value. What else do we know, anyway?

At the very least, our writings will be for self-purification. And, if you so want to use your disciples, their books may also be valuable guides to human society.

As all I have is what you have given me, here are a few gems, offered respectfully back to you, to be shared with your ever-increasing family of appreciative devotees.

Kṛṣṇa also becomes very beautiful. He’s already beautiful. But when a devotee serves Him, a devotee comes to Him, He also becomes very beautiful. A devotee, when with his heart and soul, serves Kṛṣṇa in dressing Him, in feeding Him, in giving Him flower, He becomes smiling. And if you can get Kṛṣṇa once smiling upon you, your life is fulfilled. \[lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.25.12, Bombay, 12 November 1974\]

Modern civilization is centered around animal-killing. *Karmīs* are advertising that without eating meat, their vitamin value or vitality will be reduced; so to keep oneself fit to work hard, one must eat meat, and to digest meat, one must drink liquor, and to keep the balance of drinking wine and eating meat, one must have sufficient sexual intercourse to keep fit to work very hard like an ass. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.27.11, purport\]

There is no difficulty in coming in contact with the Supreme Lord (*ko ’ti-prayāsaḥ*). On the other hand, going to hell requires great endeavor. If one wants to go to hell by illicit sex, meat-eating, gambling, and intoxication, he must acquire so many things. For illicit sex he must arrange for money for brothels, for meat-eating he must arrange for many slaughterhouses, for gambling he must arrange for casinos and hotels, and for intoxication he must open many breweries. Clearly, therefore, if one wants to go to hell he must endeavor very much. But if he wants to return home, back to Godhead, there is no difficult endeavor. To go back to Godhead, one may live alone anywhere, in any condition, and simply sit down, meditate upon the Supersoul and chant and hear about the Lord. Thus there is no difficulty in approaching the Lord. *Adānta-gobhir viśatāṁ tamisram*. Because of inability to control the senses, one must go through great endeavor to go to hell, but if one is sensible he can very easily obtain the favor of the Supreme Personality of Godhead because the Lord is always with him. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.7.38, purport\]

“You are OK. I am OK.” Our proposal is not that. Our proposal is: “You are not OK. I am OK.” \[lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 6.1.9, Los Angeles, 22 June 1975\]

They will never surrender. This is the sign of demon. They will never surrender. They will be killed, they will go to hell, they will accept all the thing, but still, if you ask him that “You surrender to—” “No.’’ That is demonic. \[lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.8, New Vrindaban, 24 May 1969\]

So you have taken to the vow of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Try to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. And we have got little success. One politician in U.S.A., he has remarked that “This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is increasing like an epidemic.” He has said that. “And if we do not check it, one day it may take our government.” He has opined like that. So any intelligent man can know what is the ultimate result. Everyone… Because mass of people, if they take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, then government is yours. That is a fact. And if the Kṛṣṇa consciousness government is there, no meat-eating, no smoking, no illicit sex, so many no’s, the demons will die. \[lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.7.47, Vṛndāvana, 6 October 1976\]

Some rascals put forward the theory that an animal has no soul or is something like dead stone. In this way they rationalize that there is no sin in animal-killing. Actually animals are not dead stone, but the killers of animals are stonehearted. Consequently no reason or philosophy appeals to them. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.26.9, purport\]

**Guest:** I have noticed when everybody touches their head to the floor. And this I find so disturbing \[*indistinct*\]…another man and kiss their boots. I found that \[*indistinct*\]. And to tell you frankly, I find it quite shocking, too, that people place their head on the ground before another man. And you are a man like that. You are a man of knowledge, and you’re \[*indistinct*\]. But yet I can’t see of people their heads to the floor…

**Prabhupāda:** You have been asked to do that?

**Guest:** Nobody asked me, but I see everybody else does it.

**Prabhupāda:** Then why you are feeling so disturbed?

**Guest:** Because to me, people are people, and there’s… No man is a God, and it’s just appearing as if you were…

**Prabhupāda:** But as you are thinking that they should not, they are thinking that you should. \[*laughter*\] As you are thinking they should not do like that, they are thinking you must do that. Then what is it? Difference of opinion. Your vote is single and they are so many. So you have to follow the greatest majority vote. \[lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.10, Montreal, 14 July 1968\]

He \[Śrīla Prabhupāda Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja\] lives forever by his divine instructions and the follower lives with him. \[dedication to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept me as your servant forever.

Dāsābhāsa Bhakti-vikāśa Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Lately Kṛṣṇa has taken me on a guided tour of the heart. Specifically my own heart. Usually I don’t go there, because I mistakenly assume that I already am there. I think that I am in direct contact with my own heart. Then suddenly I catch a glimpse of what is really going on there. Seems to be a traffic jam. So many stalled desires for sense gratification waiting in the taxis of my mental plans. A few broken-down past attachments straddling two or three lanes, holding up everything. Up ahead there are roadblocks due to construction in the service that I have yet to perform adequately. Peddlers sell minor distractions to the slow-moving vehicles. Isn’t it true that nothing in the external world has the slightest power over us? No armies, no nations, no laws, no kings, no foes, no demigods, no *karma,* no lovers, no haters, no relatives, no dragons, no ghosts, no fears, no addictions. Squarely in the middle of my lane to progress lies only one restriction—my own improper desires. That’s it: the fault, the whole fault, and nothing but the fault. Of course these non–Kṛṣṇa-conscious desires are given shape, complication, and fury by the mind. Whenever I think of progress in devotional service, that is ultimately where I have to start. There is no position that Kṛṣṇa’s pure spiritual potency can’t elevate, and no position that willful ignorant material desire can’t degrade. That is the definition of marginal potency. So how to do it?

I am starting with the prayer to know clearly what my obstacles are. Which desires stand in the way. Then I am praying for the determination and strength to overcome these drawbacks. This sense of my own powerlessness, along with prayer to Kṛṣṇa, who is powerfulness personified, is the solution. To remember Kṛṣṇa at all times in requesting help to overcome the only real enemy. And I will cling fast to the process of devotional service. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me to understand it without speculation. This is the solution of the *brāhmaṇa* from Avantī. We must execute our prescribed service and always think of Kṛṣṇa, tolerating all the difficulties of our past sins. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, give me the strength of character to become the devotee you want me to be.

Your servant,  
Candraśekhara Swami

## Dānavīr dāsa Goswami

To His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious anniversary of your divine, effulgent appearance day in this dark world, we prostrate ourselves at your lotus feet. You have mercifully saved me from a hellish future and engaged me in the blissful service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Although useless, I dedicate myself as your instrument.

Your Divine Grace has been specifically empowered to translate the *Vedas* into the language of modern man. This requires, however, more than mere words.

It necessitates that the translator, like Śrī Vedavyāsa, possess a complete understanding of all branches of knowledge;

It necessitates that the translator, like Śrī Nārada Muni, realize the conclusion of that knowledge, namely that Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the only lovable object;

It necessitates that the translator, like Śukadeva Gosvāmī, transmit the subject in a poetically pleasing manner;

It necessitates that the translator, like Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, personally demonstrate the behavior of one surrendered to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa;

It necessitates that the translator, like flute-playing Śrī Muralīdhara, captivate the minds of the busy;

It necessitates that the translator, like Śrī Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, print and distribute spiritual literature around the clock;

It necessitates that the translator, like Śrī Rāmānujācārya, break the teeth of the stubborn Māyāvādī philosophers;

It necessitates that the translator, like Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu, accept the heavy role of *guru* to lift the fallen souls out of the ocean of distress;

It necessitates that the translator, like Śrī Matsya Avatāra, organize a loving, self-governing Vaiṣṇava society that stays afloat amid stormy weather;

It necessitates that the translator, like Śrī Paraśurāma, fight with the demons who wish to destroy the devotees’ faith in Kṛṣṇa;

It necessitates that the translator, like Lord Brahmā, spawn qualified offspring to perpetuate the sacred science of devotion in future generations;

It necessitates that the translator, like Śrī Pṛthu Mahārāja, employ *daivī-varṇāśrama* to elevate society to the transcendental platform;

And it necessitates that the translator, like Śrī Bhīṣmadeva, impart final lessons before displaying his glorious departure from the battlefield.

For a translator to be endowed with all of the above attributes is totally inconceivable, yet you, O most humble Guru Mahārāja, have indeed received such an endowment. Utilizing these extraordinary qualities, you have accomplished the impossible, dearest Gurudeva, but you take no credit: “It is all the mercy of my spiritual master,” you say.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have marvelously done your part; now the responsibility to maintain and expand your movement, Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, rests upon us. We, as your students, should become masters and teach. We, as your well-wishers, should follow your footprints and assist. We, as leaders, should hear from the *brāhmaṇa*-Vaiṣṇavas how to protect and then fearlessly execute the task. *Vaiśyas* should produce food grains, *śūdras* should labor without false pride, women should show chastity, and children should be respectful. Everyone should chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, worship the Deity, and honor *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. In this way, we shall glorify the most glorious personality, our founder-*ācārya*, His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your insignificant servant,  
Dānavīr dāsa Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

At your lotus feet I pathetically submit this plea, in glorification of your boundless grace, transcendental kindness, and *acintya* mercy:

1\. How many births of devotional service are too great a price for achieving the association of servitors who ceaselessly engage body, intelligence, and words in your mission?

2\. O Vaiṣṇavas, with desperate earnestness I seek the boon of exclusive shelter at Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus feet. Have mercy and help me attain that most cherished abode. If I am truly humble, surely there will be no impediment.

3\. Please forgive all that I have spoken, done, heard, and thought. May the remembrance of Śrīla Prabhupāda be ever mine.

4\. O eternal father, without shame I yearn to clasp your feet and beg: Grant me your unconditional service. Except for you, no one can slay the sorrow of my wretched births in material existence.

5\. I offer these prayers to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who are nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa’s desires. Just glance upon me, and I promise to render all necessary service for fulfilling your plans.

6\. Astonished, I vibrate the *mahā-mantra,* gratefully received from your fragrant mouth. Your ineffectual servitor hereby declares his eternal subservience to the holy name:

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

Devāmṛta Swami

## Dhanurdhara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

You have many wonderful qualities, but most important you have the heart of a Vaiṣṇava. Your soft heart and compassionate nature endear you to Kṛṣṇa. He sits in your heart. Allow me to glorify your heart by describing you unparalleled capacity to bestow mercy.

Among the many good traits that a man may possess, the quality of mercy is special. Even in the personality of Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is all-attractive, the quality of mercy has a unique charm.

When Śukadeva Gosvāmī left home immediately after his birth, his father, Śrīla Vyāsadeva, lamented. To attract his son back, he began to describe the qualities of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa by quoting the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. After four verses were recited, Śukadeva Gosvāmī eagerly returned. He had heard about Lord Kṛṣṇa’s extraordinary mercy:

“Alas, how shall I take shelter of one more merciful than Him who granted the position of mother to a she-demon \[Pūtanā\] although she was unfaithful and she prepared deadly poison to be sucked from her breast?” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.2.23)

Even amongst the various manifestations of Godhead, those who are most merciful are distinguished. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is thus known as “the most munificent *avatāra*,” even when compared to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Himself, because He has the special quality of *audārya,* or unprecedented mercy. Therefore the Chand Kazi got *prema-bhakti* when he was chastised by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, but only liberation when he was killed by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa (as Kaṁsa, the Chand Kazi’s previous manifestation).

I would like to make a case, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you are also especially merciful, even when compared with other great souls.

If one receives something he is entitled to, or deserves, it is not mercy but a just reward. Mercy must be more than one has earned. Thus one’s capacity to bestow mercy can be judged by the difference between the value of what one is giving and the price one demands for it. For example, the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is deemed most munificent because He distributed the most valuable thing (love of Godhead) and demanded the least for it (He gave it freely).

Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave the highest thing, love of Godhead, to the least qualified. Born in the West and immersed in sense gratification, we had little to pay for what you were giving. It is your mercy only that we became Vaiṣṇavas.

Recently your exceptional munificence has come even more to light, as your followers begin to leave the world. In case after case, you have made extraordinary arrangements for them to remember Kṛṣṇa at the time of death. So consistently have the conditions of the last moments of your followers been uncommonly auspicious—most of the time obviously beyond their personal endeavors in spiritual life—that a phenomenon has been established concerning the extraordinary flow of mercy to those who have pleased you by service.

Since I have lived in Vṛndāvana, where several of your followers have come to leave their bodies, I have several times personally witnessed this phenomenon. I was at Mother Gaurī’s side when she passed from this world. I remember how her Āyurvedic doctor came and felt her pulse and enthusiastically declared, “Disease is finished!”, and in the next breath he contradicted himself by telling her, “You have an hour left to live.” The doctor was a Vaiṣṇava. By declaring “Disease is finished” he was referring to *avidyā,* or ignorance. He was right. She had completely given up attachment for the body and died exactly an hour later, chanting the holy name in full consciousness.

Buddhimanta pleased you by helping to inaugurate the distribution of your big books. I saw how you brought him back to Vṛndāvana at the end of his life and made all arrangements for him to advance. I wasn’t there, but I heard that just at the moment of death Aindra Prabhu, unaware of what had just transpired, appeared spontaneously in the room with an ecstatic *kīrtana* party.

There were many other recipients of your mercy. In each case the history of his or her death reveals how merciful you are to anyone who has rendered you sincere service. I remember the glorious passing away of Nakaḍi Prabhu from Canada, a sincere and pure-hearted disciple, and have heard of the incredible last moments of Nāmānanda Prabhu, one of the most successful book distributors in history. I hope most readers of this offering have also heard his story.

Most of us also know the story of Indradyumna Mahārāja’s disciple Vraja-līlā. There was also the glorious passing away of Mother Arcā-vigraha. The list goes on, too long for a single Vyāsa-pūjā offering.

A testimony to your mercy is how you have remembered your personal servant, Upendra Prabhu, who passed away this year. You had sent him to Australia practically alone to pioneer Kṛṣṇa consciousness there.

I don’t know the details, but I understand he had very much left Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You never, however, forgot his service. At the very end of his life you not only suddenly brought him back to your service, but to the place where he captured your mercy, Australia, and then you gave him your full mercy by surrounding him at the moment of death with loving devotees and *kīrtana*. How fortunate it is to have even once served you purely and have gained your favorable glance!

I’ve seen that your mercy even extends to the past generations of your devotees who have performed sincere service. There are many such stories. A Godsister who has served you sincerely for her whole devotional life recently told me of the death of her father. She described how her father was kind to her. Once on her request he folded his hands and prayed to Kṛṣṇa to find her a good husband to protect her. He repeated word for word the entreaty of his daughter. “My dear Mr. Kṛṣṇa, please find a nice Kṛṣṇa-conscious husband to take care of my daughter.”

Although her father died in a comatose condition, she was able to be with him in his last days. Your *kīrtana* tapes were constantly playing, and Rādhā-kuṇḍa water was being given to him regularly. When the doctors were not looking she even wrote the *mahā-mantra* on his body with a marking pen. Occasionally she would press her *govardhana-śilā* to his forehead. Her father died on Govardhana-pūjā, and his funeral was on your disappearance day. You so much appreciate service to your devotees.

If I were arguing in a court of law about your unprecedented capacity to bestow mercy, I would rest my case with the following stories.

**I Was Touched by the Hand of God**

Hayagrīva Prabhu, a former college English professor, was one of the first devotees to join your movement at 26 Second Avenue. He edited your books and you were pleased with his service, but Hayagrīva never seemed to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness with the fervor needed to uproot one’s bad habits. Many of them unfortunately remained.

Satyarāja Prabhu always appreciated the writings of Hayagrīva Prabhu, especially his poems written in the early *Back to Godheads*. Over the years he had developed a friendship with Hayagrīva and went to visit him in New Vrindaban when Hayagrīva was dying of cancer. By the time Satyarāja visited him he was in a very painful condition. Due to cancer his spine had become almost like eggshells. Any movement was extremely painful.

Hayagrīva happily acknowledged Satyarāja’s presence. To view his guest he slowly turned his head upward and to his right. With tears in his eyes, Hayagrīva spoke slowly, with a voice filled with appreciation for his great fortune: “A seventy-year old aristocratic Bengali gentleman arrives in New York with a mission to print books in English, and who is the first person he meets? An English professor.” Overwhelmed with the realization that he had been the object of inconceivable mercy, Hayagrīva paused, and then, with a sense of drama and also with sincere conviction and deep emotion, expressed his heart: *“I was touched by the hand of God!”*

**Go Back to Prabhupāda, Sudāmā**

In the early days Sudāmā Prabhu was Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servant. Śrīla Prabhupāda was satisfied with his service and showed him great affection. He heard Śrīla Prabhupāda say that if anyone would go to Japan to preach the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, then he (Śrīla Prabhupāda) would personally take that person back to Godhead. He eagerly volunteered for the mission. Kṛṣṇa consciousness was started in Japan, but like many devotees Sudāmā drifted back into sinful life and remained far from a Kṛṣṇa conscious life for many years. When he found out that he had AIDS and would soon die, he told one Godbrother, “Kṛṣṇa has sent the wake-up call!” He soon moved to the Hare Kṛṣṇa community in Los Angeles. I later heard the details of his death through his nurse.

She described that Sudāmā’s last days were spent in an apartment in the New Dvārakā community. At the last moments of his life his bed was surrounded by many well-wishing devotees enthusiastically chanting the holy name. He was covered with the last two weeks’ worth of garlands from Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa. His mother, a simple black lady who had known and liked the devotees since her son had joined in the late sixties, was holding his hand.
