The strong and encouraging voice of his mother, heavily accented with the typical drawl of a working-class black lady but deep in religious conviction and laden with affection for her son, entered deep within his heart. He heard, *“Go back to Prabhupāda, Sudāmā! Go back to Prabhupāda, Sudāmā!”* and left his body.

\* \* \*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I am still here and have service at your lotus feet despite my mistakes and offenses is itself a testimony to your mercy. Everything is your mercy. You are an ocean of mercy.

Your servant,  
Dhanurdhara Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

As I presumably become purified by this process of *bhakti-yoga,* which you have so magnanimously brought into my sordid life, I come face to face with the “naked forms” of my ambition for *lābha, pūjā,* and *pratiṣṭhā,* insidiously cloaked beneath the veil of holy “service.”

While engaged in the activity of distributing your message to many ill-fated souls on *saṅkīrtana,* this *dharma-dhvajī* seizes the opportunity to shamefully exploit their innocence. I pretend to be “preaching,” but you know my actual intent. You know that if I had but a spark of true Vaiṣṇava spirit, Lord Caitanya would direct countless candidates to relish *sādhu-saṅga* in the form of your Bhaktivedanta purports and Kṛṣṇa consciousness Society. Prabhupāda, the field is so ripe!

I constantly catch my mind engaging in petty games of book numbers, and even worse, in worrying about financial matters (which for a *sannyāsī* is not only illusion but downright sinful) while the conditioned souls are contemplating more serious issues, like suicide, being unaware of life’s purpose. In fact, I am much more concerned with being heralded as a wandering mendicant than I am with the actual task at hand. And I am such a fool that when others fall for the ploy, I also believe them! (*Grāmya-vyavahāre paṇḍita, tāi satya māni*.) I take advantage of this sweet exchange between the Lord and His seekers to procure a small vestige of Kṛṣṇa conscious nectar. My only hope is that you overlook my inner motives (just as Lord Kṛṣṇa did with Pūtanā Rākṣasī) and somehow appreciate my external efforts to spread your mission. In this way, you may become inclined to bestow your grace upon me in the shape of your intimate association, by which I may imbibe even a particle of your genuine compassion. Oh, what joy to be just a humble peon on your behalf, reflecting your same mood of sympathy for the *jīvas’* plight, without desiring anything in return except more service. This is my aspiration.

*bhavantam evānucaran nirantaraḥ*  
*praśānta-niḥśeṣa-manorathāntaraḥ*  
*kadāham aikāntika-nitya-kiṅkaraḥ*  
*praharṣayiṣyāmi sanātha-jīvitam*

**“By serving You constantly, one is freed from all material desires and is completely pacified. When shall I engage as Your permanent servant and always feel joyful to have such a fitting master?” (Madhya 1.206)**

I can vaguely remember glimpses of this consciousness, especially as an unassuming *bhakta*. I pray that you benedict me with the faith, purity, enthusiasm, and compassion to be a bona fide broker for your storehouse of mercy. The world is so much in need of it right now.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your shallow servant,  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept this humble offering of gratitude on this most auspicious anniversary of your divine appearance in this world.

**You Showed Us How to Live**

Some days when I serve you,  
Though nothing is as sweet,  
My strength and courage fail,  
Due to troubles that I meet.

But in those times of trial,  
I think of how you faced  
All hardships that befell you,  
And doubts become erased.

This body, for example,  
Ails more as years take hold,  
And I’m prone to lamentation,  
For I’m quickly getting old.

I recall, though, how you fought  
For Kṛṣṇa every day.  
Despite your aging body,  
You boldly led the way,

Determined and undaunted,  
Fixed in timeless truth,  
Relentlessly proceeding,  
Not looking back at youth.

Steeped in blissful service  
To Kṛṣṇa and *guru,*  
The pangs of the mere body  
Were of no concern to you.

As I fly around the world,  
Jet-lagged, cramped, and sore,  
Self-pity takes the place  
Of serving Kṛṣṇa more.

Then by His mercy,  
I think of you somehow:  
Had you not braved these flights,  
Who knows where I’d be now?

I remember how you’d land,  
Never breaking stride,  
Your smile never hinting  
Of the rigor of your ride.

Then going to your quarters,  
Your body clock ignored,  
How quickly you’d dive in  
To service to the Lord!

You didn’t want to manage.  
Your writing was the need.  
But we were so inept,  
You had to take the lead.

Though we’d often let you down,  
You always showed compassion  
And urged us on through failure,  
In transcendental fashion.

While patience and compassion  
Are foreign words to me,  
Your manner helps me think  
Of how I ought to be.

Preaching is the essence.  
That’s true, but it’s a fight  
To give the truth to others  
From morning until night.

Absorbed in easy living  
Is how I like to be.  
This constant stream of giving  
Becomes too much for me,

Until I think of you,  
Who without reservation  
Preached all day and night  
The highest revelation.

Your lectures, books, and darshans,  
Your letters and your classes,  
An endless flow of mercy  
You showered on the masses.

The misery of death  
Is one that’s yet to come.  
I feared it all my life,  
And still I fear it some.

But when death is upon me,  
I hope to follow through  
By thinking of your courage  
As Kṛṣṇa reclaimed you.

You stayed right in the battle,  
All suffering transcended,  
Steeped in serving Kṛṣṇa  
Until your *līlā* ended.

If I can keep you with me,  
I know I’ll have the power  
To also think of Him  
In that decisive hour.

I thank you from my heart.  
But words will not suffice.  
I have to do as you did.  
That’s how I’ll pay the price.

If I give all to Kṛṣṇa,  
If I achieve life’s goal,  
You’ll take me back to Godhead,  
The treasure of the soul.

You showed me how to live.  
You showed me how to die.  
My life is now worth living,  
And you’re the reason why.

Praying to be situated eternally at your lotus feet, I remain  
Your humble servant,  
Guṇagrāhī dāsa Goswami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories unto you!

When I first joined your young Society in Detroit, Michigan, in 1970, I was relieved to find shelter from the miseries of material life. During those first few days and weeks I was content to cleanse the temple, associate with devotees, hear lectures—and enjoy the Sunday feast. I memorized my new address—8311 East Jefferson Street—thinking I would serve you happily in that little building for the rest of my life.

Then one day you came to visit our temple. Your arrival address changed my perspective on Kṛṣṇa consciousness and altered the very direction of my life. As you spoke, I saw that Kṛṣṇa consciousness was bigger than my little shelter. With gravity you said from the *vyāsāsana,*

\[This\] is a very important movement because it is correcting the human civilization. There is a great defect in modern civilization—people are accepting this body as self, and based on this mistake everything is going wrong. Great philosophers, scientists, and theologians do not know this defect. This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is meant to correct the wrong foundation of the human civilization.

I was awed to be part of a movement that seemed destined to change the world. By your grace, it appeared that my simple little shelter was going to unfold into something much greater. I daydreamed that perhaps one day I’d even have to leave Detroit and go to a bigger city, like New York!

Sure enough, a few months later I found myself in New York—on my way to Paris. You had called upon a devotee in America to oversee the expansion of your movement in France, and I had been asked to accompany him. As we sat before you in your quarters in the New York temple, you asked us to “preach boldly and spread the glories of the holy name of the Lord.” Once again I realized that I was part of something other than my own destiny. By some causeless mercy I had become part of your devotional service to the Lord! As we were about to depart, you asked your servant to bring some of your cloth, and you offered us each a piece of your own garments. Someone may calculate the price of that cloth as only a few dollars, but for me it was the most priceless gift I ever received—for it was a manifestation of your love and an encouragement to serve your mission abroad.

In the early years in France there was little resistance to your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. In my naivete I thought the whole country would soon embrace your mercy. I couldn’t imagine why anyone wouldn’t! But soon the impious saw us as a threat. You once said it is a sign of success that people see this movement as a force to be reckoned with. I remember the first time I saw Kṛṣṇa consciousness seriously challenged. You were visiting our center in Paris, and we had arranged a big program for you at a hall called Salle Pleyel. As you began your lecture, sitting on a large and beautiful *vyāsāsana,* some anarchists came forward and shouted at you to get down off the *vyāsāsana* and sit on the ground. You challenged back that you would sit on the ground if they thought themselves qualified to sit on the *vyāsāsana* and speak the absolute truth. They backed down. At that moment I realized that changing the world might not be so easy and sometimes we may be called upon to defend the Lord.

As you preached around the world, your movement grew—and by your grace so did I. After a few short years in devotional service I found myself accepting responsibility in your Society. I was no longer a teenager experimenting with Kṛṣṇa consciousness; rather I was obliged to act as a representative of a pure devotee of the Lord. During your presence, it seemed the growth of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement was matched only by the advance of Kali-yuga, and sometimes I wondered who would win the race. But you answered that question when you said to Guru dāsa in London in 1973, “One day scholars will note how this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement saved the world in its darkest hour.”

We don’t know when that “darkest hour” will come, but seeing the sinful activities of the people in general, I can’t imagine it’s far away. Neither can we know exactly what role we, as your followers, will play in helping you preach at that time. But from experience we understand that along with the instructions of the spiritual master comes the ability to execute them and that the greatest challenge inevitably gives rise to your greatest mercy. If we remain pure and preach strongly, that “darkest hour” will give way to a new era of light and your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will be accepted far and wide.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the challenges ahead must be far greater than those of the present or the past, and although 8311 East Jefferson Street is a great distance away now, I feel shelter in the service of your lotus feet. May your order to “preach boldly and spread the glories of the holy name of the Lord” be the purpose of my existence, life after life.

Your servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Īśvara Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories unto you!

Where are you now? I feel your separation very much, and I am missing your association. I know you went to the place to which you have always belonged, to stay beside your good friend. You went back to become the eternal child and play with Kṛṣṇa in His *līlā—*as if for any moment you had been away from such a place.

I think that of all you have taught us, this is the best teaching: that “the reality is on the other side of the mirror.” That which we think is true is only a reflection, and a very bad one, of the real world. In that world everything is happiness, everything is love, everything is a play. Not a frolic play but a true one, where all beings know that they do not have to push the present, for life will never end. Life there is like this: everyone is always full of the desire to live wholeheartedly in the moment and to love Kṛṣṇa, to be with Kṛṣṇa.

You showed better than anybody else this desire to live and love. Your love was unlimited because it came from a very strong desire to serve. Love is attention dedicated to others, so that they can grow. You cared about us, and this made us feel safe and confident, nourished and healthy. In love there is no selfishness, and the desire of the pure devotee is to give Kṛṣṇa’s love to the world and to everybody. In a broader sense, we can also say that love is selfishness, for those who love will fulfill their desires, not caring for the pressure of anybody else. What would become of the world if it weren’t for this “selfish love” of the pure devotees?

And you had plenty of this love, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I always saw a very strong desire in you, a desire that was shown by your leaving your comfortable and safe position in Kṛṣṇa’s play and coming here to help those who, like myself, were on this side of the mirror, overcome by the indolence of darkness.

If materialism comes from contaminated desire, love and spirituality come from the desire to serve. And you gave us the best example of service, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

I know we can trust you because of that. You will never disappoint us, because you proved your attitude was to help, to awake, to redeem souls and take them to the true world. And this you have done expertly as you danced to the rhythm of *saṅkīrtana*.

You also showed us that to lead our lives with a loving attitude we had to always be ready to risk ourselves for Kṛṣṇa. Being a genuine master, you nurtured us and took great care of our tender devotional creepers. But you also showed us that to love is to risk our lives for the beloved, for devotional life is always a risk; only in this way can we grow up and get close to Kṛṣṇa.

Isn’t it out of love that the bird throws its little birds out of the nest? Isn’t that the only way they can learn how to fly? You also left us in this world so that we could learn how to fly. You trusted our capacity and taught us that to risk is to love, and that this is the way we grow up.

It is natural to depend on the *guru,* because we are made by your mercy. But now, after so many years, I think I have understood what dependence is. You taught us that we could not use the *guru* as a support stick or as an artifice to build a false reality. To follow the *guru* is to live in happiness, in freedom. The freedom is within everyone, and the *guru,* with his infinite love, can awaken it. You showed us that real dependence is not harmful like addiction to a drug. You showed that it is we, your spiritually weak children, who have to learn how to walk and to live with Kṛṣṇa and experience our love for Him.

Thinking on these points, I can only increase my love for you, since you were so magnanimous and knew how to take care of your children. You knew how to teach them how to go back to the eternal home, by themselves. You raised healthy children who can face life, with risks—the risks of love.

Today we commemorate your appearance in this world. You came to show how to live an actual life without the help of the support sticks. I think I understood now how I can depend on you and on your instructions. I can only hope that I can meet you again, in the eternal play. And I hope, as well, to pass on this teaching to those you send to be taught by me.

Your servant,  
Īśvara Swami

## Jayadhvaja Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Bhāva-śraddhāñjali**

*viśvaṁ saraḥ sarasijaṁ prabhupāda āste*  
*bhaktāḥ subhāva-bharitāḥ bhramarāḥ bhramantāḥ*  
*gandhaḥ samasta-bhuvane bhagavat-prasāraḥ*  
*sārasvatīṁ tam iha vai prabhupādam īḍe*

**Śrīla Prabhupāda, the disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, is the lotus adorning the lake of the universe, and his devotees are like bumblebees roaming around the world to spread the fragrance of the message of their lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to whom we constantly pray.**

*kṛṣṇa-bhakti-prasārāya kṛṣṇa-premāmbulocanaḥ*  
*catur-daśa-parikramya viśvam adhyāpayaccha yaḥ*

**His lotus eyes are filled with devotion and love for Kṛṣṇa, and he circumambulated the world fourteen times to teach and spread** ***kṛṣṇa-bhakti*****.**

*prajāpateś ca śaraṇaṁ yayau yā dharaṇī purā*  
*sā kalau śaraṇaṁ yāta prabhupādasya dhīmataḥ*

**Just as in ancient times mother earth took shelter of Brahmājī, in Kali-yuga she took shelter of Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is most learned.**

*brahmā-nārada-vyāsādyāḥ bhavat-tanuṁ samāśritaḥ*  
*yat kiñcit pravadanty ete bhinnaṁ naiva kadācana*

**Brahmā, Nārada, and Vyāsa speak through you, keeping the** ***Vedas*** **intact. Just as Hayagrīva fought with the Madhu demon and saved the** ***Vedas,*** **you fight with the scientists, Māyāvādīs, and** ***bhogī yogīs,*** **who mislead those searching for Lord Vāsudeva.**

*khe tārā bahavaḥ santi candras tv ekaiva vartate*  
*guru bahulyam atrāsti prabhupādo jagad-guruḥ*

**Just as there are many stars in the sky but only one moon, there are many** ***gurus*** **in this world but only one Jagat-guru—Śrīla Prabhupāda.**

*asmād dhrug-gata-tāpānāṁ rāga-dveśādināṁ prabho*  
*kṛpā-candana-śītena nāśana kuru sarvathā*

**O Śrīla Prabhupāda! With the cooling** ***candana*** **of your mercy please always dispel the heat of attachment, aversion, and other dualities in our hearts.**

*bhavābdhau ca nimagnaṁ māṁ panthā naivātra dṛśyate*  
*jayadhvajaṁ pāhi pāhi bhaktānām abhayaṅkara*

**O Prabhupāda, who make the devotees of the Lord fearless! I have fallen into this ocean of birth and death and find no way out!**

Please protect and deliver this fallen soul, Jayadhvaja.

Bhavac-caraṇānurāgi-kiṅkaraḥ—your fallen servant,  
Jayadhvaja Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

What Śrīla Prabhupāda showed us can’t be faked.

We can admire Śrīla Prabhupāda, worship his lotus feet, follow him, learn from him, even share to some extent in his transcendental qualities. But the authentic depth of his love for Kṛṣṇa—where else is such a treasure to be found? And it is that exalted love that underlies all that Śrīla Prabhupāda showed us.

That we cannot fake. So when I try to seem grave like Śrīla Prabhupāda, I come off superficial and pretentious. When I try to seem learned like Śrīla Prabhupāda, I get condescending, puffed up, carried away by my own tiny erudition. When I try to be humorous like him, I become a clown. When I try to seem renounced like him, I start turning cold, artificial, and dry. I try to be bold like Śrīla Prabhupāda, and I risk doing things disastrously foolish. I try to be humble like him, and I delight in how well I do it. When I try to act spontaneous, it’s a contrivance. When I try to seem pure, I fall victim to a contaminated mind.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is our authentic master. I pray to carry out his desires as an authentic servant.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I would like to thank the Lord again and again for choosing me to take charge of your Centennial Celebration. It was a unique experience in my spiritual life. I’ve never done anything as intensely for such a long period of time as this service. I have been personally greatly benefited.

Your Centennial was a blessing upon us. Every day we sing *dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam:* “I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.” Your Centennial compelled us to do just that and even more: we remembered you so many more times daily and offered our humble obeisances unto you all the time.

In some way, our lives, which had been revolving around Centennial planning and execution for almost a decade, now feel a bit vacant. The great pleasure we took in shouting at the top of our voice or hearing “Śrīla Prabhupāda Centennial ki jaya!” has become part of history. We ate Centennial, slept Centennial, dreamt Centennial, walked and talked Centennial—and suddenly it is all over. Soon a memorial to the Centennial will be erected in Māyāpur to help us remember your Centennial.

Many things happened. We are now distributing awards to those who performed excellently. But very little has been done, really. Our Centennial achievements were but a scratch on the surface of all the offerings you actually deserve. While drawing up the Centennial master plan, we took stock of the actual accomplishments of ISKCON since its founding and realized that the many things you instructed us to do were left unfinished, hardly achieved, never started, or done in a distorted way. Many aspects of your mission remained undeveloped or neglected. A complete offering meant achieving excellence in all the areas of activity you gave us, if we were really serious to fully please you. At least we have a list of tasks that were not done, imperfectly executed, or left uncompleted, and we can now follow up by working on completing these tasks properly.

Applying this reasoning to each and every thing you asked us to do, we find there is just so much more to do.

We tried to inspire the world as much as we could, using the Centennial as a catalyst. The credit goes to your many sincere followers, leaders, and managers worldwide for organizing spectacular Centennial events throughout the year. However, in the realm of internal development—spiritual, social, educational, moral, ethical, constitutional, economical—we have barely begun. This major task is still ahead of us.

By the grace of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, *padayātrā* did get organized in a little over one hundred countries. *Padayātrīs* around the world should be commended for this special achievement. But where are the “millions of carts all over the world” that you desired? Although what we achieved sounds like a grand success, it simply falls short of your ambition. And this is considering the quantitative aspect of your instruction only. You also gave us a quality target. The day you inaugurated the bullock cart *saṅkīrtana* in 1976, you told us to increase the taste of the simple villagers for chanting the *mahā-mantra* so that they would remain wherever they are, happily glorifying the Lord, satisfied with the meager resources He has arranged for them. In this way, you instructed, they would stop their migration from village to town and town to city, where they only lead an unnatural life full of all sorts of inconveniences. The day this migration of the villagers toward city life is reversed will be the day we can consider the *padayātrā* mission you gave us successful. In this light we haven’t sufficiently pleased you by our hundred-country *padayātrā,* which stressed quantity rather than quality.

As their Centennial offering of love to you, the devotees here in Delhi have built a huge temple complex, the Śrī Śrī Rādhā–Pārtha-sārathi Mandira cum Glory of India Cultural Center. But as it nears completion Gopāl Krishna Mahārāja is already reminding everyone that you actually wanted a dozen temples in India’s capital!

In many ways, then, your Centennial has not ended. Now, on this occasion of the 101st anniversary of your appearance, we beg you to bless and inspire us so we may carry on with the unfinished tasks.

This life is for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please use us in any way you like that pleases you the most. And if you want, we will gladly be born again and continue to serve you in future births.

Thank you for this realization that I am  
Your eternal servant birth after birth,  
Lokanāth Swami.

## Mahā-Viṣṇu Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*(1) śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma, kevala bhakati-sadma,*  
*vando mui sāvadhāna mate*

**“The lotus feet of our spiritual master are the only way we can obtain pure devotional service. I bow down at his lotus feet with great awe and reverence.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I thought that just by some lightning flashes on a moonless night I could perhaps cross the marshes where deadly quicksand lies. I thought that my speculations, experiments, creations, and investigations could prevent my dark destiny ahead. When I saw the devotees paying their obeisances before your *vyāsāsana* in London 1971, my bent and faithless mind thought, “Soon they will learn they need not bow down to anyone because they themselves are as good as the *guru*!” But soon, by your disciples mercy, I also learned to gratefully bow down at your lotus feet.

*(2) yāhāra prasāde bhāi, e bhava toriyā yāi,*  
*kṛṣṇa-prāpti haya yāhā haite*

**“My dear brother, (my dear mind), it is through the grace of the spiritual master that we cross over this material existence and obtain Kṛṣṇa.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is true. As soon I was engaged by your disciples in my first devotional service—stair-cleaning in the London temple—I felt such relief that even some tears from my crippled heart helped wash the stairs up to your room. I felt accepted as the youngest in your family of Kṛṣṇa’s servants.

*(3) guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya*  
*ār nā koriho mane āśā*

**“Make the teachings emanating from the lotus mouth of the spiritual master one with your heart, and do not desire anything else”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as my spiritual master, surely it is you who must receive my surrender. Otherwise, how can I ever please and know Kṛṣṇa? Hearing from you, obeying you, and giving your message of “Hare Kṛṣṇa” in this *saṅkīrtana* movement attracts Kṛṣṇa to cleanse our hearts of all other desires. If we repeat your instructions to others who follow them, surely Kṛṣṇa will also cleanse their hearts too.

*(4) śrī-guru-caraṇe rati, ei sei uttama-gati,*  
*ye prasāde pūre sarva āśā*

**“Attachment to the lotus feet of the spiritual master is the best means of spiritual advancement. By his mercy all desires for spiritual perfection are fulfilled.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I saw the attachment of all the devotees who expected your arrival in London in 1971. When I first saw you, as you came through the front door, I was surprised that you were in no way appeared flamboyant or larger than life but rather very normal, self-composed, and familiar, like a father returning home after a day’s work. My attachment to you is very meager. I simply take advantage of your movement to maintain my life, doing the minimum service, only satisfying my seniors enough so that they do not eject me and continue to engage me in more service. I somehow or other hold on to your lotus feet because I don’t know where else to go. Please arrange things so that my attachment for you will start to grow. Some devotees nowadays wonder, “Can Prabhupāda’s disciples really give the perfection that fulfills all desires?” In answer, I remember your verses to your spiritual master:

The Line of service  
As drawn by you  
Is pleasing and healthy,  
Like morning dew.

The oldest of all,  
But in a new dress.  
Miracle done,  
Your Divine Grace

*(5) cakhu-dān dilo yei, janme janme prabhu sei,*  
*divya-jñāna hṛde prokāśito*

**“He who has given me the gift of transcendental vision is my Lord, birth after birth.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your simple words—“You are not that body”—carried by your agent (*vartma-pradarśaka-guru*), who was certainly not in order, stopped my foolish mouth and mind in wonder. From your temple in London he brought me the holy names—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare—which became my only hope in life.

*(6) prema-bhakti yāhā hoite, avidyā vināśa yāte,*  
*vede gāya yāhāra carito*

**“By his mercy divine knowledge is revealed within the heart, bestowing** ***prema-bhakti*** **and destroying ignorance. The Vedic scriptures sing of his character.”**

Now I begin to realize that you are Kṛṣṇa’s dear devotee who gave me *dīkṣā* before Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara. If I belong to you, I belong to Kṛṣṇa eternally too. You have awakened in me and many others the desire to learn to love Kṛṣṇa, God, more and more, so you are glorified in all the world’s scriptures.

*(7) śrī-guru karuṇā-sindhu, adhama janāra bandhu,*  
*lokanātha lokera jīvana*

**“O spiritual master, ocean of mercy and friend of the fallen souls, you are the teacher of everyone and the life of all people.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your order even I, with a ruined life, was found and reprieved. I was then only one of many, but now there are so many more for us to find and reprieve as I try to follow your order (and Lord Caitanya’s) to become a spiritual master myself.

*(8) hā hā prabhu koro doyā, deho more pada-chāyā,*  
*ebe yaśa ghuṣuk tribhuvana*

**“O master! Be merciful to me, and give me the shade of your lotus feet. May your glories now be proclaimed throughout the three worlds.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to become your sincere and worthy follower. Please arrange for me to be kicked into proper shape so I will dance in step and sing in tune with your beloved true followers, who are taking all three worlds back to Godhead in your glorious name!

Mahā-Viṣṇu Swami

## Nava-yogendra Swami

My dear Gurudeva Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who are an ocean of mercy and who are very kind upon the fallen souls, like myself!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have no intelligence to glorify a *mahā-bhāgavata* like Your Divine Grace. Kindly forgive me if there is any mistake in glorifying Your Divine Grace.Śrīla Prabhupāda, my desire is that I want to be a good instrument in your great mission of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Actually I have no qualification, but your mercy is limitless. Because of your mercy I am still hanging around, and I want to continue as long as I live. By your causeless mercy I have lost all taste for this material world. Gurudeva, please bless me so that I can become totally detached from matter and attached to your lotus feet, which are my only shelter. Gurudeva, I beg these benedictions from you.

*(1) ohe!*  
*vaiṣṇava ṭhākura, doyāra sāgara,*  
*e dāse karuṇā kori’*  
*diyā pada-chāyā, śodho he āmāya,*  
*tomāra caraṇa dhori*

*(2) chaya vega domi’, chaya doṣa śodhi’,*  
*chaya guṇa deho’ dāse*  
*chaya sat-saṅga, deho’ he āmāre*  
*bosechi saṅgera āśe*

*(3) ekākī āmāra, nāhi pāya bala,*  
*hari-nāma-saṅkīrtane*  
*tumi kṛpā kori’, śraddhā-bindu diyā,*  
*deho’ kṛṣṇa-nāma-dhane*

*(4) kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite pāro*  
*tomāra śakati āche*  
*āmi to kāṅgāla, ‘kṛṣṇa’ ‘kṛṣṇa’ boli’,*  
*dhāi tava pāche pāche*

**(1) O venerable Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura, O ocean of mercy, be merciful to this servant of yours and purify me with the shade of your lotus feet. Your lotus feet I humble hold.**

**(2) By helping me control the six urgesand purifying me of the six faults,bestow upon your servant the six good qualities.O master, please give me the six kinds of holy association.I have sat down here hoping to have your company.**

**(3) Alone, I do not find the strength to chant the holy name of Hari. Please be merciful and, blessing me with a drop of faith, bestow on me the great treasure of the holy name of Krsna.**

**(4) Kṛṣṇa is yours, and you can give Him to me, for such is your power. This wretch is simply running behind you, crying out, “Krsna! Krsna!”**

1\. The six urges are those of speech, the mind, anger, the tongue, the belly, and the genitals.

2\. The six faults are overeating or collecting more materially valuable things than required, overendeavoring for material objectives, unnecessary talking of mundane affairs, being to much attached or too neglectful of scriptural rules and regulations, associating with worldly-minded persons, and desiring mundane achievements.

3\. The six good qualities are enthusiasm, confidence, patience, performing the regulated activities of devotional service, giving up the association of nondevotees, and following in the footsteps of the previous *ācāryas*.

4\. The six activities of holy association are offering gifts in charity, accepting charitable gifts, revealing one’s mind in confidence, inquiring confidentially, accepting *prasādam* (spiritual food) from others, and offering *prasādam* to others.

Your most fallen disciple of all,  
Nava-yogendra Swami

## Nityodita Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, I fall at your feet and cry. My tears are not the cooling tears of ecstasy that an advanced devotee would shed, but rather my tears are burning hot. Each tear is a humiliating confession of my stubborn material desires that are the continuing cause of my inability to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

As my eternal spiritual master, you have tried your best to drown me in the ocean of nectar. Your typhoonlike words, coming at me from all directions from your books, tapes, letters, videos, and disciples, are inundating the whole world. But I, like a stubborn worm, feel uncomfortable in such a surcharged atmosphere and with great determination gleefully crawl back into my abominable stool-filled mentality of desiring sense gratification.

In your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 10.11 you state, “There are very, very learned scholars who have put forward the philosophy of devotion. But even if a devotee does not take advantage of their literatures or of his spiritual master, if he is sincere in his devotional service he is helped by Kṛṣṇa Himself within his heart.” So, I am such a devotee who does not take advantage of his spiritual master or his literature, and on top of that I am not sincere. So what is to be my fate?

Actually, after serious consideration I’ve concluded that your devotees are my only hope. The community of *mahātmās* is my only strength and refuge. I pray to take shelter of the association of your devotees, which is your transcendental body.

Many times during the past several years I’ve stood in front of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vṛndāvanacandra and prayed to Them to protect your community. The trials and tests that the Brijabāsīs here are being put through are as great as any *tapasya* of great *yogīs* in bygone days. Somehow or other the Lord is protecting His devotees and has not given us any test greater than we can handle. Now we are coming to the greatest test of all, the true test of our love for you: Can we cooperate with our Godbrothers and Godsisters to fulfill your vision of a Kṛṣṇa conscious community?

You attracted me to New Vṛndāvana with your motto of “plain living and high thinking.” Little did I know that to manifest such would become my life’s mission. This year, despite all the obstacles, we are offering you a new dairy barn right next to the temple to lovingly care for Kṛṣṇa’s milk cows. By bringing the cows back to the center of the community, we hope to rekindle your vision for New Vṛndāvana in the hearts of all. You said, “The special feature of New Vṛndāvana will be cow protection, and by doing so we will not be the loser.”

Your other *sannyāsī* disciples are great preachers and *gurus*. I have no such potency. I simply pray that you will continue to allow me to serve you in this humble way, as the servant of the servants of Kṛṣṇa’s cows.

Your unworthy servant,  
Nityodita Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Although in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (10.38) Lord Kṛṣṇa mentions that of secret things He is silence, still He reveals to Arjuna secret and still more secret knowledge. Similarly, in his books Śrīla Prabhupāda reveals to us confidential, more confidential, and most confidential knowledge. Śrīla Prabhupāda follows in the footsteps of Rāmānujācārya, who, when his *guru* gave to him a secret *mantra* that would benefit everyone, immediately revealed it to others. His *guru* at first became angry but was then pleased when he understood Rāmānuja’s pure motive. Lord Kṛṣṇa also says that anyone who reveals His secrets to the devotees is most dear to Him and thus becomes eligible to return to the spiritual world.

Below is a small selection of the secret information that Śrīla Prabhupāda reveals within his books. Śrīla Prabhupāda would like us to understand these secrets and distribute them to others. As Śrīla Prabhupāda said in Boston in 1969:

So everything is there. You can take advantage of these facilities. Therefore we have opened this center. You come. You try to understand and be benefited. We are not asking you that “Give us thousand dollars. Then we shall open our secret.” Our secret is all open secret. You simply take advantage of it. Try to understand with your reason, with your sense, everything.

**The Secret of Service to the Guru**

***The secret of Vyāsa-pūjā***

The plain truth so vividly explained in the *Gītā,* which is the central lesson of the *Vedas,* is not understood or even suspected by the most powerful scholars of the empiric schools. Herein lies the secret of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā. When we meditate on the transcendental pastimes of the Absolute Godhead, we are proud to feel that we are His eternal servitors, and we become jubilant and dance with joy. All glory to my divine master, for it is he who has out of his unceasing flow of mercy stirred up within us such a movement of eternal existence. Let us bow down at his lotus feet. \[*The Science of Self-Realization,* Ch. 2\]

***The Secret of advancing in spiritual life***

Satisfaction of the self-realized spiritual master is the secret of advancement in spiritual life. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 4.34, purport\]

***Secret of getting Lord Kṛṣṇa’s benedictions***

If we strictly try to serve the spiritual master, his order, then Kṛṣṇa will give us all facilities. That is the secret. Although there was no possibility, I never thought, but I took it little seriously by studying a commentary by Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura on the *Bhagavad-gītā*. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* the verse *vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana,* in connection with that verse, Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura gives his commentary that we should take up the words from the spiritual master as our life and soul. We should try to carry out the instruction, the specific instruction of the spiritual master, very rigidly, without caring for our personal benefit or loss. \[Lecture on the disappearance anniversary of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, Los Angeles, 9 December 1969\]

**Prabhupāda’s Secret of Success**

***The secret of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s success***

My Guru Mahārāja gave me hint that book publication is more pleasing to me than *maṭha-mandira*. So I took it and I began to publish books, and that has come successful. *Kāryaṁ karma*. I took it, “Oh, Guru Mahārāja wants that books should be published. So let me concentrate on this instead of—” My creating so many centers, big, big temples, that is not my primary duty. My primary duty is to write books. Therefore I am going on still. These are coming automatically. Maybe this is the secret of my success. \[Morning walk, Bombay, 2 April 1974\]

***Prabhupāda’s humility***

Actually, that is the secret of my success, not that personally I have done anything wonderful, but that because those who are helping me are sincere, they have done the work, that is the reason for our success all over the world where others have failed. \[Letter to Karandhara, January 1973\]

**Preaching**

***Prabhupāda’s open secret***

You write to say that you do not know what is my desire, but my desire is an open secret. I simply want all over the Western countries people may take this simple formula of chanting, dancing, and eating Kṛṣṇa *prasādam,* and being happy. \[Letter to Tamāla Krishna, September 1969\]

***Purity is the force***

Before me, many swamis went to the Western countries to preach this *Bhagavad-gītā*. Not a single person became a devotee of Kṛṣṇa.… And now *Bhagavad-gītā* is being presented as it is, thousands are becoming devotee of Kṛṣṇa. This is the secret. \[Lecture on *Bhagavad-gītā,* Ahmedabad, 8 December 1972\]

***Utility is the principle***

One should not give up anything which can be utilized in the service of the Lord. That is a secret of devotional service. \[*The Nectar of Devotion,* Ch. 14\]

**Engaging Others in Kṛṣṇa’s Service**

***The secret of engaging others in Kṛṣṇa consciousness***

The secret will be to engage them as they like to be engaged, that is, supposing I have got some education, I am business student, or I have got some skill or talent, I am typist or musician or something like that, so I will like to utilize these things for Kṛṣṇa only if I am encouraged in a certain way, very tactfully, and I must not be discouraged by too much forcing me at first to accept everything of shaving the head, rising very early, going for street *saṅkīrtana,* like that. No, let me come gradually, let me study also Kṛṣṇa consciousness and see how it is practical and sublime. Gradually I may get some taste for these other things and agree to do them voluntarily and intelligently. \[Letter to Śukadeva, December 1972\]

***The secret principle of service***

So we have to mold our life in such a way that we shall always remember Kṛṣṇa. Therefore remembering Kṛṣṇa is my primary business. And we have to act in such a way that we may not forget Kṛṣṇa. That should be the principle. That is the secret. Therefore it is equally good for anyone because we can engage anyone in the business of Kṛṣṇa. If somebody has no, I mean to say, knowledge, he can simply sweep over the floor of Kṛṣṇa’s temple. That will make him remembering Kṛṣṇa, that “I am cleansing the floor of Kṛṣṇa’s temple.” He’s as good as the editor of *Back to Godhead*. \[Lecture on *Bhagavad-gītā,* Los Angeles, 25 November 1968\]

**Surrender to Kṛṣṇa**

***The secret of surrender to Lord Kṛṣṇa***

They do not know the secret of surrendering to Kṛṣṇa. Such surrendered devotee sees that everything is part of Kṛṣṇa’s plan, that whatever is meant to be, I am doing that, so let me do it with my full attention to every detail, let me become absorbed in such service, never mind what it is, but let all other considerations be forgotten and only my desire to do the thing best for Kṛṣṇa’s alone pleasure be my motive. \[Letter to Jayapatāka, December 1972\]

***Secret wealth***

The devotees are generally without material prosperity, but they have a very secret treasure house in the lotus feet of the Lord. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.8.27, purport\]

**Spiritual Advancement**

***The secret of learning to love God***

**Reporter:** What is the secret to learning to love God?

**Prabhupāda:** Secret of learning God?

**Jagadīśa:** Learning to love.

**Prabhupāda:** Yes. The secret—that he must be a first-class man. Otherwise he does not understand what is God, and what to speak of loving Him. \[Press conference, Chicago, 9 July 1975\]

***First guru, then Kṛṣṇa***

While teaching Rūpa Gosvāmī, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, *guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja:* one can achieve the seed of devotional service by the mercy of the *guru,* the spiritual master, and then by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. This is the secret of success. First one should try to please the spiritual master, and then one should attempt to please the Supreme Personality of Godhead. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.28, purport\]

***The secret of success in Kali-yuga***

Our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement stresses the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* only, whereas those who do not know the secret of success for this Age of Kali unnecessarily indulge in the cultivation of knowledge, the practice of mystic *yoga,* or the performance of fruitive activities or useless austerities. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Ādi* 17.24, purport\]

***The secret of keeping spiritual strength***

The secret of success will depend on yourself keeping on the spiritual strength by regularly chanting and following the regulative principles, and side by side you have to act on preaching the gospel, and it will go on without any impediment. \[Letter to Citsukhānanda, March 1970\]

***The secret of not being a victim of māyā***

If one is always careful about falling down and prays to Kṛṣṇa that he may not fall down on account of greater strength of Māyā, then Kṛṣṇa will give him special protection. This was advised to Arjuna by Kṛṣṇa Himself. Arjuna was a great fighter, statesman, and at the same time a great devotee. A statesman and military commander has very little time to perform the routine work of devotional service, but Kṛṣṇa advised him especially to be engaged in his occupational duty, and at the same time always be thinking of Kṛṣṇa. This is the secret of not being a victim of Māyā. \[Letter to Gargamuni, October 1969\]

***The secret of being as strong as Lord Kṛṣṇa***

This same man, he’s *guru,* so long he gives the real knowledge of Kṛṣṇa. And the same man, he’s ordinary man, as soon as he cannot give. Same thing, just like a stone doll, when it is worshiped according to the regulative principles—Kṛṣṇa. And the same doll, kept in the sculptor’s showroom, it is stone. So if we keep our movement pure, then you are as strong as Kṛṣṇa. And as soon as you deviate from it, immediately, ordinary. This is the secret. \[Room conversation, Honolulu, 3 May 1976\]

***The secret of hearing and chanting***

A professional speaker cannot impress transcendental ecstasy within the hearts of the listeners. However, when a realized soul who is engaged in the service of the Lord is speaking, he has the potency to inject spiritual life within the audience. One should, therefore, seek the association of such pure, unalloyed devotees, and by such association and service a neophyte devotee will certainly develop attachment, love, and devotion for the Supreme Personality of Godhead. \[*The Nectar of Devotion,* Ch. 17\]

***The secret of conquering the Supreme Lord***

Lord Kṛṣṇa told King Yudhiṣṭhira that He is conquered by the love of one who has conquered his senses. One who has not conquered his senses cannot conquer the Supreme Personality of Godhead. This is the secret of devotional service. To conquer the senses means to engage them constantly in the service of the Lord. \[*Kṛṣṇa Book,* Ch. 72\]

**Books**

***“The Nectar of Devotion” and “Kṛṣṇa”***

I am very much glad to learn that you have appreciated the subject matter of *The Nectar of Devotion* and *Kṛṣṇa* while working on it in the press, and actually these two books will keep us constantly in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The secret of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is open in these two books. \[Letter to Madhusūdana, June 1970\]

***Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta***

The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* pastimes of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu constitute a very secret literature. It is the life and soul of all devotees. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 25.283\]

***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam***

The secret of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī’s name in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:* “Sometimes the critics of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* find it difficult to find Rādhārāṇī’s holy name, but the secret is disclosed here in the word *ārādhitaḥ,* from which the name Rādhā has come.” \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 8.110\]

Secrets of learning *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:* “No one can learn *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* who is associated with persons engaged in sex life. That is the secret of learning *Bhāgavatam.* Nor can one learn *Bhāgavatam* from one who interprets the text by his mundane scholarship. One has to learn *Bhāgavatam* from the representative of Śukadeva Gosvāmī, and no one else, if one at all wants to see Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa in the pages. The secret of knowing *Bhāgavatam* is mentioned here. No one can give rapt attention who is not pure in mind. No one can be pure in mind who is not pure in action. No one can be pure in action who is not pure in eating, sleeping, fearing, and mating. But somehow or other if someone hears with rapt attention from the right person, at the very beginning one can assuredly see Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa in person in the pages of *Bhāgavatam*.” \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.3.44, purport\]

***Bhagavad-gītā***

The secret of the *Bhagavad-gītā:* “This is the art, and this is also the secret of *Bhagavad-gītā:* total absorption in the thought of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.” \[*Bhagavad-gītā,* Introduction\]

The secret of understanding the *Bhagavad-gītā:* “So this is the process of understanding: you have to become dear friend of Kṛṣṇa. Then you will understand *Bhagavad-gītā*. Otherwise, for thousands of years you may go on reading *Bhagavad-gītā;* you will not learn even a word of it. That is the secret.” \[Lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Delhi, 12 November 1973\]

**Other Secrets**

***The secret of obtaining peace and fearlessness***

The difference between a person in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and a person in bodily consciousness is that the former is attached to Kṛṣṇa whereas the latter is attached to the results of his activities. This is the secret of Kṛṣṇa consciousness—realization that there is no existence besides Kṛṣṇa is the platform of peace and fearlessness. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 5.12, purport\]

***The secret of obtaining all good qualities***

So, people are trying to elevate the status of the society by so many things. But they do not know the secret. The secret is that if one is trained only to become a devotee of Kṛṣṇa, all good qualities automatically will be visible in his person. \[Lecture on *Bhagavad-gītā,* London, 26 July, 1973\]

***The secret of life***

“Otherwise” means the act which is done for the satisfaction of the Supreme Lord, that is immune from all reaction, but anything which is not done for the satisfaction of the Supreme Lord, that will oblige me in so many bondage of obligation, and I will have to repay that after many, many continuation of this repetition of births and deaths. That is the secret of life. \[Lecture on *Bhagavad-gītā,* New York, 20 May 1966\]

***The secret understanding in all Vedic literature***

Everything happening within time, which consists of past, present, and future, is merely a dream. That is the secret understanding in all Vedic literature. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.29.2b\]

***The secret of peace in society***

Kṛṣṇa is first of all interested to see whether in the society the *brāhmaṇa* and the cow is properly respected. *Namo brahmaṇya-devāya go-brāhmaṇa-hitāya ca*. His first business is to see that the *brāhmaṇa* and cow is being properly honored. Then *jagad-dhitāya*. Then automatically the whole world will be peaceful. This secret of success they do not know. Nobody is prepared to become *brāhmaṇa,* and so far cow protection is concerned, it is in the oblivion. This is the whole world position. Therefore it is in chaotic condition. \[Lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* New Vṛndāvana, 10 November 1976\]

***The secret of prasādam and why a woman is liked***

**Prabhupāda:** They want very palatable things, the tongue. *Tā’ra madhye jihwā ati lobhamoy sudurmati,* very greedy. So if you supply them *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* at the same time to satisfy their greediness, then you conquer. This is the secret.

**Gopāla Kṛṣṇa**: You conquered a lot of devotees by prasadam.

**Prabhupāda:** Oh, yes. Yes. Automatically he becomes devotee: “No, we shall become, remain here.” This is the secret. Why the woman is liked? Because the woman, if she is trained up to give satisfaction to the tongue, to the belly and the sex, straight line, she becomes favorite immediately. This is woman’s business. And people are hankering. The *karmīs* especially, they are hankering after these three things: palatable dishes, fill up belly, and sex. That’s all. If the woman can do it, she conquers over the husband immediately because these three things they want. Take things very practically. \[Morning walk, Māyāpur, 21 January 1976\]

***Secret of going back to Godhead easily***

So this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is not on the bodily platform. It is on the spiritual platform. To come to the spiritual platform, it is necessary that you reduce or make nil sex life. So if one remains *brahmacārī* throughout the whole life it becomes very easy for him to go back to home, back to Godhead. This is the secret. \[Lecture on *Bhagavad-gītā,* Nairobi, 27 October 1975\]

***The secret of determination***

Thus, refraining from sex enables one to be very determined and powerful. It is not necessary to do anything else. This is a secret people are not aware of. If you want to do something with determination, you have to refrain from sex. \[*The Path of Perfection,* Ch. 4\]

\* \* \*

By Śrīla Prabhupāda’s and Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, let us become instruments to reveal these secrets to whomever we meet. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu said that this is the secret to making everything auspicious in this Age of Kali.

Praying for your revelations,  
Your insignificant servant,  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Rasānanda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your beautiful lotus feet. There is a photograph—the most wonderful of all photographs. In that photograph the nails of your lotus feet move the viewer to another reality. In this reality illusion is noticeable by its absence. Where is suffering? Anxiety? Envy? Madness? Fear? The radiance of those nails’ rays is cooler than unlimited full moons. It carries us to boundaries of love of Godhead. Simple nails. What wonderful physical existence! Just nails—which can alert us to this world of birth and death and transport us to ceaseless rapture of sensitivity. They look like pearls, but they are not. They are astonishing, beyond the reach of description. I am trying very hard to be able to describe them, but I am dumbfounded. How can I describe them? They are everything for me. Let me absorb myself in this sight. I am entirely protected. No fears, concerns. Your lotus feet are everything for me. I take shelter at your lotus feet.

Your menial servant,  
Rasānanda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my repeated obeisances offered to you again and again with deep gratefulness and affection.

Once, in an interview for devotees, I was requested to describe in one sentence what I most appreciate in you. Immediately the following words came to my mind: Śrīla Prabhupāda always lives in Kṛṣṇa and still cares for us.

In the Ninth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* the Supreme Lord Himself talks about His intimate relationship with His pure devotee: “The pure devotee is always within the core of My heart, and I am always in the heart of the pure devotee. My devotees do not know anything else but me, and I do not know anyone else but them.”

When speaking this verse, the Lord must certainly have had you in His mind. Although you are so near and dear to the Lord and I am so fallen, I am truly moved by your care and attention for the fallen souls—first and foremost myself.

**Two Who Are So Unequal**

You are a resident of the spiritual sky—I am a fallen soul of this world of death.

You are very dear to Lord Mukunda—I am a great offender.

You are always beyond the modes of nature—I am a Ping-Pong ball battered by them over the table of this world.

You are after the sweet beverage of love of Godhead—I am addicted to the deadly poison of name, fame, and glory.

You are in full control of your mind and senses—I am attached to all the attractions of this world.

I feel desperate—sometimes you seem so far. How can I ever reach you? How can an ant touch the moon?

**A Mercy-Bridge Over the Amazing Gap**

In my despair falls a luminous shaft of light: I know without a doubt of your quality of being merciful to the fallen. You seem to be made out of mercy and compassion. How else could you have come to this world, preached here, and made arrangements to stay accessible even after having gone further?

However, there is one qualification for maintaining and protecting my connection with you: “To keep oneself free from material qualification and attain the Lord’s favor one must be sincerely anxious to render service to the Lord. This is the only qualification necessary” (Cc. *Madhya* 19.135, purport).

There are many reasons why you are unique among all the *ācāryas* who came to this world. But for me your most wonderful attribute is your intense desire and skill in engaging even the most fallen in the service of the Lord.

When reading your books, hearing your tapes, remembering your example, and hearing about you, the fallen soul undergoes a magical transformation: The desire to serve the Lord appears—and with it the strength to do it.

Please never allow my innumerable bad qualities to cause me to become so covered that I cannot think of you always. My simple and straightforward realization over the years is that my connection with you is my lifeline. Just as the strong rope that can save a drowning person needs a minimum of three strands, so my connection with you needs (1) Constant Awareness of your glories throughout the three phases of time, (2), Love, and (3) Service. Without that living connection I will certainly be swept away by the strong current of *māyā*.

Whatever was done nicely throughout the year, whatever thought was good, whatever I understood, is all your mercy. Today please allow me to turn to you and request you: Please continue to be merciful toward me, tolerating my innumerable bad qualities, and kindly continue to engage me in your service. That is my natural position—somewhere serving at your lotus feet. With great hope I remember how Kṛṣṇa said to Durvāsā Muni, “Because My devotees are completely devoid of material desires, I sit only within the cores of their hearts. What to speak of My devotee, even those who are devotees of My devotee are very dear to Me.” And your comment: “Following the instructions of a devotee is more valuable than following the instructions of the Supreme Personality of Godhead directly.”

I am confident this little offering will meet you fully immersed in devotional service.

Thank you very much for nevertheless taking the time to listen to your most insignificant servant,  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, people quote you to argue their points. Some say there should be no initiating *gurus* after you, and they quote you to prove their statements. Others say that of course there should be initiating *gurus,* and they also quote you. Some quote you as having said that you completed only fifty percent of your work and therefore you wanted *varṇāśrama* at Gītā-nāgarī. Others quote you to prove that women should be controlled and protected because they are less intelligent. Still others quote you as saying that this does not apply to devotee women. Back and forth it goes, so much so that we can become confused as to who you are. Śrīla Prabhupāda, are you just a collection of quotes culled from the Folio? Are you exactly your *arcā-vigraha* and nothing more than that? Are you what any one devotee defines you as? And if it is left up to me to define you, are you only what I can contact?

Please allow me to get beyond your official form as the institutional head. That doesn’t mean I want to dethrone you; it means only that I want to go beyond my usual, limited conception of you and to awaken to my eternal relationship with you.

The “*guru*” prayer in the Gāyatrī *mantras* states that the *guru* is pleasing to Kṛṣṇa. The spiritual master is not an ordinary person, and our relationship with him is also not ordinary. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have an eternal relationship with you. I don’t know your form in *kṛṣṇa-līlā* at present, but at least I know you as you appeared (and continue to appear) to me in this world. You are a saffron-robed *sannyāsī* and you are present in your books.

Today I read that sound, *vāṇī,* is the real essence of the spiritual master’s association. Your books are literally preserved sound, but your *vāṇī* is also a living presence in my heart. You cannot be measured or limited. Therefore, I simply ask you to protect me. Please give me the strength to obey you. Dear spiritual master, my own *guru*, you know my failings. I am not a bold preacher, I don’t chant with attention, I still have doubts after so many years, I have silly material attachments, I find fault with others and don’t always rejoice in their association—so many shortcomings. Please forgive me even though I am powerless to clear myself of these *anarthas*. Because I am powerless, I go beyond confession and ask you to please accept me despite my faults. Only you can help me to remove them. Please illumine my heart and give me the inspiration to progress in spiritual life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to praise you. I want my heart to break and I want to fall down on my knees in worship. Emotion felt in loving service to you is the perfection of emotion. Please let the day come when I can cry tears of love, feeling my unworthiness, in praise of you—that praise I didn’t offer enough of when you were present and that I haven’t offered enough of since you left. Let me cry because I am not serving you enough and because I haven’t achieved a decent level of advancement in all these years. I know that all that is required is to accept your love and to love you back. You have given me so much.

I’m getting old, and that means I’m closer to joining you, or at least I hope I am. I am certainly closer to my death, that insignificant event in the world when this one cipher of a *jīva* will sputter out like a candle and die. Where will I go? Please take me to you.

Your servant,  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Here are some thoughts that came to me this year as I tried to write something in your glorification, an appreciation.

I like to offer my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet because…

You understood us—our fallen condition and yearning for truth.

You loved us—for what we were, seekers of spiritual life.

You cared for us—by coming to the West.

You delivered us—through your teachings and example.

You attracted us—with your purity, boldness, and straightforwardness.

Much more could be said and is said by my wonderful Godbrothers /sisters and Godnephews. By your mercy I am surrounded by a wonderful family of devotees. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Let me sometime in the future fully surrender unto your lotus feet, and then like my Godbrothers and Godsisters are doing now, do something wonderful for you.

I beg to remain your humble, attempting servant,  
Smita Kṛṣṇa Swami.

## Umāpati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā is often called “the most blessed event,” and the first occasion of this blessed event came 101 years ago, when Śrīla Prabhupāda appeared in this world.

Within the time of my own memory, the first occasion of the blessed event came in 1966, but I did not know it was Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance day. I felt that it was a blessed day, but I did not know why. It was the day after my first, and most grueling, Janmāṣṭamī fast.

I have not forgotten that Janmāṣṭamī. I had never seen my watch move so slowly or an afternoon that could last so long. Then evening came and stayed endlessly. Finally, finally, midnight came. We raced up the stairs to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s apartment and sat down, eagerly awaiting our feast, our reward for a day of suffering.

Then Śrīla Prabhupāda came out of the kitchen with a platter of cut fruit and gave us each a few pieces. That was all. It didn’t look like much, but we ate it and felt perfectly satisfied. This was one of my first surprises in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The next day I had a feeling of a blessed event, but I didn’t know why. I thought it was because I could eat.

The first time I knew why this day is a blessed event was in 1968. I flew from New York to Montreal on Janmāṣṭamī night to see Śrīla Prabhupāda. Fasting was not so difficult anymore. (It’s easier if you are active than if you sit around feeling sorry for yourself.) The next day we celebrated Vyāsa-pūjā in the temple with Śrīla Prabhupāda. We did not know what do, so Śrīla Prabhupāda had to tell us that we should each give a little talk in honor of the spiritual master. And for the first time I stopped for a minute and thought about why this day is the most blessed event.

And every year now I like to take some time and ask myself why this day is the most blessed event. The first reason, of course, is that if Śrīla Prabhupāda had not come, we would not have become servants of Lord Kṛṣṇa. We would have lived and died like dogs.

But there is another reason: If Śrīla Prabhupāda had not come, we would not have become servants of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Just as the name of Kṛṣṇa has become synonymous with life itself, so have the instructions of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Just as the thought of life without Kṛṣṇa has become inconceivable, the thought of Kṛṣṇa consciousness without Śrīla Prabhupāda is also inconceivable, so inconceivable that we cannot think of building a temple without a special place for Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Because Śrīla Prabhupāda has entered our lives, our lives have become an eternal blessed event. And today is the most blessed of blessed events because it is the anniversary of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s appearance. And the blessings increase every year as we take a little more time to remember just why Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā is the most blessed event of all.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇā Swami

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories unto your divine lotus feet!

Recently I was offered a gift which, upon being opened, revealed a treasure of spiritual significance recorded in the form of a photograph. Upon opening the photo album, I noticed that the first picture was a scene which, up until that moment, I had carried only as a memory. And yet, it was foremost amongst the many impressions stored in my heart. Everyone loves this photo, but those who were so blessed as to be present when it was taken are aware of the special context in which it was filmed. It is a rare and precious assemblage of revelations.

It is the summer of 1972 at New Vṛndāvana. Everywhere I look there are the bright faces of devotees who have traveled here from many temples far and wide to celebrate the Appearance Day of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, along with the most blessed event, your Vyāsa-pūjā. Like flower airplanes descending from the spiritual sky, caravans of traveling parties are arriving daily, carrying enthusiastic, effulgent young *brahmacārīs* holding *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. The atmosphere, though tranquil and serene, is filled with spiritual excitement and loving anticipation.

Last-minute finishing touches on the temple in Bahulaban are nearly complete. The early-morning *kīrtanas* mingle with sounds of banging hammers. Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vṛndāvanacandra have moved into Their new home just in time for your return visit to New Vṛndāvana. Lingering in the air is the smell of fresh paint mixed with the sweet perfume of forest flowers decorating the Deities and the altar.

Janmāṣṭamī morning arrives. You ascend the *vyāsāsana* and begin *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class. You look at each one of us lovingly, knowing our hearts—their weaknesses, their *anarthas* and hidden desires, their strengths, their longings and ambitions for spiritual advancement. I will never forget your first words: “Everyone is searching for satisfaction.”

This simple truth, spoken with complete realization by you, my spiritual master, illuminated my darkened life. As if to give confirmation, affirmation, and visualization of the effect of your words, the golden rays of the rising sun, cresting above the horizon, begin to shine through the stained glass window behind you. This one old window, rescued from an abandoned church, is the temple’s only source of direct sunlight. As you speak, the light reflected off the shards of multicolored glass casts a pattern of dancing colors on the temple room floor. Punctuating each sentence of your lecture, the rays of the rising sun are moving slowly from the base of the altar toward their cherished goal at your lotus feet.

As I gaze at the sunshine now bursting through the window and engulfing you in brilliant light, I recall a relevant *śloka* from the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*

*kṛṣṇe sva-dhāmopagate dharma-jñānādibhiḥ saha*  
*kalau naṣṭa-dṛśām eṣa purāṇārko ’dhunoditaḥ*

**“This** ***Bhāgavata Purāṇa*** **is as brilliant as the sun, and it has arisen just after the departure of Lord Kṛṣṇa to His own abode, accompanied by religion, knowledge, etc. Persons who have lost their vision due to the dense darkness of ignorance in the Age of Kali shall get light from this** ***Purāṇa*****” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.3.43).**

The eternal wisdom of this sacred verse comes alive at that moment. It boldly speaks of the grace and love of Guru and Kṛṣṇa, awakening my heart with a fresh taste of spiritual appreciation.

Your lecture is finished, our questions are just beginning. The sunlight now reaching the *vyāsāsana* offers its obeisances as it washes your lotus feet with its warm and golden rays. I feel as if I were witnessing history in motion on this most auspicious morning. The verses of great sages from *yugas* past, the faces of aspiring *bhaktas* of the present, the sunlight flowing through an ancient stained glass window, and your words emblazoned by pure devotion—the sights and sounds of this divine vision stretch across the boundaries of mundane time and geographical limits.

The inmates of New Vṛndāvana sit here before you, clutching our tiny stories of loneliness and pain, our lives so fragmented, broken and useless. Yet, as we let go and allow the light of your mercy to shine through our lives, as we surrender our hearts, listening with complete faith to your words and then acting upon them, our lives become meaningful, colorful, and brilliant. Like the pieces of glass in the stained glass window—individually, we each create a portion of the picture brought together as a whole by the mercy of Śrī Guru. Collectively, we create a transcendental scene of Lord Caitanya’s pastimes continuing down to the present day on this most blessed Earth.

As I reflect on your words, “Everyone is seeking satisfaction,” I thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for teaching me on that Janmāṣṭamī morning that as our faith deepens, so too does our level of spiritual satisfaction, that genuine satisfaction lies in *bhāgavata-bhakti*. I pray to never forget that moment when I first realized that in your presence my life was now completely fulfilled, that with you as my spiritual master there was no greater or more perfect satisfaction to be found in the three worlds.

Cherishing this scene from the core of my heart, I continue to reflect on the way you personify the words of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in a manner which gives meaning and satisfaction to our quest today and always.

Looking back with fondest recollections at your picture in the album, a cherished memory from bygone days becomes a living reality here and now and will surely continue to inspire us forever. I can feel the warmth and illumination, as if the sun were rising behind you. I listen with anticipation to hear what you will say.

You said that the paintings which adorned your books were like windows granting *darśana* of the spiritual sky. This picture is more than that. It is like a portal leading to Goloka Vṛndāvana, with you continuing to encourage us to come and join you there.

Aspiring to forever be the servant of your servants,  
Varṣāṇā Swami

## Vedavyāsa Priya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept hundreds of my prostrated obeisances at your Centennial appearance.

In fact, your disciples and granddisciples did so much variegated and glorious glorification of you throughout 1996 and 1997 that my remembrance of you increased manyfold. Therefore at every remembrance I would like to pay my humble obeisances, and I look up to you for your mercy in the form of your service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how can I serve you, since I am like a Johnny-come-lately? I hope I can serve you a lot. Of course, I am seeking help and advice from my seniors by serving them, but still you are my father, and I remember you more than anybody and anything. Therefore kindly inspire me more to do some challenging service at your lotus feet. This is my wish.

I do not know whether the Bharatpur Orphanage project is really challenging, but somehow or other I have come to Bharatpur and started Kṛṣṇa-izing the whole *āśrama* community, simply on a resolve that the children are Brijbāsīs and that their heritage is directly Kṛṣṇa consciousness, not a hodgepodge of some foreign religions that is being imposed on them. And also, I am hoping to please you by this Bharatpur connection for another reason: this is Bharatpur Mahārāja’s property, and he wanted to serve you by sending that silver swing in 1972 for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara in Vṛndāvana but could not do so. Maybe I can try to heal your displeasure.

I frequent your Vṛndāvana temple and try to serve there in order to get more enthusiasm—I know, these and other engagements are not enough to be called a challenging service. Therefore please bestow your mercy on me. I need it the most because I do not know how to glorify you properly.

I still remain an insignificant beggar, begging for your mercy,  
Vedavyāsa Priya Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to you.

Blessed was that holy day  
When you did appear  
In Calcutta; *Abhay* meant  
“Someone who knew no fear.”  
Holy was that blessed day  
When you did advent;  
Logic is so strong with you,  
None could circumvent.

Sacred was that holy time  
When you crossed by boat;  
Teach us all Lord Kṛṣṇa’s way,  
Keep us all afloat.  
Great were you to walk the streets,  
New York Bow’ry bums;  
Brave the city you did come,  
Pulled us out of slums.

Oh, how great! I can’t conceive,  
Mercy comes to rush;  
Cooking meals and serving too,  
Wait in line for us.  
Gurudev! Please look at me,  
Glories I will chant;  
Compassion for the fallen,  
Nothing that you can’t.

Writing books and sleeping not,  
Clever is your plot;  
Caught us all and drowned us too,  
Fortune is our lot!  
Changed our names and made us love  
Lord Gaurāṅga’s song;  
Sang away and danced some too,  
Playing on the gong.

Even when you passed away,  
Teaching love you were;  
Made us work and toil and cry,  
All of us concur.  
Conquered me you’ve somehow done,  
Give my life to you;  
Nonsense gone—surrender now,  
Chant your glories, too.

I’m so small and you’re so great,  
Mercy please do give;  
Don’t forget me though so small,  
That is why I live.  
Other than that I don’t want,  
Take it all away;  
In the end I’ll die somehow,  
Then with you I’ll stay.

We’ll serve your mission boldly—  
Give up ev’rything;  
Chant Lord Kṛṣṇa’s holy name,  
Always we will sing:  
Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa,  
Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare /  
Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma,  
Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

*Your servant eternally,*  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Aarhus

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, forgive me,  
Because I am no poet,  
But I long to take shelter  
At your lotus feet.

Transcendental light in a fallen age,  
Your divine instructions we want to embrace.  
Who could we turn to  
For love and protection?  
Your books and example  
Have shown the direction.  
You brought the greatest benediction to man:  
You brought the nectar of *saṅkīrtan.*

Your lotus feet traversing the surface of earth,  
Your purpose that the glories of the Lord may be heard,  
An ocean of mercy  
On misguided youth  
By your grace receiving  
The Absolute Truth.  
You taught us to chant the Lord’s holy name;  
You taught us we only have ourselves to blame.

Kindness even to the insignificant,  
Compassion for even the smallest ant,  
Endowed with all riches,  
Bestowed with success,  
Yet humbly removing  
All others’ distress,  
You were tolerant, patient, and actually free,  
You were chanting the name of the Lord constantly.

As Lord Caitanya’s dearmost tool,  
You descended to free every rascal and fool.  
Your last instruction:  
That we work together  
And don’t fight with each other  
About who is better.  
You were writing and printing, expanding your press  
Just to turn every man toward Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
We are fallen indeed.  
Help us, please,  
To cling on to your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!

Your grandchildren in Aarhus, Denmark.

## Adelaide

Our most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances. All glories to your divine appearance day!

For many years, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there have been different types of difficulties obstructing the preaching in Adelaide. Now, by Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s desire, the arrangement has been made for a new team of devotees to take up the task of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this city.

Our realization in taking up this service is that we are simply dependent upon the mercy of Guru and Gaurāṅga to be successful. It is your desire that this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement be spread here, and you have entrusted us with this sacred service. But we realize that because of our desire to enjoy sense gratification we are once again obstructing the preaching mission.

Therefore we pray for your help, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please give us the strength to purify our hearts of lust so that our service to you may be unobstructed. Please help us purify our hearts so that those who come in contact with us will be able to find genuine spiritual shelter and guidance. Please help us serve our worshipable Deities, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, with deep devotion so that They will be pleased to shower Their mercy upon all of Their servants here. And please help us create within the temple a mood so sweet and transcendental that those who visit will be attracted to Kṛṣṇa.
