Your mercy is all that we are made of, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please help us, who are weak of heart, to fulfill your desire.

Your servants in Adelaide, Australia.  
(written by Keśava dāsa)

## Agartala

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We are incapable of properly glorifying you. It is stated that by the mercy of the spiritual master a dumb man can deliver eloquent speeches. Our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us so that someday we can appropriately glorify you at every moment for all of eternity by our every thought, word, and deed.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how indebted the people in the future will feel because of your endless endeavor to save them from the hellish condition! Facing great risk, you never hesitated to try to save them from the hallucination of material life and guide them to the Absolute Truth, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the genuine messenger of Lord Gaurāṅga, and you are fulfilling His desire that in every town and village the holy name—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare—will be chanted and the sincere souls will be merged into the ocean of bliss and transcendental happiness.

Under the guidance of our most beloved spiritual master, His Holiness Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami, our wholehearted energy is being used to present your ideals and teachings through preaching, book distribution, and establishing educational institutions. In these institutions, education for the realization of the purpose of the human form of life is being inculcated.

Your insignificant servants in Agartala, India.  
(written by Dīnabandhu dāsa)

## Ahmedabad

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet, which purify the three worlds!

*Śrī Caitanya-bhāgavata* says, “When a devotee of Kṛṣṇa dances in glorification of the Lord, he purifies the earth by his feet, the directions by his glance, and the heavens by his two raised arms.”

We cannot estimate the glory of that wonderful home of Śrī Gour Mohan De and mother Rajani in which you appeared a hundred years ago. A pure devotee is a resident of Goloka Vṛndāvana; therefore the place where he descends must be like Vṛndāvana. The *Bhāgavatam* says,

*bhavad-vidhā bhāgavatās tīrtha-bhūtāḥ svayaṁ vibho*  
*tīrthī-kurvanti tīrthāni svāntaḥ-sthena gadābhṛtā*

**“My lord, devotees like your good self are verily holy places personified. Because you carry the Personality of Godhead within your heart, you turn all places into places of pilgrimage” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.13.10).**

Just as Lord Caitanya, who is Kṛṣṇa Himself, disguised Himself in His youth first as a naughty boy and then as an arrogant scholar, so you, a *nitya-siddha* sent by Kṛṣṇa from Goloka Vṛndāvana, appeared in order to become involved in school and college and then in India’s nationalist movement.

It was in Gaya, which Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu visited as a householder and where He heard the glories of Pāda-padma Tīrtha (the lotus feet of Lord Viṣṇu), that the tears of ecstatic love began to flow from His eyes—tears that never stopped for the rest of his life. By the will of the Lord, His Gurudeva, Śrī Īśvara Purī, also visited Gaya at that time. The Lord requested him to initiate Him. Both of them were absorbed in loving ecstasy. The Lord cooked for Śrī Īśvara Purī and fed him to His heart’s content. This humble event was the beginning of a continuous display of the topmost symptoms of love of God by Lord Caitanya—symptoms the world cannot even imagine, what to speak of experience! In the same way, your meeting with your Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, was the auspicious beginning of the much bigger round of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, which is destined to flood the whole world with the holy names of the Lord.

Your staunch faith, your determination, your austerities, your humility, and your transparency to Kṛṣṇa’s will enabled you to carry out the mission of the Lord wherever you went, just as King Bhagīratha brought mother Gaṅgā wherever he traveled. This *saṅkīrtana* Gaṅgā is even more glorious than the actual Gaṅgā, because it is ever-increasing and it has spread all over the world.

The more one thinks of your greatness, the more one finds oneself helpless. Who can estimate your glories? Any intelligent man can see that the impossible has been achieved. Of all services rendered to the Lord, preaching His glories is the highest. As you said, “Preaching is the essence of spiritual life. Preaching is also the highest welfare work for the human society.” There are various ways to preach. The best way is to distribute your transcendental books. You impressed the biggest scholars of the world and baffled them with the strict personalist concept of God. The whole Western world is full of impersonalism and voidism. Even when they have a personal concept of God, it is generally embraced in order to exploit God rather than serve Him. Without service, one cannot appreciate the personal beauty, personal sweetness, personal mercy, or personal greatness of God. Very graciously and kindly but firmly you delivered them—*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*.

In personal dealings you are very, very liberal, but in philosophy “no nonsense” is your trademark. All your activities were simply meant to preach, to transform the heart. You were never too ill, never too tired to preaching like a lion. All your sincere followers consider their lives successful if they can be touched by even the slightest bit of your preaching spirit. You devised many tricks to attract the conditioned souls to Kṛṣṇa. In India you built beautiful temples in Vṛndāvana, Bombay, Hyderabad, and Māyāpur.

Following in your footsteps, on Rāma Navamī day your servants in Ahmedabad offered to your lotus feet a very, very beautiful temple, Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Dhāma. Over five hundred devotees attended the opening, which featured a three-day *yajña,* a gorgeous *śobha-yātrā,* an astounding *abhiṣeka* by thousands of people with *sahasra-tīrtha-jala,* and a week-long musical program by India’s leading artists. The celebration simply took over all of Ahmedabad city. Over two hundred thousand people visited the temple over a ten-day period, and some of them cried to see the beautiful forms of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindajī, Śrī Nāthjī, Śrī Śrī Sītā-Rāma, Lakṣmaṇa, and Hanumān, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Your Divine Grace, and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. This was our meditation for the last four years. This is the first of the three major temple offerings to you in connection with your Centennial. Stalwart ISKCON leaders like Rādhānātha Mahārāja and Lokanāth Mahārāja described the temple and the Deities as simply fabulous and breathtaking. The energy and vibrations that everyone felt at the festival made everyone feel your presence, Śrīla Prabhupāda! It was the first time after your disappearance that eleven Deities were installed on one occasion. Your Godbrother Śrīla B.V. Purī Mahārāja kindly came and with tears in his eyes blessed us, saying, “Your services have been accepted by Kṛṣṇa and your Guru Mahārāja. Kṛṣṇa has come here to stay. Before I came here I thought it must be some ordinary, simple temple. I never thought it would be so big, so nice. It is great and wonderful!” All the newspapers covered the opening on the front page four to five times.

Of course, this is the beginning. We must use this facility to glorify the holy name, fame, and pastimes of the Lord, and to impart the pure spiritual knowledge you have so kindly delivered in your books. We hope that next year we can offer you a nice museum like the one in Los Angeles and a wonderful multimedia theater. We pray to you on this auspicious day to imbibe a fraction of your preaching spirit, your humility, your simplicity, and your mercy for all the conditioned souls.

Hoping to remain eternally at your lotus feet,  
Your servants in Ahmedabad, India.

## Amsterdam

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the saffron dust particles of your lotus feet.

The devotees in Amsterdam are so much enlivened by your presence in your *mūrti* form here on our tiny altar. You sit regally yet humbly, accepting our daily offerings of prayers and obeisances. It is only your mercy that allows us to survive, and even thrive, in this hell-hole of Kali-yuga.

We remember you quoting your Guru Mahārāja that in the big cities there must be an institution for the narration of *hari-kathā*.

Certainly there is every likelihood that without your mercy many of us would have simply lived (and died) in an abominable condition in this city of Amsterdam. But now we have only one good reason for being here, and that is to somehow be instrumental in serving you in your indefatigable desire to fulfill your spiritual master’s mission. You yourself have single-handedly done this “in grand style,” yet you allow us to try to utilize this human form of life (to which you actually elevated us) in your service. As we experience ourselves surfacing from the ocean depths of our passionate and ignorant conditioning and getting a glimpse of the priceless treasures toward which you are guiding us, we contemplate our good fortune again and again. (Someday we will experience Kṛṣṇa’s cavorting with His friends and Mother Yaśodā’s loving dealings with her darling son on His return from His cowherding, or, instead of being harassed and pinched by mundane sex desire, we’ll beg to be allowed to do something—anything—to enhance the joyous union of the Divine Couple.)

Why us? We don’t know, and we will not waste time speculating on this point. We are so grateful, however, that we will simply endeavor to develop the determination to do the impossible—to uproot the endless *anarthas* that are standing between us and this sublime, ultimate goal. We will begin to uproot these *anarthas* when we can properly appreciate and honor one another as your sincere and devoted followers. This is indeed the test of our love for you. We experience this from moment to moment, and it is the barometer and mainstay of our individual and collective offering of service unto your lotus feet.

We are already so pleased that we get to perform the *tapo divyam* of rising early to be with the Lord and His devotees—to glorify you first thing in the morning, to worship Śrīmatī Tulasī-devī, and to chant the sacred *hari-nāma;* then more glorification of Your Divine Grace and your worshipable Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi; then hearing from your faithful followers the nectar they have excavated from the gold mine of your books; and then of course honoring the remnants of food offered to the Deities. We are your pampered family members and dependents. But this pampering evokes the desire within us to reciprocate by acquiring a greater capacity for *tapo divyam,* to become better instruments for tasting and spreading the glories of devotional service in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

“There is undoubtedly trouble in executing penance. But the trouble in executing *bhakti-yoga* is transcendental pleasure from the very beginning.” \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.9.9, purport\]

We try to reciprocate with your endless flow of mercy by endeavoring to qualify ourselves to absorb more of it. We want the nectar of your Bhaktivedanta purports to be more vital to us than food or air. That is the level we aspire for. If we can achieve this, we will, as you promise in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* become eligible to experience Lord Kṛṣṇa within the sphere of our present activities. O Śrīla Prabhupāda! What treasures you are delivering! Even the most unqualified among us feel impelled to somehow or other make them available to those who are not yet aware of them. Amazingly enough, our lack of qualification is our qualification—it’s all too good to be true, but indeed it is the only real truth!

As Śrīmatī Rukmiṇī-devī, in her historic conversation with her beloved Lord, aspired to join the rank and file of transcendental beggars, we also aspire to join them. Meanwhile we intend to spend our fleeting existence in these tottering material forms serving Your Divine Grace. Then only can the mercy of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His associates and descendants be available to us. Without you we have no hope and no scope for even desiring, and what to speak of attaining, such mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda, our life is for you.

Your servants in Amsterdam, The Netherlands.

## Austin

My dear eternal father, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet!

It’s been a long time since I’ve written you. Before you departed in 1977 we exchanged letters many times. The letters that I received from you are my most valuable treasures. Even though I do not expect a letter back from this letter, I am confident that you are hearing me. I’m sure that your reciprocation will be just as real and just as potent as it was when I would receive a letter from you in the mail.

I can never thank you enough for accepting me as your disciple. Even though I am highly unqualified, possessing so many bad qualities and so many bad habits, you kindly agreed to accept me as your disciple and have even given me (amazingly enough) brahminical initiation.

I’m always thinking of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and hankering for that day when I can again see you, hear you, speak with you, and serve you in your personal presence as I did previously. I always cherish the memory of that time in Atlanta in 1975 when over a hundred of us were in your divine presence in the temple room. You asked that someone should get a chair for your scientist guest, Dr. Wolf, so he would not have to sit on the floor. So many devotees were wondering where to find a chair, but no one could find one. By the inconceivable mercy of Kṛṣṇa I was the most lucky disciple who was able to spot a chair sitting there unnoticed right behind your *vyāsāsana*.

I could not believe my inconceivable good fortune! Swimming in an ocean of ecstasy, I then presented this chair to your guest for your pleasure. When again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, will I be in your personal presence and please you by devotional service?

Even though I know you are with me right now, watching over me like a guardian angel at every moment, still my senses are so dull that I cannot perceive your presence. I am foolishly thinking that you are absent, and am crying like a puppy dog who has become separated from his master.

In my initiation letter, written from London on August 12, 1971, you requested me to “make our Austin center a grand success.” Now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, after twenty-six years of hankering to be able to carry out this order, by your mercy and the mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas I am situated in Austin with a small ISKCON center two blocks from the University of Texas campus. We are ecstatically worshiping Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara, caring for Śrīmatī Tulasī-devī, and preaching.

We are fully confident that by your mercy Lord Caitanya’s movement will continue to expand more and more here. Because we are exclusively dedicating ourselves to pleasing you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are fully confident that even though we are deficient in so many ways, Kṛṣṇa will preserve what we have and carry what we lack. It is said, “Along with the order of the spiritual master comes the potency to successfully carry out his order.” We know that whatever facility we need to be able to make the Austin center a grand success will be granted by your mercy.

On February 4, 1972, you wrote me a letter from Bombay stating that if I liked you would come to Austin. Even though, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I was not able to take advantage of your offer at that time, we are hoping that your offer still stands. Of course, now that you are physically departed from this plane, we do not expect you to come here physically. So our humble request to you is that you fully manifest your divine presence here so that we may feel fully connected with you at every moment. We’re hoping and praying that this pain of being separated from you can give way to the bliss of always being in your divine presence by your divine mercy.

Your humble servants in Austin, Texas, USA.  
(written by Saṅkarṣaṇa dāsa Adhikārī)

## Baltimore

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. It’s been over twenty years since you were inspired by a picture of Nitāi-Gaurasundara to come to Baltimore, even though that journey wasn’t part of your itinerary. Your visit increased the potency of the Baltimore *yatra* and brought everlasting fame to the beauty of our Deities.

Many things have changed in your ISKCON since that visit. From a material viewpoint it appears that things have diminished in Baltimore, that the preaching has waned, and that ideological splits and fault-finding among your disciples threaten further discord. You foretold of these events when you spoke of the passing of the *ācārya*. Chaos would follow, you said. This we have seen, as we still live in the remains of what once was. Many of us were wounded in the transition from your physical departure to current times and bear the emotional scars. Fortunately, time is a great healer, bringing growth and maturity, and the power of the holy name and sincere prayer creates wonders in the heart.

The most difficult thing is to not be stuck in complaining and criticizing but instead to focus on developing a shared vision of what can be. We need this positive vision desperately. As the saying goes, “Without vision, the people perish.” If the vision is to come, it must be inspired by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and shared by the many—consensus is the way for the future. Though the spiritual basics of *sādhana* remain the same, the strategy of joining together and creating unity needs to be adapted to meet the times. Unconditional love, tender loving care, compassion, empathy, encouragement, honesty, enduring friendships, forgiveness, healing, and a nonjudgmental attitude are needed in abundance. If problems are really opportunities, we have a wonderful opportunity to make spiritual advancement and please you through cooperation and preaching.

We have firm faith that the outcome of these current trials and tribulations will prove to be beneficial to our movement and will add to your glory. They will serve to instruct the generations of devotees to follow: they can learn from our mistakes. There is no doubt that Lord Caitanya’s mission will flourish for the next ten thousand years—with or without us. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us be instruments!

As your servants in Baltimore, we all pray that you will inspire us to render pleasing, favorable service and to assist you in carrying out your mission. We pray to be able to maintain and increase our life-giving *sādhana* so as to continue to become purified from our material conceptions of life. We pray to give up the fault-finding mentality, to see the good in every devotee, to strive for unity in diversity, and to really appreciate the wonder and rarity of devotees. As an offering to you, let us work together to benefit everyone through preaching and true community! Please save us from creating offenses to advanced devotees—or to any devotee or sincere spiritual aspirant, and free us from the “us versus them” mentality.

We pray to be able to become your pure instruments so we may become touchstones of love of God, thus transforming our consciousness and that of those trapped in the dark and foreboding Kali-yuga. Let us purely love Kṛṣṇa and see everything in relationship to Him. Let those attachments that currently linger in our hearts and prevent us from tasting pure love of God be dissolved by the fire of devotional service. We also pray to remain faithful to you and to the ISKCON you envisioned, and to not become bewildered.

We all express our gratitude to you for giving us this most precious gem of devotional service and the knowledge that can take us out of the material world. Thank you for inspiring us that “*impossible* is a word in a fool’s dictionary.” Truly, everything is possible with the combined mercy and help of Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga. Please excuse our offenses and lead us on the right path!

Your loving servants in Baltimore, Maryland, USA.  
(written by Karṇāmṛta dāsa and Arcana-siddhi-devī dāsī)

## Baroda

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We fall down at your feet in humility and respect. We acknowledge the fact that your mercy is our life and soul. You have so kindly picked us up from the ocean of suffering—repeated birth and death in the material world—and benedicted us with the movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

*Paraṁ vijayate śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam!*

On this, the annual observance of the auspicious occasion of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, we pray that you might be pleased with our humble offering of the newly constructed *mandira* of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara here in Baroda, Gujarat.

It has been a long struggle to get something ready here: lack of both men and money have been the obstacles. Now the new dawn of expanded preaching seems to be appearing over the horizon here.

The grand structure is ready. We humbly offer it to your lotus feet. We rededicate ourselves to your sacred mission of saving the suffering fools—*jīvātmās* illusioned by *māyā,* thinking that now they have become economically advanced and therefore happy—from the reality of *punarapi janaman punarapi maraṇam*, repeated birth and death in the material world in any one of the eighty-four lakh species of life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā, we beg for your mercy—the mercy to continue to push on your mission and to not be allured by the bogus philosophies that are showbottle: they look like medicine, but THEY DO NOT WORK. Those are the philosophies that contradict your teachings. They contradict Vaiṣṇava traditions. They pander to sentimentalists. They divert the attention of the foolish from your direct instructions. It happened in the past in your presence. It is happening again and will continue to happen in the future. It is actually Māyā’s trick to lead us away from your direct instructions and the path of *sanātana-dharma*. Kindly bestow upon us the requisite intelligence to reject the bogus and stick to the right path back home, back to godhead, which you pointed out as clear as day again and again in your books and your discussions with us, your disciples.

Kindly bless all of your followers so they will be able to realize and practice the essential teachings of *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā,* as explained by Lord Kṛṣṇa in the following verse, and in this way to become peaceful:

*vihāya kāmān yaḥ sarvān pumāṁś carati niḥspṛhaḥ*  
*nirmamo nirahaṅkāraḥ sa śāntim adhigacchati*

**“A person who has given up all desires for sense gratification, who lives free from desires, who has given up all sense of proprietorship and is devoid of false ego—he alone can attain real peace” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **2.71).**

Your humble servants in Baroda, India.

## Belfast

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Kṛṣṇa. Please accept our humble obeisances.

You may be pleased to know that you have devoted followers here in Belfast, Northern Ireland. We are working to spread the glories of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu on your divine order.

There has been so much political violence here over the centuries in the name of religion that the people have become cynical, angry, and confused. The mundane government recently pledged to bring peace to the people of Northern Ireland. They instigated a cease-fire and a peace process. For a short time it was successful. Now again there are many soldiers on the streets and people live in constant fear of violence and disruption of everyday life.

From your teachings in *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* we understand that worship of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the prime entity, is the only true peace process. You state in *Bhagavad-gītā* 5.29, “The greatest peace formula is simply this: Lord Kṛṣṇa is the beneficiary in all human activities.”

To this end we maintain our small temple with its programs and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava Deities. We run Food for Life for the homeless and for university students. We distribute books door-to-door and on the streets, where we also perform *harināma*. There are devotees living as congregational members raising children in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and working in various occupations. There are other preaching programs such as cultivating the Hindu community, *nāma-haṭṭa,* school visits, Vaiṣṇava community care, cooking classes, and vegetable and flower growing.

We are only able to do this by your causeless mercy. We depend on your grace to empower and inspire us to continue this most important work. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, be merciful upon us so we can please you and Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you for all you have already bestowed upon us. You saved us by attracting us to serve you in this revolutionary movement to bring genuine, lasting peace and happiness to us individually, collectively, and worldwide.

May we remain your ever-indebted servants.

The devotees in Belfast, Northern Ireland.

## Berkeley

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet, the footprints of which are our guides at every step of life, and beyond this life to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa, to whom you are most dear.

You write in your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 4.1: “Human life is meant for cultivation of spiritual knowledge, in eternal relationship with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and the executive heads of all states and all planets are obliged to impart this lesson to the citizens by *education, culture, and devotion*.” \[italics ours\].

What do you understand by these three means of imparting “this lesson”?

*Education:* We know that you speak and write repeatedly about the need for *training* in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, by example and precept. This is to happen, we can understand from your teachings, by individuals for other individuals; but although this may take place randomly, it would be better if it were organized. Therefore you established the International Society for Krishna Consciousness as a place where people can come and receive *training* in the cultivation of spiritual knowledge. You saw to the development of *gurukulas,* you spoke of developing *varṇāśrama* colleges, university courses, GTU affiliation. People spend fortunes for training in all sorts of skills. Can we, following your example, offer them the training they need most?

*Culture:* In a lecture in Māyāpura you hint at what the nature of “culture” is, referring to a point you made to a student here in Berkeley:

“When I was speaking in Berkeley University sometime in the year 1966, one Indian student stood up and he said, ‘Swamiji, what this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will do? We require now technology.’ So I replied, ‘Yes. You are after technology. So you are a beggar. I am not a beggar. I have come here to give something. That is the difference*.* I have come here to give some culture, and you have come to imitate the Western civilization by technology. That is the difference. You’ll remain a beggar, I shall remain a giver. That is the difference.’ So still I am maintaining that position of giving, not taking.”

“Giving some culture” suggests a “culture of giving,” for as you say, you “shall remain a giver.”

Thoughtful people all sense the urgency of developing this culture of giving, but they seek in vain for models. Good intentions flourish, but exploitation remains the theme song. Can we, following your sublime example, with proper training, give ourselves freely to impart the lesson?

*Devotion*: You often wrote and said, “*Bhakti,* devotional service, is meant only for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Nārāyaṇa, Viṣṇu, or Kṛṣṇa, not for anyone else.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.29.11–12, purport).

In a conversation with a scholar who much admired you, Stillson Judah, one of your disciples asked about “death-of-God theology,” and you concluded that the difficulty is that no one knows what God is:

**Revatīnandana:** Did they actually seriously think that the Father was dead?

**Dr. Judah:** Yes. (break)

**Prabhupāda:** ...dead, then what kind of God he is?

**Dr. Judah:** Yeah. He wouldn’t be any.

**Prabhupāda:** If the God dies and ordinary animal also dies...

**Dr. Judah:** Yes, it was what they called “atheistic Christianity.” (laughter) But as I say, I do not feel that this is representative of Christianity today. And I think that the very fact that this “death-of-God theology” did become so popular at one particular period is one of the particular reasons why more people have wanted, then have rejected this and have wanted to find some experience of God, find it in their lives to prove, as it were, that He does exist...

**Prabhupāda:** Actually, nobody has got clear idea of God. This is the difficulty. Nobody knows… \[Morning walk, Los Angeles, 25 June 1975\]

So devotion means devotion to the Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Can we, with proper training and with the culture of giving, and by being eagerly devoted to the Personality of Godhead, execute your order to impart this essential lesson to human society?

Let us learn (it is never too late to learn!) how to apply your instructions to realize your vision of an International Society for (imparting the lesson of) Krishna Consciousness. We here in Berkeley—as we have all experienced a renewal of enthusiasm and determination to execute your orders faithfully in recent months—pray to you to continue to bestow upon us your great kindness and to allow this one of your many projects to be realized as you envisioned it, as a great center for education, culture, and devotion.

Your servants in Berkeley, California, USA.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa-kṣetra dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your Centennial Year brought with it a successful outcome to Bhaktivedanta Manor’s campaign to remain open to the public. Even the staunchest atheists had to admit that this indicated your continued influence with the course ISKCON is taking. Now that this cumbersome burden has been lifted, we find that there is a new pressure that we are carrying—the pressure of your great expectations for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the UK. Bhaktivedanta Manor has a pivotal role to play in the fulfillment of your mission. We are feeling excited about the future yet also a little daunted by the magnitude of the task lying ahead of us.

But we take hope from the fact that we are somehow connected to you—as your children and grandchildren. You have shown us that things that appear impossible for the common man are in fact do-able for one who has full faith in the order of the spiritual master.

My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, You are so kind upon this useless soul, but I do not know why You have brought me here. Now You can do whatever You like with me. But I guess You have some business here; otherwise why would You bring me to this terrible place? \[*Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma*\]

It is your kindness that we have inherited this mission from you—spreading the glories of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda throughout the UK. Otherwise, who would want to stay in this cold hell?

Most of the population here is covered by the material modes of ignorance and passion. Absorbed in material life, they think themselves very happy and satisfied, and therefore they have no taste for the transcendental message of Vāsudeva. I do not know how they will be able to understand it. But I know Your causeless mercy can make everything possible, because You are the most expert mystic. How will they understand the mellows of devotional service? O Lord, I am simply praying for Your mercy so that I will be able to convince them about Your message.

Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we need your mercy in order to properly understand the message of Godhead and communicate it in a way that others can understand.

All living entities have become under the control of the illusory energy by Your will, and therefore, if You like, by Your will they can also be released from the clutches of illusion. I wish that You may deliver them. Therefore if You so desire their deliverance, then only will they be able to understand Your message. The words of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* are Your incarnation, and if a sober person repeatedly receives them with submissive aural reception, then he will be able to understand Your message.…How will I make them understand this message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness? I am very unfortunate, unqualified, and the most fallen. Therefore I am seeking Your benediction so that I can convince them, for I am powerless to do so on my own.

Somehow or other, O Lord, You have brought me here to speak about You. Now, my Lord, it is up to You to make me a success or failure as You like.

The success of Bhaktivedanta Manor depends upon your blessings. We can only endeavor to satisfy you by strictly following your directions: practicing the principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness personally and working hard to give them to the general populace.

O spiritual master of all the worlds! I can simply repeat Your message, so if You like You can make my power of speaking suitable for their understanding. Only by Your causeless mercy will my words become pure. I am sure that when this transcendental message penetrates their hearts they will certainly feel engladdened and thus become liberated from all unhappy conditions of life. O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands. So if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance. O Lord, make me dance as You like. I have no devotion, nor do I have any knowledge, but I have strong faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. I have been designated as Bhaktivedanta, and now, if You like, You can fulfill the real purport of Bhaktivedanta.

How inspiring these words of yours are! You felt yourself incapable of the task lying ahead of you. Yet you gave yourself wholly to it, simply depending upon Kṛṣṇa for its successful execution. And Kṛṣṇa reciprocated so wonderfully with you.

As your children and grandchildren, we have inherited this daunting mission. We pray that we can also inherit some of your wonderful attributes so as to be fit to receive Kṛṣṇa’s mercy—*yathā bīja, yathā yoni*. Without Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, how will it be possible to fulfill your great expectations for the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement in the UK? We have the seed of Kṛṣṇa consciousness within us—received from you. But we also have so many other influences—social conditioning bringing misconceptions and worldly desires, past *karma,* and *kāma-bījas*. Through following the process you have outlined, we can nourish the devotional attributes received from you and extinguish the unnatural influences.

*kṛpālu, akṛta-droha, satya-sāra sama*  
*nidoṣa, vadānya, mṛdu, śuci, akiñcana*  
*sarvopakāraka, śānta, kṛṣṇaika-śaraṇa*  
*akāma, anīha, sthira, vijita-ṣaḍ-guṇa*  
*mita-bhuk, apramatta, mānada, amānī*  
*gambhīra, karuṇa, maitra, kavi, dakṣa, maunī*

**“Devotees are always merciful, humble, truthful, equal to all, faultless, magnanimous, mild, and clean. They are without material possessions, and they perform welfare work for everyone. They are peaceful, surrendered to Kṛṣṇa, and desireless. They are indifferent to material acquisitions and are fixed in devotional service. They completely control the six bad qualities—lust, anger, greed, and so forth. They eat only as much as required, and they are not inebriated. They are respectful, grave, compassionate, and without false prestige. They are friendly, poetic, expert, and silent” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **22.78–80).**

Our ability to genuinely serve you will come only as a consequence of truly following your example. Thank you for providing such a wonderful example, for explaining things to us so fully, for making such great personal sacrifices so as to give us the chance to take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Without your intervention in our lives, we would not have had the opportunity to live full and happy lives, nor would we have any true purpose to live for. Because of you we have the chance to go back to Godhead. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please continue to supervise and correct us as we endeavor to further develop Bhaktivedanta Manor in order to please you.

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor, UK.

## Bhaktivedanta Village

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

We would like to express some words of appreciation on this auspicious anniversary of your appearance in this world.

We are very grateful for the outstanding example you have set during your whole life.

When you were just a small boy, you started your own Ratha-yātrā festival and distributed *prasādam* to your neighbors.

Later on, when your Vaiṣṇava father forbade you to choose your own wife, you humbly surrendered to his desire.

You took initiation from His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura and completed your household duties. As a *gṛhastha* you were always serving the preaching centers in India and thinking how to please your Guru Mahārāja and Kṛṣṇa. You wrote many Kṛṣṇa conscious articles in English, even though it was not your native language, just to fulfill your spiritual master’s desire. You distributed your devotional magazines in India at this time and started a small society of devotees.

You had to struggle so hard during your life with your family, with neophyte disciples, with civil authorities who always tried to put hindrances on your path, and with envious Godbrothers. Always fresh and enlivened, you struggled through all these obstacles and were always victorious because the Supreme Lord Himself was steering your boat, just as He guided Arjuna on the battlefield. As Lord Kṛṣṇa says at the end of *Bhagavad-gītā, yatra yogeśvaraḥ kṛṣṇa…:*

“Wherever there is Kṛṣṇa, the master of all mystics, and wherever there is Arjuna, the Supreme archer, there will also certainly be opulence, victory, extraordinary power, and morality. That is my opinion.”

Prabhupāda, at times in the early days you would cook for everybody, and later on you managed hundreds of centers all over the world, installed Deities and inspired disciples to maintain nice standards of worship. You did the accounting for your Society, preached to thousands of great personalities of this world, and trained hundreds of disciples. You taught them how to follow in your footsteps in a humble and surrendered way.

Lord Kṛṣṇa always sent you nice boys and girls to assist you in the correcting, editing, printing, publishing, and distribution of your books. This book distribution was always your main concern, and you wanted us to overflood our Western cities with your transcendental literature, without considering the time, the place, or who was qualified or unfit to receive this treasure of *kṛṣṇa-prema.*

Fulfilling Lord Caitanya’s desire, your mission is doing the most magnanimous welfare work for humanity at large.

We are happy to inform you that we are building a nice school with six rooms here at Bhaktivedanta Village in order to train children according to your instructions. We have also opened a small restaurant this year in the town nearby, and nice preaching is going on there.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us so that we can become pure disciples and assist you efficiently in your transcendental service to Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gauracandra.

At your lotus feet we humbly bow down.

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Village in Dole, France.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa-kīrtana-devī dāsī)

## Boise

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**To Honor Your Divine Grace**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, 1996 was the year of Your Centennial.  
We welcome the year at the Rockies cathedral.  
We resolved to read your pastimes, do *harināma,*  
And feed *prasādam*  
And distribute books—1,008 *Gītās As It Is*.  
But one desire remains:  
How to get Boiseans  
To honor Your Divine Grace?

We have no intelligence,  
But Kṛṣṇa proclaims,  
“I preserve what My devotee has  
And provide what he lacks.”  
Deep in our insides He guides—  
“Every Wednesday hold Prabhupāda nights.”  
The very first night,  
To everyone s delight,  
He sends your favorites:  
Hippies and the disenfranchised.  
But we worry—  
It is January,  
And how to get Boiseans  
To honor Your Divine Grace?

Kṛṣṇa drops another hint:  
“Remember what I said in the Kuru Valley?  
Let it be heard again in Treasure Valley!”  
Ayush, only eight,  
Takes the challenge great:  
He recites *Gītā’s* top one hundred and eight.  
And that is just great;  
*The Song of God*  
Strikes in every heart a chord,  
And from then on there is no problem  
How to get Boiseans  
To honor Your Divine Grace.

Ravi Iyer is enthusiastic:  
“Won’t it be fantastic!”  
*Harināma dān:*  
The congregation pledges then and there  
150,000 names—*that* no one can ignore.  
It is heard across the town:  
“We won’t put our pencils down!”  
The kids promise not to play any games  
Till they write 150,000 names  
And present them to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Baṅkibihārī.  
On June ninth, World Holy Name Day,  
On behalf of Abhay Charan De,  
It is a silent miracle,  
And from then on there is no problem  
How to get Boiseans  
To honor Your Divine Grace.

The potency of the Holy Name is working.  
Vishnu is born in April, so quiet and calm;  
You can tell he appreciates the charm  
of *harināma.*  
Two other souls become lucky:  
Sudhir and Sulekha become Śrīhari and Śyāma-sakhī. They pledge on May 29  
To recite  
27,648 names each day  
For the rest of their lives.  
Then one day Ravi G. comes home excited:  
“VPACS is official!”  
Suddenly it becomes quite clear:  
Now we can distribute from sunrise to sundown.  
It is another way to get Boiseans  
To honor Your Divine Grace.

Twenty-eight hours of chanting on World Holy Name Day!  
Who can forget the roller coaster tunes’ sway?  
Supplemented by interfaith, hymns, and praise.  
Lest we forget,  
That night is lit  
With an unusual glow of the Holy Names;  
Two hundred thousand is the claim,  
Presented to you by children and adults alike.  
To indicate your acceptance, you send a messenger on bike  
Who comes in the middle of night,  
Takes *prasādam* and disappears out of sight.  
You send us on Ratha-yātrās, especially to New York,  
so Gopal can pick up *The Advent of Kṛṣṇa.*  
Little do we know,  
That 90-minute audio  
Will get Boiseans  
To honor Your Divine Grace.

Chandu, Chintu, Radha, Revati,  
Maria, Mark, Parjanya, Ashley,  
Varsha, Shatakshi, Ayush, Śrīhari,  
Ravi, Gopal, Jini, and Sunny,  
and who can forget little Vishnu?  
The entire second generation on Janmāṣṭamī  
Wants to act in “The Birth of the Lord in Bhārata-bhūmi.”  
We remember, as you sit surrounded by Boiseans in the open garage,  
Listening to Devakī’s pleas and Kaṁsa’s barrage.  
It is a sure bet  
The play had a lasting effect.  
To our surprise the audience proclaims,  
“This play deserves more than a garage.  
Bhaktivedanta Youth Theater deserves a theater.”  
And they reserve the MK center  
To honor Your Divine Grace.

The next day,  
On your appearance day,  
You come for your Vyāsa-pūjā  
To bless us in your *arcā-vigraha.*  
You arrange the *lakṣmī* through Sharad from Male,  
And the *vyāsāsana* through Rasika Shekhare.  
Eleven *kalaśas* of holy waters  
We gather to bathe you, and so do 1,008 holy *tīrthas*.  
Now there is no stopping Boiseans  
From honoring Your Divine Grace.

The events so far have been around the temple,  
But Lord Jagannātha wants others in the city to sample.  
So He arranges His appearance  
In BSU Homecoming Parade’s decadence.  
Through *harināma* and hundreds of packets of *prasādam*  
Comes a taste of Kṛṣṇa’s kingdom.  
We know you are there along with the Lord;  
Otherwise, who would give us the president’s award  
And hold off Indraji and the cloud  
Until after we are inside the temple, safe and sound,  
To honor Your Divine Grace?

A few days later,  
in the Special Events Center,  
350 Boiseans stop  
To participate in honoring Your Divine Grace.  
You adorn the center stage  
As Gaṇapati Swami amazes  
The audience with “Jaya Prabhupāda!”  
The young and aged alike express their gratitude  
To honor Your Divine Grace.

Leela and Vishnu Tattva fly in from LA  
To perform Vedic ballet.  
They glorify Lord Jagannātha and you with dance  
As the entire audience goes into a spiritual trance.  
Thanks to all the *mātājīs,*  
Everyone gets a packet or two of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy.  
And then “The Advent of Lord Kṛṣṇa”—*kṛṣṇa-kathā* supreme—  
Once again enacted by the Bhaktivedanta Youth team.  
Oh, what a sight to see!  
350 Boiseans clap  
To honor Your Divine Grace.

An event a month—we have just finished October.  
There is more in November.  
The congregation resolves enthusiastically  
To celebrate Feed the World Day quite frantically.  
“There is more joy in giving  
than receiving.”  
They decree a thousand plates of *prasādam*  
To be distributed to the rich and poor  
Without discrimination: the kids, parents, single and married—  
All pitch in day and night  
With all their might.  
On the day itself, all the roads are closed;  
“How will we cross the police line?”  
Inquires a worried Darlene.  
“Don’t worry,”  
says Ram Gopal as he loads the pickups with Śrī Hari.  
A caravan of three—  
truck, car, and van—  
leaves Govinda’s in downtown and drives to the police line.  
Darlene looks anxiously  
As a cop appears instantly,  
And a miracle happens, for rather than stopping the devotee,  
He stops the float casually  
and lets Kṛṣṇa’s cuisine pass freely.  
It is a day that turns nonbelievers  
into believers.  
Those who have not cooked before now cook.  
Those who have not preached before preach.  
Those who have not lifted anything before lift.  
Those who have not chanted before chant.  
And through the media, those who have not heard before hear.  
All this and more  
To honor Your Divine Grace.

Another event happens in November to keep in our memory.  
We think it is your mercy:  
Little Partha follows Vishnu,  
And so do *Bhāgavatams* and *Bhagavad-gītās,*  
Into the homes of Boiseans  
To honor Your Divine Grace.

On World Enlightenment Day  
we distribute what is dearest to your heart—your books.  
You are guiding us,  
We again feel,  
For how else can it be that  
A Mr. Ahmed, who has never thought about Kṛṣṇa,  
Gets within a couple of hours your book titled *Kṛṣṇa*  
To honor Your Divine Grace?

Almost the same way as we started, it all ends quite solemnly,  
Reciting *The Song of God*—*Bhagavad-gītā*—in its entirety.  
We know you have made a mark in our stonelike hearts  
When the Boiseans start  
To honor Your Divine Grace.

Even though this was your year,  
You gave us something very dear:  
Love of Kṛṣṇa and mercy of a pure devotee,  
And a realization that without service we can get never  
One who lives forever,  
A pure Vaiṣṇava, Your Divine Grace.  
Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

All your servants in Boise, Idaho, USA.  
(written by Ananta-rūpa dāsa)

## Bologna

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at the dust of your feet.

This year I feel very grateful to you for your kind benediction of engaging me in this transcendental wonderful service of *saṅkīrtana*. I am becoming more and more aware of how important and urgent it is to distribute your nectarean books. From the very beginning the temple president, Bhakti-bhūṣaṇa Swami, engaged me in this miraculous service. Meditation on your qualities is the perfection of all desires.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.2.16) there is a nice verse beginning *śuśrūṣoḥ śraddadhānasya:* “O twice-born sages, by serving those devotees who are completely freed from all vice, great service is done. By such service, one gains affinity for hearing the messages of Vāsudeva.” At the moment, this is the profound meditation that helps me continue this service of *saṅkīrtana*. You gave a personal instruction to Vaiśeṣika Prabhu when he had just joined the movement. After the temple president praised his enthusiasm for distributing your books, you told him, “You must also read them.” That’s the way to become enthusiastic to distribute your books. Some days back I was so afraid to go out, but just by reading half a page of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* I became so ecstatic that I got the strength to dispel the illusion that I would be miserable by going out.

Your books are very, very powerful. The only desire I have deep in my heart is to understand the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness; that transcendental knowledge will help me stay in ISKCON and continue distributing your transcendental books. Without your transcendental movement, I feel vacant. Better to die than to tolerate separation from your pure disciples. All glories to your transcendental divine appearance!

Your devotees in Bologna, Italy.  
(written by Durgama dāsa)

## Brisbane

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Even after more than twenty years since your departure to the spiritual world, many of your dedicated followers still write and speak about newer and newer realizations in regard to the specific instructions you gave to them, what to speak of your other unlimited instructions on Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Their appreciation of your glories has deepened and broadened and will continue to do so eternally. Being intimately related with the unlimited Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa, your glories are also unlimited.

Before Your Divine Grace came to the West, Vedic culture was the way of life for some pious Indians only. Out of those followers, most of them had misconceptions about Vedic injunctions, were confused as to who God is, and misunderstood Him as impersonal or thought that Kṛṣṇa is the same as any other demigod. The few who worshiped Lord Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Person and tried to follow His instruction in the *Bhagavad-gītā*—“Surrender unto Me”—didn’t know how.

When you came to the Western world, you benedicted both—Indians as well as Westerners. The Indians who didn’t know how to surrender were blessed with knowledge of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who taught by His personal example how to surrender. Most of these Indians had never heard of Lord Caitanya, the most magnanimous form of Kṛṣṇa. Your presentation of *Teachings of Lord Caitanya* and the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* is an invaluable benediction.

Of course, this doesn’t mean that these and other transcendental literatures were for those pious Indians only. Nor does it mean that *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the *Bhagavad-gītā,* and other books you translated were meant only for those who were unaware of Vedic knowledge. All your contributions, including your writings, spoken instructions, establishments, and letters will remain the eternal guiding principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness for all classes of people. Most of us were completely ignorant about Vedic culture, and you introduced us to the real business of human life—devotional service, beginning with chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. We remain forever indebted to you and can try to express our gratitude only with sincere efforts in preaching your sublime message throughout the world.

Your appearance on this planet is as purifying as the Lord’s, because your purpose in coming is one with His. We have no doubt that for generations to come you will be remembered as a great *ācārya*—an authority on Kṛṣṇa consciousness—just as Madhvācārya and Rāmānujācārya are remembered today. In future millenniums, when Śrīla Vyāsadeva incorporates more transcendental pastimes in his writings, your name will certainly appear among the great personalities and pastimes in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*.

Meanwhile, we, the younger generation, are trying to know you through the lives of your dedicated followers, and we see your miracle at work.

Your servants in Brisbane, Australia.

## Budapest

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga! All glories to your divine appearance!

It seemed for long, long centuries that it was impossible to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Western world because people here are so fallen, so demoniac and godless.

You proved that nothing is impossible: You made numerous Vaiṣṇavas around the world by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. Your preaching extended immeasurably and still extends through your disciples. The waves of your mercy are unlimited and have reached our really fallen and unworthy country also.

By following your example, we want to preach bravely and effectively. This is possible under the direction of our spiritual masters. Our preaching is limited only by our own material contamination. You said that if in our movement everybody were one hundred percent surrendered, pure devotees then we could conquer the world in eighteen days, just as the Pāṇḍavas triumphed over the Kauravas.

Please purify us from all contaminations so we will be better able to serve your mission.

Your servants in Budapest, Hungary.

## Cape Town

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda  
Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

A train rolls into the station situated opposite our preaching center. Upon seeing our temple a passenger loudly chants, “Hare Kṛṣṇa!” The sound vibration of Kṛṣṇa’s holy name enters the confines of my room—and I think of you. I think of how happy you become when others chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. I can almost see the broad smile light up across your face. What to speak of favorably, even if someone chants in a mocking or a joking way you would consider it a great success. In fact you considered this the success of your entire mission. And those who knowingly or unknowingly took part in the *harināma saṅkīrtana* mission would also ultimately be successful in attaining the goal of life. Your deep faith in the holy name and enthusiasm for spreading the holy name make me think of this verse from *Śrīmad Bhāgavatam* (6.2.14):

*sāṅketyaṁ pārihāsyaṁ vā stobhaṁ helanam eva vā*  
*vaikuṇṭha-nāma-grahaṇam aśeṣāgha-haraṁ viduḥ*

**“One who chants the holy name of the Lord is immediately freed from the reactions of unlimited sins, even if he chants indirectly (to indicate something else), jokingly, for musical entertainment, or even negligently. This is accepted by all the learned scholars of the scriptures.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, there are many such persons here in Cape Town who spontaneously chant Hare Kṛṣṇa upon seeing the devotees, the temple, or any other paraphernalia related to your ISKCON movement. You considered ISKCON your body, and we too are part of your ISKCON body, and through us you are making everyone chant and dance. Our position will be glorious as instruments in your hands—“Please make us dance.” Your pleasure is our happiness, and your pleasure is to see all these sinful rascals chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, becoming freed from the endless suffering of material existence. So we wish to increase your pleasure by being servants of the holy name and distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the best of our ability. We are laying the foundation by distributing hundreds and thousands of your books, and we will try to increase until every literate person has a book. And to those who are unable to read we shall feed *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* accompanied by the loud chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*.*

This is your transcendental formula, arranged by your transcendental intelligence, which was given to you by the Supreme Lord. This is how you spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the whole world, and this is how it will continue to spread. Please give us the faith and intelligence to follow you, and thus we will always be with you.

Your humble servants in Cape Town, South Africa.  
(written by Viśvambhara dāsa)

## Chamorshi

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

By the mercy of your Centennial we were able to celebrate many events and enroll many more devotees. We consider that you sent them to help us expand the farm. The immediate task before us is to look after the cows better. We are also working toward self-sufficiency as one of our goals. During the Centennial we produced 25,000 kilograms of rice. This was our largest production ever, and we wish to grow more in future years. We want to organize Food for Life with the surplus grain.

During the Centennial year we started a regular book distribution program. We also organized Centennial festivals in dozens of surrounding villages, including a *padayātrā*. Our efforts must have pleased you. The proof is that you sent so many new devotees.

We beg for your mercy so we will be able to follow your instructions in regard to establishing Kṛṣṇa conscious, self-sufficient communities.

Your servants at the Chamorsi farm community in Maharashtra, India.

## Chicago

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. We pray to always be engaged in their service. Recently our anxiety for serving Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī rose to new heights. We are struggling to maintain a nice standard of regulated service, and this is a very big challenge with the few devotees that are presently engaged.

Recently you sent encouragement specifically related to Deity worship in the form of a transcribed conversation you held with some of your disciples. You instructed them that to accept anxiety for Kṛṣṇa’s service is a way to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You called it “Kṛṣṇa anxiety,” and you used Mother Yaśodā as an example of the perfection of this type of anxiety. You also explained that the rules and regulations in the *śāstra* help us experience this anxiety.

The neophyte might confuse this anxiety with the mundane variety commonly experienced in the material world, but you assured your disciples that such anxiety would lead to the eternal service of Kṛṣṇa.

Our own realization is lacking, and many times we find ourselves exhausted, frustrated, and discouraged by this anxiety. This is due entirely to our mundane vision. On this occasion we are praying that you give us the necessary strength to continue with enthusiasm and determination in the service of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī. We are convinced that this will please you very much.

Your servants at New Sevakuñja, Chicago, Illinois, USA.  
(written by Vāṇīnātha Vasu dāsa)

## Copenhagen

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our must humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

The following has been stated in the Eleventh Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.3.21):

*tasmād guruṁ prapadyeta jijñāsuḥ śreya uttamam*  
*śābde pare ca niṣṇātaṁ brahmaṇy upaśamāśrayam*

In the *Bhakti-sandarbha*, *Anuccheda* 202, Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī has defined the meaning of the words *śābde pare ca niṣṇātam* in the following statement: *śabde brahmaṇi vede tātparya vicāreṇa niṣṇātaṁ prāptaṁ, pare brahmaṇi-bhagavad-ādi-rūpāvirbhāveṣṭu aparokṣānubhāvena*—One who is expert in explaining the purport of the *Vedas,* which are *śabda-brahman,* and who is devoid of all material expectations and requirements should be accepted as a genuine spiritual master. One should take shelter of such *guru* through the process of hearing in order to know the truth regarding *sādhana,* the means, and *sādhyā,* the goal.

An instructor who is a topmost devotee of the Lord (*parama-bhagavad-bhakta*), who is adept in relishing *bhakti-rasa* and evoking spiritual emotion in others (*sārasa*), and who is capable of extracting the essence of anything (*sāragrāhī*), is known as a speaker without attachments, or *nirāga-vaktā*.

*Parama-bhagavad-bhakta*. Your Divine Grace, this is the perfect definition of your divine self, for through your life and your preaching among devotees and ordinary people of the Eastern and Western world, you showed all the qualities of a *parama-bhagavad-bhakta*.

In one of your purports in the *Bhagavad-gītā* you wrote as follows: “So if one quits his body at the end of life chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare, he certainly reaches one of the spiritual planets, according to the mode of his practice” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 8.14, purport).

Therefore Your Divine Grace has given us *The Nectar of Devotion,* which is the summary of the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu,* to learn the mood and practice of devotion to Kṛṣṇa. Who can extract the essence of anything, who is a *sāragrāhī*? You are the perfect *sāragrāhī,* Śrīla Prabhupāda, for you adjusted the formularies of devotional practice and gave us the essence:

The Yamunā and the Ganges are sacred, and sometimes devotees in India insist that the Deity must be worshiped with water of the Ganges or Yamunā. But here we understand *deśa-kāla* to mean “according to time and country.” In the Western countries there is no river Yamunā or Ganges—water from such sacred rivers is not available. Does this mean that the *arcā* worship should for that reason be stopped? No. *Salilaiḥ* refers to any water—whatever is available—but it must be very clear and collected purely. That water can be used. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.8.55, purport\]

You are the ideal speaker without attachment (*nirāga-vaktā*):

When I was alone in Vṛndāvana, I never attempted to construct *maṭhas* or temples; rather, I was fully satisfied with the small amount of money I could gather by selling *Back to Godhead,* and thus I would provide for myself and also print the literature. When I went to foreign countries, I lived according to the same principle, but when Europeans and Americans began to give money profusely, I started temples and Deity worship. The same principle should still be followed. Whatever money is collected should be spent for Kṛṣṇa, and not a farthing for sense gratification. This is the *Bhāgavata* principle. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.13.34, purport\]

**A Prayer to Your Divine Grace**

On this auspicious anniversary of the appearance of Your Divine Grace, allow us to prostrate ourselves at your lotus feet and offer this humble prayer:

Allow us to surrender ourselves at the lotus feet of Your Divine Grace, for this is the only way to distribute the glories of the holy name as you taught us, Your Divine Grace.

You were empowered with Śrī Nityānanda’s grace to teach the world the glories of the holy name because you do not have any motive but to serve the divine name.

You have given us a divine mission, and carrying out this mission is full of bliss if we become fully attached to your divine lotus feet, which give full knowledge and eternal bliss. You are the only means. Let us chant the glories of the divine name.

Let us not fall prey to the material temptations, which are full of pain. Please give us shelter. Please give us Your Divine Grace. Please allow us to chant the glories of Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, as you taught us, Your Divine Grace.

Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa *līlā,* or *rāgānuga-bhakti,* are far away for us, but you are always near to us if we follow the *sādhana* that you have given us, as you tell us in your holy books: “The disciple and spiritual master are never separated because the spiritual master always keeps company with the disciple as long as the disciple follows strictly the instructions of the spiritual master” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.47, purport).

Please accept this humble offering from us, and bless our temple to carry on without deviating from or adulterating your divine instructions. In this way you will always remain near to us.

Your humble servants in Copenhagen, Denmark.  
(written by Rāma Govinda dāsa)

## Cuenca

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances prostrated in the dust of your lotus feet, and also at the feet of your disciples, who are spreading this transcendental dust around the world, expanding your ever-increasing mercy in the lives of the fortunate souls who open their hearts to your message of *bhakti*.

Six years ago when the Centennial campaign started, we were moved to make a humble attempt to increase your preaching mission in South America. We bought a piece of land, and by your unlimited blessings we are now slowly growing as a new transcendental branch of your ISKCON tree. Now we beg for your compassionate glance, so that with your kind supervision this community will grow, with its only purpose to broadcast the glories of the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Always desiring to please you,  
Your granddisciples and eternal servants in Cuenca, Ecuador.

## The Czech Republic

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!To remember, glorify, worship, and serve such a pure devotee as you is the perfection of one’s life. Therefore on this most auspicious day of your appearance we all fall down in the dust of your divine lotus feet and pray:

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In our effort to remember and glorify you, we try to offer you not only flowers, *ārati,* and delicious preparations but also deeds according to your desire. We hear about you from your wonderful disciples, who explain to us that on the spiritual platform you are always present and that you are always protecting ISKCON by your great spiritual power. Your divine instructions are a torchlight for us.

There is no doubt that you are here with us today for this gathering. You are our only hope, and we realize that we are all made of your mercy. Without your great tolerance and mercy we would never be able to obtain Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We can never glorify you enough, because if you had not come none of us would be here today. We owe our lives to you. Therefore we should completely surrender our entire lives to your lotus feet. This will qualify us to satisfy the previous *ācāryas* and Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Nothing more is to be gained than this.

Therefore we all fall down in the dust of your lotus feet and pray to be sincere servants of your faithful servants, who act according to your example.

Day after day we go out distributing your books, and we can see that there is no chance for this crazy world. People are completely bewildered. They are misguided, and at every moment they seek new ways of satisfaction. But we know from you that the only solution to their material problems is a spiritual solution—surrender to Kṛṣṇa.

So therefore we all fall down in the dust of your divine lotus feet and pray to be enthusiastic, determined, tolerant, and patient in our preaching efforts, according to your example.

We are fallen and limited. How can we represent your mission in this society?

We must at least change our characters and start being devotees. If we remain the same, people will not believe us. We learn from you that the real preacher is one who teaches by his example. And we also learn from you what the real Vaiṣṇava is: “He is more satisfied to serve than to enjoy himself.” You said this and proved it by your life.

So therefore we all fall down in the dust of your divine lotus feet and pray to change our hearts according to your example.

What more can we pray for?

Actually, you have already given us all we need.

Better we all fall down in the dust of your divine lotus feet and pray to be able to take all this without speculation, deviation, or selfishness and become fully Kṛṣṇa conscious and give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others, according to your example.

Your devotees in the Czech Republic.  
(written by Adhika-dayā-devī dāsī)

## Dallas

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your service, which pleases the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Following in the footsteps of the Supreme Lord Śrī Nityānanda Prabhu and Śrīla Haridāsa Ṭhākura, immersed in and drinking the nectar of their mercy for the fallen conditioned souls, you came to the West to save us who were suffering greatly due to our forgetfulness of our constitutional position in relation to the Personality of Godhead.

Many years of our lives we have wandered aimlessly, this way and that way, at the commands of our minds and senses, inviting dangers of many varieties to befall us. Existing in full anxiety and lurking in the depths of darkness, we faced destinies sure to bring forth many tribulations. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our savior. You have gathered us out of the ocean of distress and, out of your causeless mercy, given us the weapon of knowledge. By your mercy we can sharpen this sword and defend our souls from the infiltration of the qualities of ignorance, namely attachment, lust, greed, fear, and anger.

Due to your equal vision of seeing everyone as the eternal servant of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you also confidently armed us with the most powerful weapon of *yoga*. You have linked us directly to the all-powerful Personality of Godhead by means of the Lord’s name, form, pastimes, and paraphernalia. You have very kindly instructed us to absorb our minds in Lord Govinda in many ways, thereby offering us the most confidential and highest path, by which we can utilize our minute independence favorably and confidently in order to please your dear Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Indeed, you have given us the greatest treasure, and it would be extremely foolish not to accept this gift of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You know very well that if we develop feelings of love and appreciation for the Lord, the topmost symptom will be that we would vigorously and enthusiastically preach His glories for the welfare of other living beings rotting in the material nature. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us remain assembled at the shelter of your lotus feet, for there is no other haven in the three worlds. Your instructions are available to remind us to keep our minds fixed on Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, and we have full assurance that we will achieve success.

Thank you for this opportunity to express our gratitude for your divine service of introducing the most powerful process, by which everyone can achieve love for the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kālacāndjī Mandir,  
Dallas, Texas, USA.

## Debrecen

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept our respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please kindly accept this insignificant offering for your wonderful Vyāsa-pūjā celebration.

By your causeless mercy we’ve gotten the opportunity to be a small part of your great spiritual family. You’ve really built a big house which the whole world can live in. Although you never came to Hungary personally, you’ve come nevertheless by your disciples and your books. We all can see how people’s lives change when they read your books. In this way they become candidates for the perfection of this life: to attain pure love for Kṛṣṇa.

It is amazing to see how our own lives have changed, turning into that of real human beings. But the most wonderful thing is that we can be a part of this preaching mission. And out of your and your disciples’ causeless mercy we can give real hope to the people in general that they will become successful in their lives.

Indeed, we are not able to give anything in return for all those wonderful gifts you gave us. Therefore, we can only try to distribute this mercy to as many people as we can and help them to also be a part of this wonderful spiritual family of yours and Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s. Please give us your blessings so that we can perform our services for your pleasure and full satisfaction and also become pure devotees of the Lord. Thank you very much, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your eternal servants in Debrecen, Hungary.  
(written by Śacīsuta dāsa)

## Denpasar

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble *daṇḍavat* obeisances unto your holy lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are a most exalted personality who descended to this material world. Only you could spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness and mitigate the enmity among people, spreading true friendship in this world. You are the only disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s who could build a house where everyone from all over the world can stay peacefully.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are very gracious and full of compassion for the fallen souls who are entangled in the clutches of Māyā. Due to your mercy and grace, you paid little heed to the infirmity of your physical body and preached Kṛṣṇa consciousness energetically, spreading Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission all over the world, glorifying the holy name of the Lord and delivering the fallen souls who have forgotten their real identity as servants of the servant of the servant of the Lord, the Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so full of causeless mercy and compassion that you dropped the dust of your holy lotus feet in Jakarta, Indonesia, in 1973, and initiated a *mātājī* devotee, who at that time became your only disciple in Indonesia, Aravindākṣī-devī dāsī. You know that Kṛṣṇa consciousness would find fertile ground in Indonesia, and therefore you sent Gaura Maṇḍala Bhūmi dāsa Prabhu from Australia to preach here and translate your books into Indonesian. By your mercy and the efforts of Gaura Maṇḍala Bhūmi dāsa, we are also getting your causeless mercy. Your beloved disciples (*gurus*) then came to Indonesia and accepted us as their disciples. They have planted seeds of *bhakti* in our dirty hearts, so full of ignorance and darkness. *Guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*. We, your grandsons and granddaughters, always pray for your causeless mercy so that we can continuously do some service to the lotus feet of your beloved disciples, our spiritual masters, who link up our prayers to your holy lotus feet. *Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi:* “By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master one cannot make any advancement.”

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, no one surpass your glory. We hope that your greatness will eternally be glorified all over the three worlds. *Ebe jaśa ghuṣuk tribhuvana*.

**Exalted Personality, Śrīla Prabhupāda**

When the age is already old,  
At that time one begins to be conscious  
That, with death coming soon  
And fear beginning to cover,

There is no rope one can hold,  
There is no ground to be stepped on.  
Water tastes just like a thorny stone.  
Full of anxiety, the unsafe soul.

There is no other way,  
There is no other guidance  
Except Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teaching—  
Eternal teaching for all ages.  
Liberator of the fallen souls,  
Liberator of the universe,  
Liberator of the world’s society.

Śrīla Prabhupāda is waking up the sleeping souls,  
Waking up all the spirit souls all over this world,  
Who will take shelter in his house,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda’s house for children and grandchildren.

Let us be happy in Prabhupāda’s house.  
Let us work together in Prabhupāda’s house,  
The eternal house to get success.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Haribol! Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Your servants in Denpasar, Bali, Indonesia.  
(written by Vidura dasa, Bhagavatī-devī dāsī, Sadāśiva dāsa, and Śaktirūpa-devī dāsī)

## Denver

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our humblest obeisances again and again at your lotus feet on this auspicious day.

You built a house in which the whole world could live. Out of millions and millions of people, how is it possible that we have received the fortune of accepting your mercy? In a conversation with George Harrison in London you commented, “We are inviting everyone, ‘Come here. Such a nice house given by George. You live here comfortably, eat nicely, and chant Hare Kṛṣṇa.’ We don’t want any factory work.” You laughed. “Simply *karatāla* and *mṛdaṅga*. Still, people do not come. They’ll prefer to go to the factory, whole day work in the hell” (*A Transcendental Diary,* Vol. 3, p. 390).

We want to always stay in your house, in the association of your followers, and somehow or other assist you in accommodating more and more souls who are swirling in the tumultuous waves of Kali-yuga. The shelter you can offer is virtually unlimited. Please help us become proper caretakers and representatives of your mood.

In your house we stay together, although we are from diverse social and economic backgrounds, ethnic groups, and ages. As the years go by our family naturally expands as we find camaraderie among our spiritual brothers and sisters, aunts and uncles, nieces and nephews, children and grandchildren, mothers and fathers, grandmothers and grandfathers, friends and congregational members. In that association we gather the strength and inspiration to continue inviting people to your house, through the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

“He is a perfect *yogī* who, by comparison to his own self, sees the true equality of all beings, in both their happiness and their distress, O Arjuna” (Bg 6.32).

Only a short while ago most of us were tasting the bitter fruits of sinful life. You continuously remind us that the cause of our suffering is our forgetfulness of our relationship with Kṛṣṇa, and that the cause of our happiness is knowing Kṛṣṇa to be the supreme enjoyer of all the activities of the human being, the proprietor of all lands and planets, and the sincerest friend of all living entities. Without hearing from your books, we forget and become dominated by the modes of darkness and ignorance. Indeed, to spend our entire life studying your books would leave us hankering to read them again and again, to understand them better and better.

You wanted that all of your disciples learn the art of distributing your books. It’s inconceivable that although we may be lacking in so many ways, being shy, clumsy, totally inexperienced in sales, insecure, etc., we are able to transcend our material disqualifications and approach strangers from any type of background. We sometimes experience a kind of transcendental reciprocation while distributing your books that makes us certain you are pleased.
