We know that more and more of your books are falling into the hands of intelligent people who will be able to give guidance to others in the future. As the modern civilization frantically blows itself up, your books will serve to reconstruct society.

We are indebted to you for teaching us how to live in such a way that everything we do helps us to advance spiritually. Indeed, even our physical health and sanity improve by our chanting the holy name, honoring *prasādam,* being straightforward and clean inside and out, and following the four regulative principles. We do not know of any other spiritual leader who boldly demands such high standards of purity for initiates. Although our conditioning in this Western civilization dictates sinful activities as the “American way,” we are most grateful for the freedom from anxiety that these regulative principles for civilized human beings promote.

Although love for God is an internal affair of the heart, our external actions will automatically manifest our internal consciousness. One such symptom is cleanliness. Attempting to turn *mlecchas* into *brāhmaṇas* is as difficult as trying to wash coal so that it is no longer black. We pray that you will help us become “revolutionarily clean.” In the West, who ever heard of regularly bathing two or three times a day? Cleanliness is superficial here, with synthetic suites and deodorants, plastics and rugs, mops and poisonous chemicals. Forget about mental cleanliness. But you personally taught us how to clean by getting down on your hands and knees to scrub the floor. And by inspecting the grounds and buildings during your morning walks at different temples, you noticed the various discrepancies. Especially by establishing Deity worship you have trained us to have a reason to be always clean. In a letter to Bhadrā dāsī in 1971 you wrote, “Kṛṣṇa doesn’t need a nice offering. He appreciates the sincere endeavor. A clean kitchen is more important than a nice offering. If the kitchen is clean and maintained nicely, then the offering will be nice. If the offering is so-called nice but the kitchen is not cleansed, then Kṛṣṇa doesn’t like that offering.”

It is with deep gratitude that we aspire to faithfully serve you for all time.

Your eternal servants at New Badarikāśrama, Denver, Colorado, USA.  
(written by Kamaliṇī-devī dāsī)

## Devasadan Mandir

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Every year at the Devasadan Mandir on this auspicious day, all the devotees try to remember your unlimited mercy upon us, by which we are able to continue serving you. You came to Detroit and established one of the major temples in the world by converting an auto baron’s mansion, the Fisher Mansion, into the residence of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī. You gave specific guidance how to use this wonderful facility for preaching. Now after so many years we are able to realize the power of your words, as the neighborhood has changed from the worst to the best in Detroit. We have also realized that the more strictly we follow your instruction, the faster we get the results.

During the Centennial celebration of your divine appearance, we desired so many wonderful things to please you; we planned collectively, and we also achieved some of the major goals. Still we have a long way to go to offer you a well-established Devasadan Mandir. We need your blessings to have the special strength to do this. We want to take care of our most beloved Deities—Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī, Śrī Śrī Jagannātha Swami, Baladevjī, and Subhadrā Mātā, and Lord Śrī Nāthjī—in the most opulent way. We also want to establish a system where all your dedicated devotees at the Devasadan Mandir can be taken care of very nicely so that they can constantly work on book distribution and other forms of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness, such as *harināma, nāma-haṭṭa,* Food for Life, and tours of this most opulent temple. We want to establish a strong *gurukula* so that more devotees will be attracted to come here. We also want to utilize all the things you gave us in Kṛṣṇa consciousness so that many more souls will be attracted to dedicate their lives to the service of Kṛṣṇa

We do see a steady flow of new *bhaktas* being attracted to serve you at your temple, but we get bewildered when we see that some of them go back into the clutches of Māyā. Please give us special mercy so that we do not fail in facilitating all the souls sent by you to serve Lord Kṛṣṇa. Every time we read the prayer to Lord Kṛṣṇa you wrote on the *Jaladuta,* we realize how fortunate we are to be allowed to take shelter at your lotus feet.

Now by your mercy only we have the most gorgeous Deity worship, enthusiastic book distribution, *harināma* parties, the Bhaktivedanta Theater, a glorious exhibit of your pastimes in Detroit, a renovated garden, a renovated restaurant, Śrī Nāthjī Hall with your book exhibit and library, and sumptuous *prasādam* feasts. We do want to please you by improving all of this up to the best standard.

Please continue pouring your mercy on all of us, for without that we would all be lost in the material world. Please hold our hands tight because we have a tendency to run away from you. Please pray to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī so that we can serve Them until we leave our bodies.

Your devotees at the Devasadan Mandir, Detroit, Michigan, USA.  
(written by Jagadguru dāsa)

## Durban

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble *daṇḍavats* at your beautiful lotus feet.

On this auspicious day of your appearance on the planet, we, your followers in Durban, desire to worship your divine lotus feet every day during *guru-pūjā*. We are such unqualified devotees that we do not understand why we are doing this. Therefore we need your presence with us eternally. Because most of us are very neophyte, we do not know proper Vaiṣṇava etiquette, what to speak of austerities.

Now we find ourselves in a big ISKCON family, and due to different cultural backgrounds and different upbringings we find differences amongst ourselves. Still, we are trying to be united through devotional service; so please shower your mercy upon us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so wonderful, and also very merciful, but we conditioned souls find it hard to understand your causeless mercy. When you established your great ISKCON movement, not only did you established the Society but you also gave us everything we need to progress on the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by a drop of your causeless mercy make these servants of yours more humble than a blade of grass. Please make us understand the cause and reason for your establishing this life-saving movement. As we are minute and dependent souls, we feel that now that you are not physically present we are not getting more bona fide knowledge. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are going here and there to find it. But you told us that whatever we need to know to progress on the spiritual path you gave in your books. Therefore, instead of running here and there we must study your books very, very carefully and also engage ourselves in pure devotional service.

Our progress to push on this movement for the highest welfare of humanity is simply due to your spiritual potency and superexcellent devotion to the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Just as Lord Caitanya made even the animals chant the holy name of the Lord, you have come to preach to us although our consciousness was lower than that of the animal kingdom.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we, the devotees of the Rādhā- Rādhānātha Mandira, are proud to inform you that after a long struggle we have finally been able to change the name of Solar Street to Bhaktivedanta Swami Circle.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You live forever in your instructions, and your disciples live with you.

Your insignificant servants in Durban, South Africa.  
(written by Śyāmlāl dāsa)

## East Cape

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā! All glories, all glories, all glories to your most divine mercy!

Due to your divine mercy, we now have eleven initiated devotees. Your family here is growing steadily, but at the same time Kali is launching stronger attacks. Needless to say, Kali is formidable, and so we appeal to you to remove our doubts so that all of us can have one school of thought: the school of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your Divine Grace once said, “Your love for me will be shown by how you co-operate with one another after I am gone.” In this material world it is difficult for us to co-operate, and the only means is to surrender to your divine lotus feet. We beg, Please, please, please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the mercy by which we can always be eternally connected to your lotus feet! Always and eternally engage us in your service so that we can be your soldiers in the war against Māyā. We beg you to please forgive us for our countless offenses and give us the knowledge to become more fixed up in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, with your divine instructions you have saved so many fallen souls, and we thank you for being our savior as well. Please give us the wisdom to some day appreciate all you have done for us. We humbly beg you to help us remain under your expert control and always engage in spreading this wonderful Hare Kṛṣṇa movement.

Your fallen granddisciples in East Cape, South Africa.  
(written by Puruṣottama Kṛṣṇa dāsa)

## East Hartford

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! What can we say? You are great, and we are insignificant. You are pure, and we are contaminated. You are perfect, and we are full of faults. You are with Kṛṣṇa twenty-four hours a day, and we are in illusion most of the time. You are free from false ego, and we can’t help but act on it. You are serving and pleasing your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda, every moment of the day, and we are doing very little for you. You are the perfect renunciant, *yukta-vairāgī,* using everything for Kṛṣṇa, and we are deeply imbedded in mundane attachments.

Lord Kapila instructed his mother, “Every learned man knows very well that attachment for the material is the greatest entanglement of the spirit soul. But that same attachment, when applied to self-realized devotees, opens the door of liberation.” Attachment to your lotus feet is our only hope, and we cannot deny that you have softened our hearts. Your mercy is our inspiration and gives us hope that someday we will be with you eternally. How can we, in our condition, sufficiently glorify you who are above and beyond our limited understanding? We can only make an attempt to extol your unlimited transcendental qualities.

Of all your transcendental qualities, it is your compassion and understanding that touch us the most. You understand how fallen we are, and you are compassionate and patient in dealing with us. You are truly *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī:* it pains you to see the fallen souls suffering. Knowing that only Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s message could deliver us from our ignorance, out of compassion you sacrificed your own comforts and happiness for us to become “happies,” not hippies. You once said, “You don’t know how happy I was living in Vṛndāvana.” You were completely satisfied living at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple in Vṛndāvana, but you sacrificed that to come to America and help us fallen souls return back home, back to Godhead. Your plan wasn’t limited to America, but also included India and the rest of the world. You have made pure love of Kṛṣṇa available to everyone in every part the world. As a perfect physician, you administered the perfect medicine for alleviating our material disease. You have done so much for us, and all you are asking in return is for us to take this medicine, to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and lead a holy life.

Just because you are not physically present before us does not mean that you have forgotten about us, or that are no longer making sacrifices for us. You are forever alive and preaching through your books, and you mercifully appear in the minds, hearts, and dreams of your devotees. We hope to associate with you more and more as time goes on.

Your servants in East Hartford, Connecticut, USA.

## Eger

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept my humblest obeisances! All glories to you!

I think even tens of thousands of words wouldn’t be enough to praise you deservedly and to thank you for all the help we have gotten from you. So instead of writing thousands of words, I would rather pray that at least a tiny fraction of sincere desire should be in my offer to continue this service.

Preaching has been going on in Eger for six years, and during this time a lot of devotees who were introduced to Kṛṣṇa consciousness here have moved on to other centers in the country. Fortunately, there’s always a small group that remains here to continue the preaching.

Caitanya dāsa Prabhu started preaching here in September 1994. Since then a revolutionary transformation has occurred here. About eighty people come to the open programs, and on bigger occasions up to 120 attend. Young people tormented by suffering due to drugs, alcohol, cigarettes, and sex form the largest segment of visitors.

And those lucky ones who have stayed in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement have undergone a tremendous change. They have gradually rid themselves of their wicked habits, and slowly they are starting to preach widely about their life’s changes. More and more friends, parents, acquaintances, and classmates are being introduced to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Of course, it is not just the youth who have found shelter here, but the devotees and those inquiring are almost exclusively young people.

We rented a new center in the city last April, and the new temple room was scheduled to be finished a few weeks ago. It was a two-week marathon, and a lot of devotees helped redecorate the temple by providing money and other kinds of services. We wanted it to be finished by Gaura-pūrṇimā, and we invited many guests to come to the program.

This year we would like to preach to a wider audience, to distribute more books, and to start a Food for Life program! I hope the new center will attract more new people. But it will happen only if we can accept your guidance. So I would like to pray that I can become serious about chanting the holy name and following the principles. And in this way we can fulfill your desires.

Your eternal servants in Eger, Hungary.  
(written by Avadhūta-raya dāsa)

## Finland

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our *daṇḍavats* in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

You came to this world full of disasters,  
Where people think themselves to be masters.  
The four sinful habits are the life of the man;  
They are jumping for hot pleasures like seeds in the pan.

You came to this world full of disaster  
To help people understand faster,  
Though the smoke of *karma* makes them not think  
Whether their hot lifestyle really stinks.

You came to this world of ignorance  
In order to give the people an actual chance  
To give up false pleasures in karmic dance  
And in Kṛṣṇa conscious satisfaction advance.

You came to this world of ignorance  
With a message of lasting relevance:  
Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy forever;  
Thus be finished with material fever.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for coming to us.  
Without your grace, we would have only one bus—  
The one heading for the cycle of birth and death.  
For saving us, we owe you an eternal debt.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for coming to us  
To give us Lord Caitanya’s *prema-ras.*  
You have made us part of your *saṅkīrtan* troops,  
So now we march over Māyā’s groups.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!  
Without you this planet would have lost it face.  
It would have had no genuine direction,  
Confused by the laws of action and reaction.

All glories to Your Divine Grace,  
Who came from far beyond outer space!  
Please help us to serve your mission nicely,  
To spread the lords mercy to everyone wisely.

Your lowly Śabara-servants in Finland.  
(written by Ajamīḍha dāsa)

## Gītā-Nāgarī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to you!

It’s time to make our report, to let you know how we are doing at your Pennsylvania farm. Only by your mercy are things going on.

Prabhupāda, you said that where the Lord is present in His Deity form, where His holy names are chanted, where His service goes on, that place is a Vaikuṇṭha outpost, as good as Vṛndāvana. Gītā-Nāgarī is of that nature. It is not so outwardly impressive, but it is full of inner warmth and charm. Devotees come on pilgrimage here to soothe their souls, to walk the dirt paths. They see peacocks and deer, and more importantly, they hear from *sādhus*, who also find Gītā-Nāgarī sacred, a place where they may spend time undisturbed to read your books, to write, to speak their realizations to sincere souls who gather to hear. A sweet Vṛndāvana-like mood.

Gītā-Nāgarī is meant to be a village in which the *Bhagavad-gītā* is both sung and lived. “As they surrender to Me, I reward them accordingly. Everyone follows My path in all respects.” To live in community is a challenge. It requires constant surrender. Slowly, slowly we learn lessons of patience and tolerance, kindness and compassion. We are becoming a family.

Kṛṣṇa’s land is here, lying fallow but ready and waiting. Soon spring planting will begin. Kṛṣṇa’s cows are here, being cared for lovingly by a band of your sincere and dedicated servants. Many old cows and oxen are in the *gośālā,* peacefully living out their remaining years. We have yet to realize that within their dung resides the goddess of fortune. Their presence here reminds us of their purpose. We haven’t forgotten your words, Śrīla Prabhupāda: “Where are the oxen? Working oxen is the real alternative to slaughtering the animals. They have to understand the practical use of the oxen.” So do we, Prabhupāda, so do we. We must work the oxen. Please bless our attempts to serve. And please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, forgive me for asking you to ask your Lord Kṛṣṇa to carry what we lack and maintain what we have.

There is new life here. A new generation of children is growing up. Friendships thicken through the years, as we go through the fire of purification side by side.

Varieties of devotees, old and young, from Russia and Africa, from city and suburb, have come with their offerings of service. Under your guidance and the guidance of your disciples, Their Lordships are the central focus. Together at festivals we all follow Rādhā-Dāmodara as They go out on Their ox-cart, mystically playful, observing Their old friends—the trees, the cows, Tuscarora Creek, and the bounty of wild natural life. We swing Them and sing to Them in Their forest setting. Dressing Them and decorating Their hair with flowers, sewing for Them, cooking for Them, going out and distributing your books for Them, speaking of Them, typing, editing, and proofreading for Them, preaching in colleges about Them, working at jobs remembering Them, living simply as a way to glorify Them, and collecting funds to complete Their new temple are some of the ways we attempt to serve.

We thank you for such unfathomable sweetness in this life, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We beg for the privilege to continue trying to serve Rādhā-Dāmodara, you, and your servants.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to you!

Your humble servants at Gītā-Nāgarī, Pennsylvania, USA.

## Hong Kong

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We are living in a world of *change,* but you have given us the *unchanged* message. You teach us that this world is not a fit place to live in and that and we should practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this life and go back home, back to Godhead.

In this area of the world, this year we have to undergo a social change. Our dining club (temple) may also have to be changed. The external world may go on changing, but your teachings to us will never change. We may be living in the most sacred holy *dhāma,* or we may be living in the most hellish city; we may be serving as a full-time *pujārī,* or we may have to work in the outside world; we may have to support a family as a responsible *gṛhastha,* or we may preach as a renounced *brahmacārī* or *sannyāsī*—in any case, your teaching to us is that our devotion should never waver.

You teach by your example. For you, living in a gorgeous temple is the same as living in a simple hut. Many *gurus* and *svāmīs* went to the West and changed their dress, their eating habits, and even their teachings for the benefit of name and fame. But you didn’t change anything and presented to us the message of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*.

We know that success in spiritual life and preaching will surely come if we follow your teachings without change. The world may go on changing, but our goal and dedication will not change.

Your servants in Hong Kong, China.

## Johannesburg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

In the *Caitanya-bhāgavata* we read how dear Śrī Advaita Ācārya is to Lord Caitanya. It is due to his love for the lord that Lord Caitanya not only favored him with extraordinary mercy but also became the servant of his desires. In the same way, Lord Caitanya is always living in your heart, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and fulfilling all your desires with relish. You gave your life for establishing the mission of the Lord, and in return the Lord is making inconceivable arrangements to glorify Your Divine Grace.

Indeed, the Lord sees Himself as a debtor to you. Thus He concludes, “I am not able to repay My debt for your spotless service, even within a lifetime of Brahmā. Your connection with Me is beyond reproach. You have worshiped Me, cutting off all domestic ties, which are difficult to break. Therefore please let your own glorious deeds be your compensation” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.32.22).

Therefore whatever wonderful accomplishment we may see in ISKCON is simply due to the desire of the Lord to extol your glories, and whatever wonderful quality a devotee may display is simply due to that devotee being a surrendered follower of Your Divine Grace. You always live with your sincere followers, and through them your express intentions and desires are infiltrating into this material realm from the spiritual sky.

This is how you will always live in your ISKCON and guide it for the next ten thousand years. All obstacles and calamities will ultimately become your servants and begin to glorify you, the greatest saint that ever walked amongst us. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja writes, “Everyone who saw Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu could understand that He was performing a miracle, but they did not know how it was that He could see on all four sides” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 11.232).

In the same way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, being fully endowed with the mercy of the Lord, you are performing a miracle by reciprocating with thousands of sincere souls simultaneously and guiding them to the Lord. Even learned scholars are baffled as to how you could be sustaining this society in all four directions, but for one who knows how powerful your mercy is, this is not at all bewildering.

Your every word is worshiped as *śāstra*. Your words have the power to give us Kṛṣṇa. Indeed, your every action is cherished by your followers and will live with them to carry them beyond the realm of duality. You will live in their hearts forever.

Let us become true and sincere followers of Your Divine Grace. Let us always remember that we are insignificant servants of Your Divine Grace. Let us always live in the shade of your lotus feet.

Your servants in Johannesburg, South Africa.  
(written by Rāghava Paṇḍita dāsa)

## Kiev

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā. All glories to your divine qualities and pastimes! All glories to your appearance in this Golden Age of Lord Caitanya’s advent!

We are so fortunate to have been born in this age of the *saṅkīrtana-yajña* and to have been given the chance to serve the dust of your lotus feet.

*“sādhu-saṅga”, “sādhu-saṅga”——sarva-śāstre kaya*  
*lava-mātra sādhu-saṅge sarva-siddhi haya*

**“The verdict of all revealed scriptures is that by even a moment’s association with a pure devotee one can attain all success” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **22.54).**

Actually, the dust of a Vaiṣṇava is his followers. So we are the most lucky souls, for we can serve the servants of the most confidential servant of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. We serve your servants, who are the embodiment of all your perfect Vaiṣṇava qualities.

We associate with you by means of listening to and inquiring from your compassionate disciples and by means of reading and distributing your divine books. They are enlightening us with the moonshine of Lord Caitanya’s mission.

Last summer, during your Centennial year, some of our devotees had the great opportunity of distributing your mercy on Padayātrā Europe. And for the first time we also had a *padayātrā* here in Kiev. Along the way, you displayed many pastimes. Looking at you on the cart, people would ask us, “Who is this? Is this your tsar?” Yes, it’s really so. You are the tsar in the chariot of our hearts. Through your associates you’re giving us orders and instructions, and we’re fighting with the ignorance of this material existence, trying to spread your mission.

Your purity is so powerful, your books are so effective, like *astras* that easily destroy the demoniac desires in the hearts of the conditioned souls. And Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam* is the sharp bullet of Lord Caitanya’s graceful movement.

It is only by your endless mercy that year after year our temple increases the amount of books and *prasādam* distributed. It is only due to your disciples who deliver to us your bona fide faith and devotion to your beloved Lord and thus inspire us constantly that we can make our insignificant accomplishment in preaching the holy names of the Lord. We are simply the marionettes in your lotus hands.

O dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bestow upon us your blessings so that we can learn to cooperate with each other on our path of devotion to Śrī Kṛṣṇa. We are begging you to please give us the desire to always satisfy you and the Supreme Lord by participating in the fulfillment of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s divine plan.

Let there be no other path, and let there be no other desire. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The servants of your servants in Kiev, Ukraine.

## Liverpool

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

Your gift of love we cannot repay—

*kṛṣṇa—sūrya-sama; māyā haya andhakāra*  
*yāhāṅ kṛṣṇa, tāhāṅ nāhi māyāra adhikāra*

**“Kṛṣṇa is just like the sun, and** ***māyā*** **is just like darkness. If the sun is present, there is no question of darkness. Similarly, if Kṛṣṇa is present in the mind, there is no possibility of the mind’s being agitated by** ***māyā’s*** **influence” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta*****,** ***Madhya*** **22.31).**

Your presence is just like the sun. You brighten our lives and shine through the darkness of *māyā* in the form of transcendental knowledge, *prasādam,* and the chanting of the holy name. For these precious gifts we can never repay you. Whenever we follow your divine path, we cannot be taken back to darkness.

Chanting the holy name—  
You have given us the greatest gift.  
Chanting the holy name—  
Causes all darkness to shift.  
Chanting the holy name—  
The *prasādam* tastes so nice.  
Chanting the holy name—  
As we follow your advice.  
Chanting the holy name—  
Transcendental knowledge we can hear.  
Chanting the holy name—  
Māyā’s shackles you do shear.  
Chanting the holy name—  
Please always give shelter so complete.  
Chanting the holy name—  
At your divine lotus feet.

Your servants in Liverpool, UK.  
(written by Cakra-nadī-devī dāsī)

## Ljubljana

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! And all glories to your disciples and followers!

After pondering on how to describe your glories the best, I found it more and more obvious that there is no end to them. Let me therefore rather concentrate on glorifying only one of your unlimited qualities. But now I face another problem—which one to choose, when one is more sublime than the others? Still, there is a wonderful quality decorating your personality that is somehow special and evokes many different thoughts in our minds: you are absolutely the greatest transcendental cheater in this world.

It is said that we live in the world of the cheaters and the cheated, and some may find more or less truth in these words. But as far as I am concerned, after thoroughly deliberating on this statement, I cannot agree with it. Why not? Because in the ultimate issue people do not get cheated but only get what they desire. Yes, they factually want to be cheated, and when they get cheated at every step they get only what they wanted. That means there is no cheating going on: it is just a common occurrence.

So what then is the real cheating? As far as our humble self can say, the real cheating is what you did to us, conditioned souls in the degraded modern world, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda. You gave us God consciousness, the last thing we would have ever considered accepting on our own accord. But by the magic of your pure desire to save us from the hellish existence, you outwitted us and bewildered our minds. Thus we joined your movement without even realizing what we actually did. We rejected everything that was supposedly giving some kind of meaning to our lives and accepted completely new standards of self-sacrifice and renunciation. In the world, where sense gratification is the standard of enjoyment and the only purpose in life, this is a very brave step. What made us do it? Was it our knowledge? Was it our intelligence? Was it our determination? I say it is only your magic trick, which hypnotized us and wouldn’t let us go.

To back up this point here are some examples. Marko, an average teenager, lived with his parents in a block of flats in a small Slovenian town. He had a friend who was a little bit into Kṛṣṇa consciousness and, as is usual in the beginning, somewhat fanatical. He would go to people and say, “Say ‘Lokeśvara’ and you’ll get purified!” and do similar extraordinary things. Marko tolerated his strange behavior and didn’t take him too seriously. But he didn’t spare words when it came to making fun of his friend.

One day Marko’s friend forgot his *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* on the desk in his room. Marko was just playing games on his computer. The book attracted his attention, so he took it in his hands and read a few lines. After a few seconds he put it back and continued playing the game.

The next day a thought crossed his mind: “Do you remember the story you read yesterday in that book? How does it go again? What was it all about?” The worst thing was that the thought wouldn’t go away. He couldn’t tolerate it! He rushed to his friend and beseeched, “Give me that book, give me that book!”

The friend replied, “Wait, wait, I can’t give it to you just like that; maybe your hands are contaminated.”

“I don’t care,” said Marko, “just give me that book! I will wash my hands—just give me that book!”

So with all enthusiasm Marko began to examine the book, trying to find the section with that particular story he couldn’t drive away from his mind. He searched for a long time, but the story was just not there. Why was it so difficult to find it? He had no peace of mind. He couldn’t put the book down. He saw no other choice but to read it from the beginning, and thus in a very short time he read the entire *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. Strangely enough, he didn’t find the story he was looking for. But another thing happened—he became very much interested in the philosophy described in the book. He attended a program by the devotees put on in his town, and this gave him more food for thought. He continued reading your books, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and very soon he visited a temple for the first time and liked it there very much. After a few months he joined the temple and began engaging in service at your lotus feet. At present he is happily distributing your transcendental literatures. By now he has read the *Bhagavad-gītā* many times, but he still hasn’t found that story. What happened to it? This was obviously a transcendental hoax.

Another devotee had a similar experience. One day his mother sent him to discard the garbage in the dust-bin. He grudgingly went to do it. As he started to empty the can, he peeped inside the bin and saw some books there. He took a look at them, and one of them attracted his attention very much. Its title was *Rāja-vidyā, the King of Knowledge*. He read the first few lines, and although the book was dirty and tattered, he took it to his room and started reading it. He regarded the book as very mystical but couldn’t understand anything. His then dull brain could comprehend only one small detail: chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa is very auspicious. He liked the *mantra* and started chanting it. He did it on his own, in a somewhat special way—he sat down on his balcony in the middle of the winter with practically no clothes on and uttered, “Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.”

He read the book three times, and when his sister brought him the First Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* he read half of that also. His sister would often tell him, “Be careful. You are very much prone to others’ influence; watch out that the Hare Kṛṣṇas don’t get you.”

He replied, “The Hare Kṛṣṇas? Get me? Come on, not me! How can you say something like that?”

Of course, by Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement he soon met devotees for the fist time. He immediately got himself a *japa-mālā* and started chanting a few rounds more seriously. He liked everything about Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and thus he quickly jumped up to the association of devotees. At present he is happily engaged in translating your transcendental literatures, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

From the dust-bin to Vaikuṇṭha. And still I wonder, “What is it that actually made us come and stay here?” You cheated us blindly, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you.

I know there are hundreds and thousands of similar examples of devotees being cheated by your divine words. And I know that is glorious. If it weren’t for your cheating us, we would still be rotting in the trash-bin of material existence. All we can do is fall at you lotus feet and praise your incredible intelligence, by which you tricked the untouchables into engaging in the most exalted activity in all three worlds.

In the name of all the devotees here at the Hare Kṛṣṇa Center in Ljubljana, Slovenia, I thank you for all this. Without you we are nothing.

Your unworthy servants in Ljubljana, Slovenia.  
(written by Narākāra dāsa)

## Madrid

Querido Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our humble and prostrated reverences before your divine lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have done what no one else could ever do. In the middle of Kali-yuga you have firmly established a powerful spiritual institution that, acting according to real spiritual values, is dedicated to educating and helping all kinds of people. You strongly taught that one should develop pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you transmitted the process by which anyone can develop that pure love for Kṛṣṇa, and you created various means for broadly spreading that spiritual consciousness to all human society.

Your mission is ecstatic and liberating, and you have often shown your matchlessly superior capacity to transmit devotional service to the conditioned souls. The residents of the material world are very poor in spiritual life, and they don’t know what’s the best way to walk. They have all been deeply damaged by the influence of the illusory energy, *māyā,* and in their confusion they are ignorant of everything. In fact, they are so often attached to the various pleasures of the senses that they don’t even question the possibility of changing their way of life. They are always full of anxieties and fears, feeling empty and unsatisfied at heart, and each moment they are deluded by false friendships and the bombardment of misleading materialistic propaganda. They are becoming more and more materialistic, helpless and crazy people. In that way their suffering is increases as their consciousness becomes degraded. In this situation, their only hope is *your mercy*. Their only hope is to know you.

Being completely conscious of the strong desire of your spiritual master to spread the glories of Kṛṣṇa and save the fallen souls, you came to us where we lived and made a superhuman effort to take us out of the miserable conditions of material existence.

Armed with the beautiful qualities of the pure servants of the Lord, you have given us all the facilities we need to progress; we simply need to willingly accept your merciful instructions. So, step by step, by virtue of the transcendental process that you have given us, we are becoming attached to Kṛṣṇa and his pure devotees.

So, all auspiciousness surrounds our lives nowadays, and for this we are eternally grateful. All our good fortune is due only to you. Actually, if it hadn’t been for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our lives would have been awfully degraded and unfortunate. If you hadn’t come to us and mercifully instructed us, we wouldn’t be heading for a birth even as human beings, let alone as devotees. This is one of the most outstanding aspects of your powerful person: your amazing capacity to change the destiny of conditioned souls.

In truth you are the chief transcendental revolutionary, and you have created a real army of transcendental revolutionaries who are constantly increasing their numbers and who are attached to you and faithful to you, ready to sacrifice their lives in your service.

There is no doubt that the understanding and determination to follow you come from your faultless example and instructions. In spite of the great benediction that has been given to us, however, Māyā is stronger and cleverer than we are, and she regularly captures her prey and has severely damaged several sections of your movement. Of course, everything is due to our weakness, insincerity, lack of full faith, lack of real surrender, and our superficiality and immaturity. We have never seen that this damage was due to any lack on your part of any ability to give us shelter.

The conclusion is that without you we can’t advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and that we need you more than anything, because we generally are of limited intelligence in our attempts to offer you faithful devotional service. We lack the purity and dedication you have always shown, and we get confused and make mistakes too often.

The way, at least in part, to counteract these problems is to always stay engaged in distributing your incomparable gifts: your marvelous books, the delicious *prasādam,* the nectarean and transcendental holy name of the Lord, and lots of other things you have given us.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us the chance to always engage in devotional service. We pray to you to illuminate our intelligence so we will not make the serious mistake of going against your desires and the aims of your holy institution. Please forgive our unending offenses and material and childish attitudes. We rely utterly on your mercy.

Your blessed lotus feet always shine in our hearts. Desiring to stay in the dust of those immaculate lotus feet, we prostrate eternally before you.

Your unqualified servants in Madrid, Spain.

## Manchester

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Thank you for allowing us to spend another year serving your mission here in Manchester. When this time comes around each year, it gives us the opportunity to pause and reflect on what you have given the world. Being immersed in so many day-to-day problems and conflicts, even in the course of service, we can easily lose sight of the “forest for the trees,” as they say, and forget that we are actually proceeding on the final great journey back to the supreme abode. But that is the reality. As long as we simply adhere to the process you have given, then liberation is our certain goal.

There is a devotee here in our temple who is working on a paraphrase of your *Bhagavad-gītā* verses. She recently completed the rewording of 6.40, and I wanted to include an extract of that here, like an offering of Ganges water to worship Mother Gaṅgādevī.

Because the devotees are always engaged in My devotional service, they are already liberated from material existence. Their activities are completely auspicious because by virtue of their devotional service they will never take birth again. And though they undergo the most severe austerities and tests in the course of their devotional service, they need have no fear that they will meet with either material or spiritual harm because I am always protecting them. Therefore, My dear friend, be assured that if you try to please Me you will surely overcome all difficulties. \[based on *Bhagavad-gītā* 6.40\]

This is what you have bestowed upon us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is quite impossible to repay you, and thus we are left with no choice but to dedicate our body, words, mind, and indeed life to your service. We therefore pray today that you will glance upon us with compassion and mercy. It must be obvious to you that we are hopelessly riddled with countless *anarthas,* that although we may be on the auspicious path, its end for us must be somewhere far off in the distant future. We often wonder how much of our day is actually spent in your service, and how much in the service of our minds and senses. Although we rush about in a busy, busy way, apparently intent on rendering service, it sometimes seems that our material desires and attachments impel us more than anything else. That we are chasing will-o-the-wisps, thinking them to be Kṛṣṇa conscious objectives.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please do not allow our attachments to overwhelm us again. Please keep us always fixed in the certain knowledge that your service is the only way to secure happiness. Please help us always see the auspicious sandalwood of your service and not just the thick forest of material existence. Help us avoid becoming entangled in the creepers and weeds of mundane ambition and hopes. Sometimes the forest seems impassable, but when we remember you and Kṛṣṇa we always find that a path is revealed. Give us the strength of purpose to always take that path and not remain for a lengthy detour in some seemingly pleasant clearing. Steer us away from the bottomless pits of politics and quarrel. As you have told us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Māyā is fully intent on diverting us from the path. Therefore, dear lord, take us by the hand and lead us safely through. We are still children. How can we ever pass through this forest alone? You are our only hope. Please do not lose patience as we stumble and even break free of your hold to run and explore the woods. Drag us by the hair, if need be, but please never allow us to lose your shelter.

On this most auspicious occasion of your one hundred and first Vyāsa-pūjā, we fall helplessly at your feet. You are *patita-pāvana* and *jagat-guru*. You are ever with us, and we are ever your servants. We thank you from the bottom of our hearts for all that you have done for us. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants in Manchester, UK.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa-dharma dāsa)

## Manipur

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this auspicious day of the Vyāsa-pūjā of the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, I beg to offer my humble obeisances to him.

I also humbly pray for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s blessings for the timely completion of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇacandra Maṇi-mandir in Manipur. By building this prestigious project we are fulfilling the wishes of Śrīla Prabhupāda in making Manipur, one of the oldest Vaiṣṇava states, one of the most wonderful places in the entire world.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 9.22.32 reads,

*kareṇumatyāṁ nakulo naramitraṁ tathārjunaḥ*  
*irāvantam ulupyāṁ vai sutāyāṁ babhruvāhanam*  
*maṇipura-pateḥ so ’pi tat-putraḥ putrikā-sutaḥ*

**“Nakula begot a son named Naramitra through his wife named Kareṇumatī. Similarly, Arjuna begot a son named Irāvān through his wife known as Ulupī, the daughter of the Nāgas, and a son named Babhruvāhana by the womb of the princess of Maṇipura. Babhruvāhana became the adopted son of the king of Maṇipura.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s purport reads as follows:

It is to be understood that Pārvatī is the daughter of the king of the very, very old mountainous country known as the Maṇipura state. Five thousand years ago, therefore, when the Pāṇḍavas ruled, Maṇipura existed, as did its king. Therefore this kingdom is a very old, aristocratic Vaiṣṇava kingdom. If this kingdom is organized as a Vaiṣṇava state, this revitalization will be a great success because for five thousand years this state has maintained its identity. If the Vaiṣṇava spirit is revived there, it will be a wonderful place, renowned throughout the entire world. Maṇipuri Vaiṣṇavas are very famous in Vaiṣṇava society. In Vṛndāvana and Navadvīpa there are many temples constructed by the king of Maṇipura. Some of our devotees belong to the Maṇipura state. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, therefore, can be well spread in the state of Maṇipura by the cooperative efforts of the Kṛṣṇa conscious devotees.

With this purport Śrīla Prabhupāda has inspired many *kṛṣṇa-bhaktas* from many parts of the world to express an interest in coming to Manipur and helping to spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, thus maintaining Manipur’s Vaiṣṇava identity. Many of them have visited, and after experiencing this Vaiṣṇava state they express an interest in coming to work here. We are very eager and happy to welcome many more *kṛṣṇa-bhaktas* from all parts of the world.

Toward fulfilling Śrīla Prabhupāda’s wishes, as expressed in the above quote from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* His Holiness Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami has launched a prestigious project for building a temple that will be unique in the entire northeastern region of India. This project comprises the construction of the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇacandra Maṇi-mandir, the Rāsamandal, and the Cultural and Amenities facilities. The construction of such a unique temple has, in fact, become necessary in Manipur to help promote the cultivation, preservation, and propagation of *sanātana-dharma*. This is part of the ongoing effort to maintain Vaiṣṇavism in Manipur.

The younger generation in Manipur, the hope for tomorrow, have for many reasons been very frustrated by the so called revitalization activities. This is all due to this Age of Kali. It is high time that these frustrated youths be awakened to divine consciousness so that their body, mind, intelligence, and precious time will be fully concentrated on activities of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The movement is quite popular in Manipur, but in some quarters it is seen as insignificant because of the increasing tendencies, particularly among the youth, of anger, quarrel, lust, hatred, false prestige, and other symptoms of the Age of Kali.

The projected Center of Bhāgavata Culture would ultimately become Eastern Vṛndāvana. The concept of this temple complex would be to attract as many Kṛṣṇa devotees as possible from all parts of the world and to provide them with the opportunity to thoroughly learn the teachings of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda. This temple complex will be a cultural, educational, and spiritual facility.

Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami had planned to complete the construction of the temple component by 1999, coinciding with the 200thbirth anniversary of the great Kṛṣṇa-devotee Rajarshi Bhagyachandra Mahārāja, who ruled Manipur in the 18thcentury and who was an exponent of the unique Manipuri dances glorifying the *rāsa-līlā* pastimes of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

However, meting this target for completion of the temple will be difficult, mainly because of the tight financial position. The fund-raising programs have not been very successful and encouraging despite our best efforts. Manipur is a tiny state, where resources for raising adequate funds are very limited. The present law-and-order situation is an added problem.

We therefore most humbly pray for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s blessings so that all the world-wide noble and gentle souls in general and Kṛṣṇa conscious devotees in particular may extend their generous support and financial help for achieving this ambitious plan of completion of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇacandra Maṇi-mandir by 1999. It will also be very auspicious if the Deities—Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇacandra, Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Śrī Jagannātha, Śrī Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā—are housed in their permanent abode by the time the 200thbirth anniversary of Rajarshi Bhagyachandra Mahārāja is celebrated.

Lastly, let me take the opportunity to quote the *Agni Purāṇa:* “One who builds or helps to build a temple of Lord Viṣṇu is liberated along with eight generations of his forefathers.”

And in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.27) Lord Kṛṣṇa says, “Whatever you do, whatever you eat, whatever you offer or give away, and whatever austerities you perform—do that, O son of Kuntī, as an offering to Me.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants in Manipur, India.  
(written by Rādhā-mohana dāsa)

## Markham

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories to your lotus feet,  
That showed us Kṛṣṇa and made our lives complete.

All glories to your lotus feet,  
That told us to distribute books in every town and on every street.

All glories to your lotus feet,  
That taught us that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not just for the elite.

All glories to your lotus feet,  
That taught us to listen to Kṛṣṇa’s glories so sweet.

All glories to your lotus feet,  
That had us enjoying *prasādam* feasts.

All glories to your lotus feet,  
That showed us following *māyā* is obsolete.

All glories to your lotus feet,  
That taught us to abstain from eating eggs, fish, and meat.

All glories to your lotus feet,  
That told us it is the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* that we should simply repeat.

All glories to your lotus feet,  
That taught us to preach to each and everyone we meet.

All glories to your lotus feet,  
That showed us how many *gurus* were there just to cheat.

All glories to your lotus feet,  
That showed us it is Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa that we should lovingly greet.

The servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara in Markham, Canada.

## Melbourne

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

As a servant of your spiritual master, you took the words of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the teachings and the practice, to lands far from the spiritual world. Endowed with the potency of Lord Caitanya’s preaching mission, you spread the holy name to all corners of the globe.

How grateful we are for your unflinching, unwavering faith in the order of your spiritual master! How much we owe you for saving us from degradation and elevating us to the spiritual world!

Thank you for calling me to my spiritual life. Thank you for the distant ring of the *karatālas* in the *saṅkīrtana* party, awakening me to the real purpose of life. Thank you for my first taste of *prasādam,* which, if it weren’t for a friend long since lost, I would have thrown away. Thank you for the cookbook that taught me the diet of a transcendentalist is perfected when he offers all food to God. Thank you for the association of the devotees I met on the street who encouraged me to take the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa seriously. Thank you for the realization, which came in moments of loneliness and despair, that someone somewhere in the world is chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and that wherever I am chanting I will never be alone.

Thank you for impelling me to take this process seriously so I can now count myself among the ranks of devotees in the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Thank you for the friendship and support one finds in the association of pure devotees. Thank you for allowing me to be a part of real life.

Your servants in Melbourne, Australia.  
(written by Aniruddha dāsa)

## Mexico City

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains. Such is the mercy of the spiritual master.”**

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvanmanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**“One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.17.27).**

O lord and master, by Your Divine Grace’s mercy this country and the whole world is receiving Lord Caitanya’s message. You struggled greatly to come to the West. Your disciples are astonishing the world, but everything is guided only by your love and spiritual strength.

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who simultaneously have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed” (*****Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad*** **6.23).**

On behalf of all the devotees in this temple, on this most blessed day we ask you for the benediction of giving us always the remembrance of your lotus feet and the determination to serve you forever.

At the present moment we finally have satisfied one of your wishes that you expressed when you came to Mexico. Today, Śrī Śrī Rādhā–Madana-Gopāla are in the main hall of the house, as you requested to your disciples when you came here.

We request that you give us the intelligence and life to be able to fulfill even your slightest wish for this country and the world. We still have so many more to fulfill.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, how can we put aside all our desires and put yours in the center? How can we avoid envy, anger, lust, greed, and ignorance? You have already given us the secret and shown us the example of your gracious life. It’s so difficult for us conditioned living entities. Only by your love and mercy can we keep going on and deliver this message to all, without distinction of religion, color, or race.

You are the embodiment of the mercy of the golden Lord Caitanya. Every day we appreciate how fortunate we are to have the chance to assist you in this wonderful pastime of yours. How close we are to you, and how far at the same time!

Please teach us how to grasp your lotus feet, which are the only way to get out of this world, where everyone has forgotten Kṛṣṇa. In this world you are one of those special, rare souls who never forgot the Lord. As Kṛṣṇa spoke the *Gītā* at Kurukṣetra for Arjuna’s benefit and that of all humankind, in this age you and Lord Caitanya came for the very same purpose. You are also teaching us how to fight against our minds and senses. The Lord has kindly given us his word in the *Gītā*, but we are doubly fortunate because we have your wonderful purports as well, which are for the benefit of all.

Your disciples and granddisciples in Mexico City, Mexico.  
(written by Rohiṇī-nandana dāsa)

## Miami

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

We are once again called together by this auspicious occasion to renew our relationship with you and rekindle our dedication to your mission.

How strange and ironic it seems that while we were begging to receive your blessings you were relentlessly urging us to accept your blessings. If we were so eager to receive your blessings, then why did you consider it necessary to petition us, in thousands of letters, and more, “Please accept my blessings”? It is because you have not expressed your blessings simply as some thoughts of good will but as your perfect and practical instructions on how we can become Kṛṣṇa conscious.

You instructed us repeatedly to chant sixteen rounds, to follow the four regulative principles, and to fully dedicate our lives to propagating the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. You guaranteed us that by sincerely following this simple prescription we could qualify ourselves to go back to Godhead, even in this lifetime. This is a wonderful blessing, but how many of us really accept it?

How many of us continue to distract our attention from your mission, dreaming up scores of ways to justify our failure to follow your instructions? You said that “impossible is a word found in a fool’s dictionary,” so we have no excuse for our neglect. From the beginning you knew well that time would cause most of us to become heavily burdened by responsibilities of family life and other obligations, and that we would all face many challenges that would seem to fully justify our neglect of your divine instructions. So your plea to us, “Please accept my blessings,” really means “I have given you everything that you need to perfect your lives, so please accept it as I have given it and become Kṛṣṇa conscious!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although you have selflessly poured upon us your unlimited blessings, we are foolishly trying to shield ourselves from those blessings, not realizing their real value. Please consider our pitiable condition and force us to take the seemingly bitter medicine of your blessings so that we can perfect our lives and become purely instrumental to you in carrying on this great mission.

Your humble servants in Miami, Florida.

## Moscow

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances unto the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to your International Society for Krishna Consciousness!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be kind to us and accept our invitation: come and visit Moscow!

Of course, your schedule is very busy. You are on a preaching tour, establishing ISKCON somewhere. But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there are so many of us struggling here in Moscow, trying to follow your instructions, and we have never met you!

\[No, this will not work. You will reply that if we indeed follow your instructions there is no question of separation.\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have always liked to preach in big cities. And, do you know that the Communist rule collapsed twenty years after your first visit to Moscow, exactly as you predicted?

\[No, surely you are updated better than that. And what is there interesting to see in the streets of today’s Moscow: giant Marlboro Man advertisements? People are morose as before, still being cheated. “Just see,” you will say. “What are you doing to help them??”\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have distributed millions of your books here! But the people are becoming more and more materialistic. Also, fewer and fewer can afford to buy a book in this country, where working for six months without receiving payment is becoming normal. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we need your inspiration and transcendental directives how to cheat Māyā and increase the distribution of your books.

And, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the devotees have their own radio station in Moscow, Radio Kṛṣṇaloka, broadcasting twenty-four hours a day. If you come for a talk show, you can reach twenty million people, all of Moscow and St. Petersburg! Also, we can make a festival for you in a rented hall, where you can address thousands of guests: our Moscow congregation.

So you will come?...

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our temple is not the Fisher Mansion. It’s just an old eight-apartment building. But we are trying to make it nice for you. We are presently painting the temple building and the boundary wall and windows. A team of devotees is feverishly working on the staircase renovation. When everything has been nicely renovated and the *mahā*-cleanup is over, we will decorate the temple elevation with festoons of multicolored balloons. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please tell us what we have to do to finally get a new temple in Moscow? When will we have decent facilities for our own devotees and for the hundreds of visitors? What is Kṛṣṇa teaching us by not letting this dream come true?

You will perhaps like it that our place is so close to the metro station. You will immediately devise schemes to get all the passersby to come in and visit us. “Why don’t you put a billboard at the station, ‘Visit the Hare Krishna Temple and Make Your Life Sublime’?” Why don’t you serve out cookies there and give out invitations?”

I wonder how you will like our temple room. So much work and devotion went into it. You will greet our Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi—I hope Their Lordships will tell you that They like it here. You will perhaps notice Their opulent fresh flower garlands and other decorations on the altar. You always wanted Deities to be offered fresh flowers. Just a word of appreciation from you will send our *pujārīs* into ecstasy!

I think you will also like your life-size *mūrti,* regally sitting on the gorgeous *vyāsāsana*. During your *guru-pūjā,* so many devotees come to offer *daṇḍavats* that they have to do it in two rows simultaneously, and still there are queues! (Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have read in the memoirs of your senior disciples that when you were present, women devotees used to offer *daṇḍavats* like the men, in front of you. Should we reestablish that tradition? I cannot imagine it!)

We will put you in the guest department because it has the nicest facilities in the whole temple. You will be pleased to note that the two rooms for guests are full of people all day long: Indians, scholars, life members, sponsors—“But what do you have to offer to ordinary devotees?” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you look concerned. You must have noticed crowds of people hanging around, unengaged. What can I tell you? We do not even have place in the temple for all the educational programs we are conducting! We are sending preachers to develop the Nāma Haṭṭa groups. But this place is so small that we cannot even have a decent Sunday program here! (Am I finding excuses? Will you now tell me that the problem begins in the consciousness?)

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, will you kindly dissipate my anxiety? So many times I read in your books and lectures how you used to invite everyone: “Come and live with us.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, this country is in a state of chaos. So many desperate people would be happy to drop everything, shave up, and move in here to do some small service for a plate of *prasādam* and a place to sleep—just to escape the harsh reality outside. But is this what they really need? Śrīla Prabhupāda, if only we had your compassion and intelligence, we would know how to best help them with our limited resources.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you would surely like to take a tour around the temple grounds. Here is the *mahā-prasadam* shop. Yes, it’s called Matchless Gifts. It’s very popular, not only among devotees. Here is the guards’ department; they take care of crazies and thieves. Here is the BBT. This little kitchen in the backyard produces cakes for selling in supermarkets. People here love *prasādam* cakes! Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless this program. May we monopolize the Moscow cake industry with our Kṛṣṇa cakes!

And here is another shop, with books and paraphernalia. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, now you look incredulous—“You’re only up to the Fourth Canto??”

No excuses. Yes, we are just small kids and all we can do now is look guilty. But, our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you said it yourself that the grand–spiritual master is very kind to his spiritual grandchildren. Please, therefore, show us your mercy, for we need it: in the form of smiling appreciation, of an instruction, or of your transcendental anger. We simply pray that we never lose the connection with you, the feeling that “Let’s get ourselves together now and do something really nice: Prabhupāda is coming!”

Your humble servants at the Begovaya temple in Moscow, Russia.  
(written by Dhyāna-kuṇḍa-devī dāsī)

## Mumbai

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our millions of prostrated *daṇḍavats* at your divine lotus feet. By your causeless mercy you have given shelter to all of us at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Mandir of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

It gives us tremendous joy to know that this temple has manifested here as a result of your desire to have Kṛṣṇa conscious preaching in downtown Mumbai—You expressed this desire when you visited the Bharatiya Vidya Bhavan with your disciple Śyāmasundara Prabhu some twenty-five years ago.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know that preaching in Mumbai is very dear to you, and we are astonished at our good fortune to be able to assist you in our insignificant way. Our only hope is to earn your pleasure by preaching in colleges to the youth of Mumbai, by distributing your books in large numbers, and by expanding congregational preaching. Through these and other such programs we pray that we may bring many fortunate souls to the shelter of your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are eternally grateful to you for the supreme gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that you have so mercifully given to us and to the whole world.

Your humble servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Mandir in the Chowpatty section of Mumbai, India.

## Murāri Sevaka

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him. When will Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, who has established within this material world the mission to fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya, give me shelter under his lotus feet?”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the source of all auspicious mercy.  
How much is your mercy?  
The depth of the ocean? The height of the sky?  
The length of the horizon or the distance to the moon in the heavens?

And why have you showered your mercy upon us—  
The ignorant, impersonal, rich and poor fools?  
Your mercy is causeless, the reasons are sure—  
To bring Back to Godhead these most fallen souls.

You teach them to chant and dance for the Lord—  
This mercy is giving the philosophy for weak minds to absorb.  
This mercy freely given, our offenses you forgave—  
Your mercy again shows in the tolerance always.

Due to your mercy Murāri has survived—  
To push on your project we give you our lives.

“The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.”

Your servants at Murāri Sevaka, Tennessee, USA.

## Nagpur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated *daṇḍavat praṇāmas*. All glories to your divine self on your most auspicious appearance day! All glories to your divine movement, ISKCON, which is sweeping the world with your mercy!

It was almost one year ago, on Rāma Navamī here in Nagpur, Maharashtra—our biggest preaching day of the year—when you made your appearance in your magnificent *mūrti* form night before the eyes of thousands of cheering people, who were chanting the throwing flowers into the air.

We were participating in a gigantic Shoba-yātrā procession. Rāma Navamī Shoba-yātrā in Nagpur is one of the biggest Shoba-yātrās in India. Over seventy religious groups participate, with many floats depicting *rāma-līlā* and *kṛṣṇa-līlā,* dancing groups, and *bhajan* and *kīrtana* parties. Crores of people line the city streets. The procession is 1.5 kilometers.

Every year for the past five years we have won first prize for our Hare Kṛṣṇa *kīrtana* party. This year the *kīrtana* exploded into ever-increasing ecstasy as you appeared in your *mūrti* form—right in the middle of the Shoba-yātrā—right into our lives!

Two devotees had driven by truck from Māyāpur, bringing your *mūrti* form. None of the ISKCON Nagpur temple devotees were expecting your divine appearance. We were astonished when you emerged out of the crowd, draped with flower garlands. Two devotees carried you to our *ratha,* and you joined us on the procession. We all dropped down and paid obeisances to you on the *ratha*. Immediately we performed *guru-pūjā,* and as devotees fanned you with a *cāmara* and peacock fan, all the spectators wondered, “Who is the great personality?”

We have made it a goal of our center to inform all the people of this region of Maharashtra of the greatness of your personality. You have inspired us by your deeds and the kind instructions in your books to try to connect people with you so that they can become fortunate like us. Fortunate like us: to live in your house—a house the whole world can live in. Fortunate like us: to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and dance and take delicious *prasādam*. Fortunate like us: to daily rise early at 4:00 am and then gaze lovingly at the wonderful lotus-eyed forms of our dear Lordships, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi.

Your personal arrival in the form of your *mūrti* in your Centennial year has made a vast difference in our temple community. When the king comes, he brings his entourage. You are the king of transcendental personalities, and many transcendental personalities have also come to Nagpur, following in your wake.

Your beloved servant Lokanāth Swami Mahārāja, who is the *guru* of most of us here, has kindly come and visited us several times this year to purify our existence. He brings your transcendental teachings. He is a bright flame that reminds us of your towering personality. He is a living monument to you—perfect in both his preaching and behavior. Most importantly, he brings an infectious dedication to you.

Your arrival has also seen the coming of more devotes to stay in the *āśrama* here. Jaya Jagannātha dāsa and Rāsa-rasika dāsa are full-time book distributors. Anurāga dāsa and Nara-nārāyaṇa dāsa have come to raise the standard of Deity worship to a new level of devotion. Rādhā-kānta dāsa is breaking new ground in college and youth preaching.

Books are the basis. Your have come to Nagpur, and now the devotees of ISKCON Nagpur are distributing your books as never before. Your Prabhupāda marathon was triple the size of previous years. Devotees have saved enough money through book sales to purchase a jeep for preaching and book distribution—a first for Nagpur. For the first time we are reporting our scores to be included in the world *saṅkīrtana* newsletter. You always said that book scores gave you life. We are hopeful that this endeavor by our devotees is pleasing to you.

Another first for Nagpur during your Centennial Year was Lord Jagannātha’s Ratha-yātrā festival. This wonderful festival created a sensation among the Nagpurians, as thousands flocked to the festival grounds to be addressed by your senior ISKCON leaders.

In so many ways you have blessed us during the course of your Centennial year—Feed the World Day, Holy Name Day, a wonderful ten-day Padayātrā Week, and World Enlightenment Day.

Please give us the benediction that we can serve you more purely, and please continue to send your devotees to preach here and guide us fledgling *bhakti-yoga* candidates so that someday we may become like you, a Vaiṣṇava.

*ohe!*  
*vaiṣṇava ṭhākura, doyāra sāgara,*  
*e dāse karuṇā kori’*  
*diyā pada-chāyā, śodho he āmāya,*  
*tomāra caraṇa dhori*

**“O venerable Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura, O ocean of mercy, be merciful to these servants of yours and purify us with the shade of your lotus feet. Your lotus feet we humbly hold.”**

Today we held an *iṣṭagoṣṭhī* to discuss the upcoming Rāma Navamī Mahotsava. We have planned many improvements in our celebration. We plan to feed 5,000 people Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* inaugurated by a 5-*mṛdaṅga* roaring *kīrtana*, then a feast for 200 life members and guests and this year’s Shoba-yātrā—11 hours of nonstop *kīrtana* with Western devotees, with a float of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha, and Your Divine Grace. You will be constantly fanned with *cāmara* and whisk for the entire 11-hour procession as you again give your *darśana* to crores of Nagpurians. This will be wonderfully attractive for the conditioned souls of Nagpur and purifying for us.

Best of all, the *iṣṭagoṣṭhī* expressed a mood of cooperation, which is the most pleasing feature for you. You said, “Your love for me will be shown by how you cooperate together to serve me after I am gone.” This morning all the devotees enthusiastically came forth with ideas to glorify you. This is also a new blessing you have brought to ISKCON Nagpur. Please continue to shower your mercy upon us.

Make us dance, make us dance, make us dance as you like.

We pray that, just as you have come into our temple room, you will stay with us always by entering into our hearts as we go out of the temple to preach on the streets, in the shops, in the homes, and in the schools and colleges of Nagpur and surrounding areas.

We have heard that when you were shown a map of India and asked where we should have centers, Nagpur, being the geographical center of India, was one of the locations where you said we should have a temple. At present our temple is very small. We want to fulfill your desire that we have a big, beautiful temple project with a guesthouse and so many other facilities to propagate and practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is our fervent prayer that you give us your blessings so that we may fulfill this desire of yours. Please give us strength to serve you. Please let us become humble servants of the servants of your servant.

The most unqualified aspiring servants of your servant,  
Your devotees in Nagpur, India.  
(written by Ekalavya dāsa Brahmacārī)

## Nava-jiyaḍa-nṛsiṁha-kṣetra

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept our humble obeisances.

On this very auspicious day of your holy appearance in this world, we would like to express our gratitude to you. If it were not for you, there would be no Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. When you registered your ISKCON society in 1966 in New York City, this was the most important activity in the whole century. It was in fact the culmination of years of meditation and plan-making from generations of *ācāryas*. To say that you perfectly fulfilled the mission of your spiritual master and Lord Caitanya is by no means an exaggeration. You are the pride of the whole disciplic succession.

As time goes by, your real glory and achievement will become more and more revealed. The world will understand who has been walking on the surface of this globe, blessing mother Bhūmi at every step. The places you visited and the temples you established will become famous places of pilgrimage. The Temple of the Vedic Planetarium especially will let everyone know who is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and who is His most beloved devotee.

Your literary contribution is astonishing. Your books will provide all the needed information for individuals to become fully Kṛṣṇa conscious, as well as for the respiritualization of the entire society. It is indeed very gratifying and intoxicating to be able to read your books. Never before was it possible to understand the highest philosophical concepts in such an easy way. Now people from all parts of the world have access to the absolute truth because your books are available in practically all languages of the world. Never before were so many religious and philosophical books sold so successfully all over the world.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow us to continue to participate in this wonderful movement and in this way change the demoniac face of this modern society. Let us not be overwhelmed by material desires, for your mission is too important. Let us not become sidetracked, for time is short. Let us not quarrel, for this will give you unlimited pain.

Your servants at Nava-jiyaḍa-nṛsiṁha-kṣetra, Germany.

## New Delhi

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace for giving me unlimited mercy, which I do not deserve.

Presently my service is in New Delhi, serving His Holiness Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja by helping to manage the temple and construct the new temple project. I am very happy to be involved in the construction of this project, for I understand that it was your personal desire and instruction to Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja to establish at least one big temple and ten small temples in Delhi. The estimate cost for the present project is at Rs 21 crores. We have spent Rs 13 crores, and we are coming close to the completion. On behalf of all temple devotees, I want to thank you for your blessings to make this project a success. There is no doubt in my mind that if we continue to follow your instruction we will not only be successful in fulfilling your desire but will also receive your mercy in the form of your instruction for our own benefit, and thus we will become worthy to help give your mercy to many thousands who will be visiting this temple.

We beg to remain your humble servants.

Your devotees in New Delhi, India.  
(written by Trikālajña dāsa)

## New Dvārakā

**The Will of Kṛṣṇa**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on your appearance day!

On June 10, 1976, you said the following in a *Bhāgavatam* lecture here in Los Angeles: “My point is that this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement has come to your country by the will of Kṛṣṇa. So there is favorable situation because the will of Kṛṣṇa is there, that ‘Now the Westerners, especially the Americans, they should become Kṛṣṇa conscious.’ That is His will. So you take the opportunity, cooperate with Kṛṣṇa, and you will be successful. Thank you very much.”
