I am happy to report that the devotees of New Dvārakā are following your instruction, with faith in your vision that it is Kṛṣṇa’s will that this movement spread in America. They are taking the opportunity of this favorable situation to cooperate with Kṛṣṇa, and they are becoming successful.

Morning, noon, and night the temple is crowded with devotees desiring to get the sublime *darśana* of Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Lord Jagannātha, Lord Balarāma, and Subhadrā. Their worship is being conducted at a very high standard. Each day They appear expertly dressed in attractive outfits just suited to the season. Their altars are always decorated with gorgeous flower arrangements. They receive wonderful offerings of food prepared by skilled and devoted cooks. The devotees here are always trying to make the temple more pleasing to the Deities. At present, they are remodeling the entire temple and altar. The paintings in the temple are being cleaned and restored. Your *vyāsāsana* is always the object of care and attention. Each festival day, and particularly your appearance day, is observed with great ceremony and elaborate temple decorations.

Many devotees work at cleaning the temple, inside and outside, and cleaning the Deity paraphernalia. Other devotees are constantly maintaining the temple grounds, keeping the lawns nicely trimmed and the gardens splendid with flowers in all seasons. Still others are keeping all the residential buildings in good repair.

It is very wonderful to see so many devotees attached to their particular services, and carrying them out with such attention and devotion.

Each day, devotees come to your quarters to seek inspiration and solace in this sacred place. Many devotees render silent and invisible service to keep these rooms spotlessly clean and in good condition.

By the efforts of your devotees, there is in New Dvārakā a library of your books and tapes and videos, along with a computer equipped with the database of all your writings and transcripts of your lectures and conversations. Each week in this library, devotees gather to hear your senior disciples speak their recollections of you and your teachings.

In a *Nectar of Devotion* lecture given in Vṛndāvana on November 6, 1972, you said, “Surrender means I must be prepared to execute the will of Kṛṣṇa, not my whims, but Kṛṣṇa’s will. Kṛṣṇa’s will is that you become Kṛṣṇa’s devotee.”

The residents of New Dvārakā are taking advantage of all the opportunities you have given us to become devotees and make progress in devotional service. The temple leaders are always present throughout the morning program, and the devotees are following their good example.

The temple is blessed with many new *bhaktas* and *bhaktins,* whose devotion and dedication to Kṛṣṇa consciousness are an inspiration to the entire community. The temple is also blessed with many senior disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda, whose collective experience and wisdom insures that everything goes on according to your desires and plans. Another source of inspiration is the second-generation devotees who have taken seriously to the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, following the rules and regulations, attending the programs, and rendering various kinds of service, ranging from making garlands to distributing books.

The devotees of New Dvārakā are dedicated to your books above all others. One of the favorite new pastimes of the residents of New Dvārakā is to attend seminars devoted to intensive study of your basic books, such as *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, The Nectar of Devotion, The Nectar of Instruction,* and *Śrī Īśopaniṣad*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the perfect example of a soul completely dedicated to fulfilling Kṛṣṇa’s will. It was Kṛṣṇa’s will that you come to save us, and you did. And it is your will that we stay the course and help others become Kṛṣṇa conscious. You said in a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture in San Francisco on March 13, 1972, “We are executing the will of Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa says that ‘Anyone who takes up this responsibility of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, or God consciousness, he is very dear to Me.’”

Your servants here are trying in so many ways to execute the will of Kṛṣṇa by preaching. Several times a week devotees are going on *harināma* parties to various parts of Los Angeles to chant the holy names of the Lord. Each day parties of dedicated men and women go out to perform a service very dear to your heart—the distribution of your books. Backing up the book distributors are many devotees who support the book distribution effort.

There is also an enthusiastic congregation, the members of which compete to hold *nāma-haṭṭa* programs in their homes each week. Each day, people crowd into Govinda’s restaurant and gift shop to experience the taste of *prasādam* and purchase books and other items for practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Other devotees are working to publish your books and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness through television, radio, the Internet, and other media. Still others are engaged in raising and educating children in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And even this does not exhaust the variety of services being performed for your pleasure here in New Dvārakā.

Perhaps the most wonderful thing is not the external manifestation of these various services, but the inner sense of love for you that each of your servants cherishes in his or her heart. And even more wonderful than that is the sense they have of wanting to love and serve you just a little bit more each day.

We have perfect faith that if we just cooperate with you in fulfilling Kṛṣṇa’s will, we shall automatically achieve the ultimate perfection of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

On behalf of all the devotees of New Dvārakā, I thank you for giving us the opportunity to assist you in fulfilling Kṛṣṇa’s will. I pray that you will be constantly in our thoughts as we execute the various services you have kindly given us. Please give us your full blessings.

Your servants in the New Dvārakā community, Los Angeles, California, USA.  
(written Drutakarmā dāsa)

## New Gokula

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our humble obeisances on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day. All glories to Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda and Their *saṅkīrtana* movement! All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Their empowered messenger bringing Their glories to all the world!

**The Messenger Bowing Down Before You**

In the temple I prayed  
Before your merciful forms.  
I came seeking your aide.  
As my head touched the floor  
I heard an inner voice say,  
“The impossible becomes possible  
by Gaura-Nitāi’s grace.  
The lame man can walk  
and the blind man can see  
if Gaura-Nitāi’s mercy is invoked by a plea.”

So, kneeling, I prayed  
to my most powerful Lords,  
who are Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma  
but in Gaura-Nitāi’s forms:  
“One drop of Your mercy  
is all that I need  
to attain success  
on this Vyāsa-pūjā eve.

To glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda and Your Lordships, I pray  
for perspective and knowledge to use as keys  
to unlock Your mercy so the whole world can see  
how You, Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda, came  
not just into the world five hundred and twelve years ago,  
but into our hearts, with the planting of seeds  
by pure devotional service through Śrīla Prabhupāda’s deeds.

I am here now, with my head on the floor,  
worshiping his Lords with his love to implore.  
O Gaura-Nitāi, You are ours now only because  
Of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s vow: “I will go to the West and preach in English.”

He came with such struggle,  
the world has not seen,  
to bring Your message  
to the spiritually unclean,  
causing us to bathe  
in pure love of God.  
With all of our hearts  
we thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

How, in this life,  
could we ever deserve  
Your mercy upon us,  
and all others in this world?  
O Gaura-Nitāi, You sent us Your love;  
like the cascading Ganges,  
it came like a flood,  
brought by a figure  
imbued, who proclaimed  
that we too could reach You  
by chanting Kṛṣṇa’s holy names.

You would become ours  
as You had become his:  
by singing and dancing  
and honoring *prasāda*.  
He taught us the way  
To attain pure love of God.  
Thank you,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda.

In this historical moment of time,  
where the present and future meet and divide,  
the 101st anniversary  
of one held so dear is here.  
What can we do? How can we say?  
What is in our hearts today?  
Only to You, Gaura-Nitāi, can we pray.

He came with Your message,  
Yet You sent him, we know.  
So between Your sending him  
and his wanting to go  
lies the greatest love  
the world will ever know.  
This love pours like rain  
falling from clouds,  
flooding the world  
with Your loving grace.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are  
love’s own messenger sent;  
the curtain of darkness  
by your mercy is rent.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
thank you again  
for Gaura-Nitāi’s mercy,  
love without end.

Our hearts now are melting,  
and all dirt will be washed away  
in this tide of pure love of God.  
Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Paraṁ Vijayate Śrī Kṛṣṇa Saṅkīrtanaṁ Ki Jaya!

Your servants at New Gokula Dhāma, Vancouver, Canada.  
(written by Padyāvalī-devī dāsī)

## New Goloka

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All of us offer our obeisances unto you on this special day of your Vyāsa-pūjā. We offer our praises unto you again and again, because in you and your mission we find a fathomless depth of inspiration from which we can draw each and every moment in our service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. You are a rare world-teacher who taught us everything about the grammar of the Absolute Truth. As Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself was a Sanskrit grammarian, you are our master of the grammar of devotion, for you brought to us the knowledge and practice of the complete process of *bhakti*. With the grammar you gave us, we can now begin to speak the language of devotion ourselves and make a devotional statement in this world with *ahaitukī bhakti,* “selfless” devotion to God.

A simple definition of “grammar” is “the study of words and their relationships to each other in sentences.” A transcendental grammar is the study of finite and infinite souls and their ultimate and eternal relationships of love, *prema,* within this divine language. Grammar is also “the rules for the formation of words.” While we are learning the rules of grammar in the divine language of devotion, it is called *vaidhī bhakti*. When we become fluent in the grammar of the devotional language, it is called *rāgānuga bhakti*. Whether one is to learn and practice (*sādhana*) a language, or whether one is fluent (*rāgānuga*) in a language, grammar forms the internal structure upon which all communication in language is based, and it is no different for the language of devotion, or *prema-bhakti*.

You have shown us the internal structure of the language of the Absolute Truth in a transcendental grammar that is hardly convoluted and tedious, as mundane grammars are, but is pristine and profound. The grammar of the Absolute Truth is not of a different structure than that of the language we speak, because all language is ultimately intended to express the nature of the Absolute Truth. Therefore, there is a subject, a verb, and an object in all language. The problem with worldly languages, however, is that they are filled with fleeting, impermanent “subjects” and ultimately meaningless “actions” of verbs, for the purpose of gaining fleeting, impermanent experiences or “objects.” Worldly languages represent corrupt dialects that utilize a grammar intended for use in the divine language of devotion. The selfish actions of these verbs of worldly action create karmic reactions for the subject self, resulting in suffering and bondage. This transcendental language requires an eternal subject, an eternal action, and an eternal object, which forms the first requirement of the grammar of the Absolute Truth.

Through the various *śāstras* that you brought to us, you taught us about the subject self. The soul of this world experiences a false self, *ahaṅkāra,* in the conditioned state. Here the soul desires a life of enjoyment, *bhukti,* and instead experiences the futility of it all, as the so-called enjoyment results in suffering. You explain that real happiness and joy are not possible for one who ignores one’s true spiritual relationship with God. For one who speaks the language of this world and of conditioned life, all actions and all objects are intended to benefit the conditioned soul. In Sanskrit, verbs whose actions benefit the subject self have a name, *ātmanepada*. Those egocentric worldly actions intended to benefit the self are those of the “selfish” soul, the soul of desires, *kāmātman*.

You have also taught us that the subject self can be centered instead upon the true self in a liberated state. Liberation occurs when the soul rises above the world of dualities and is situated perfectly in the transcendent self. Here the soul is truly self-centered, i.e., centered on the true self. Yet the language of devotion requires the subject self to be self-less. Thus you have made it clear that liberation, *mukti,* is not a goal in itself but rather a stage in the self’s evolution to its fully realized position. In the liberated state, the self experiences the self-fulfillment of inner contentment in contemplation, but not the self-forgetfulness and utter self-abandonment of ecstatic love, *prema,* in which the self is wholly moved to the divine object in the language of devotion.

Beyond liberation it is possible for further self-involvement as a *yogī*. After the soul experiences the freedom of liberation, *mukti,* it is possible for it to gain various types of mystical perfections, *siddhi*. Once again, the soul is focused upon itself, even if in transcendence, what it can achieve or gain. The self may be aware of the supreme as Brahman or even Paramātmā, but such awareness is still related in a self-involved or self-interested fashion. Therefore, the self that strives for *bhukti, mukti,* or *siddhi* is selfishly involved on some level. The natural qualities of the soul are already those of joyfulness, freedom, and perfection, respectively, and therefore there is no need to strive for these in the fully self-realized state. It is only the self that strives for *bhakti* that overcomes all the selfishness in these strivings and yet achieves the same benefits in a state of selfless devotion to God. In the language of devotion, the soul is not self-concerned, nor is it self-interested, self-centered, self-involved, or, in short, self-oriented in any way. It is now wholly oriented to the divine object, the all-loving and supremely compassionate Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. In Sanskrit, transitive actions which are intended to benefit the object are called *parasmaipada*. The soul knows only these kinds of actions in relation to the supreme object.

In so many ways and in so many words, you have patiently tutored us in this spiritual language: the subject is the eternal *jīvātmā,* who is the devoted soul, the *bhakta,* who is the agent of the verbs of *bhakti,* actions of devotional service to God, for the sake of the eternally beloved object, who is *bhagavān,* the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Here the subject abandons itself for the supreme beloved, and forsakes all else, including itself, for the sake of the Supreme Person. That is why the supreme grammarian Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself prays in the language of selfless devotion, *ahaitukī bhakti,* “O my Lord, please let there be for me, birth after birth, only selfless devotional service unto You” (*mama janmani janmanīśvare bhavatād bhaktir ahaitukī tvayi*).

This is the marvelous and wondrous grammar that you have given to us for learning the language of the self-surrender of love in devotional service, *prema-bhakti*. You taught us and continue to teach us this “grammar” of selfless devotion. As mundane teachers teach students a foreign language by speaking to them in the very language they are learning, you speak to us while immersed in the language of devotion, in order to impart to us the language of selfless devotion. It is rare, exceptionally rare in this world, to find one such as yourself, whose example is so powerful that it is able to open even our ears and our eyes to Your Divine Grace, the very “paradigm” of selfless devotion to God. We thank God for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we beg you, please, to continue to teach us how to speak all day and night, in all circumstances, throughout all of our lives, this language of devotion.

Your loving servants,  
The devotees in the New Goloka community, Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA.  
(written by Garuḍa dāsa)

## New Govardhana (Australia)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

O swan-like sage!

Offering humble obeisances at your lotus feet, we beg for your compassion as we stumble on in shaky pursuance of your pleasure. Through your satisfaction all auspiciousness will ensue. You gave many formulas for such spiritual success, such as distributing your books, developing *varṇāśrama,* and preaching. However, even your basic rules are difficult for us neophytes to follow. Śrīla Prabhupāda, for us to chant sixteen attentive rounds, to follow the basic principles, to deal respectfully with each other, and to serve the Lord lovingly has proved difficult. We are like babies in spiritual life. Thus, lofty plans for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness have been hampered by our own disqualifications.

With our heads bowed at your beautiful lotus feet, which are the abode of all auspiciousness, we beg for your mercy. Please bless us with the sincerity and spiritual intelligence to see that following your basic instructions is the gateway to happiness and success for us all, individually and collectively. Help us to see that these holy names you have kindly delivered are our rescue ropes, brought here by you from the spiritual world to save us. Let us experience the value of appreciating and respecting each other and everyone we come in contact with. Give us the fortitude to follow the regulative principles. Enrich our hearts with the desire to serve the Lord and His parts and parcels.

You possess all good qualities because you are Kṛṣṇa’s very dear devotee. Since your heart is the reservoir of Kṛṣṇa *prema,* it is overflowing with love and compassion. Though our hearts are sinful and bereft of love, we beg you to pour your blessings upon us and to forgive all our transgressions.

Your feeble aspiring servants at New Govardhana, Murwillumbah, Australia.

## New Govardhana (San Diego)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The opportunity to take shelter of Your Divine Grace is the greatest opportunity for which we can ever aspire. The great benefit received by those who approach Your Divine Grace, personally or through your books, followers, movement, etc., is incalculable. As more and more of your books are distributed, countless souls are benefited. As you have informed us, if they read even one word their life will be changed.

Boldly crossing the seas, you delivered Lord Kṛṣṇa’s sacred instructions to every town and village on the surface of the globe. Meditating on the mission of the *ācāryas,* you wrote your Bhaktivedanta purports, thus unlocking all the secrets of this *rāja-guhyam,* most secret knowledge, and making this relishable juice available for one and all.

Your lotus feet, the resort of swans, tinged with the hue of the lotus, appear to be softer than its petals, yet they ceaselessly traverse this earth, spreading everywhere the supreme shelter of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s holy names and trampling down our doubts, which can never remain in their luminous presence.

Moving quickly, as if floating on air, your legs exhibit spiritual opulence normally unseen in this world. This opulence was formerly described by Lord Brahmā in his depiction of Goloka Vṛndāvana, wherein he states that for the inhabitants of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s abode “every walk is a dance.”

Your right arm rising and falling as you offer the *cāmara,* you beam with devotion as you invite and attract Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma to appear in this world. Residing with you in Rāmaṇ Reti, they purify this universe. Suddenly raising your arms high, you plunder the storehouse of love of God opened by Lord Caitanya and His associates. Throwing flowers into the crowd of wildly dancing devotees, you most magnanimously distribute its contents, not caring who is fit or unfit, when or where such distribution should take place. Your divine dancing drowns us with mercy.

Tap...tap....tap.... the most harmonious sound, your graceful fingers deftly striking the keys of the typewriter in your rooms at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, weaving together the inspiration of the Gosvāmīs with your own realizations—a composition so rare and dear that it fully captures the attention of both the Lord and the living entities, thus reviving the living entities’ love for the Lord and enhancing the Lord’s love for those who faithfully hear it.

One hand clutching your cane, the other raised making a point, you soundly defeat all challengers. Tirelessly training your disciples, you quickly dispose of all varieties of cheaters, materialistic scientists, bluffers, rascals, and rascaldoms.

Your lotuslike eyes, laden with the infinite compassion of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, pierce through all of our defenses and touch our souls, reminding us that we are *nitya-kṛṣṇa-dāsa,* that your only desire is to help us regain this position, and that you are there to help us at every step of the way.

Now becoming fiery, blazing with the ultimate righteous wrath of Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva, almost taking away our very breath by the fury of your glance, fully animated, you destroy the Māyāvādīs. This further exhibits your compassion by saving us from the impersonal calamity.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, your qualities and pastimes are vast and oceanic. Like a little bird running along the shore, we are unable even to begin to comprehend the vast magnitude of this nectar ocean, but by your grace this nectar is so sweet, even one drop held in our tiny beaks is sufficient to satisfy us eternally. As you wrote back to one disciple, “You say that you would read only one book if that was all that I had written, so you teach others to do like that.” Please surround us with your followers, who by their words, deeds, and writings increase and enhance our remembrance of you. Please protect us from those who in any way obscure this remembrance.

Your aspiring servants and grandservants in New Govardhana dhāma, San Diego, California, USA.  
(written by Vaikuṇṭha dāsa)

## New Jhārikhaṇḍa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The essence taught in the Vedic lessons  
is that we need our Guru’s blessings.

Only a captain like you can lead us to perfection;  
a boat won’t go forward if each is rowing his own direction.

So better to ask the way than go astray;  
a straight road can easily show the way.

Do right or get left—the Guru’s test;  
surrender to Kṛṣṇa and He’ll take care of the rest.

Our success will come in can’s, not can’t’s;  
without your mercy we haven’t a chance.

Instructions seem like a bitter pill to swallow,  
but they leave sweet effects if we follow.

Let’s show our gratitude by our attitude;  
here in Laguna we’re glad we did.

Your humble servants at the New Jhārikhaṇḍa Mandir, Laguna Beach, California, USA.

## New Kusuma-sarovara

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Our beloved grandfather, please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you and your ISKCON!

Every morning, when we see your picture, our hearts are moved. The look in your eyes expresses your ironlike determination, and your smile is warm like sunshine.

When you set your lotus feet on the *Jaladuta,* this world was changed. You went to bring the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa to forgotten lands, where there was no *harināma-saṅkīrtana*. You went to create devotees in lands populated by impersonalists and voidists. You went to revive the eternal memory of the soul’s relationship with Kṛṣṇa and relieve the misery in the lands of forgetfulness.

When you left this world, your sons and daughters took up your mission. Some of them came to our country to give us your mercy, to let us see the determination that shows in your eyes and the warm sunshine of your smile. Ours was a land of forgetfulness, but you have remembered the unfortunate souls here.

Now it is our turn. We, your grandchildren, must become serious about continuing the mission. We must take advantage of your books and your teachers, and carry your mercy to those who are still suffering the pain of impersonalism and voidism.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us so that we can follow the order that your sons have transmitted to us: “Dedicate your whole lives to distributing Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books.”

We think of this order every day, and when we see the determination in your eyes and the sunshine of your smile, our faith grows and we are assured that by your mercy everything is possible.

We offer our obeisances again and again at your lotus feet.

Your granddisciples in New Kusuma-sarovara, Khasadeśa.

## New Māyāpura

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet again and again.

You had known of this project some time before you visited here. You had seen the photos, heard of the plans, and approved a loan of US$10,000.00 from your BBT funds to make the purchase of “New Bahulaban” possible. But when, in 1975, you first set your lotuslike feet on this now sacred ground, as you stepped out of your car to be greeted by the devotees, your first words were, “Not New Bahulaban, this is New Māyāpura.” Your entourage and whoever could get close gathered close to you, all ears converging, eager but unsure they had heard correctly. In the midst of the general excitement, the roaring *kīrtana* and flowing flower garlands celebrating your arrival, someone dared to ask, “What did you say, Śrīla Prabhupāda?” “New Māyāpura,” you stated categorically. There was general astonishment. As the news rippled through the assembled devotees, ecstatic cries were heard: “Haribol! Haribol! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!”

Now it is twenty-seven years later, after what seems a lifetime of serving Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda-Mādhava and Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, after an unbroken string of blissful Kṛṣṇa conscious festivals and inconceivable happenings; many hundreds of devotees later; with former *gurukulīs* born and educated here now mature adults with responsible lives and children of their own; after many false starts, heart-rending mistakes, and constant reevaluations; after all of that, New Māyāpura’s residents are still valiantly struggling to understand and realize what you expect of the New Māyāpura community.

When did Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu confidentially whisper the name New Māyāpura into your ear? What transcendental vision was revealed into your heart at the time? More likely than not, no one will ever really know the answers to these questions. How are we, your aspiring servants, in our own small way, to serve the divine mission that sprouted from your heart like a beautiful lotus and is now blossoming all over the world, giving shelter to all those who are of sincere heart? How may we assist you in serving your Guru Mahārāja as you eternally engage in increasing the pleasure of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu?

In this dreadful age, impersonalism and voidism are gnawing at the very fabric of life. All living entities are suffering greatly. Look where we might, we find no place of solace, no room for comfort. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the only rampart, the only shelter; all else is but burning devastation and hellish madness. Only if you empower us in devotional service in spite of our blatant disqualifications can we be of assistance to you in realizing your magnificent vision of a renewed, prosperous, and invigorated humanity blissfully engaged in the service of Gopāla, the son of Nanda Mahārāja and Yaśodā.

Against all odds, in the midst of desolation, false religion, and catastrophic atheism, you picked up the standard of the holy name, proclaiming boldly that the golden age of the Golden Avatāra is upon us. Your mighty roar has rallied the devotees of the Lord from all over the world. In the ranks of your stalwart disciples and other faithful followers, we feel very insignificant, like that tiny spider trying to help Hanumān and his powerful associates construct a bridge of stone across the ocean to Śrī Laṅkā for the pleasure of Lord Rāmacandra.

Remembering even the tentative efforts of your servitors and all those years of sincere efforts by all those devotees who struggled here on your behalf, kindly be extra merciful upon us. By your mercy let us raise the profile of this community closer to what you have envisioned, and may the full import of “New Māyāpura” become revealed to the world. On this auspicious day of your appearance, our hearts overflow with gratitude for all you have given us, and we fervently pray that our lives may reflect your timeless teachings.

Your servants at New Māyāpura Dhāma, France.

## New Nandagram

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Due to your mercy, so many people are taking to Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. It is due to your preaching that many are seeking a lifestyle to better achieve this end. The ideal lifestyle is *varṇāśrama,* and you said that *varṇāśrama-dharma* should be introduced all over the world. All over the world your disciples are struggling with this request, but it is this struggle to satisfy your desire that purifies, that will bring us closer to you, that will make us a family.

Your slogan “simple living, high thinking” is the key to our success and also our greatest challenge. Our background of “complex living, low thinking” is hard to overcome. To attempt an agrarian lifestyle is difficult for one brought up on a diet of convenience and instant achievement.

Try we must, and try we will, as this is your desire. Success or failure is not important; to follow your instruction is, for it is by following the instruction of the *guru* that Kṛṣṇa is pleased. Although you are not here physically, please continue to shower us with your mercy, please bless, inspire, and guide us in our endeavors, and please accept our prostrated obeisances at your feet.

May we always remain your servants,  
The devotees at the New Nandagram community in Bambra, Australia.

## New Orleans

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

We here at New Saṅkīrtana Purī may be small in number, but we are becoming BIG in our desire to please your lotus feet.

As our eternal father and teacher, you have taught us that the greatest responsibility of a disciple is to act firmly and obey the representative of Kṛṣṇa, and that one should accept the instructions of the spiritual master as one’s mission in life.

Whatever problems and inauspicious situations we have encountered in the past here at the New Orleans Mandir were primarily due to not accepting the responsibilities you have bestowed on us.

We feel very awkward and incapable of carrying out the mission that lies before us. We are continually wasting valuable time with personal considerations for sense gratification that breed petty jealousies and discontent. In short, we are still neophytes, struggling to conquer the raging senses.

But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us hope, hope that even in this dark concrete jungle called New Orleans your mission—the mission of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī—will continue on despite our shortcomings.

We pledge to continue the sweet struggle of preaching Lord Caitanya’s message to the fallen souls here, and beg for your blessings to have the spiritual strength and purity to surmount all obstacles that inhibit us from following your divine instructions.

Your eternal disciples and granddisciples in New Orleans, Louisiana, USA.

## New Pāṇihāṭī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

By your mercy it is possible for fallen, forgotten souls like ourselves to have an opportunity to come in contact with the message of Godhead, which can deliver us from the cycle of birth and death. The true measure of your greatness is that you have not only personally spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world but have inspired many others to take up this mission as their life and soul. All over the world devotees are working hard day and night trying their level best to push forward this great movement for the benefit of all mankind. How astonishing! How amazing! Even after your departure, so many wonderful developments are unfolding for your pleasure and for the pleasure of all the previous *ācāryas* and the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. New temples are being competed all over the world—in Bangalore; New Delhi; Māyāpur; Lima, Peru; Houston, Texas; Africa; Bihar; Hyderabad; and many other places too numerous to mention. Millions of books are being distributed by the dedicated Vaiṣṇavas who brave the streets of the world on a daily basis to benedict the fallen souls. In general your movement is healthy and strong, and we hope that you are feeling joy, satisfaction, and transcendental pride because of the efforts of your followers.

We here at New Pāṇihāṭī Dhāma are praying for your blessings so that we too might be able to offer you something of significance. Our desire is to establish this temple as a *tīrtha* dedicated to the memory of your pastimes here in 1975. The devotees and members here have decided that under no circumstance will we ever give up your property here in Atlanta. Your visit here sanctified this spot, and now it is our duty to expand the glories of this *dhāma* to all the living entities throughout the universe. What a wonderful opportunity we have here, and we intend to take full advantage of it for your pleasure. Please look over us and give us the intelligence to take shelter of your instructions in our lives at all times.

Your humble servants at New Pāṇihāṭī Dhāma, Atlanta, Georgia, USA.

## New Ramaṇa Reti

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Community:** a body of people living in one place or district or country and considered as a whole; a group with a common interest. (The Oxford Dictionary)

There are many different types of communities throughout the world—religious communities, political communities, communities based on ideals like ecology, *yoga,* or farming, communities that focus on the immediate results, communities that focus on the long-range result. Such communities can be found in any place, city, or country, in any state or union; they may have members of many various age groups and economic backgrounds. Thus there are many varieties to this concept.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, have also established communities—communities located in all varieties of places, countries, and political climates. So why should any notice or interest be taken of such communities? Just as one does not construct a building on a foundation of sand or unstable ground due to its uncertain future, a community that is not based on a strong and solid foundation will also not last. What, one may ask, is the difference in the foundation of these communities you have established? The answer is clear: *guru* and Kṛṣṇa. Your development of communities was not based on lofty political ideals. They were not based on alternative environmental principles. They were not based on the varieties of sense desires. They were based on the eternal and illuminating guidance of the instructions of the Vedic principles concerning one’s relationship through loving service to the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You often admired the fact that men and women of so many different nationalities were happily engaged in serving under this ISKCON banner. Not only different nationalities, but different backgrounds, different natures, different visions. This is due to the wonderful expertise you showed us of how to ignite that dormant service to Kṛṣṇa in one and all.

Here in New Ramaṇa Reti, Alachua, Florida, we are also your community. We rise each day to worship the Deities of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. We attend the *kīrtanas* and listen to a class on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. We observe the festivals of the Vaiṣṇava holidays with great pomp and celebration. We also work very diligently to set an example both for and within the greater secular community of how to live in the God conscious, Kṛṣṇa conscious way you taught. Our members represent you in many different fields to many different types of people, who all come away with greater respect for the society you have built. Additionally, we work very hard to preserve what you have given by educating our next generation in these same principles of Kṛṣṇa conscious living. This, one may say, is the focus of this community of yours. With preschool, kindergarten, and elementary day schools, and *āśramas* for middle and high school levels, we have arranged for educating all ages of your grandchildren. All of this is a testament to your wondrous influence on our lives. Never before have so many of us felt the loving, caring hand and guiding force of someone who wanted nothing in return save and except our becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Even as we prepare to take note of your departure from our mundane vision some twenty years ago, your dominant influence and guiding principles are still holding together communities all over this planet—communities that are not based on sense gratification of so many varieties but on the real goal of human life, which you gave us: going back to home, back to Godhead. For this each of us daily and eternally bow to your feet and beg for your continued and never-ending mercy. Please allow us and help us to remain suitable followers and instruments in your efforts to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Help us to show that life can be lived with the highest moral, ethical, and personal standards if one will but place *guru* and Kṛṣṇa as that foundation stone.

We desire to remain,  
Your humble servants in New Ramaṇa Reti, Alachua, Florida, USA.

## New Remuṇā

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories unto you on this most auspicious anniversary day of your appearance!

Since time immemorial we have been traveling all over the material creation, wandering from one planet to another. Somehow, by the will of Providence, we received the causeless mercy of the Supreme Personality of Godhead from a bona fide spiritual master—Your Divine Grace.

We would like to thank you for letting us engage in some service under your expert guidance. We are sure that simply by following your instructions we will be able to cross the ocean of material sufferings and go back to our original home, the spiritual world.

We are not at all qualified to have this privilege of taking care of one of your biggest preaching centers in the world. However, by your mercy and the mercy of the whole *paramparā,* we pray that you inspire us more and help us maintain a clear understanding to create a climate of trust and safety for increased harmony and cooperation among all the devotees of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṣīra-cora-gopīnātha.

Your servants in New Remuṇā Dhāma, in Toronto, Canada.

## New Śyāmakuṇḍa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you and your ISKCON! All glories to your dear disciples!

We feel very happy to celebrate your Vyāsa-pūjā at this auspicious time. This is another opportunity for our spiritual advancement. By your causeless mercy, transmitted by your disciples, we have had the great fortune to receive your spiritual books. Under your care and help, we are becoming devotees now. We have received a new life. We are on the splendid path of devotional service. What great fortune!

Whenever we associate with you by reading your books, all ignorance and material contamination are cleansed from our hearts and we can taste the nectar of devotional service. You have built a spiritual library and a bridge leading the fallen souls back home, back to Godhead.

You are Kṛṣṇa’s bona fide representative in this world, a perfect example of a spiritual master. History will show that your great contribution will be more and more recognized by the world. Even now, we can see more and more what our spiritual master, your disciple, meant when he said that you have given perfect and ultimate answers to all questions in human life. Anybody who wants peace, love, and happiness can find them in your instructions. Your torch of knowledge is guiding people out of ignorance and onto the right path.

Please bless us that we may follow your transcendental footsteps and broadcast your glories and the glories of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Please bless us that we may always increase our devotional service.

Please accept our humble obeisances again and again.

Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!

Your fallen grandsons and granddaughters in New Śyāmakuṇḍa, Khasadeśa.

## New Varṣāṇā

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’m afraid to approach you. I don’t want to let go of my attachments. I don’t want to be humbled, to be changed. I just want to be seen as a good guy. I know you can cut out all the dirty things in my heart, but I hold back. A coward! A thief who stays at a distance and hides or runs when the law comes too close.

How will I change if during your physical presence I was like this? I’m worse now, being even more fearful to approach, associate with, and reveal my heart to my more advanced brothers and sisters. I only care to associate with my mind or those who tolerate or accommodate my nonsense. I’ll avoid situations which expose me. I know that you, like the sun, which can purify a filthy place, can purify my filthy heart, but I keep myself in the dark.

Now what chance is there for me? Somehow, at least physically, I am around your ISKCON body, God knows how. So many, more sincere than myself, have gone here and there. Even though my mind goes here and there constantly, perhaps I’m too lazy, scared, or proud to drag my body after it. Still, somehow you’ve let me stay, despite my condition.

The quality of a *sādhu*, to cut out the disease in the heart, was fully manifest in you. In your presence you constantly cut (in a loving way) and patched up the sores. As long as there is a pinch of disease, *kṛṣṇa-prema* will not manifest. I want *kṛṣṇa-prema* with a dirty heart. My unfortunate cheating tendency.

Now my intelligence is bewildered, exposed to charlatan thoughts and ideas of spiritual “prostitution.” What solution is there? When will I offer myself to you for operating? In my diseased condition, what use am I to you? But where are you, the perfect transcendental physician? As I open the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and read, “We should discuss *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* daily, as much as possible, and then everything will be clarified, for *Bhāgavatam* is the essence of all Vedic literature. It was written by Śrīla Vyāsadeva when he was self-realized. Thus the more we read *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the more its knowledge becomes clear. Each and every verse is transcendental.”

In your last days with us you spoke these words: “You disciples are all intelligent and experienced men. You can go on managing this movement. If I depart, there is no cause for lamentation. I will always be with you through my books and my orders. I will always remain with you in that way.” “And even if I go, where is the harm? I have given my ideas and directions in my books. Just you have to see it. I think I have done my part, is it not?”

You are still here—guiding, pushing, chastising, cutting, enthusing… Although you have departed (in one sense), the great boat which you brought is still here, and for those who follow the guidelines you are still the transcendental captain. Your books are the torches to light up the darkness. We should be the bearers.

In each page, line, word of your books you are calling out to me (and each and everyone), “Come to Kṛṣṇa.” Again and again, “Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa (without offense), 16 rounds minimum, attend *maṅgala ārati,* go for *saṅkīrtana*, dance, hear *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* study, distribute my books, don’t leave ISKCON, work cooperatively together, follow the vows, worship the Deity as I have shown you, don’t change anything, control your senses, eat only *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* regulate your life, associate with devotees, serve.…” The directions are never ending, but am I making a sincere effort to follow?

As during your presence I stayed at a distance, even now I do not come close, and thus remain cold and dark. Scared that if I come too close to you (in the form of your books) I will have to change, my nonsense attachments will be severed.

You are still fully available, but I run away. A film negative is dull and useless, but by proper exposure it becomes a beautiful picture. By exposing myself to you, I may also become beautiful. But I remain in animal consciousness, running away in fear. How unfortunate! O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please kick me, push me, pull me, whatever is required to expose me, so that I may develop into a beautiful positive, pleasing to you.

You are giving everything. No one but you can save the world (and me). You are the only means for me to cross the ocean, but this fool takes hold of the dog’s tail. If I have a pinch of love for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’ll never leave your movement. Your books will be my life and soul, the association of your sincere followers my protection, your order my breath itself, your words my very sustenance. When will I look nowhere for shelter other than your lotus feet? How else can I think of repaying you for the love you have showered upon me? I am eternally indebted. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants at the New Varṣāṇā farm community, Auckland, New Zealand.  
(written by Janānanda dāsa)

## New Vraja-dhāma (Hungary)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please, accept our humble obeisances! All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Four years ago Somogyvamos was just a little turn-again village, a godforsaken hole. The people here had never heard about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It was just one place among the many where the people eat the animals they feed, fight with their neighbors, and at last die without having achieved any real benefit in their lives. The same sad story as everywhere.

But now Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara are here, flooding the whole country with Their mercy, and here also are Their fascinating palace with its bright colors and other wonderful features, enticing the people all around the area, and here are the devotees receiving the visitors, preaching to them and maintaining the farm. The children on the streets cry “Rādhe-Śyām” upon meeting the devotees. The mayor is satisfied and optimistic, thinking “Maybe the presence of the Hare Krishnas is not just a good business; maybe it will give vitality to the area and cause some changes to take place…”

Some change has begun. And this is your holy mission, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda—to bring some change in the mundane life of the people, to give them a chance to catch a rope extending down from the spiritual world, to liberate the world, especially the Western part of it. So many great personalities appeared in the past—Lord Buddha, Śaṅkarācārya, Madhvācārya, Rāmānujācārya, Lord Caitanya, the six Gosvāmīs, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, and Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī. All of them were very powerful preachers and could easily have done this task. But it was your mission to spread Vaiṣṇavism all over the world. This is your unique position.

And this is our great fortune—that you dropped a rope even to the sentimental Hungarians and that New Vraja-dhāma could manifest almost in a few moments. Now we have the wonderful opportunity to take part in your mission in many ways. We can preach directly to the visitors, give them the *mahā-prasādam* of Their Lordships, and show them how we are protecting the cows, building the *varṇāśrama* system, and creating a spiritual atmosphere—all of which may touch their hearts and plant the seed of the *bhakti* creeper.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda! We are grateful for this matchless gift you have given us, this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. And the way we can show our gratefulness is to carry on with your mission. We beg you for your blessings so that our desire to preach will grow stronger and stronger day by day.

Your servants in New Vraja-dhāma, Hungary.

## New Vraja (Khasadeśa)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Thank you for giving us so much. What you have given us is far beyond anything else in this world. By your instructions we have come to understand that the only path to satisfaction in this world is the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. This is the most sublime and pure knowledge.

Difficulties may come and go in the material world, but as soon as the devotee takes to the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, he can see his destination and relish the sweetness of devotional service at every step. Thus the problems of life in the material world become insignificant. By your incomparable grace, this path of devotional service has manifested itself as the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement.

The *Bhagavad-gītā* says that one should find a spiritual master, but how could we have ever found you, even if we had looked all over the universe? We would not have even known whom to look for. But you are so compassionate that you sent your sons to seek us out and find us.

We did not know the difference between material life and spiritual life. Like so many others, we mistook spiritual life for a better version of the material world, a kind of improved sense gratification. But you so kindly sent your books to teach us the difference between the enjoyer and the enjoyed.

How can we thank you for the matchless gift you have given us? We can only pray for your blessings so that we can stay in the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement forever.

Thousands and thousands of *daṇḍavats*.

Your granddisciples in New Vraja, Khasadeśa.

## New York (Brooklyn)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.22; the sages at Naimiṣāraṇya to Sūta Gosvāmī).**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories to your wonderful personage, all glories to your divine appearance day, all glories to your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, and all glories to your unadulterated pure loving devotional service offered to the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Guru-Gaurāṅga and Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindadeva.

In this dark, iron age of Kali, filled with quarrel and hypocrisy, a fortunate soul is indeed rare and very difficult to find. Everyone is envious of everyone else, people are short-lived, and they have poor memories and insufficient intelligence to save them from Kali’s miseries. The unfortunate *jīvātmā,* bound to multiple lifetimes of suffering by the mundane laws of this horrible prison house of birth, old age, disease, and death, is cast about by the dominant waves of illusion in Māyā’s vast ocean of nescience.

Māyā’s mercy upon the rebellious *jīva,* while appearing cruel, is but a proper reward for the foolish conditioned souls’ forgetfulness of their real occupation, their real friend and master, and their real eternal life of bliss and love with Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the supreme benefactor and well-wisher of everyone.

In *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu quotes a wonderful verse to Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda that appropriately stresses the important effect a great, pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa such as Your Divine Grace has on the fallen, unfortunate souls of this hellish Kali-yuga:

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**“According to their** ***karma,*** **all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.”**

So by your sweet mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have gone from unfortunate to most fortunate in this very lifetime. We have been picked up from the sewer of material sinful life and washed clean by adherence to your simple and sublime instructions. Thanks to Your Divine Grace, even though we were perpetually sad, we have now become eternally glad. Even though we were once quite mad, by your miraculous mercy we are now always sober and dedicated to a lifetime of devotion to *guru* and Kṛṣṇa.

We were lost and now we are found, we were blind and now we can see, and we were dead and now we are alive with eternity, knowledge, bliss, and love thanks to Your Divine Grace’s soothing, enlivening, oceanic mercy of *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

We are thankful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your precious mercy in this lifetime and an eternity more, but we always remember just how fallen and rotten we really are, and we try to become humble servants catching hold of one tiny particle of sacred dust from the path traversed by the great *mahātmā* Sanātana Gosvāmī:

*“nīca jāti, nīca-saṅgī, patita adhama*  
*kuviṣaya-kūpe paḍi’ goṅāinu janama!”*

Sanātana Gosvāmī said to Lord Caitanya,

**“I was born in a low family, and my associates are all low-class men. I myself am fallen and am the lowest of men. Indeed, I have passed my whole life fallen in the well of sinful materialism” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta Madhya*** **20.99).**

O most munificent Śrīla Prabhupāda, we thank you for the saving grace we receive from your loving care, which strengthens us to rise from our fallen position in the lap of the witch called Māyā. Please engage us always in your menial service and don’t let us become perpetual slaves of illusion. Please don’t give up on us, even though we seem to always swerve toward the dark jungle of materialism inhabited by the uncultured aborigines. We want to serve you forever in any menial way you may require, we want to strive to please Your Divine Grace every second of every minute of every day of every year of every lifetime that you may want us here to assist you in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and we want to learn to love Your Divine Grace and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa eternally as our masters and lords, our dearmost fathers, friends, and well-wishers. Please give us one tiny drop of your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so we can attain to the platform of pure loving devotional service in this lifetime and thereby please Your Divine Grace and make you joyously smile and say, “Thank you very much; now just chant Hare Kṛṣṇa!”

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.”**

We pray to remain your insignificant menial servants forever.

Your sons and daughters at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Temple in Brooklyn, New York, USA.  
(written by Rāmabhadra dāsa)

## Panama

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept at your lotus feet my most humble obeisances and the obeisances of all the devotees here.

I have to tell you that today, and increasingly for last thirty-two years, your vivid presence can be felt in the world—in the countries, cities, temples, and hearts of all the faithful devotees who always remember you. It’s so great to feel this, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You have always been with us, giving guidance!

At the start, your instructions seemed drastic and hard to follow. But today those same instructions are the *amṛta,* and the devotees who follow them are very joyous and attain the bliss of serving your divine lotus feet. All glories unto you, O blessed Śrīla Prabhupāda, who have given us Śrī Kṛṣṇa in the form of loving devotional service!

Please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me surrender myself to your lotus feet to serve you for the rest of my days and eternally glorify your transcendental activities, which you perform for the divine pleasure of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa! All glories unto you, who gave us pure love for God and taught us to keep it inside our hearts, inspiring us and encouraging us to return to the supreme place, Goloka Vṛndāvana! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda, I glorify you forever!

Please accept this humble tribute of love and surrender from  
All your eternal servants in Panama.  
(written by Hari-cakra dāsa)

## Paris

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you on this auspicious day of your appearance!

The transfer of a great *ācārya’s* spiritual legacy has always been difficult. What he gives being always much more than any individual can receive, some feuding amongst the heirs naturally arises. In the material realm this fighting is the result of envy and greed and ends in trials and court cases. In spiritual circles, the desire to get more of the transcendental wealth left by the father is something quite positive if it results in the sharing of a better understanding of the heirs’ good fortune.

This understanding requires, however, the essential quality of humility among the peers who aspire to be enlightened by the father with transcendental knowledge. Whenever this humility is shaken, the inheritance—empowerment by the previous *ācārya—*is lost, and suffering, frustration, and mutual criticism take place.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, even in the midst of the raging winds of Kali-yuga, your family has matured in the shade of your lotus feet. No one in this family claims to be the only chosen son of God, or the only recipient of your unlimited mercy. But those who keep confidence in your powerful words are themselves empowered. They deepen their relationship with you and Kṛṣṇa and have a strong faith in the brilliant future of your movement. Even if this faith is threatened on all sides, those who go on serving your movement to the best of their capacity, knowing well that you are the only solace in this dark age, see more and more the vision you had for them and for the congregation of devotees. They don’t claim to be your only successors but feel the urgent need for any person who claims some understanding of your inheritance to first test his understanding in the crucible of practical action. Then only will there develop love for you and your followers.

“If you love me, then love my dog” is a motto you quoted a few times. In all circumstances, the dog remains faithful to his master and seeks his instructions. Loving you means loving your disciples, your transcendental family, and your institution. So many devotees around the world never saw you. But they heard from you and about you, and that is enough for them to take shelter in what you have established. How you make yourself available to hundreds and thousands of conditioned souls is certainly a wonder. Kṛṣṇa gave you the full intelligence to spread His glories widely around the globe, and now Kṛṣṇa helps the worldwide devotees glorify you.

The Centennial year was just the beginning of a worldwide movement of glorification of Kṛṣṇa’s pure representative for the modern world. Thousands of holy rivers bowed down to you in the greatest joy, demigods showered flowers upon you, and devotees dedicated themselves even more to you and to pushing on your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Let us go on glorifying you if we want the blessings of the whole spiritual world upon our heads on this earthly planet, a planet sanctified by the dust of your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!!

Your devotees in Paris, France.  
(written by Nitāi-Gaurasundara dāsa)

## Philadelphia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious occasion of your appearance, we offer our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. It is at the lotus feet of Your Divine Grace we beg to remain, for there is no other shelter we wish to seek within this world.

You have taught us, *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi:* “By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you alone are that pure Vaiṣṇava who, without regard for personal concern, journeyed to what you called “a remote corner of the world” to plant the seed of devotional service within our hearts. By your example you taught a fallen and misguided generation how to cultivate that seed by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and performing devotional service. Knowing our condition, you so expertly made the process of *bhakti-yoga* easily attainable by telling us, “Simply chant, dance, and when you are tired take *prasādam*.” This we were able to do.

You allowed us to become your spiritual sons and daughters and transformed our lives by your words and deeds. You often smiled in delight at our feeble attempts to serve you, and as our loving father you sometimes chastised us to make us stronger. After your disappearance from this world in 1977, it was difficult for many to comprehend performing devotional service without your physical presence. But just as your arrival in America demonstrated your expertise in time and circumstance, your departure from this world, while we were so young, taught us that service to you in separation is the essence of our relationship with you.

In *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* Lord Caitanya asks Śrī Rāmānanda Rāya, “Of all kinds of distress, what is the most painful?” And Rāmānanda Rāya replies, “Apart from separation from the devotee of Kṛṣṇa, I know of no unbearable unhappiness.”

For those of us who were blessed with your personal association, the memories of being in the presence of Your Divine Grace will forever sustain us. By your mercy, though, you are ever-present and continue to guide your disciples and, now, granddisciples. You are still lifting us when we fall and gracing us with your smile when we attempt to serve you. Since your departure, another generation has come to this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and although you are no longer personally present, you continue to plant the seed of devotional service within their hearts as well. We are receiving, without cause, your loving guidance and transcendental blessings. Through this causeless mercy, your spiritual presence may be known. Thus, you offer the shelter of your lotus feet to all who sincerely approach you. You continue to be our ever well-wisher, and on this most glorious of days, we, your disciples and granddisciples, beg to remain forever seated within the cooling shade of your lotus feet.

Your humble servants in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, USA.  
(written by Saṅgītā-devī dāsī)

## Phoenix (Mauritius)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to this very auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā! All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Seizing this golden opportunity, I would like to express some words of appreciation, although it is a very insignificant attempt. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I can never understand the greatness of your transcendental attributes and qualities. Nor will I try to. I can only get a glimpse through your books and dedicated disciples.

I always wonder whether you know me, because I have neither seen you nor met you in person. However, last year my Guru Mahārāja, Girirāja Mahārāja, related a wonderful dream he had about us. Curious as I am, I inquired about that dream. Then he related the whole dream to me. There was a big pandal program in Bombay, and Your Divine Grace was supposed to deliver a speech there. My Guru Mahārāja was also organizing for that festival. And my service was to set up the *vyāsāsana* on the stage. I organized and put a very decorative cushion, which mysteriously disappeared. I anxiously ran to my Guru Mahārāja, inquiring from him whether he knew what had happened to the cushion. He nodded. I was very anxious. Then at one point there were Your Divine Grace and my Guru Mahārāja in a room, and I entered that room to find out about the cushion. Then my Guru Mahārāja’s dream broke. He was wondering about the significance of that dream. Then he told me that Your Divine Grace had accepted me in his entourage!

Needless to say, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how happy and blissful I was when I heard this from his lotus mouth! I became more convinced that you are aware of my insignificant existence in ISKCON. Thanks to your devoted and dedicated son, who introduced me to your divine lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as you selflessly served your Guru Mahārāja, I beg for the same enthusiasm, courage, and determination so that I may also serve my Guru Mahārāja’s attempt to serve your lotus feet. Right now his main service to you is to build a proper temple for Their Lordships Srī Srī Rādhā-Golokānanda in Mauritius. So on this very auspicious occasion of your appearance day, we pray for your mercy so that we can be successful in our humble attempt to please Guru and Gaurāṅga.

Always desiring and praying for your mercy, we beg to remain,  
Your eternal servants in Phoenix, Mauritius.  
(written by Vrajeśvarī-devī dāsī)

## Pretoria

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This is our first offering to you as a temple, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Until now we have been traveling and distributing your books throughout South Africa as our attempt to please you, but recently we have become convinced of the need to increase the number of *saṅkīrtana* devotees in South Africa. Thus we have established this preaching center, and by your mercy the result has been beyond our greatest expectations.

And factually, the credit is all yours. It is you who have written the books. It is you who have created the framework of this ISKCON society and given us the guaranteed process to fill in all the details. It is you who gave us Gaura-Nitāi to inspire us, gave us the holy name to maintain us, and gave us *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* to nourish us. It is you who gave us our spiritual masters to guide us. It is you who gave us this mission of taking Kṛṣṇa consciousness to foreign fallen places, to witness the magic of lost souls re-encountering your worshipable Lord. It is you who so wonderfully followed the previous *ācāryas* so as to preserve the potency of this *bhakti-yoga* process. And this is our offering to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is flourishing even in such a place as Pretoria, South Africa, the capital of the former apartheid regime.

Please be pleased by our humble efforts to reciprocate with you for what we have received from you. We are young devotees, biting off more than we can chew, shooting for the rhino. But people here are coming to know and love Your Divine Grace, Lord Caitanya, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. They are taking the process seriously and making rapid advancement. They are resisting the rejection of their culture, the beatings of their own family members, the ridicule of their peers. They are sticking their necks out to stand up for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because they know you are giving the Absolute Truth. They have taken shelter of you after every other process has been explored, every other avenue exhausted. And they are satisfied, for at your lotus feet they are finding the real thing.

All we have to do is be chaste to your instructions: chant our sixteen rounds, follow the principles, preach at every opportunity, and be careful to maintain your institution. Then this process will work—for us and for them.

Please, if you so desire, entrust us with this service of expanding your book distribution mission in this tip of Africa. Please send us some new *saṅkīrtana* devotees. Please guide us to serve them by training them and inspiring them to increase the pleasure of your Guru Mahārāja. Please overlook our deficiencies and shortcomings and engage us as your instruments as we struggle to execute your desire. Please allow us to serve you in this way.

Your young grandchildren in Pretoria, South Africa.

## Rāḍhadesh

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

“I bow down to the beautiful lotus feet of my spiritual master, by whose causeless mercy I have obtained the supreme holy name, the divine *mantra,* the service of the son of Śacīmātā, the association of Śrīla Svarūpa Dāmodara, Rūpa Gosvāmī, and his older brother Sanātana Gosvāmī, the supreme abode of Mathurā, the blissful abode of Vṛndāvana, the divine Rādhā-kuṇḍa and Govardhana Hill, and the desire within my heart for the loving service of Śrī Rādhikā and Mādhava in Vṛndāvana.”

“O spiritual master, O giver of divine wisdom, O friend of the fallen, you are the giver of your own bliss, and you are the only ocean of mercy. Although dwelling in Vṛndāvana, you have descended for the welfare of fallen souls like myself, and you are preaching divine love of Rādhā for Kṛṣṇa. Please be kind upon me.”

“I am a tiny part and parcel of the Supreme Lord, and I am always and in every way His eternal servant. As I am always hoping for His mercy and kindness, I offer myself to His most dear servitor, my spiritual master.”

On this auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā day, please allow us to meditate on one fraction of your transcendental teachings, by which you have offered us the opportunity to be delivered from the ocean of material miseries.

Throughout your books you teach that

- Kṛṣṇa is the only master
- Kṛṣṇa is the only friend
- Kṛṣṇa is the only proprietor
- and knowing this is the perfection of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*Kṛṣṇa is our only master.*

On a morning walk in Vṛndāvana, Viśāla ran up to the front to ask you a question. “Śrīla Prabhupāda, five years ago you told me that by reading the first nine cantos of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* one will be fully Kṛṣṇa conscious. What does it mean—to be fully Kṛṣṇa conscious?” After rejecting several answers offered by your disciples, you said, “Real Kṛṣṇa consciousness means to know Kṛṣṇa is master and I am His servant; that is real Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” This shows your simple and deep understanding of our real position, and the goal you want us to achieve by following in your footsteps. You are the perfect servant of Kṛṣṇa.

*Kṛṣṇa is our only friend.*

Kṛṣṇa is so much our friend that He travels with us from body to body, witnessing us being tossed around by the modes of material nature, and waiting patiently but eagerly for us to turn to Him. As soon as He notices a slight desire on our part to come to Him, He makes all arrangements to draw us back to His lotus feet. Therefore He sends us a perfect, pure devotee like yourself to save us from repeated birth and death. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the perfect manifestation of Kṛṣṇa’s friendship. You have offered the world the process for bringing all living entities back to their only friend, Kṛṣṇa.

*Kṛṣṇa is the only proprietor.*

In your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 9.18 you mention that Kṛṣṇa is the original source of creation and the ultimate rest after annihilation. Kṛṣṇa is therefore the eternal cause of all causes. And since Kṛṣṇa is also the source of our generation, no one can be a better well-wisher and friend than He. By your teachings you have shown us how to offer ourselves and everything back to the only proprietor, Kṛṣṇa. By our serving you, our spiritual master, and our always being engaged in pleasing you with body, mind, and senses, and by our fully surrendering at your lotus feet, fulfilling all of your desires and instructions, you will take us back to our only proprietor, Kṛṣṇa.

The humble devotees of here pray to be always engaged in loving service to Your Divine Grace. We beg you to forgive our offenses and help us become better servants of you, our perfect master.

Your servants at Rāḍhadesh, Belgium.

## St. Louis

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We take the opportunity of this most blessed day to pray at your lotus feet.  
We pray that you never ignore us even though we neglect you.  
We pray to you for protection from all the material desires in our hearts.  
We pray that you keep us always humble, thinking ourselves a speck of dust at your lotus feet.  
We pray for the intelligence to accept your guidance in our lives.  
We pray for the realization that you are living with us even now.  
We pray for your causeless mercy to be able to follow your divine instructions.  
We pray to you for some desire to serve.  
Most of all we pray to you to give us some taste for chanting Kṛṣṇa’s holy name.

Your fallen servants in St. Louis, Missouri, USA.

## San Jose

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The world was a different place before you came to the West: the West did not have the nectar of *prasādam,* of knowledge, of mercy.

The concept of God was very impersonal: thanks to your wise teachings, we learned to see God as the Supreme Person that He is.

And now your followers are striving hard throughout the whole world to spread the glory of love for God and service to Him.

Now, thanks to your teachings, which you imparted in so exemplary a way, the world is awakening, little by little, to the reality of being and to the understanding of human life.

In the four corners of the planet, Hare Kṛṣṇa has been heard, a book telling about Kṛṣṇa has been distributed, *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* has been shared. Thanks to you, the world’s Kṛṣṇa awareness is on its way! So much has been done in such a short time that the seed of *bhakti* is now growing and fructifying among people of good will.

We have received so much from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that even if we turned the ocean into ink it would not be enough to write of your glories properly.

Every being who has changed his life thanks to your teachings owes you an infinite debt, which he can begin to pay by following them properly, to his eternal benefit.

Once again, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can only try to thank you with the uninterrupted effort of trying to follow your example and teachings.

Your servants in San Jose, Costa Rica.  
(written by Parameśvarī dāsa)

## Scotland

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to this auspicious day of your divine appearance, Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The peaceful, spiritual atmosphere of Vṛndāvana-dhāma, in the vicinity of the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple and Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *samādhi*—such holy places are certainly the abode of saints and *sādhus* such as yourself, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You could have stayed in that serene, sanctified residence of Vṛndāvana, absorbed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Yet, out of compassion you left there and came to this Western world, full of corruption and materialism. You came to deliver the highest truth—like a soldier on a mission in foreign lands.

On the *Jaladuta* steamship, on your way to America, you wrote a prayer: “I have left Bhārata-bhūmi just to execute the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī in pursuance of Lord Caitanya’s order. I have no qualification, but I have taken up the risk just to carry out the order of His Divine Grace. I depend fully on their mercy so far away from Vṛndāvana.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for coming to us fallen souls, who were wasting away in an animalistic society. You came and not only raised us up to the human platform but gave us the greatest opportunity: to become Vaiṣṇavas, devotees of Kṛṣṇa. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are indeed a *paramahaṁsa*—a swanlike devotee who can take nectar out of a dirty place. Thus you gracefully crossed the world and extracted the nectar from the lands of degradation. By your preaching the message of Lord Caitanya, you are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.
