It pierced with intensity and simultaneously caressed with compassion. I felt transparent. You saw the pride and arrogance in my heart. You knew what I was thinking. The next thing I knew, I was face down on the tarmac offering obeisances and watching your lotus feet pass by. That moment has changed my life forever.

Your merciful glance is the basis of many disciples’ stories of glorification of their first meeting with Your Divine Grace. In each case the object of that glance has been personally and profoundly affected. Each one of us treasure that glance as our very own—a powerful reminder of the unlimited potency of your mercy.

The most profound aspect of your glance is that it reaches out for an eternity. Today, so many years later, and despite your physical absence, your glance continues to penetrate my mind as well as caress my heart. Every day of my life I can still feel that you are looking into me, guiding me. You always knew my heart and my mind, and yet you continue to accept my pathetic, proud, and arrogant attempt to serve Your Divine Grace.

In your presence I never had the courage to ask anything of you, not even a simple philosophical question. Now, today, I boldly request that you keep your merciful glance upon me. Never kick me away—keep me close, Śrīla Prabhupāda—I beg you. Give me the intelligence to know how to act and how to serve your ISKCON mission according to your desires.

On this day of your auspicious appearance I bow down thousands of times with great awe and reverence at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

Your servants at Bala Books.  
(written by Prahlāda-priya dāsa)

## Bhakta Program Institute

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious day of your appearance!

Under the influence of illusion we are struggling to survive in this material world, forgetting that we are actually eternal, full of bliss and knowledge. This has been our condition for millions of lifetimes, and thus we are known as eternally conditioned souls. It is very difficult for us to realize our natural position, and out of habit, which has become second nature, we try repeatedly to find some happiness in the desert of the material world. And although we are baffled in all our attempts, still we will not give up trying. In this way our future is not very bright. Seeing our condition, you felt great compassion, and following the order of your Guru Maharaja, His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, you came to the West to save us.

You created the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, which is like a boat traversing the ocean of forgetfulness that is this material world. This Society is most wonderful. A normal boat, after beginning its journey, is no longer accessible to those left behind. It is also usually only in one place at a time, and when in one port it cannot be boarded by people in another port. Arriving at its destination after a long journey, it must retrace its course back to the starting point to pick up more passengers. An ordinary boat has no capacity to minimize the size of the ocean it is crossing.

But in the case of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness we see something quite different: Although many passengers are getting on the boat and departing on their journey back home, the boat is still at the port boarding more. And although all the passengers are on the same boat, some are at the beginning, some at the middle, and some near the end of their journey, while others have already arrived home safely. Although one person may decide to board in Africa, another may board in New York, while simultaneously another is packing his bags and embarking in China, and many others are climbing the stairs up to the deck from all points in the planet. And although this ocean is so vast and expansive that great sages and scientists are baffled in their attempts even to understand it, just by one’s approaching this boat the ocean becomes smaller than the water contained in the hoofprint of a calf. Another wonderful aspect of this transcendental vehicle is that at every port you are there to welcome the new arrivals as they come on board. Your soothing transcendental words pacify the hearts of all the travel-weary lost souls after their long sojourn in the material world.

Actually, this transcendental boat called the International Society for Krishna Consciousness is a spiritual entity nondifferent from the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Out of your unlimited compassion on the fallen souls of this dark age, you have made those lotus feet accessible to all.

Here at the Bhakta Program Institute, we pray that you allow us to be transcendental stewards on your ship. We can help the new arrivals to settle happily into their cabins and get to know and like their fellow passengers. We can remind them that till now they were lost but they have began the journey back home to meet again with their real father. We can teach them how to participate in the activities and that, if they desire, there are many positions available for new crew members. And if we can help them to understand and know the captain of the ship, Your Divine Grace, then our lives will be successful.

We pray that you will allow us to be a part of your transcendental mission and that we may eternally remain as sailors on your transcendental ship, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, ISKCON.

Your servants at the Bhakta Program Institute at New Vṛndāvana, West Virginia, USA.  
(written by Vijñāna dāsa Adhikārī)

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This year it will be twenty years since Your Divine Grace left our mundane vision to return to Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. Your leaving has caused great distress of separation, but we know that by following your instructions we can overcome all distress and feel your mercy constantly. In this way we can understand these words of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s: “He reasons ill who says that Vaiṣṇavas die…” You have not died. You are deathless. Simply Kṛṣṇa has removed you from our clouded vision—vision that is covered over by thick layers of Māyā’s dust. In the last few weeks we have received fresh digital copies of the movies made by Yadubara dāsa during your manifest years on this planet. This is our newest effort to somehow keep you here with us, to view your form and activities and enable people removed from you by time and space to have that moment’s association, to see and hear you giving class, to walk with you on those early morning walks, to develop the same mood of separation and service that we aspire to.

You are our link in the chain of spiritual masters that will hand down to us the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. You being the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, it is your *siddhānta,* your teachings, your example that will be the standard for all devotees around the world for thousands of years to come. There can be no substitute, and your true followers will accept no substitute. We will watch these tapes our whole lives. We will listen to your classes our whole lives. We will read your books our whole lives. We will follow your schedule set up in ISKCON temples and chant the number of rounds you have prescribed our whole lives.

You are Kṛṣṇa’s favorite—*bhakto ’si priyo ’si*. Spiritual advancement means only to become favored by Kṛṣṇa, and one does this only by being favored by you. Your ISKCON exists only so that people in general may become *bhakto ’si priyo ’si*. When we see your ISKCON devotees distributing your books, preaching the philosophy, and taking up your mission, we know that you are pleased, and this makes them into Kṛṣṇa’s favorites.

We pray that we can become worthy recipients of your mercy and that we can direct all our efforts to this end.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Archives.

## Bhaktivedanta Center

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. Unlimited and eternal glories to Your Divine Grace!

It is the disciple’s duty to glorify his spiritual master; however, it is simultaneously his great pleasure. You state that a disciple actually glorifies his spiritual master by acting on the *guru’s* instructions to preach to the innocent and defeat the envious, not by simply paying lip service to him. Hopefully we at the Bhaktivedanta Center can try to glorify you by our attempts at preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

If we had a lifespan equal to that of Lomasa Muni (whose duration of life is that when one Lord Brahmā dies, one hair falls from the Muni’s body, and he continues to lose one hair each time a Lord Brahmā dies, until the Muni is hairless, and only then does he leave his body) we couldn’t even begin to delineate a fraction of your glories, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but we’ll continue to try. Your exemplary life is wonderful to behold, and in particular we wish to recount some of your extremely humble narrations regarding your taking *sannyāsa*. As with *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* you tell of these incidents as they were. Your own words depicting your situation before and after accepting *sannyāsa* may appear to the novice to be describing mundane difficulty, but this is not the case. Your words serve only to increase our understanding of the pastimes of the *mahā-bhāgavata,* whose vision is that everyone else is serving Lord Kṛṣṇa nicely except himself. These events give a glimpse into something of the breadth and depth of the pure devotee’s determination and humble mood. But most of all they endear you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to anyone who comes in contact with you.

Many times you mentioned that prior to your taking *sannyāsa* your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, appeared to you in dreams, imploring you to take *sannyāsa,* but you were horrified. You thought, in general, that Vaiṣṇavas were poor, and as you didn’t want to be poor, you said, “Oh, I cannot do this.” You related that you thought that first of all you would become a very rich man, and then you would use the money for preaching. But you went on to say that Kṛṣṇa made a trick and broke everything, and He obliged you to take *sannyāsa*. You explained that this is Kṛṣṇa’s special favor, special mercy—that He takes away all of one’s money and breaks His devotee’s profit by force, so that the devotee is in a helpless condition and thus turns to Kṛṣṇa. The devotee becomes hopeless and his relatives neglect him; there are no friends and no love from the family, and so the devotee surrenders to Kṛṣṇa. The example you use is that there is a bad boy playing and the father says, “Come on,” but the boy won’t come and begins crying, “No, I won’t go.” But the father’s business is to drag him.

You recollect that your *guru* was so kind to you, and still is, because he forced you to take *sannyāsa*. Pulling your ear, you said, he brought you to this line, although in the beginning you were not willing. You said you were forced to come to the position of *sannyāsa* to carry out the order of your spiritual master, and you took Kṛṣṇa as the only worshipable Lord. You explained that now you could understand how much you were relieved by accepting this life, that you never expected to become a *sannyāsī* and tried your best to keep yourself in this material world, but several times Kṛṣṇa frustrated you and brought you by force to this life, and now you are happy. “I can understand how much favor has Kṛṣṇa showed me. I didn’t understand in the beginning.” As a child you wouldn’t accept any medicine, and so your mother would force it within your mouth with a spoon although you would lock your teeth and resist. You go on to say that in a similar way you didn’t want to accept the *sannyāsa* order but your Godbrother B.P. Keśava Mahārāja forcefully made you drink this “medicine.”

One day when you were living alone after taking *sannyāsa* and were publishing *Back To Godhead* in Delhi, a bull thrashed you, and you fell down on the footpath with a severe injury. You stated that you had “very days of tribulations,” but it was all meant for the good. And so you admonished your disciples, “Don’t be afraid of tribulations, but go forward and Kṛṣṇa will give you protection.”

Humbly you said, “According to the Vedic system one takes *sannyāsa* at the fag end of life, just like I have taken; while I was going to die I took *sannyāsa,* and so something is better than nothing.” Of course, that “something” is wonderful to behold, and is still producing spiritual benefit and variegatedness that are unrivaled. You further declared that a devotee should be one hundred times more active after retiring from family life and should take some responsibility for working for Kṛṣṇa and execute it to his best capacity.

How chaste you have been, and are, to your spiritual master’s instructions! This, you affirm, is the secret of success in devotional life. And how wonderfully you set the flawless example! In spite of what would appear to be insurmountable odds, you persevered, and one of the fruits of your *tapasya* is that we have the International Society for Krishna Consciousness today. Of course, you told us that there is no material impediment to devotional service, but as the *ācārya* you proved the point beyond reproach by your impeccable activities.

We hope that somehow or other we can follow in your footsteps. We only have to take your instructions to heart and act on them, as success is inherent in them. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Begging to remain in your lotus shadow, we remain  
Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Center in Charlottesville, Virginia, USA.  
(written by Amita-ācāra dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Institute

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace, whose wonderful instructions cut our material attachments and purify our consciousness!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You live forever in your instructions (*vāṇī*). Just as the Vedic literatures cover every subject matter and field of knowledge, your instructions cover every aspect of life, material and spiritual. Even the most doggish nondevotees, including ardent materialists and scientists, can attain the perfection of the human form of life if they somehow take shelter of the instructions in your books (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 10.1).

Your instruction to spread this movement all over the world is certainly essential and most auspicious, and when we follow it but do not imitate your preaching methods, we become purified, enlivened, and recognized by Lord Kṛṣṇa, Lord Caitanya, and Your Divine Grace. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you considered the Bhaktivedanta Institute “as the most important preaching arm of ISKCON,” with the responsibility for “increasing the prestige” of your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Your Divine Grace instructed us, “Organize a conference in every major city of the world. I will send you all the men and money you want.” Following this instruction, we organized the Second World Congress for the Synthesis of Science and Religion in January 1997 in Calcutta, in honor of your divine appearance in this city in 1896. Almost 2,000 scientists and scholars came to see you glorified. Locana dāsa prepared a wonderful bust of Your Divine Grace especially for the Congress. In the first B.I. conference in 1977, you said that a bust should be kept in the conference room. The well-known scientist and Nobel laureate Charles Townes offered a garland to Your Divine Grace to formally inaugurate the Congress, and other speakers followed his example.

Lord Kṛṣṇa seemed to have personally chosen and kept a venue for the Congress intended to glorify Your Divine Grace, His pure devotee. We obtained the brand-new 2,600-seat auditorium, with truly international-standard conference facilities, at Calcutta’s prestigious Science City, a campus developed by the government of India and dedicated to promoting scientific consciousness among the general public.

A special session was held to glorify Your Divine Grace on the evening of the third day. Several scholars who had personally associated with Your Divine Grace spoke. Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami, your International Director of the Bhaktivedanta Institute, made the first presentation, detailing your contribution to contemporary scientific and religious thought and quoting extensively from your instructions. Professor S.P. Olivier, who had received Your Divine Grace at the University of Natal at Durban, recalled the great impact your visit created among Indians and others. He gave a detailed appreciative exposition of ISKCON’s various current activities in South Africa, appreciating its Ratha-yātrā festival, the Food for Life program, etc.

Dr. Dora Bazan from South America spoke about the relevance of the Eastern world-view to saving the modern world from its crisis of gross materialism. She characterized Your Divine Grace as the savior from the East. Dr. Gregory Benford fondly recalled his discussions with Your Divine Grace in the gardens of the Los Angeles temple and spoke on the topic “Śrīla Prabhupāda: As I Saw Him.” Dr. N.D. Desai (Śrī Nāthjī dāsa), a prominent businessman from Bombay and an initiated devotee of ISKCON, described your views on business ethics. In the concluding talk for the evening, Jayapatāka Swami spoke on the topic “Śrīla Prabhupāda: Savior of the Western Youth.” He recalled several of your rational arguments that appealed to and attracted the Western youth in the 60s.

The session glorifying Your Divine Grace was greatly appreciated by the entire gathering of 2,000 scholars. Dr. Durwood Foster, a very eminent Christian theologian and an invited delegate to the Congress, remarked, “I never expected that the Prabhupāda Session would have so much scholarly content. I expected some formal words of praise, but ended up learning a lot about the important contributions of Śrīla Prabhupāda, as well as the philosophical depth and international stature of the ISKCON movement.” Dr. Foster was so much inspired that he wanted Your Divine Grace represented at an upcoming conference on the shapers of the world religions.

Prof. Edward MacKinnon perhaps best summed up the achievement of this conference on the occasion of your Centennial appearance in the following words: “The threat to the environment and ecology is the most pressing problem facing humankind today. It is very clear that this problem cannot be solved unless we work out a unified view of the world where man and nature have more in common than what separates them. It is very clear from the presentations at this Congress that the Vedic literatures have the most comprehensive unifying relationship between man and nature. How will other religions and science incorporate this point of view?” How could a scientist trained up in steep materialism come up with such a conclusion? It can only be understood as your divine mercy.

By your own desire and blessings, we have commenced an M.S./Ph.D. program for Consciousness Studies in Bombay in collaboration with Birla Institute of Technology, Pilani, Rajasthan. Prominent scientists, educators, and political leaders came for a one-day conference on the theme of “Science in the 21st century,” which was held as part of the inauguration of the first part of this program. This event was well appreciated as another achievement of Your Divine Grace and received nationwide publicity. With Lord Caitanya’s blessings, through this M.S./Ph.D. program we hope to train an army of qualified students to present your wonderful teachings appropriately in academic journals and publications.

A devotee of mother Ganges worships her with a few drops of water collected from mother Ganges herself. In the same way, we can also worship you only with the very same things you have so kindly given us. We pray that Your Divine Grace may kindly accept our endeavors as an offering.

We look forward to the coming years with great anticipation, confident we will see your desires and plans manifested in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, which was transplanted by Your Divine Grace into the Western world and then spread all over the world.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute in Mumbai, India, and Oakland, California, USA.

## English Padayātrā

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

I was traveling on a train to Vṛndāvana last October when someone asked me, “Swamiji, can you show me a miracle?” I never thought I’d get such a question. I know you were once asked this. So I replied, “You really want to see a miracle?” He nodded enthusiastically. And I pulled out a photograph of *padayātrā* in Kenya (our last stop). “Look, this is Gaura-Nitāi and my Guru Mahārāja riding on a bullock cart with African *bhaktas* chanting and dancing. You’ll never see a greater miracle.” The man was really impressed. “This is indeed the greatest miracle,” he replied, nodding profusely. Then he went off, returning minutes later with our breakfast.

It was you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who puts on the magic shows. Padayātrā in one hundred countries by the end of 1996—completed. I’ve seen plenty of magic—there was never a shortage of magic. We worshiped your *mūrti* from Belfast to Moscow, finding ourselves in the wildest of situations.

Once in the desert of Jericho, Palestine, we approached a PLO refugee camp. About twenty men came forward and picked up rocks. Nowhere to run! Just keep chanting! Somehow the first man smiled, banged his rocks together like *karatālas,* and danced, followed by his companions. There we were, dancing in the desert in a country where almost everyone carries a gun—protected! And your mercy was flowing, even in this lowest of places, Jericho. (Situated next to the Dead Sea, Jericho is geographically the lowest town on the planet, being almost 1,300 feet below sea level.) You are giving the mercy, and we can distribute that mercy and simultaneously receive it automatically. This is our process. “It’s so simple that you’ll miss it,” you warned us.

Nineteen ninety-six is over. Belfast, June 1, 1990, now seems so far away. But instead of reminiscing too much, better we plan for the glorious celebration of your 108th appearance anniversary. 2004 ki jaya! Hitch up the bulls! All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants on the English Padayātrā team.

## Festival of India

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glory to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

We, the Festival of India crew members, past and present, wish that we could fully express the feelings of gratitude we feel toward you. Unfortunately, even volumes of the finest language of appreciation would never begin to approach repaying you for your kindness. As long as your instruction that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is a life of festivals stands before us, we will never be able to conclude our service to you.

Yes, festivals are the way of life for all devotees. Yes, we thank you for allowing us to serve all the devotees. By your divine grace we most certainly have the greatest of all boons—a chance to serve the Vaiṣṇavas. Saving us from our egotistical nonsense ideas, you give us seemingly unlimited opportunities to do the needful. The words “thank you” will never be enough.

Qualified by your mercy only,  
Your devotees who work with the Festival of India.  
(written by Phāṇi Bhūṣaṇa dāsa)

## Food for Life Global

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam*!

I want to sincerely thank you for blessing us to continue with our Food for Life services. By your mercy, we successfully inaugurated Feed the World Day on November 23, 1996. All the devotees enjoyed themselves, and we are now looking forward to observing it every year.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, there is no doubt in our mind as to the value of this service. We’re all convinced by your statements and encouraged by the on-going realizations we get as we serve. That is the nature of spiritual activity. Serving Kṛṣṇa’s *prasādam* is certainly a highly spiritual activity. In that sense, your *prasādam* distributors are just as important as your book distributors. I say this with confidence because I know that you said, “Spiritual life begins with the tongue.” Besides that, honestly speaking, there would not have been a Hare Kṛṣṇa movement if it weren’t for *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*! I dare say, there would not be a devotee on the planet today if it weren’t for *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*!

*Prasādam* is Kṛṣṇa’s special mercy, and we all feel so fortunate to be your instruments in dispersing that mercy to the people of this world. Our hope is that in doing so we will become dear to Kṛṣṇa and Your Divine Grace, and thus confirm our “ticket” back to the spiritual world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the years go by our appreciation for your urgent and relevant message increases; simultaneously, we are becoming more and more disgusted with this material world. There is so much pain and suffering in this world, and it would seem that only a madman would want to remain here. But it wasn’t your transcendental mission to provide some magical escape route from this prison house of the material world. On the contrary, you came to transform, to re-unite the Lord’s energy with loving devotional service. Your mission was not the restrictive and narrow-minded, exclusive kind, nor was it the watered-down, wishy-washy inclusive approach. Rather, you came, heralding the authority of the *Vedas,* to offer a facility for all to engage in the loving service of the Lord, regardless of caste, color, or creed.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we feel honored to be members of your *prasādam* distribution mission, especially at this time, when the world needs the mercy of the Lord more than ever. And who knows what lies ahead for the next few years!? Comets, solar and lunar eclipses—so many inauspicious signs are there. Speculation abounds! Whatever happens, though, know for certain, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we are ready, like dutiful soldiers sleeping with one eye open, awaiting your command—prepared to lay down our lives for the sake of the innocent.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, last year, one of your grand disciples lost his life while delivering *prasādam* to a hospital in Grozny, Chechnya, during the war. His name was Andrew Sivitsky from Novgorad, Russia. Andrew had only joined the front line two days before. He wasn’t a prominent Vaiṣṇava, nor was he a significant member of the Food for Life team. But he wanted to help people and he felt that the best place to help people was in Grozny. In honor of Andrew’s sacrifice, the Grozny city council erected a memorial. On behalf of the Food for Life devotees around the world, we beg your blessings upon Bhakta Andrew Sivitsky.

Our dear master, please also bless us to remain faithful, strong, and fearless in your emergency mission to save the world!

Finally, Śrīla Prabhupāda, last year Food for Life Global, with the assistance of the Chintamani band in Russia, offered Your Divine Grace a special audio CD glorifying *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. We are happy to announce that that CD is now available to the devotees. We would like to finish our homage this year with the words to one of the songs on it:

**A Prayer to Prabhupāda**

Prabhupāda, we are your servants—  
Food for Life family.  
Meditation, inspiration—  
You are everything to me.

Prabhupāda, we’re here to serve you—  
Your mission is so bold.  
Save humanity from insanity—  
No discretion you had told.

Prabhupāda, you told us clearly—  
“Feed them with all your might.  
I will pay for, don’t you worry—  
Everything will be all right.”

Prabhupāda, we see your vision—  
You showed us how to love.  
Expand *prasādam*—don’t be meager—  
Thy provision from above.

Prabhupāda, you have the answer—  
Unite the world through the food!  
Global family—stop brutality—  
Let *prasādam* set the mood.  
It’s proficient and sufficient, to qualify us all...

To return home, back to Godhead,  
In this life, if you will.  
Such a blessing—it’s depressing—  
That we cannot see it still.

Prabhupāda, we need your mercy—  
To carry out your plans.  
“Kitchen religion”—secret mission—  
Please help us understand.

Prabhupāda, you have the answer—  
Unite the world through the food.  
Global family—stop brutality—  
Let *prasādam* set the mood.

Prabhupāda, you are so dear to—  
Rādhārāṇī-gopī—  
chanting dancer, cooking was Her—  
favorite activity.

Prabhupāda, we long to please you.  
Prabhupāda, we long to please you.  
Prabhupāda, we long to please you.

Your servants, the prasādam distributors at Food for Life Global.  
(written by Priyavrata dāsa)

## Hare Kṛṣṇa World

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your merciful lotus feet.

Thinking back to my early days in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, when my service, like that of so many of your followers, was going out with a bag full of your books, I remember how I used to pray for your mercy to help spread this movement. I felt my little part was an arduous task that I was unqualified for.

Yet it seems that my service then was simpler than what I’m currently inspired to do for you. Like many others who joined your Society, I am becoming aware of the social services required to keep your followers and their children centered in Kṛṣṇa consciousness in your ever-expanding movement.

This newspaper of yours, *Hare Krishna World* (do you like the new name?) collects a variety of information that involves all aspects of devotees’ lives. There are letters, editorials, announcements, and news stories about every aspect of instituting a healthy, well-rounded Vedic society. Just this most recent issue has addressed the value of *bhakti-śāstrī* courses for adults, *gurukula* education for children, the scientific study of Vedic culture, caring for elderly devotees, establishing self-sufficiency through *varṇāśrama-dharma,* protecting cows and employing oxen, increasing understanding with other religionists, honoring great Vaiṣṇavas, worshiping the Deity, and promoting enlightened leadership throughout the world.

These days I find myself caught up in the need to help establish a New Ramaṇ Reti school board, to educate our youth and protect them from abuse, and to engage teenagers in activities that are interesting and enlivening so that they will always see their future in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I am involved in working with single mothers who need to be able to support their children and also give them proper attention. These efforts I offer to you with devotion.

These devotional activities help me realize that I am just a common laborer working on constructing the house you have engineered and are in complete charge of. In your role as grand architect you have known all along about the many different roles we would all have to play to build it completely. I had no idea of your great design. Who of us had eyes big enough to see it all? Still, you keep building, adding more to facilitate all the members of your spiritual society at all stages of life. I am just getting glimpses of it, Śrīla Prabhupāda. There is no question that you are present to guide this construction, and that you have remembered the needs of everyone to help them progress in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

I am still unqualified to fulfill the roles required of me. I am counting more than ever on your inspiration, instructions, and direction to carry out your desires in a way that you may find pleasing. There are many problems to solve on the way to seeing this house completed, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please give us all the understanding, devotion, and courage necessary to do whatever it is you want done so that every devotee is clear on his or her duties, and so that each is respected and valued for his or her contribution to the house you built, in which the whole world can live.

Your servants at the Hare Kṛṣṇa World newspaper (formerly ISKCON World Review).  
(written by Sarva-satya dāsa)

## Institute for Vaiṣṇava Studies

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept my most respectful and humble obeisances at your glorious lotus feet. All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

With joy I address this Vyāsa-pūjā offering to your lotus feet, with a pleasure that springs from my gratitude towards Your Divine Grace.

Your compassion for all people, your faith in the holy name, and your heartfelt, constant prayer that we all dedicate ourselves to chanting the *mahā-mantra* conquered me from the start and awakened me to spiritual life. Today, more than twenty years later, because of your mercy, this same chanting of the holy name, no longer merely a duty I am bound to by the holy *dīkṣā* vow, has become my best companion in the early morning and the very essence of my daily life. Thank you infinitely for this immense gift. Please accept my grateful sentiments and always keep me engaged in service to Your Divine Grace and the holy Vaiṣṇavas.

Your faith in the sacred Vedic scriptures, your constant commitment to translate and commentate them, the superhuman effort you undertook so that these could be translated, published, spread, and taught throughout the world—all these have instilled in me a deep-rooted love for the same. My faith in the *śāstras,* in the process of *bhakti,* and in Śrī Kṛṣṇa has gradually taken form and gained strength thanks to your books, your lessons, and your constant example of a *paramahaṁsa*. Please accept these grateful sentiments and always keep me engaged in serving you and the holy Vaiṣṇavas.

Your faith in the visible forms of God, Śrī Mūrti, the rapt and constant adoration that you offered Them, your heartfelt preaching to explain to us Their significance and transcendental position, the superhuman effort you sustained so that they could be installed and adequately adored on the altars of our temples throughout the world—all this has instilled me with faith, love, and abandon at Their lotus feet. Please bless me so that I can purify my heart, and keep me constantly and joyously engaged in the service of Them, you, and the holy Vaiṣṇavas.

Your faith in pure loving service offered to God has unveiled for me a spiritual /alchemical process through which I, a common mortal, have learned to transform ordinary food into God’s mercy, *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. Your teachings on the absolute value and prodigious purifying force of food offered in sacrifice to Śrī Kṛṣṇa have instilled me with joyous faith. Your very pronunciation of the word *prasādam* is unforgettable for me and evokes sentiments of joy and reverence in me. Your satisfaction in honoring and seeing devotees honor holy *bhāgavata-prasādam* increases my appetite, spiritual pleasure, and desire to nourish myself only with this. May the unlimited power of God’s mercy in the shape of spiritual food purify my heart, keep me constantly in a sentiment of increasing gratitude toward you, and always joyously engage me in service to you and the holy Vaiṣṇavas.

Your unshakable faith in the words of your *guru* and Śrī Kṛṣṇa and your compassion for the conditioned souls have modeled your life in a constant wonderful crescendo of inspired preaching activities. You tirelessly traveled through all the continents of this vast planet to bring Kṛṣṇa’s message to the unaware and to false scholars: with your constant and exemplary behavior you spread the ancient science of *bhakti* among the “civil” atheists of Kali-yuga. Although you were constantly traveling all over the world, your pure spiritual consciousness always remained focused on Vṛndāvana-dhāma, at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, merged in Their holy names, qualities, forms, teachings, and divine pastimes. Please accept my humble and grateful prayers and always keep me engaged in serving Your Divine Grace and the holy Vaiṣṇavas.

With the wish to better serve you, we remain  
Your servants at The Institute for Vaiṣṇava Studies (Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta), Italy.  
(written by Matsya Avatāra dāsa)

## The International Society for Cow Protection

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to you, our father, teacher, guide, and protector! All glories to you, the savior of our souls and the messenger of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s affectionate pastimes with his foremost and favorite animals, the cow and bull!

In your books you have described how Lord Kṛṣṇa began His earthly pastimes in the family of cowherds. As a young boy He spent His time in the company of His cowherd friends, herding and protecting the calves, cows, and bulls in the pasturing grounds of Vṛndāvana. Lord Kṛṣṇa was first and foremost a cowherd and secondly the son of the king of Vraja, Nanda Mahārāja. The spiritual, economic and cultural needs of Nanda Mahārāja’s community were centered on the protection of the animals who provided them with their most important foodstuffs: milk, its by-products, and grains.

You described how the udders of the cows were dripping with abundant milk, how the calves suckled gently of their mothers’ milk, and how the bulls plowed the agricultural fields, producing grain. You, our spiritual father, described to us, your fallen children, not only the entrancing pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s early life but a productive, economically secure, spiritual lifestyle. In *Kṛṣṇa* book, Chapter 5, you inform us:

It is also significant that Vasudeva inquired about the welfare of Nanda Mahārāja’s animals. The animals, and especially the cows, were protected exactly in the manner of one’s children. Vasudeva was a kṣatriya, and Nanda Mahārāja was a vaiśya. It is the duty of the kṣatriyas to give protection to the citizens, and it is the duty of the vaiśyas to give protection to the cows. The cows are as important as the citizens. Just as the human citizens should be given all kinds of protection, so the cows also should be given full protection.

Vasudeva continued to say that the maintenance of religious principles, economic development and the satisfactory execution of meeting the demands of the senses depend on cooperation among relatives, nations and all humanity. Therefore, it is everyone’s duty to see that his fellow citizens and the cows are not put into difficulty. One should see to the peace and comfort of his fellow man and the animals. The development of religious principles, economic development and sense gratification can then be achieved without difficulty.

You gave us the solution for a perfect life: protect our fellow man and animals, especially the cows. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your position as the servant of Lord Kṛṣṇa, beseech Him to bestow upon us the intelligence, strength, dedication, and good fortune to protect the cows by successfully establishing Vrajapura Farm, ISCOWP’s own ox-power facility. The purpose of this facility is to show an agrarian lifestyle based on the protection of the cow and bull and to distribute the knowledge of ox-power through seminars, one-to-one instruction, literature, videos, and e-mail.

Vrajapura is the name given by you (in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.5.6, purport) for Nanda Mahārāja’s residence. ISCOWP has just begun to restore and build Vrajapura Farm for your pleasure, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Its fifty-seven acres of pastures, woods, numerous springs, and a creek will provide annual shelter for at least twenty cows and bulls and be the site for spreading the knowledge of ox-power. With the help of its members, ISCOWP desires to follow the example of Nanda Mahārāja by giving all protection to the cows, who, you have told us, are to be considered as important as the human citizens.

One may ask, Should we think that the description of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s boyhood is a story whose major importance is as a bedtime story for our children? Or, like many contemporary children’s stories, is there also a major message for society in the story’s telling? The answer is in your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (8.24.5 purport):

When Lord Kṛṣṇa appeared, He purposefully became a cowherd boy and showed personally how to give protection to the cows and calves. Similarly, He showed respect to Sudāmā Vipra, a real *brāhmaṇa*. From the Lord’s personal activities, human society should learn how to give protection specifically to the *brāhmaṇas* and cows. Then the protection of religious principles, fulfillment of the aim of life and protection of Vedic knowledge can be achieved. Without protection of cows, brahminical culture cannot be maintained; and without brahminical culture, the aim of life cannot be fulfilled. The Lord, therefore, is described as *go-brāhmaṇa-hitāya* because His incarnation is only for the protection of the cows and *brāhmaṇas*. Unfortunately, because in Kali-yuga there is no protection of the cows and brahminical culture, everything is in a precarious position. If human society wants to be exalted, the leaders of society must follow the instructions of *Bhagavad-gītā* and give protection to the cows, the *brāhmaṇas* and brahminical culture.

Your servants, in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s cows and bulls, at the International Society for Cow Protection (ISCOWP).

## ISKCON Foundation

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Prabhupāda’s Victory**

(A Poem for Future ISKCON Leaders)

Come, my children, and hear the tale  
Of Prabhupāda’s victory, and how he sailed  
Alone to challenge the godless West  
And give the world Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

’Twas in Calcutta, under British rule,  
Our young Abhay was fresh from school,  
Dressed in khadi and newly wed.  
“Come meet this *sādhu*,” his best friend said.

Bhaktisiddhānta, straight and tall,  
Made his point (he never stalled):  
“You have Western education;  
Preach for Kṛṣṇa in Western nations!”

Abhay replied, “But who will listen?  
Our country is still ruled by Britain!”  
His *guru* said, “No ruling state  
Can make Caitanya’s movement wait.”

Capturing his *guru’s* vision,  
Abhay took up Caitanya’s mission.  
Some forty years then hurried by.  
A Swami now, the day arrived

When, standing on a weathered deck,  
He ventured west and turned his back  
On all familiar things he’d known,  
His friends and his Vṛndāvan home.

On rolling sea, in tiny berth,  
No place to stand on solid earth,  
His stomach failed, and then his heart.  
Would journey’s end come at its start?

The man quite small, the sea gigantic,  
But Kṛṣṇa calmed the harsh Atlantic,  
Carrying his precious son,  
As Matsya bore the *Vedas* on.

What was to come, no one could tell,  
For one who would march straight through hell  
To speak what his *guru* had spoken,  
Disciplic chain to stay unbroken.

In Boston’s bay he wrote a poem:  
“Lord Kṛṣṇa, I am far from home.  
For you, my Lord, I take this chance;  
Now kindly make your puppet dance.”

First to suburbs, then to town,  
To Bowery bums lying on the ground,  
To roommate mad on LSD,  
The Swami went on fearlessly.

Beneath a massive, spreading oak,  
The humble swami boldly spoke:  
“Free yourself from *māyā’s* pain  
By chanting Kṛṣṇa’s holy name.”

Those who heard were precious few;  
First came one, and then came two  
To join with his unaided voice,  
To chant the Name and to rejoice,

In tasting food he called “*prasād,*”  
Encountering a personal God,  
Relishing the *kīrtan’s* beat,  
Sitting at the Swami’s feet,

They rode with him to spiritual heights  
And spent such blissful days and nights  
That happily they gave up sin  
And vowed not to take birth again.

The Swami took these youthful hearts,  
Gave them temples and farms to start,  
And books to print for BBT,  
To benedict humanity.

Chanting and dancing, madness cured,  
Growing happy, calm, and pure,  
His students called him Prabhupāda,  
Servant at the feet of God.

Like Vāmana, of giant steps,  
Prabhupāda flew east and west,  
To India he brought his men  
To make the Indians proud again,

Restating what Caitanya taught:  
*Bhakti* for all, Brahman for naught;  
Complete with potent references  
To the *Gītā As It Is*.

His followers from foreign nations  
Caused an Indian sensation.  
Projects sprung up by the score,  
The largest in Śrī Māyāpur.

In just ten years he spanned the world!  
The ISKCON banner he unfurled  
Flew high and proud in every nation.  
His books sold in local translation.

His teachings not to be ignored,  
He turned the faithless to the Lord,  
Installing Kṛṣṇa’s Deity,  
To serve with pomp and purity.

None could equal his success  
Promoting Kṛṣṇa consciousness.  
Completing his great victory,  
He went to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

Prabhupāda said though we must die,  
We’ll find ISKCON in the spiritual sky.  
Prabhupāda is there, and Kṛṣṇa, too.  
I’d love to join them. Wouldn’t you?

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the ISKCON Foundation.  
(written by Kalakaṇṭha dāsa)

## ISKCON Prison Ministry

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my heartfelt respects.

You are preaching so vigorously all over the world. Your ISKCON is doing so much to re-awaken the Kṛṣṇa consciousness of lost *jīvas*. I am so proud of your disciples and granddisciples. I am honored to call them my Godbrothers and Godsisters. For me your ISKCON continues to be a preaching adventure. And it’s especially exciting when we receive such letters as these from prisoners:

**Four new guys!**

Kṛṣṇa is really giving me the mercy, 4 new guys came through who are now reading Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books. It is really a pleasure I tell ya to walk by some guy’s cell and see ’im with one of Prabhupāda’s books opened up in his face.

Abhimanyu dāsa

**The Best Therapy**

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda! I am very sorry for my bad writing, but they give me so many pills a day, I am like a walking drug-store. The only good thing about my chanting is that four different doctors, at different hospitals I have been in, have said they considered the chanting on *japa* and reading Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books as a better form of treatment than that of Western medicine and psychotherapy. However, this is not the case here, at least as far as Kṛṣṇa consciousness is concerned. I will start on my homage to Śrīla Prabhupāda; however, I think I will not be able to finish it at the end of sending this letter, until I can personally reflect on the last 22 years since I read his first book. I still read every day for at least 2 hours or more a day.

Hare Kṛṣṇa!  
Your servant,  
Citta Hare dāsa

**Opening New Doors**

I received the newsletter and find it very informative and would like to receive more. The bottom line is that ISKCON has opened a door to a new dimension for me that I see through dimly for lack of a better word. The knowledge and understanding is great since I’ve heard about and sought after it. Thank you, until I hear from you again!

Hare Kṛṣṇa!  
Bhakta Wayne Groves

**Dancing in Slovenia**

We have visited two more prisons, including one female prison, wherein 20 women showed up, and a correctional facility for youngsters. Afterward everyone took prasadam and they became so enlivened that we all danced together. At present I am working on approaching more prisons which have not already responded to our invitations.

In October we donated 23 books, 6 music tapes and kanthi mala neck beads.

The DOB at Mirna is the largest prison in Slovenia. If the administration allows, we will visit there for two weeks.

ISKCON Prison Ministry Slovenia  
Bhaktin Ana Kocijan

**Thanks for ISKCON**

I’d like to take this opportunity to thank you for the Krsna literature and japa beads. I appreciate the newsletter you send to me. I’d like to do some art work for you. I’ll do what I can. I’d also like to tell you what a big help ISKCON has been for me here. It has given me a new perspective toward life and a sense of pure virtues I need to guide my life by. Thank you for everything. I am indebted to you.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.  
Jeffrey Logan

**A Special Parole**

Sorry it took so long to thank you for the books. I was very excited when they called me down to pick them up. I thought later that it was just like they had handed me my parole papers. So thanks for the parole.

There are 8–10 of us who get together now.

There are still many demons for me to overcome. Still, with chanting and focus on the Lord, I know I can overcome them.

Bhakta John O’Shea

\* \* \*

You’re wonderful, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Most prisoners on this planet are busy trying to become first-class prisoners through economic development. Here at ISKCON Prison Ministry we aspire to become free from the shackles of this material world and become pure devotees. Please don’t give up on us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We are weak and unfortunate. Please make us fortunate and bless us with your smile and your merciful glance.

Your eternal servants and inmate yogīs at ISKCON Prison Ministry.  
(written by Candraśekhara dāsa)

## ISKCON School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

While certainly we feel unqualified to serve you in what you called “the most important project,” we hope that you will kindly give us the real qualification—to give our students pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you have given it to us. Please benedict us so that our students will feel so inspired by your instructions and example that they will gladly dedicate their lives to Lord Caitanya’s mission.

Your servants at ISKCON School, Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA.  
(written by Ūrmilā-devī dāsī)

## ISKCON Television (ITV)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.**

**Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

Even the sophistication of an advanced computer program does not recognize the word “impersonalism.” Its artificial intelligence signals its unfamiliarity by drawing a squiggly red line under it. Yet, gradually, by your mercy, this expression, like “Hare Kṛṣṇa,” will become a household word. After many years in the Movement, I have simply taken this term for granted. I use it in my preaching as if everyone should already know what it means. But I find myself having to explain the purport of “impersonalism” to people just beginning to become familiar with you and your teachings. When I use your examples, I can see them nodding their heads in understanding. “Just like you have a father, and he has a father—and they are all persons; so, like that, God, Kṛṣṇa, He is the original father, and He is the original person, not that He is ‘imperson.’”

Your presentation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is so uniquely simply and sublime. Practically anyone can understand once they hear your message. Sometimes I think that I can express the philosophy with my own examples, but they don’t seem to have the same effect. So I find myself endeavoring to remember and repeat all that you have explained. You do it so perfectly. Why should I try to change what you have illustrated and stated so perfectly. My favorite explanation of yours is the very first one that I can remember: “Look at your hand? Are you your hand? Are you your leg? Are you your nose? Who are you? You are the soul!” What relief I felt after looking at my hand, leg, and quickly crossing my eyes to get a glimpse of the tip of my nose. Whew! I am not this body. Spiritual life had just begun.

As I continue to read and review your purports, lectures, and conversations, I increasingly appreciate not just what you say but how you say it. Your uncompromising politically incorrect presentation of the Truth is decisive and bold: Women are less intelligent; the moon is farther from the earth than the sun; family life is skin disease. Talk about controversial! What to speak of your sense of humor:

**Anchorman:** Why do you shave your heads?

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** To keep a “cool” head. \[Then you grin from ear to ear, and devotees bliss out on your “in” joke.\]

Whenever I see the “I love your smile” sticker that devotees used to distribute in every mall and parking lot in America, I think of your childlike happy countenance that you radiate when you talk about Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so much fun; you are so heavy; you are so direct. In your words and in the videos I can experience your many moods. You are a jewel with an infinite number of facets. If I could truly see with transcendental eyes, I could continue to experience those many aspects of your personality in the body that you bequeathed to anyone willing to partake, the International Society of Krishna Consciousness. Yes, you still breathe all the life that lives in that body. It only exists because of you. No matter what happens in its growth and development, you are present to continue to guide it.

On a walk from the temple in Māyāpur, you pointed to some plants and asked what they were. “They are only weeds,” replied one of the devotees. “They have no value.” “Everything has a value,” you said, “but…you don’t know how to utilize it.” In your ISKCON, every experience has a value—more than a material value: a transcendental value. I pray that you give me the eyes to see the spiritual value in all that I see and do so that I can use it properly in your service. Then I will truly learn the most profound meaning of “personalism.”

This year at your Vyāsa-pūjā, ITV offered you the completed “New World of Hare Kṛṣṇa.” You inspired (and financed) the first movies in ISKCON, and we pray to be able to offer you many more. All of us at ITV thank you for the opportunity to render some insignificant service to your divine lotus feet. We beg to remain

Your fallen servants at ISKCON Television (ITV).  
(written by Nṛsiṁhānanda dāsa)

## ISKCON Women’s Ministry

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We set pen to paper, fingers to keyboard, to offer you our respect, gratitude, and love. Your words and example, imbued with spiritual potency and unadulterated by material concepts, have given purpose to purposeless lives, morals to the immoral, proper action where there had been only impropriety.

One day we may understand the opulence you generously and graciously offered us. We may understand how our debt to you is beyond calculation, beyond our ability to reciprocate.

This day, the day of your appearance, we simply remain grateful, we remain at your feet, and we continue to hear from you with all the attention we can muster. By your mercy we may become aware of our Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. And that, you told us, will please you the most.

Your servants at the ISKCON Women’s Ministry.

## New Vrajamaṇḍala Gurukula

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories eternally to you!

Mañjarī (five years) softly pulls my jacket. “May I offer the prayer today, please?” Every day, after our day of school, the children offer you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, a free prayer. These little boys and girls are the children of your grandsons and granddaughters, a new generation that also lives in the flow of your mercy.

Mañjarī starts: “Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us such a beautiful school, for giving us Rādhā-Govinda Candra, for allowing us to live with the devotees, for these trees, for my friends, and for the *prasādam*. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are so nice. When I am older, please help me to be your devotee. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!”

Our school is in your farm in Spain, New Vrajamaṇḍala. It is a very beautiful farm, and the children feel naturally very happy here. Some of them are very young, but they already understand that everything we have comes from you, that you have been Kṛṣṇa’s instrument to give spiritual birth to their parents and friends, and that you are the sweet and wonderful granddad that will always be available whenever they need help.

Dearest Prabhupāda, we can only speculate about what our future would have been if your books and disciples had not come across our way, but we can say for certain that our destiny was going to be dark and tragic. Now we are under your protection, safe as long as we follow your instructions.

Please engage us in trying to help the new generations of your followers. Please help us remain always humble in your service.

Unlimitedly thankful,  
Your servants at the New Vrajamaṇḍala Gurukula, Spain.

## New Vrindavan

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

On this most auspicious occasion, we followers of Your Divine Grace wish to glorify you to the highest extent of our frail human capacity. Such praise of you is never enough, never fully descriptive of the glorious truth of your personality. Still we beg you to please accept this effort to honor you on this holiest day of your divine appearance.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for fulfilling the order of your Guru Mahārāja to preach the message of Mahāprabhu in English throughout the world.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for crossing the Atlantic at an old age on the *Jaladuta,* undergoing so many hardships. You wrote beautiful verses to your dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, asking to be a puppet in his hands, to dance at his command.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for persevering through cold New York blizzards, and through having to step over Bowery bums in the hallway, and living with a crazy boy on LSD, and using refrigerators with meat in them, and through so many other hardships.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for planting the seed in the “Matchless Gifts” storefront, for making sure your boys and girls had plenty of “ISKCON bullets” for fighting Māyā. Thank you for giving *kīrtana* to the hippies and seekers who were looking for something higher than LSD—and the *kīrtana* kept them “high” forever, no more coming down. Thank you for making the love feast with a dozen delicious preps, and for giving initiation to several boys who left after the ceremony, leaving you to clean up.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for leading your disciples eight blocks to Tompkins Square Park amidst the jeering and cat-calls. Thank you for sitting on the oriental carpet under the large oak tree and starting *harināma-saṅkīrtana* in America. Thank you for singing in a loud, sonorous voice for all to hear, as many hippie musicians joined in with Congo drums, bamboo flutes, mouth organs, wooden clackers, tambourines, and guitars. Brahmānanda and Acyutānanda danced in ecstasy for hours, doing “the Swami step.” Thank you for bringing Lord Gaurāṅga’s movement to this country of meat-eating, drug-taking, sex-mad low-born persons, who were somehow still eligible to receive your mercy.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for not compromising with the Goldsmiths and Ginsbergs and the Fugs on the issue of sexual “freedom.” Thank you for not changing the philosophy for the Americans.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for leaving New York to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to San Francisco. Your New York boys were shocked to hear of your planned departure and tried to dissuade you. They could not conceive of Kṛṣṇa consciousness going beyond the Lower East Side. Thank you for meeting the hippie reception at the airport and chanting at the Mantra Rock concert to all the crazed hippies. Thank you for igniting the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement on the West Coast and all over the world.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for living forever in your books. Your books are supremely powerful and are nondifferent from yourself. They can deliver all the fallen souls for the next ten thousand years. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We know that to glorify you is our duty and our highest pleasure. We also have come to understand that to glorify you with words is not enough. Although our sentiment is sincere and true, still, the highest praise we can offer you is to obey you with our actions and deeds. Our words are nice and fitting upon this holiest of days, but the ultimate question is this, our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda: Are we obeying you with all our heart and soul?

To really please you, and to really honor you, is to obey you. We want to be in the first of the two categories described in your sublime essay to your Guru Mahārāja: “Some obey him wholeheartedly, while others do not obey him directly.”

Now, more than ever before, we find the absolute urgency to reassess our motives, to take inventory within our hearts and consult with the *caittya-guru:* Are we following the instructions of Your Divine Grace? We must examine ourselves, both individually and collectively, to make sure we are following your will. Only then will we please you and be happy. Unless we meditate on your orders, your words, twenty-four hours a day, how can any of us be happy? If we are feeling any anxiety or hankering, then it means we have not taken your orders to heart as we should.

We at the New Vrindavan community are aware more than ever of the absolute necessity of adhering to the will of our divine master. We are no different from the rest of collective ISKCON in the hard lessons we sometimes have to learn from even the slightest deviation from the divine teachings given in your books and personal instructions. The wonderful devotees who live here are very intelligent and sincere, and they are very determined to follow your instructions to the letter, more than ever. We also pray that the collective society and leaders also see that absolute necessity of following Your Divine Grace strictly, thus avoiding the unpleasant school of hard knocks, the thorny road so many of us have traveled.

First are your basic orders. Chant sixteen rounds and follow the four regs. We have to do this. Once asked what your highest pleasure was, you replied that you simply wanted us to love Kṛṣṇa. To distribute books was the highest priority, but you said most important is that we love Kṛṣṇa. You explain this in your purports to the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Ādi-līlā*, i.e., that the spiritual master does not want money from the disciple but that he is most pleased when the disciple becomes advanced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and gets love of God. Also, your main order is to be merciful to others. You want us to distribute this Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others by book distribution, *hari-nāma, prasādam* distribution, etc.

We know that to really please you and to become advanced and get love of Kṛṣṇa, we first of all must follow all your orders and instructions. We cannot imagine advancement or success. Success comes only with obedience to you, nothing else.

Your most basic instruction was “you’re not your body.” Some of us wonder why you hammered us on this, over and over. Some of us take this in a simplistic way, thinking we’ve got it down. Sadly enough, most of us have a long way to go. The covering of *ahaṅkāra,* which causes “I, me, and mine,” is very thick. So we must meditate on this instruction without being overconfident. It is not that easy, as you made it clear so many times.

We are gathered together today on this most glorious day in the history of the human race, the appearance of the greatest Vaiṣṇava the world has ever seen. It is our duty to glorify you as the greatest personality and broadcast this message everywhere on the planet. May we glorify you with word and deed. May we act in the way that we speak. May we honor you as our spiritual father by following your will and instructions. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at New Vrindavan, West Virginia, USA.  
(written by Viśoka dāsa Adhikārī)

## The Prabhupāda Trust

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the purified dust of your divine lotus feet.

As the world *ācārya,* Śrīla Prabhupāda, you initiated everything in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and everything spiritual in the devotees’ lives—including transcendental book distribution. As a member of the original BBT Library Party, I learned that you not only put copies of your original Delhi-printed, brick-colored, and rexin-covered three-volume *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* translations into some of the New Delhi libraries we visited, but you also distributed someone else’s books as well—many years earlier. Someone very important!

It was 1976, and we had just come down to Vṛndāvana from Kashmir, where a Muslim university took a full set of your then-published books, and a standing order for future volumes. When I told you this you smiled broadly. “Muslim!” you said, and you became excited in a way that was very wonderful to see—and feel. You were especially pleased whenever your books were appreciated by the scholars and residents of certain places, including the Muslim areas and China. While we felt so fortunate to be doing this service, it was abundantly clear that we were really doing nothing: you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, like Lord Rāma, were inspiring your Library Party monkey soldiers to act—and then you were giving the credit to us (to myself and Vaiyāsaki in this case)!

Eyes wide (and brightening the world), you said, “There is a tunnel!” It was amazing to learn that you knew of that long tunnel on top of the Himalayan foothills, which one must pass through to reach the Vedic-described “heavenly valley” of Kashmir. We began to discuss it, and you seemed so interested in all the details: Just before entering the tunnel, I had seen a sign noting its height in meters above sea level, and I told you this, but you knew it very well and smiled, correcting me with another number.

“How did you know that place, Śrīla Prabhupāda?”

“I know that tunnel,” you said, “because I went to Kashmir to distribute the books of my spiritual master!”

You started book distribution, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as well as traveling *saṅkīrtana,* by distributing the powerfully authoritative books (*Brahma-saṁhitā* among others?) of your own spiritual master!

And your mercy and inspiration just keep increasing, surcharging and enlivening the devotees.

When the noble swan slips its feathered head into a placid lake, wavelets gently ripple outward in ever-widening circles and ever-diminishing strength, until they finally reach the shoreline. But when you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the pure *paramahaṁsa* swanlike devotee, entered within this material pool, not only did you not get wet—although walking among us, your pure consciousness was always pure, sublime, and fully transcendental to the illusory energy—but those sweet wavelets of nectarean Kṛṣṇa consciousness that you initiated have been actually increasing in size with the passage of time! This only proves to your devotees the incredible vitality of your continued guidance, inspiration, and spiritual presence. More conditioned souls than ever are being given the opportunity to awaken their dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness by associating with your transcendental literatures. And those waves you started with your spiritual touch will continue to grow and crest until they reach tsunami-like proportions—and then they will get even larger!

Please give us the strength and intelligence to someday engage in devotional service with some level of serious determination. There is nothing more interesting or exciting in this world than the universal mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Please continue to rain your causeless mercy on us so that we may someday realize this, so that someday we may bring you a spark of spiritual happiness.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Śrīla Prabhupāda ki jaya!!!

Your eternal servants at The Prabhupāda Trust.  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa dāsa)

## Prabhupāda Village

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day of your appearance in this material world! You are our guide and hope in this Kali-yuga. May we somehow please you with our service.

We have gathered here in the foothills of North Carolina to become a fulfillment of your vision for a self-sufficient farming community. We are a product of your desire and are eager to serve you in whatever way you see fit. We, who have come together from many different rooms of your ISKCON house, have a gift for you, one that shows our love for you and each other—our cooperation amongst ourselves. This gift will be given again and again as time and situations test our determination and resolve.

We are seeds of your vision for *varṇāśrama,* eager to grow, waiting for the gentle rain of your mercy, nourished by your instructions, in which we are planted. We have sprouted in the warm soil of association. You can see that we are maturing as each devotee that comes fits into their natural *varṇa,* and being thus properly situated has the freedom to develop their Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And thus our lives are simplified, our needs basic, and our preaching potent.

Under your watchful eye, please keep us safe from the disease of diversion. Please open our hearts to hear your guidance. Let us become your arms and legs, your voice, for fulfilling your mission of establishing *varṇāśrama*. Let us do for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, what you need to get done. Let us help you by being an example, let us be ready to engage those who come to the farms, and let us thus be instrumental in establishing Kṛṣṇa consciousness for the next ten thousand years. Our lives are for you to use.

Your servants at Prabhupāda Village, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina, USA.  
(written by Hara-kāntā-devī dāsī)

## Radio Krishna Centrale

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you! And all glories to Śrī Gaurāṅga and Śrī Nityānanda, the Lords in the heart of the pure devotees!

Out of an unlimited amount of good fortune I met Your Divine Grace, my lord and master. All of a sudden you appeared and reached the innermost location of my heart (where I, the soul proper, was lying down, frozen and unconscious) and decided to make your station there.

You didn’t bother to take into consideration whether that habitat was appropriate or not. It was unclean, cold, and dark. Still, out of your causeless and unconditional love you decided to remain. Full of kindness, you lifted me up in your compassionate warm embrace and injected new life into me, shaking me from my deadly slumber.

Your splendor dispelled the darkness obscuring my cavelike heart, chasing away all the bats and bugs (sinful reactions) that had been nourishing themselves on my blood (consciousness) since time immemorial. My eyes began to open, my ears began to hear, and my real heart began to pulsate. A new birth. I was initiated to follow you on the way out, back home, trying to apply your instructions and directions the way you wanted me to. My heart started to tickle, and I felt the desire to please you increasing.

Then, all of a sudden, just as you had come, you disappeared. I could not believe it. I closed my eyes and, in a moment, everything dropped down into darkness again.

Fear grabbed my heart and I heard the scary noise of the bats and bugs coming back! And not alone even! But followed by other sneaky dangers. “Unfortunately, when the *ācārya* disappears, rogues and nondevotees take advantage and immediately begin to introduce unauthorized principles in the name of so-called *svāmīs, yogīs,* philanthropists, welfare workers and so on.” I run here and there, trying to hide. I called you in anguish and devastation. I tried to find you in every corner.

Then I found you.

You had never left.

Just by my remembering Your Divine Grace, you were there. Remembrance of your divine lotus feet destroyed all difficulties. I opened my eyes, and while serving your instructions I observed all the bats, bugs, and dangers flush away, like fog in the sunlight. “Devotional service is necessary not to drive away an insignificant stock of sins, but to awaken our dormant love for Kṛṣṇa. As fog is vanquished at the first glimpse of sunlight, one’s sinful reactions are automatically vanquished as soon as one begins serving a pure devotee; no separate endeavor is required.”

Śrī Gaurahari, the Lords of the lords, most powerful and magnanimous, resides in everyone’s heart. By His power He can make one remember or forget. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, even if I am a useless fallen soul, please remain in my heart, please don’t ever leave it. Transform it into a temple. I’ll keep cleaning it again and again, so that you’ll be pleased to watch my love for Kṛṣṇa awaking.

Please make me your eternal servant.

Your servants at Radio Krishna Centrale, Terni, Italy.  
(written by Tridaṇḍi dāsa)

## Rūpānuga Vedic College (Vidyāpīṭha)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On January 12, 1969, you wrote in a letter:

So you have now taken charge of the sunrise of New Vrindaban. Our program is there for constructing seven temples. One Rupanuga Vidyapitha—that is a school for educating brahmanas and Vaisnavas. We have enough of technological and other types of educational institutions, but perhaps there is none where actual brahmanas and Vaisnavas are produced. So we will have to establish an educational institution for that purpose.

This desire of yours to establish an educational institution at New Vrindavan is the driving force behind the Rūpānuga Vedic College (Vidyāpīṭha). The RVC is an institution for college education in Vedic culture as you propounded it. It is academically affiliated with The Florida Vedic College (FVC), thus enabling us to offer college credit courses.

The RVC was established as a Centennial tribute to you in the year 1996. The campus, situated atop a hill overlooking the beautiful countryside, has classroom facilities and a Vedic library and offers the following academic program:

Devotional Degrees:

- Bhakti Śāstri
- Bhakti Vaibhava
- Bhakti Vedānta
- Bhakti Sārvabhauma
- Purohita

Academic Degrees:

- Vaiṣṇava Minister
- Bachelor of Vaiṣṇava Education
- Bachelor of Vaiṣṇava Philosophy
- Master of Vaiṣṇava Education
- Master of Vaiṣṇava Philosophy
- Master of Vaiṣṇava Administration
- Doctor of Divinity in Vaiṣṇavism

The RVC has already finished the first semester with about thirty students. There will be a special summer semester for 1997. Expansion plans could include branch campuses in Columbus, Ohio, and Miami, Florida.

In conclusion, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that the RVC will fulfill your desire for a Vedic college in New Vrindavan. In a letter you wrote on January 21, 1973, you said, “So if you study my books very carefully, then I am sure you will find out the means for applying this Krishna Consciousness philosophy in all spheres of life. There is no limitation.” You also emphasized many times that “good preaching depends on studing my books with seriousness.” Despite our limited resourses, the RVC is expanding beyond our expectations. We humbly offer this college and its progress at your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants at the Rūpānuga Vedic College (Vidyāpīṭha) in New Vrindavan, West Virginia.  
(written by Bhakta Ram Tulsiani)

## Śaraṇāgati

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You are the object of meditation for many, many thousands of people. The volume of prayers, thoughts, and activities dedicated to you is immense. Somewhere in that deep river of devotional service are the offerings from the devotees of Śaraṇāgati farm.

Our work here started with a gift. Kṛṣṇa gave us 1,700 acres of land. Now our goal is to transform a forested valley in British Columbia, Canada, into an offering. Year by year we are changing the face of this formerly unpopulated and unkept landscape. It is becoming a village of Vaiṣṇava devotees. Our technique is to work cooperatively, follow the religious principles, and happily sing the names of God.

Not only the land but the people themselves and everything they produce from this land is an offering to you. Every fruit, grain, and vegetable, every drop of milk, yogurt, and ghee, every moment of labor and every “Hari-bol”—all are part of this offering. And every dream for the future of this village is also an offering to you.

Let our small stream of devotional service join with other offerings from all directions. Let them together form a huge cascading waterfall of sparkling devotional service. And let every shimmering droplet be an expression of eternal gratitude to you for saving our lives.

Hare Kṛṣṇa. Thank you.

Your servants in Śaraṇāgati, British Columbia, Canada.

## Śrī Rūpānuga Paramārthika Vidyāpīṭha

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories to you! We offer you our prostrated obeisances again and again.

The disciple can never fully repay the debt he owes to the person who has brought him in sight of the greatest treasure, the shade of the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. How much more indebted are we to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who have given the matchless gift of *bhakti* to the whole world, to people who, in your own words, were “covered by the modes of passion and ignorance, thinking themselves very happy and satisfied absorbed in material life, with no taste for Vāsudeva kathā.” On your prayer, Kṛṣṇa made you dance, and what a dance it was! From 1965 to 1977 you danced tirelessly across the globe, crushing the rearing hoods of Kali—all varieties of sinful acts, voidism, impersonalism, etc.—just as your Lord Kṛṣṇa had done to venomous Kāliya. Your dance captivated the world, and now more and more *baddha-jīvas* are becoming enraptured by your dance and the dance of your followers.

Here in the Śrī Rūpānuga Vidyāpīṭha in Māyāpur, we are trying to please Your Divine Grace by training devotees, young and old, in cultural and specialized aspects of Vaiṣṇava life. Without pleasing you there is no meaning to our spiritual lives, no chance to go back to Godhead, no chance to taste transcendental bliss. You were always grateful to your father for training you as a Vaiṣṇava in your boyhood, so by trying to do the same for your second- and third-generation followers, we are hopeful that you will smile on our attempts and that we will one day be able to join you in your eternal service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava in Goloka. Begging for your mercy and eternal service,
